# Arbitrary negative factors: structured compatibility route

Status: active research route, not yet a proof of Problem 993.  **Critical
correction (2026-08-13):** Sections 59.4, 61, and 104 used Laguerre's polar-
derivative theorem with the polar point inside the same rotating half-plane
as the polynomial zeros.  The theorem gives preservation when the polar
point is outside that circular region, not inside it.  An exact special-
Meixner counterexample now shows that the claimed automatic second-polar
step is genuinely false even in the proposed near-strip chamber.  Therefore
the arbitrary-factor fixed-ceiling/sector theorem formerly claimed in
Sections 59--61 is reopened; the base sector theorem of Sections 59.1--59.3
and the repeated-Meixner fixed-disk theorem of Sections 55--57 are not
refuted.  Sections 62--82 audit the forest selector.  The all-order local
Poincare/Turan obligation, the odd
four-vertex path block, and the direct selector root theorem are now proved.
The oriented diagonal gamma root chain (62.4) is unnecessary.  A clean-room
audit now shows, however, that this closes the individual upper homogeneous
rows, not the full forest theorem.  Sections 83--84 reduce every shifted
lower layer to one artificial-row stability theorem and prove the full raw
two-pair selector Strongly Rayleigh, while also ruling out its naive collapsed
determinant transport.  Section 88 proves the coupled binomial normalization
on exchangeable slots and rules out every parent with an independent grade
extraction.  Section 89 extends the lower selector's unit-interval exclusion
and reduces its remaining root count to one coefficientwise coherent-pencil
Wronskian theorem; an exact differential identity shows that the same theorem
also supplies the formerly separate all-range Turan inequality.  Section 90
identifies the minimal artificial ceiling with the raw degree-four selector and
isolates the exact path-allocation transport still missing.  Sections 85--86
prove both original affine coefficient families at `r=0` in both parities,
and Section 87 reduces every `r>=1` coefficient to one Newton difference / one
local boundary-triple inequality.  Section 91 proves both shifted boundary
predecessors at `r=0`; its initially proposed termwise planar-layer
strengthening is now exactly refuted, while the required compensated sum
remains positive in the first large obstruction and has an Euler-transfer,
single-crossing, and disjoint reflection-pair normalization.  A direct
lower-layer Duran calculation now proves the second exceptional-root margin
in all orders, while root-free cubic and quartic certificates prove the first
margin through lower depth four.  The general first margin has an exact
Schur--Toeplitz moment reduction; a proposed single-tail-minor shortcut works
except at one known cell in the complete `d<=50` audit, while a fixed
three-minor repair passes all 53,777 exact cells in that full lower diamond.
The unequal-polar identity isolates a candidate low-depth region (104.3),
covering 17,297 of 54,050 transcription cells through `d<=50`, but the polar-
preservation error reopens that entire region as well as its unbounded
high-depth complement.
The lower artificial-row theorem
(equivalently, its unbounded-depth first Duran margin), the compensated affine
boundary-triple inequality, and final protected-induction assembly remain
open.  The public-status search was refreshed again on 2026-08-20 and is
recorded in `FINAL_ROUTE_PUBLIC_STATUS_REFRESH_2026-08-20.md`; it still finds
Problem 993 open.  The earlier route-specific literature audit remains in
`FINAL_ROUTE_LITERATURE_AND_SCHUR_LIFT_AUDIT_2026-08-13.md`.

## 1. Shift family and factor update

For a fixed input `Gamma`, put

\[
F_j(t)=S_{p-2j,\alpha+j}[\Gamma](t),\qquad G_j(t)=t^jF_j(t).
\]

The proved factorial recursion gives, simultaneously for every `j`,

\[
G_j^{+}=cG_j+G_{j+1}                                      \tag{1}
\]

when `Gamma` is replaced by `(t+c)Gamma`.  Therefore the pair needed at the
next induction step is

\[
\binom{G_0^{+}}{G_1^{+}}
=
\begin{pmatrix}G_1&G_0\\G_2&G_1\end{pmatrix}
\binom{1}{c}.                                               \tag{2}
\]

The coefficient vector generated by several appended factors is a Pólya-frequency
sequence: its generating polynomial is `prod(t+c_i)`, with nonnegative coefficients
and only negative roots.

## 2. Relevant preservation theorems

Athanasiadis--Wagner, *Veronese sections and interlacing matrices of polynomials
and formal power series*, arXiv:2404.12989, defines fully interlacing matrices by
total positivity of their laced coefficient matrix.  Their Theorem 4.3 proves that
matrix products preserve this property.  Example 4.4(b)--(c) turns this into a
convolution/interlacing theorem for fully interlacing polynomial sequences.

Savage--Visontai, *The s-Eulerian polynomials have only real roots*,
arXiv:1208.3831, Theorem 2.3, proves a related compatibility-preserving recurrence
under the two hypotheses that `f_i,f_j` and `t f_i,f_j` are compatible for `i<j`.
Corollary 2.6 gives a coefficient-ratio version.

These theorems fit the bidiagonal form (1), but only after finding the correct
ordered grid or refined family.  Merely knowing compatibility of each adjacent
pair is insufficient.

## 3. Naive full-interlacing strengthening is false

The symmetric Hankel grid `(G_(i+j))` cannot be fully interlacing.  Already at
`(p,alpha,u,v)=(13,0,1/2,4/5)` for the quadratic input
`Gamma=(1-ut)(1-vt)`, the same-degree polynomials `G_0` and `G_2` are not
compatible: `G_0+100 G_2` has only four real roots out of degree six.  At
`(21,4,1/3,3/4)`, `G_0+100 G_2` has eight real roots out of degree ten.

The coefficient-block obstruction is visible even earlier.  In the symmetric
orientation, the degree-one block has determinant

\[
[t]G_0\,[t]G_2-[t]G_1^2=-169
\]

in the first exact sample.  Reversing the columns as in (2) changes this to the
positive Turán orientation, but a fully interlacing `2 by q` grid would still imply
real-rootedness for arbitrary nonnegative weights, which the `G_0+100G_2`
example disproves.

The exact replay is `probe_naive_full_interlacing_obstruction.py`, with report
`naive_full_interlacing_obstruction_exact_20260806.json`.

Thus arbitrary factors must use the **PF constraint on the weight sequence**, not
the full nonnegative cone.

## 4. Precise next target

Prove a PF-convolution version of the adjacent-pair theorem:

> If the shift family for the two-positive-root input is evaluated against any
> finite PF coefficient sequence `a=(a_0,...,a_m)`, then
> \[
> Q_0=\sum_j a_jG_j,\qquad Q_1=\sum_j a_jG_{j+1}
> \]
> has the common-interlacer overlap required for `Q_0` and `Q_1`.

Here `a` is not arbitrary: `sum a_j z^j=prod(z+c_i)`.  Equation (1) then makes
the claim inductive and gives all appended negative factors.  The adjacent-cubic
theorem is exactly the length-two PF instance.  The first new finite structural
test is the length-three cone `a_1^2>=4a_0a_2`, rather than the whole positive
orthant.

The immediate computational experiment should therefore audit the two overlap
inequalities over the normalized cone

\[
(a_0,a_1,a_2)=(q^2,2q+z,1),\qquad q>0,\ z\ge0,
\]

for the full shifted pair, and inspect whether its collision polynomial retains a
fixed-rank tail/resultant description.  A success would turn the arbitrary-factor
gap into an induction on PF Toeplitz multiplication; a failure would reject this
route without affecting the adjacent-cubic theorem.

## 5. Length-three kernel invariant and corrected collision target

The first exact PF audit passes.  At sharp reserve `p-alpha=17`, 1,296 rational
parameter cases through reserve index eight satisfy 14,904 exact common-interlacer
overlaps.  The report is `pf_length3_adjacent_compatibility_exact_audit_20260806.json`.

There is a sharper collision reduction.  At a proposed common root `x`, write
`g_j=G_j(x)` and

\[
D_0=g_1^2-g_0g_2,\qquad D_2=g_2^2-g_1g_3,\qquad
E=g_0g_3-g_1g_2.
\]

The two common-root equations force

\[
                  (a_0,a_1,a_2)\ \doteq\ (D_2,E,D_0).       \tag{3}
\]

Consequently the PF constraint is exactly

\[
                         H=E^2-4D_0D_2\ge0,                 \tag{4}
\]

together with `D_0,D_2,E` having one sign.  This eliminates the appended-factor
parameters from the collision test.

Uniform nonvanishing of (3) is false.  At
`(p,alpha,u,v)=(23,6,1/10,1)` there is an exact positive-PF collision at

\[
x=-55365151838956793227/2432902493487393708.
\]

Both polynomials have exactly one root strictly below `x`, and their derivative
product at `x` is positive.  Thus the same indexed second-root branches meet and
the collision is benign.  The exact replay is
`certify_pf_length3_benign_collision.py`, with report
`pf_length3_benign_collision_exact_20260806.json`.

The corrected all-order target is therefore the branch implication

\[
D_0,D_2,E\text{ one-signed},\quad H\ge0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
A(x)B(x)>0,                                                   \tag{5}
\]

where

\[
A=D_2G_0'+EG_1'+D_0G_2',\qquad
B=D_2G_1'+EG_2'+D_0G_3'.                                     \tag{6}
\]

Positive `AB` says that every PF common root is a same-index collision.  An exact
root-cell audit through reserve index four finds six open PF collision cells and six
repeated-factor boundary collisions; every one satisfies (5).  Its report is
`pf_length3_nullvector_invariant_exact_audit_20260806.json`.  Proving (5) uniformly,
not excluding all collisions, is the immediate quintic-window obligation.

The same audit localizes all six open collisions further: they are second-root
collisions, and every collision point lies strictly to the left of the ground root
of the source-one Jacobi row.  On that far-left interval, if the source roots are
`rho_i` and `z=-x`, then

\[
X_j(x)=(-1)^{n+j}|X_0(x)|\,R_j,qquad
R_j=\left(\prod_{k<j}\eta_k\right)j!\,
e_j\left({1\over\rho_1-x},\ldots,{1\over\rho_n-x}\right)>0.
\]

Moreover, with `s=u+v` and `q=uv`,

\[
G_j(x)=(-1)^n z^j|X_0(x)|P_j,qquad
P_j=R_j-szR_{j+1}+qz^2R_{j+2}.                              \tag{7}
\]

Thus the remaining implication (5) can be attacked as a finite total-positivity
inequality for the elementary-symmetric sequence `R_j`, rather than as a growing
degree polynomial in `x`.  Establishing the far-left localization and then the
required `3 by 3` minor signs in (7) is the current analytic route.

## 6. Riccati collapse of the far-left variables

The elementary-symmetric variables in (7) are not independent.  Put

\[
 X_j=S_{p-2j,\alpha+j}[1],\qquad
 \theta_j=x{X_{j+1}(x)\over X_j(x)},\qquad x=-z.
\]

The source row with current parameters `P=p-2j`, `A=alpha+j` obeys the
hypergeometric differential equation

\[
 D(D+A)X_j=t(P-2D)(P-2D-1)X_j,qquad D=t{d\over dt}.
\]

At `t=-z`, use `D X_j/X_j=theta_j/eta_j` and

\[
 {D^2X_j\over X_j}={\theta_j\over\eta_j}
 +{\theta_j\theta_{j+1}\over\eta_j\eta_{j+1}}.
\]

The ODE then gives the first-order Riccati recursion

\[
 \theta_j\theta_{j+1}={\eta_{j+1}\over1+4z}
 \left(\{z(4P-6)-(A+1)\}\theta_j-z(P+A)\right).       \tag{8}
\]

Consequently the quadratic row quotient is affine in one Riccati variable:

\[
 {Y_j\over X_j}=1-(u+v)\theta_j+uv\theta_j\theta_{j+1}. \tag{9}
\]

Starting from `theta_0`, equation (8) generates every later row value.  The
exact derivative identity

\[
 {d\theta_j\over dx}={\theta_j\over x}
 \left(1+{\theta_{j+1}\over\eta_{j+1}}
          -{\theta_j\over\eta_j}\right)                 \tag{10}
\]

then generates the normalized derivatives as well.  Thus `D0,D2,E,H,A,B`
are rational functions of only

\[
                  (p,\alpha,z,\theta_0,u,v),              \tag{11}
\]

not of seven formally independent `R_j` variables.  The exact replay is
`verify_pf_length3_riccati_affine_reduction.py`; its report
`pf_length3_riccati_affine_reduction_exact_20260807.json` has status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_RICCATI_AFFINE_REDUCTION`.

There is a further useful sign observation.  Write

\[
 K_j={G_j\over X_0}\bigg|_{\Gamma=1}
     =\prod_{k<j}\theta_k,qquad
 h_j={G_j\over X_0}=K_j\{1-(u+v)\theta_j+uv\theta_j\theta_{j+1}\}.
\]

At every exact PF collision cell currently known, the four normalized
derivatives `h_0',...,h_3'` are all strictly negative.  Since the kernel
weights `(D2,E,D0)` are all positive there, both collision derivatives are
then negative immediately.  The six exact cells at
`(p,alpha)=(20,3),(22,5),(23,6),(24,7),(25,8),(26,9)`, `u=1/10,v=1`, all
have the same endpoint topology

\[
                H=0\quad\longrightarrow\quad D_2=0,       \tag{12}
\]

the same normalized derivative sign vector `(-,-,-,-)`, and a positive
derivative product.  This replaces the raw `A B` inequality by the sharper
candidate lemma

> On the actual far-left Jacobi Weyl branch, the positive-PF minor conditions
> force `h_0',h_1',h_2',h_3'<0`.

The diagnostic `inspect_pf_length3_collision_cells.py` records the exact
endpoint sources, row signs, derivative signs, and root indices.  The
Riccati reduction and the finite derivative pattern are progress, not yet an
all-order proof of the displayed candidate lemma or of far-left localization.

One part of that candidate lemma is now uniform.  If the current source row
has positive root magnitudes `a_i` and `z>max a_i`, put

\[
 r_i={z\over z-a_i}>1,qquad f=\sum_i r_i,qquad
 g=2e_2(r_1,\ldots,r_m).
\]

Then `theta_j=eta_j f` and
`theta_j theta_(j+1)=eta_j eta_(j+1) g`.  Under the far-left flow,
the numerator of the derivative of `g'/f'` is twice

\[
 W^3-3PW+2N^2+NW,
\]

where

\[
 W=\sum r_i(r_i-1),\quad N=\sum r_i^2(r_i-1),\quad
 P=\sum r_i^3(r_i-1).
\]

Writing `b_i=r_i-1>0`, this has the stable monomial-symmetric expansion

\[
6m_{111}+2m_{21}+6m_{211}+8m_{22}+2m_{31}
+6m_{221}+6m_{32}+6m_{222}+4m_{33}>0.              \tag{13}
\]

Thus the Riccati slope increases toward the largest source root.  Evaluating
there with the hypergeometric ODE and using the four current reserve gaps
`17,14,11,8` gives

\[
 {d(\theta_j\theta_{j+1})\over d\theta_j}<2,
 \qquad 0\le j\le3.                                  \tag{14}
\]

Since `uv/(u+v)<=1/2`, (14) proves

\[
 {d\over dx}\left({Y_j\over X_j}\right)
 =\theta_j'\left(-(u+v)+uv,
 {d(\theta_j\theta_{j+1})\over d\theta_j}\right)<0. \tag{15}
\]

The exact certificate is `verify_pf_length3_riccati_slope_bound.py`, with
report `pf_length3_riccati_slope_bound_exact_20260807.json` and status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_RICCATI_SLOPE_BOUND`.  Equation (15) proves that the
row quotient decreases.  The still-missing step is to control the additional
positive factor `K_j=x^jX_j/X_0` at a PF collision, i.e. to upgrade (15) to
`h_j'<0` for all four rows.

## 7. Why the two nearby literature theorems do not close the gap

Guo's higher-order Turan inequality has the sign

\[
4D_0D_2-E^2\ge0.
\]

Our PF collision condition is the reverse sign `H=E^2-4D0D2>=0`.  Thus the
Laguerre--Polya coefficient theorem excludes rather than covers the open
collision cells.

Karp's Wronskian characterization is an equivalence for a complete nested
flag.  The collision determinants here form only a partial confluent Hankel
fragment.  Karp explicitly notes that the reverse implication fails for
general partial flags, so a total-positivity conclusion would require first
constructing the missing nested flag and proving all its Wronskians
nonvanishing.  No such construction is currently known here.  These papers
remain useful structural guides, but neither can be cited as the missing
orientation proof.

## 8. Factor the PF kernel: Wronskian/staircase reduction

There is a second exact reduction which uses the cubic theorem more directly.
At a positive PF collision write the kernel polynomial as

\[
                 z^2+(c+d)z+cd=(z+c)(z+d),\qquad c,d>0.
\]

The two common-root equations are

\[
 cd h_j+(c+d)h_{j+1}+h_{j+2}=0,\qquad j=0,1,       \tag{16}
\]

where `h_j=G_j/X_0`.  After appending the first factor put

\[
                         f_j=c h_j+h_{j+1}.
\]

Then (16) gives `f_(j+1)=-d f_j` at the collision.  If
`W(f,g)=fg'-f'g`, direct exact algebra gives

\[
 W(f_0,f_1)=f_0Q_0',\qquad W(f_1,f_2)=f_1Q_1',      \tag{17}
\]

and, more explicitly,

\[
 D_0=(ch_0+h_1)(dh_0+h_1),\quad
 D_2=cdD_0,\quad E=(c+d)D_0,\quad
 H=(c-d)^2D_0^2,                                     \tag{18}
\]

\[
 AB=-{(dh_0+h_1)^2\over d}\,
       W(f_0,f_1)W(f_1,f_2).                          \tag{19}
\]

Both adjacent pairs `(f_0,f_1)` and `(f_1,f_2)` are instances of the
already-proved all-order adjacent-cubic common-interlacer theorem (with the
second one taken at the shifted reserve).  Let `N_j(x)` count roots of `f_j`
strictly below the collision point.  The values `f_0,f_1,f_2` alternate in
sign, and adjacent common interlacers imply

\[
                         |N_0-N_1|=|N_2-N_1|=1.       \tag{20}
\]

The elementary local orientation rule for a common-interlacer pair says
`sign W(f_i,f_(i+1))=sign(N_i-N_(i+1))` after the common positive leading
coefficient normalization.  Consequently (19) proves

\[
 AB>0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad N_0=N_2.           \tag{21}
\]

Thus the full derivative inequality has been reduced to one discrete bad
topology: exclude the monotone staircases

\[
 (N_0,N_1,N_2)=(k+1,k,k-1)\quad\hbox{and}\quad
 (k-1,k,k+1).                                         \tag{22}
\]

The valley and peak patterns are automatically benign.  The exact generic
identity replay is `verify_pf_length3_wronskian_staircase_reduction.py`, with
report `pf_length3_wronskian_staircase_reduction_exact_20260807.json` and
status `PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_WRONSKIAN_STAIRCASE_REDUCTION`.  This does not
yet exclude (22), but it is strictly sharper than proving four separate
derivative signs: either far-left localization plus an outer-root count bound,
or a direct no-staircase lemma for the two shifted cubic pencils, now closes
the length-three step.

## 9. Exact outer Riccati supersolution theorem

The Riccati reduction has a second coordinate which is better adapted to
(22).  At fixed `z`, every source shift is affine in `T=theta_0`.  After
clearing the nested positive recurrence denominators write the two fully
filtered rows as

\[
                         L_m(z,T)=P_m(z)+Q_m(z)T,
                         \qquad m=0,1.                 \tag{23}
\]

Here the full filter is

\[
 (E+c)(E+d)(1-uE)(1-vE),\qquad c,d>0.
\]

Its two zero curves in the `(z,T)` plane are

\[
                         T_m^*(z)=-P_m(z)/Q_m(z).       \tag{24}
\]

Put `p=2r+17, alpha=2r` in odd parity and
`p=2r+18, alpha=2r+1` in even parity.  The exact certificate substitutes

\[
                              z=r+5+w,qquad w\ge0.
\]

If `F(z,T)` denotes the source Riccati vector field `dT/dz`, direct
elimination of (24) gives

\[
 F(z,T_m^*)-{dT_m^*\over dz}
 =-{\mathcal G_m\over
 z(1+4z)(p+\alpha)Q_m^2}.                              \tag{25}
\]

For each parity and each `m=0,1`, tensor Bernstein conversion in
`0<=u,v<=1` produces nine controls for `G_m`.  Every coefficient of every
control is nonnegative in the ordinary power basis in `r,w,c,d`, and every
control is strict for `c,d>0`.  Therefore

\[
                    F(z,T_m^*)-{dT_m^*\over dz}<0
                    \quad(z\ge r+5).                    \tag{26}
\]

This region contains the complete far-left source interval.  For even
parity, the average source-root magnitude is `(3r+10)/2 >= r+5`, with
strictness of the maximum.  For odd parity, the exact second power sum is

\[
 { (r+8)(3r+8)(6r^2+76r+173)\over180}.
\]

Its excess over `(r+8)(r+5)^2` is

\[
 { (r+8)(18r^3+96r^2-673r-3116)\over180}>0
 \qquad(r\ge6),                                       \tag{27}
\]

and the six cases `0<=r<=5` have exact opposite-sign evaluations at
`x=-(r+5)`.  Hence the largest source-root magnitude is always greater than
`r+5`.

The verifier is `verify_pf_length3_outer_riccati_supersolutions.py`; its
report `pf_length3_outer_riccati_supersolutions_exact_20260807.json` has
status `PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_OUTER_RICCATI_SUPERSOLUTIONS`.

At a far-left common collision, differentiating
`L_m=Q_m(T-T_m^*)` with `x=-z` and using (26) shows that the row derivative
has the sign of `Q_m`.  Thus all continuous slope estimates are now proved;
the remaining far-left orientation statement is the purely algebraic sign

\[
                              Q_0Q_1>0.                 \tag{28}
\]

By section 8, failure of (28) is exactly the monotone root-count staircase.
Far-left localization and (28), rather than the former four separate
`h_j'` inequalities, are the two remaining length-three obligations.

## 10. Exact outer orientation and staircase exclusion

The sign obligation (28) is now closed throughout the outer region.  Write
the four quadratically filtered rows, in the affine source coordinate, as

\[
                         h_j(T)=b_j+a_jT,
                         \qquad 0\le j\le3.             \tag{29}
\]

If `q_j>0` is the `T`-slope of the unfiltered source shift `K_j`, direct
elimination gives

\[
 a_{j+1}-{q_{j+2}\over q_{j+1}}a_j
 ={\Delta_j\over q_{j+1}},                              \tag{30}
\]
where, with `q=uv`,

\[
 \Delta_j=q_{j+1}^2-q_jq_{j+2}
          +q(q_{j+1}q_{j+3}-q_{j+2}^2).                 \tag{31}
\]

All source slopes `q_1,...,q_5` are coefficientwise positive after
`z=r+5+w`.  Exact tensor Bernstein certificates give `a_0<=0` and
`Delta_0,Delta_1>0` in both parities.  They also give `Delta_2>0` in even
parity and in odd parity after `r=1+R`.  The only exception is odd `r=0`:
on `0<=w<=200`, a compact Bernstein certificate still gives `Delta_2>0`,
while after `w=200+W`, the last slope `a_3` itself is uniformly positive.
Thus, wherever a collision has not already been excluded directly, the
signs of `a_0,...,a_3` form a negative prefix followed by a positive suffix.

For the determinant step set `s=u+v`, `q=uv` and define

\[
                         M_{ij}=b_i a_j-b_j a_i.         \tag{32}
\]

If the appended positive quadratic kernel has sum `C=c+d` and product
`D=cd`, the determinant of its two affine collision rows is exactly

\[
 \mathcal R=D^2M_{01}+CDM_{02}+D(M_{03}-M_{12})
             +C^2M_{12}+CM_{13}+M_{23}.                 \tag{33}
\]

Suppose `a_3>=0`.  Since `q_4>0`, this is equivalent to

\[
                  0\le s\le s_*={q_3+q q_5\over q_4}.  \tag{34}
\]

For each of the six atoms in (33), `-partial_s M` is affine in `s`.
Its values at `s=0` and `s=s_*`, after clearing the positive power of
`q_4`, have nonnegative tensor Bernstein controls.  Hence every atom is
nonincreasing in `s` on (34).  At `s=s_*`, all six atoms have strictly
positive certificates.  The sole raw coefficient exception, odd `M_23`,
is covered by `r>=1`, by a compact `r=0,w<=200` certificate, and by direct
positivity of all six actual `(u,v)` atoms on `r=0,w>=200`.  Therefore

\[
             a_3\ge0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
             M_{01},M_{02},M_{12},M_{13},M_{23},
             M_{03}-M_{12}>0.                            \tag{35}
\]

Equations (33) and (35) make a common collision impossible when `a_3>=0`.
At any outer-region common collision, therefore, `a_3<0`; (30) propagates
this backward and gives

\[
                         a_0,a_1,a_2,a_3<0.              \tag{36}
\]

Consequently

\[
 Q_0=Da_0+Ca_1+a_2<0,
 \qquad Q_1=Da_1+Ca_2+a_3<0,                            \tag{37}
\]

so `Q_0Q_1>0`.  Combining (37) with section 9 proves `AB>0` for every
far-left positive-PF length-three collision and excludes the monotone
staircase (22) there.

The exact verifier is
`verify_pf_length3_outer_orientation_dichotomy.py`; its report
`pf_length3_outer_orientation_dichotomy_exact_20260807.json` has status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_OUTER_ORIENTATION_DICHOTOMY`.

The only remaining length-three obligation is now localization: prove that
every relevant positive-PF collision lies on the far-left source branch.

## 11. Branch correction: the outer theorem is all-branch

The final sentence of section 10 is too strong: far-left localization is
false, and fortunately it is unnecessary.  At odd reserve index `r=10`,
`(p,alpha,u,v)=(37,20,1/10,1)`, exact rational root isolation finds two open
positive-PF collision cells.  One is far-left, sampled at

\[
 x=-{41107743357641934132843\over464997964908756262868}
   \simeq-88.4041360605,
\]

while the second has one source root strictly below it and is sampled at

\[
 x=-{5749822098730165997163\over274411430433773777800}
   \simeq-20.9532893351.                                  \tag{38}
\]

Both collision points nevertheless satisfy `z=-x>=r+5=15`, both final rows
have equal exact root counts below the collision, and both derivative
products are positive.  At `r=20` another exact non-far-left cell occurs at
`z approximately 60.0466>25`.  The replay is
`inspect_pf_length3_nonfar_collisions.py`.

The Riccati comparison applies on every source Weyl branch, not merely the
far-left one.  If an actual full row is written

\[
                 R_m={X_0\over\mathcal D_m}
                       L_m(z,T),\qquad L_m=P_m+Q_mT,
\]

then at a finite collision `T=T_m^*=-P_m/Q_m`, with `d/dx=-d/dz`,

\[
 {dR_m\over dx}
 =-{X_0Q_m\over\mathcal D_m}
      \left(F(z,T_m^*)-{dT_m^*\over dz}\right).          \tag{39}
\]

The two rows share the same factor `X_0`, and their clearing denominators
are positive.  Equations (26) and (37) therefore imply a positive derivative
product at **every** common collision with `z>=r+5`, independently of the
source branch.  A collision at a pole `X_0=0` would force `Q_0=Q_1=0`; the
determinant dichotomy of section 10 excludes this as well.

The dependency replay is
`verify_pf_length3_outer_all_branch_consequence.py`, with report
`pf_length3_outer_all_branch_consequence_exact_20260807.json` and status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_OUTER_ALL_BRANCH_CONSEQUENCE`.

Thus the corrected and strictly smaller remaining length-three obligation is

\[
 \boxed{\text{exclude positive-PF common collisions for }0<z<r+5.} \tag{40}
\]

Numerical finite-tail reconstruction gives a concrete route to (40).  After
the quartic Jacobi realization and Darboux step, the current and adjacent rows
share a long prefix and have respective tails of sizes four and three.  Every
common root must satisfy one degree-six tail-Weyl cross-product equation.  In
20,000 random all-order tests, every branch-relevant tail solution below the
floor had a strictly positive full-prefix residual; residual sign changes,
and the exact collisions above, occur only beyond the floor.  This is evidence,
not yet the required exact tail-residual sign theorem.

## 12. Inner Jensen/Turan reduction and the exact endpoint

The inner obligation (40) has a useful invariant form.  For four consecutive
quadratically filtered values put

\[
 D_0=h_1^2-h_0h_2,\qquad D_2=h_2^2-h_1h_3,\qquad
 E=h_0h_3-h_1h_2,
\]

and

\[
                         K=4D_0D_2-E^2.                       \tag{41}
\]

The cubic Jensen polynomial

\[
                  J(t)=h_0+3h_1t+3h_2t^2+h_3t^3              \tag{42}
\]

has the exact discriminant

\[
                         \operatorname{disc}J=27K.             \tag{43}
\]

Thus the desired conditional inequality is precisely hyperbolicity of this
one cubic.  This explains why the higher-order Turan inequality has exactly
the required algebraic shape, while also making clear that a generic
coefficient theorem cannot be invoked without proving its real-rooted-parent
hypothesis for the scaled contiguous window.

There is an even sharper sequential form.  Choose the first positive kernel
root `c` and put

\[
                         f_j=ch_j+h_{j+1}.                     \tag{44}
\]

Then

\[
 f_1^2-f_0f_2=D_0c^2-Ec+D_2=:W(c).                            \tag{45}
\]

At a zero of (45), provided `f_0` is nonzero, the second factor is forced to be

\[
                         d=-{f_1\over f_0}.                    \tag{46}
\]

The two full collision rows are then zero, and exact residual identities give

\[
 f_0\{D_0(c+d)-E\}=h_0W(c),\qquad
 f_0\{D_0cd-D_2\}=-h_1W(c).                                  \tag{47}
\]

Consequently a positive-PF collision is equivalently a **sign-relevant Turan
zero** of the already-understood cubic family: (45) together with `d>0`.
The repeated-factor boundary `c=d=E/(2D_0)` is equivalently a repeated positive
root of the reversed Jensen cubic

\[
                 h_3+3h_2c+3h_1c^2+h_0c^3.                  \tag{48}
\]

The homotopy has a strict exact starting side.  Write

\[
 L_j={p+2\alpha\choose\alpha+j}.
\]

At `z=0`, independently of `u,v`,

\[
 h_j=(-z)^jL_j+O(z^{j+1}).                                   \tag{49}
\]

If `L=L_0`, direct simplification gives

\[
 {K\over z^6}\longrightarrow
 {4L^4(\alpha+p)^2(\alpha+p-1)(2\alpha+p+1)^2(2\alpha+p+2)
  \over
  (\alpha+1)^4(\alpha+2)^3(\alpha+3)^2}>0,                  \tag{50}
\]

and the corresponding limits of `D_0/z^2`, `D_2/z^4`, and `E/z^3` are all
strictly positive as well.  Therefore a bad inner component cannot enter from
`z=0`; it must cross a genuine positive repeated-root boundary (48), or one of
the compact parameter faces.

The exact verifier is
`verify_pf_length3_inner_jensen_turan_reduction.py`; its report
`pf_length3_inner_jensen_turan_reduction_exact_20260807.json` has status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_INNER_JENSEN_TURAN_REDUCTION`.

An exact adaptive Bernstein cover was also implemented in
`certify_pf_length3_inner_fixed_order_bernstein.py`.  At odd `r=0` it certifies
large portions of the cube, but a 100,000-cell ordered-triangle run still leaves
23,389 queued boxes.  The unresolved boxes track intersections of the `D_2=0`
and `E=0` sign surfaces; constant Farkas multipliers do not separate them.
Accordingly this is retained as a diagnostic and finite-certificate engine, not
claimed as a completed proof.  The remaining uniform target is the finite-tail
or nonlinear-multiplier theorem excluding (45)--(46) for `0<z<r+5`.

## 13. Exact second-source-root floor

The proposed branch-localization route has one exact half.  Under the classical
change of variables

\[
                       x=-{y\over4(1-y)},\qquad
                       z={y\over4(1-y)},                       \tag{51}
\]

the source row is a Jacobi characteristic polynomial on `0<y<1`.  The cutoff
`z=r+5` becomes

\[
                       y_0={4(r+5)\over4(r+5)+1}.               \tag{52}
\]

The two parity families have Jacobi data

\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
 &\deg&\alpha&\beta\\ \hline
\text{odd}&r+8&2r&1/2\\
\text{even}&r+9&2r+1&-1/2.
\end{array}                                                    \tag{53}
\]

For even `r>=2`, the leading `11 by 11` principal Jacobi block already has
two eigenvalues strictly above (52).  For odd `r>=8`, the full four smallest
orders and thereafter the leading `20 by 20` block have two such eigenvalues.
This is proved by exact LDL inertia.  The finite odd range `8<=r<4000`
contains 3,992 exact rational cases.  On the infinite ranges, every desired
signed LDL pivot is a rational function of `r`; multiplying its numerator by
its denominator and shifting `r` by `4000` (odd) or `3` (even) gives a
polynomial all of whose coefficients are strictly positive.  Hence no
recursive denominator-sign assumption is used.

Cauchy interlacing transfers the two eigenvalues from each principal block to
the full Jacobi matrix.  Since (51) is increasing, this proves

\[
 \boxed{\begin{array}{ll}
 \text{odd }r\ge8:&\text{at least two source roots have }-x>r+5,\\
 \text{even }r\ge2:&\text{at least two source roots have }-x>r+5.
 \end{array}}                                                  \tag{54}
\]

The exact verifier is `verify_pf_length3_second_source_root_floor.py`; its
report `pf_length3_second_source_root_floor_exact_20260807.json` has status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_SECOND_SOURCE_ROOT_FLOOR`.

Thus a last-two-branch localization theorem would now place every
sign-relevant Turan boundary outside the inner strip, leaving only odd
`0<=r<8` and even `0<=r<2` for finite exact treatment.  The remaining uniform
analytic obligation is precisely to prove that a zero of (45) with (46)
positive has at most one source root farther left.

## 14. Inner collision correction and exact first orientation

The proposed last-two-branch localization in the final paragraph of Section
13 is false.  Its finite reserve conclusion is nevertheless useful and is now
exact: adaptive Bernstein covers exclude every inner positive-PF collision for

\[
   \text{odd }0\le r\le7,\qquad \text{even }0\le r\le2.       \tag{55}
\]

The covers use exact one-signed-row leaves in addition to minor signs, positive
linear separators, and conditional `K-lambda*D_i` certificates.  The reports
are `pf_length3_inner_odd_r0_bernstein_rowsign_dfs_exact_20260807.json`,
`pf_length3_inner_odd_r1_rowsign_exact_20260807.json` through
`pf_length3_inner_odd_r7_rowsign_exact_20260807.json`, and
`pf_length3_inner_even_r0_rowsign_exact_20260807.json` through
`pf_length3_inner_even_r2_rowsign_probe_20260807.json`.

At odd `r=8`, however, the face `u=v=1` has a genuine repeated-factor
collision.  The normalized discriminant polynomial has degree 58 and exactly
one root in

\[
 {553104532669\over10^{12}}<y<
 {553104532670\over10^{12}},
 \qquad z=13y\simeq7.19035892470<13.                    \tag{56}
\]

At this algebraic root `D_0,D_2,E` are all strictly positive and `K=0`, so
the null kernel is a repeated positive factor.  In particular the collision
lies to the right of at least two source roots by (54), directly disproving
last-two-branch localization.

Crucially, the collision is benign.  If

\[
 A=D_2h_0'+Eh_1'+D_0h_2',\qquad
 B=D_2h_1'+Eh_2'+D_0h_3',                               \tag{57}
\]

then exact rational root isolation proves `A<0` and `B<0` throughout the
isolating interval (56).  Hence `AB>0`.  The verifier is
`verify_pf_length3_first_inner_repeated_collision.py`; its report
`pf_length3_first_inner_repeated_collision_exact_20260807.json` has status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_FIRST_INNER_REPEATED_COLLISION`.

The corrected remaining length-three target is therefore not absence or
source-branch localization, but the uniform orientation implication

\[
 \boxed{D_0,D_2,E>0,\quad K\le0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad AB>0}
 \qquad(0<z<r+5).                                      \tag{58}
\]

There is a bounded-degree version of (58).  In the affine source Riccati
coordinate `T`, every filtered row is affine in `T`; after positive common
denominator clearing, `D_0,D_2,E,K,A,B` have degrees independent of `r`.
More sharply, on the repeated boundary one may introduce the common positive
factor `c` directly.  The two collision rows are affine in `T`; eliminating
`T` gives one quartic polynomial in `c` of multidegree `(14,7,2,2,4)` in
`(r,z,u,v,c)`.  Extensive structural stress finds that every positive root of
this quartic has the two orientation factors in the same strict sign, while
the observed opposite-sign roots occur only for `c<0`.  This is route
evidence, not yet the exact uniform certificate.  It reduces the next proof
step to a fixed-degree positive-root orientation theorem rather than a
growing-degree Jacobi-root localization theorem.

## 15. Exact ratio-boundary normal form and first global charts

The repeated-root theorem is now exact for both parities on the large-`z`
half of `r>=z` (`z>=1`).  Reports
`pf_length3_repeated_positive_root_odd_original_large_z_exact_20260807.json`
and
`pf_length3_repeated_positive_root_even_original_large_z_exact_20260807.json`
have status `PASS_EXACT_REPEATED_POSITIVE_ROOT_ORIENTATION`; they use 105 and
165 cells respectively, with maximum coordinate depths 3 and 5.  On `z<=1`,
the two physical-`c` charts with `c>=2` are collision-free in one exact leaf
each for each parity, for both `w=z(r-z)<=1` and the reciprocal-`w` chart.

The complementary triangular half `r<z<r+5` is also exact for both parities
once the integer nature of `r` is used.  The `r>=1` reports
`pf_length3_repeated_positive_root_odd_z_minus_r_rge1_exact_20260807.json`
and
`pf_length3_repeated_positive_root_even_z_minus_r_rge1_exact_20260807.json`
pass in 17 and 21 cells; the previously certified fixed-order `r=0` cases
cover the omitted boundary.  Consequently the only unresolved repeated-root
domain is

```text
r >= z,    0 < z < 1,    0 < c < 2.
```

The remaining subdivision localizes to the ratio branch `k=c/z -> 2`.  Put
`D=k-2`, `U=2u+1`, and `V=2v+1`.  Exact weighted expansion, with `weight(z)=2`
and `weight(D)=1`, gives

```text
R  ~ -2^22 w^6 U V (-D^2 w + 3z),
M0 ~ -2^29 w^13 U^2 V^2 (-D^2 w + 3z),
M1 ~  2^32 w^14 U^2 V^2 (-D^2 w + 3z).
```

Adding explicit multiples of `R` produces resultant-equivalent orientations
`A0,A1` satisfying

```text
A0 ~ -2^30 D w^13 U^2 V^2,
A1 ~ -2^32 D w^14 U^2 V^2.
```

This holds for both parities (with one parity-dependent intermediate
quotient).  The exact verifier is
`verify_pf_length3_repeated_ratio_boundary_normal_form.py`; report
`pf_length3_repeated_ratio_boundary_normal_form_exact_20260807.json` has
status `PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_REPEATED_RATIO_BOUNDARY_NORMAL_FORM`.

Initial root sampling suggested the pattern

```text
sign(M0) = sign(M1) = -sign(dR/dc)
```

but this is false globally.  An exact high-precision replay at odd `r=255`,
`z approximately 0.082755981726`, `u approximately 0.724487164`, and `v`
near zero has two close positive roots

```text
c approximately 0.437499162431 and 0.437500837570.
```

Both orientations are positive at both roots, while `R_c` changes from
negative to positive and the cubic slope changes from positive to negative.
Thus neither `sign(M_i)=-sign(R_c)` nor global positivity of the cubic slope
can be used.  Finite propagation from the exact normal form/box certificates
is again the preferred route.

The derivative pattern also has an exact determinant reduction.  If
`P_i+S_i T` are the two collision rows, put

```text
J0 = (dP0/dc)S0-(dS0/dc)P0,
J1 = (dP1/dc)S0-(dS1/dc)P0,
Delta=(1+4z)(p-8)(p-9)>0.
```

Then, identically for both parities,

```text
J1 = -c*Delta*J0,
R_c*S0 = S1*J0-S0*J1+(dS0/dc)R.
```

Consequently on `R=0`,

```text
R_c*S0 = J0*(S1+c*Delta*S0).
```

The verifier
`verify_pf_length3_repeated_resultant_derivative_identity.py`, report
`pf_length3_repeated_resultant_derivative_identity_exact_20260807.json`, has
status `PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_REPEATED_RESULTANT_DERIVATIVE_IDENTITY`.  The
identity remains structurally useful, but the displayed cubic slope is not
globally positive on the positive quartic locus (as the close-root replay
above shows), so it does not by itself orient the collisions.

## 16. Two-pivot affine atlas

The first-pivot orientation polynomials use `T=-P0/S0` and clear by the
positive square `S0^2`.  A continuous-parameter cover can encounter the
artificial locus `P0=S0=0`; there the first-pivot orientations vanish even
though this does not by itself represent a bad collision of the actual source
trajectory.  The correct remedy is a complementary affine chart, not a claim
that `S0` is globally nonzero.

This obstruction itself is exact.  At odd parity, `u=v=1` and `c=1/4`, a
rational Poincare--Miranda box contained in

```text
4 < r < 5,    z>0
```

has opposite strict signs of `P0` on its two `z` faces and of
`S0+(3068/125)P0` on its two `r` faces.  Hence `P0=S0=0` occurs inside the
box.  The verifier
`verify_pf_length3_repeated_first_pivot_artificial_locus.py`, report
`pf_length3_repeated_first_pivot_artificial_locus_exact_20260807.json`, has
status `PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_FIRST_PIVOT_ARTIFICIAL_LOCUS`.  The point is
nonintegral in `r` and is not asserted to be a bad source collision; it is an
exact falsifier of the one-pivot continuous strengthening.

For a directional derivative

```text
D(T)=a+bT+dT^2,
M=D(-P0/S0)S0^2,
N=D(-P1/S1)S1^2,
R=P0*S1-P1*S0,
```

direct exact algebra gives

```text
N*S0^2-M*S1^2
 = R*(b*S0*S1-d*(P1*S0+P0*S1)).
```

Thus on `R=0`, the two pivots give identical signs up to positive squares
where they overlap, while the second pivot remains available on an artificial
first-pivot zero.  The verifier
`verify_pf_length3_repeated_two_pivot_atlas.py`, report
`pf_length3_repeated_two_pivot_atlas_exact_20260807.json`, has status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_REPEATED_TWO_PIVOT_ATLAS`.

The first exact last-box probe with both pivots processed 1,000 cells.  It
closed 396 leaves using the new pivot, 35 using the old pivot, and the rest by
the sign of the resultant, leaving 17 boxes queued only because of the cell
budget.  This is not yet a completed cover, but it confirms that the new chart
removes the dominant artificial obstruction rather than merely restating it.

## 17. One-dimensional component propagation

No separate multidimensional face theorem is needed after the repeated-factor
boundary is closed.  Fix an integer reserve `r` and fixed `u,v`, and consider

```text
I={0<z<r+5: D0,D2,E>0 and K<0}.
```

This is an open subset of a one-dimensional interval.  On each component,
the two roots of `D0*t^2-E*t+D2` are distinct and positive, and the null
weights are a positive multiple of `(cd,c+d,1)`.  The proved quartic window
theorem makes both resulting rows strictly real-rooted in the interior, so
`A*B` is continuous and nonzero; its sign is constant on the component.

The exact `z=0` asymptotics have `K>0`, so a component cannot begin there.
Every other endpoint is one of:

```text
K=0       repeated positive factor,
D0=0      a length-two adjacent-cubic boundary,
D2=0      the shifted length-two boundary,
z=r+5     the already proved outer region.
```

If `E=0` at a closure point with `K<=0`, then `K=4D0D2` forces one of the
two length-two boundaries.  Thus the repeated-factor theorem, the proved
adjacent-cubic theorem, and the outer theorem orient every possible endpoint,
and positivity propagates across every component.  Boundary `u,v` values
follow by continuity from the interior.

The exact symbolic boundary replay and dependency audit are
`verify_pf_length3_component_propagation_reduction.py` and
`pf_length3_component_propagation_reduction_exact_20260807.json`, with status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_COMPONENT_PROPAGATION_REDUCTION`.  Consequently, once
the last compact repeated-factor box is certified for both parities, the
full length-three orientation follows without another high-dimensional
subdivision theorem.

## 18. First audits beyond length three and a rejected shortcut

The exact rational-root audit now extends beyond the first PF cone.  At the
predicted sharp reserve `p-alpha=4m+9`, using `u,v` in
`{1/10,1/2,1}` and factor parameters in `{1/10,1,10}`, all tested adjacent
pairs have strict common-interlacer overlap:

```text
m=3 appended factors: 120/120 cases,
m=4 appended factors: 180/180 cases,
m=5 appended factors: 252/252 cases.
```

The verifier is `audit_pf_arbitrary_length_common_interlacing.py`; reports
are `pf_length4_common_interlacing_exact_audit_20260807.json` through
`pf_length6_common_interlacing_exact_audit_20260807.json`.  These are finite
exact route audits, not an arbitrary-length theorem.

A tempting matrix-product shortcut is false.  After smoothing by `(t+1)^2`,
the odd exact sample `(p,alpha,u,v)=(21,0,1/2,4/5)` has only eight real roots
out of ten in `F0+10000*F2`; the even sample fails with weight `100`.  Thus
two-factor smoothing does not make the entire shifted family compatible with
the full nonnegative cone.  The failing weight vector `(1,0,lambda)` is not
PF, so it does not affect the passing PF audits.  The exact replay is
`disprove_two_factor_smoothing_full_interlacing.py`, report
`two_factor_smoothing_full_interlacing_obstruction_exact_20260807.json`, with
status `EXACT_TWO_FACTOR_SMOOTHING_FULL_INTERLACING_OBSTRUCTION`.

Therefore the arbitrary-length induction must retain the PF Toeplitz
constraint explicitly; ordinary full-interlacing matrix multiplication is
still too strong.

## 19. Infinite-reserve blow-up normal form

The direct two-pivot last-box cover has a second nontransversal face.  Its
deep exact address converges to `r=infinity`, `z=1/16`, `c=1`; the apparent
difficulty is not a finite collision.  Put

```text
B=c(c-2)+4z(c+1)^2,
q=1/r,
weight(B)=1,
weight(q)=2.
```

After removing the known positive contents, both parities have the identical
weighted resultant initial form, up to manifestly positive factors,

```text
B^2*(c+1)^2 + 3*q*c^2*(4c+1)*(2c-1).
```

The leading orientation forms are multiples of this same expression.  After
subtracting explicit powers-of-`r` multiples of `R`, the next weighted forms
can be reduced modulo the leading resultant branch.  For `0<c<1/2` their
remaining factors are

```text
c*(2c-1)*(4c+1)*(7c+4)*Au*Av,
c^2*(c-2)*(2c-1)*(4c+1)^2*Au*Av,
Au=cu-4c-2u-1,
Av=cv-4c-2v-1.
```

Including the displayed outer factors in the equivalent orientations, both
have the sign of the signed branch variable `B/sqrt(q)`.  Therefore the two
asymptotic roots are benign.  For `1/2<c<2`, the initial resultant is strictly
positive, so the infinite-reserve face is collision-free.  High-precision
finite replays at `r=100,1000,10000`, `z=7/100`, `u=v=1/2` confirm the exact
branch conclusion: one root has `(M0,M1)=(+,+)` and the other `(-,-)`.

The verifier
`verify_pf_length3_repeated_infinite_reserve_normal_form.py`, report
`pf_length3_repeated_infinite_reserve_normal_form_exact_20260807.json`, has
status `PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_REPEATED_INFINITE_RESERVE_NORMAL_FORM` for both
parities.  The remaining infinity-face work is to turn these strict initial
forms into finite blow-up covers and separately resolve `c=1/2` and `c=0`.

## 20. Branch-adapted exact atlas below `c=1/2`

The monolithic five-dimensional infinity chart is unnecessarily expensive
because it approximates the curved resultant branches by rectangular cells.
The normal form shows that every asymptotic branch satisfies

```text
|B|/sqrt(q) < sqrt(3)*c < 2c.
```

Accordingly put

```text
q=t^2/1024,
B=sign*t*(2c*d)/32,
0<=d<=1.
```

This straightens both branches and factors the `c^2` degeneracy at the lower
endpoint.  On the central interval `1/16<=c<=7/16`, exact integer Bernstein
covers pass for both signs and both parities:

```text
odd,  B>0:  631 cells,
odd,  B<0:  885 cells,
even, B>0:  759 cells,
even, B<0: 1145 cells.
```

The corresponding reports are
`pf_length3_repeated_branch_core_{odd,even}_B{pos,neg}_c1_16_7_16_Dle2c_exact_20260807.json`.
Most nontrivial leaves are certified by the two corrected orientations having
the same sign on `R=0`.

The complementary region `|B|/sqrt(q)>=2c` splits exactly at `|B|=c/16`:

```text
small-B ratio chart: B=sign*c*x/16, q=x^2*y/1024,
large-B chart:       q=x/1024, |B|=c/16+y*(physical endpoint-c/16).
```

For each sign and parity, both charts have `R>0` in a single Bernstein cell.
Thus the entire central infinity neighborhood `q<=1/1024`,
`1/16<=c<=7/16` is exact.  The verifier scripts are
`certify_pf_length3_repeated_branch_core.py` and
`certify_pf_length3_repeated_branch_outer.py`.

The same core coordinates also give controlled finite-reserve extensions.
For odd parity and `B>0`, the central branch band passes exactly through
`q<=1/256` in 1853 cells and through `q<=1/64` in 5991 cells.  Hence this
branch is already uniform for every real `r>=64`, not merely integer reserve.

## 21. The lower endpoint is a second-order benign collision

At `c=0`, the first corrected `A1` face is still a multiple of the endpoint
resultant, so a second resultant subtraction and one additional division by
`c` are necessary.  In the odd case the remaining resultant factor is

```text
G = (17t^4+8448t^2+1048576)d^2
    + sign*(816t^3+196608t)d
    + 9216t^2-786432.
```

For `B>0`, both orientation remainders modulo `G` factor as products with
strictly positive coefficients.  For `B<0`, they are negative positive-factor
multiples of

```text
d(17t^2+4096)-480t,
d(13t^4+12288t^2+2097152)-(384t^3+294912t).
```

The positive root of `G` lies beyond both linear thresholds because the two
threshold evaluations factor exactly as

```text
-1536(t^2+2048)(3t^2+1024)(17t^2+4096),
-3072(t^2+256)(t^2+1024)(t^2+2048)^2(3t^2+1024).
```

Therefore both odd endpoint branches are benign.  The exact verifier is
`verify_pf_length3_repeated_c0_endpoint_normal_form.py`; report
`pf_length3_repeated_c0_endpoint_normal_form_exact_20260807.json` has status
`PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_REPEATED_C0_ENDPOINT_NORMAL_FORM_ODD`.

The endpoint face itself has now been certified for both signs and both
parities:

```text
odd,  B>0: 263 processed cells,
odd,  B<0: 389 processed cells,
even, B>0: 307 processed cells,
even, B<0: 515 processed cells.
```

After subtracting the endpoint resultant quotient from `A0` and `A1` and
dividing the corrected `A1` by one further factor of `c`, the full finite
collar `0<c<=1/16`, `q<=1/1024` also passes exactly:

```text
odd,  B>0: 327 processed cells,
odd,  B<0: 603 processed cells,
even, B>0: 363 processed cells,
even, B<0: 709 processed cells.
```

The reports are
`pf_length3_repeated_c0_endpoint_face_*_20260807.json` and
`pf_length3_repeated_c0_endpoint_collar_*_cle1_16_*_20260807.json`.
The two complementary outer charts from Section 20 each certify this lower
band in a single `R>0` cell for every sign and parity.  Consequently the
entire lower infinity neighborhood `0<c<=1/16`, `q<=1/1024` is exact; there
is no remaining thin collar at `c=0`.

## 22. Toric resolution of the upper endpoint from below

Write

```text
Q=1024q,
S=16(1/2-c).
```

The upper endpoint is covered by two toric charts,

```text
delta-dominant: S=a, Q=ab,
q-dominant:     Q=a, S=ab,
```

with `B=sign*a*d/64`.  The residual curved boundary is resolved by the two
overlapping charts

```text
branch: b=x^2,   d=xy,  covering d^2<=b,
outer:  b=x^2*y, d=x,   covering b<=d^2.
```

For either parity and `B>0`, all four combinations pass exactly: the
delta-dominant branch takes 71 cells, its outer complement one `R>0` cell,
the q-dominant branch 231 cells, and its outer complement one `R>0` cell.
Thus the whole upper infinity band is closed for the positive sign in both
parities.  For odd parity and `B<0`, the delta-dominant branch passes in 19
cells and its outer complement in one cell.  The first orientation pair
alone leaves a modest interior cell in the q-dominant branch unresolved;
this is the previously identified artificial zero locus of that pivot, not
a resultant collision.

The first attempted two-pivot replay exposed a verifier bug rather than a
mathematical obstruction: the complementary tensors `N0,N1` were constructed
but the shared leaf predicate was hard-coded to inspect only `A0,A1`.
`certify_pf_length3_repeated_branch_core.py::leaf_reason` now checks either
pair, and a synthetic regression cell verifies that only the complementary
pair certifies it.  After that repair, however, one deep q-dominant cell still
subdivided indefinitely.  Exact and high-precision replays located the limiting
point on

```text
R=A0=A1=N0=N1=0.
```

This is not a finite common-root collision.  Writing the two affine root rows
as `q_i(T)=P_i+S_i*T`, the limiting point has `S0=S1=0` but `P0*P1!=0`.
Thus the common root is the projective point `T=infinity`, corresponding to the
source pole, and both finite-`T` pivots fail only because they divide by a
vanishing slope.

Use instead `U=1/T`.  The homogenized rows are

```text
qtilde_i(U)=S_i+P_i*U,
```

and the projective Riccati numerator is

```text
z*(p+alpha)^2*U^2
  -(p+alpha)*(z*(4p-2)-alpha)*U
  +(1+4z)*p*(p-1).
```

Substituting the common root `U=-S0/P0` and clearing by the positive square
`P0^2` gives the projective orientation pair `I0,I1`.  At the former limiting
cell both are uniformly negative, so that cell certifies in a single exact
Bernstein step.

The full odd-parity, negative-`B`, q-dominant branch now passes exactly:

```text
1725 processed cells,
863 certified leaves,
maximum coordinate depths (0,5,4,4,4),
no unresolved cell.
```

Of these leaves, 284 are supplied by the projective pair (221 on `R=0` and
63 directly).  The report
`pf_length3_repeated_half_toric_core_odd_Bnegative_q_branch_projective_full_20260808.json`
has status `PASS_EXACT_PF_LENGTH3_REPEATED_HALF_TORIC_CORE`.  The verifier is
`certify_pf_length3_repeated_half_toric_core.py`; the symbolic projective
orientations are built by
`verify_pf_length3_repeated_resultant_reduction.py`.

Once the remaining sign/parity variants in this toric atlas pass, the entire
infinity neighborhood `0<c<1/2`, `q<=1/1024` will be closed.  The remaining
length-three obligation will then be the bounded-reserve complement not
already covered by the finite-scale branch charts.

## 23. Exact memory-bounded continuation of the odd negative outer chart

The first projective-pivot outer-chart run reached its deterministic 5000-cell
cap with no mathematical failure:

```text
5001 processed cells,
2490 certified leaves,
21 pending dyadic cells,
maximum coordinate depths (12,16,16,14,14).
```

The leaf histogram was 1934 first-pivot negative-on-`R=0`, 358 `R>0`, 176
`R<0`, and 22 projective-pivot negative-on-`R=0`.  Report
`pf_length3_repeated_half_toric_core_odd_Bnegative_q_outer_projective_full_20260808.json`
has SHA-256
`5A37F4FADB956007F8976A150DD372323E3B32B40E39C53A210D6C8634842C4B`.

An ordinary exact tensor-stack continuation exhausted Windows commit because
every deferred sibling retained a full dictionary of large Python-integer
Bernstein tensors.  A first memory-bounded implementation retained only the
immutable root atlas, one live restricted cell, and deferred address strings.
It reconstructs the next sibling from the root when a leaf closes.  On the
actual 21-cell frontier its first 100 cells reproduced the previous tensor
stack exactly:

```text
61 deferred addresses,
29 certified leaves,
maximum depths (9,13,13,12,12),
leaf histogram 23 first-pivot negative-on-R=0, 5 R>0, 1 R<0,
identical current address.
```

This run used bounded memory but required 2778.766 seconds to reach the
checkpoint.  The source loaded by that worker had SHA-256
`9F0F634D9E987C2C779A934BDC52DC08667AB20E8A0267403F96DBB50283ED31`.

The faster replacement uses the exact triangular inverse of integer-scaled
midpoint Bernstein restriction.  After a leaf closes, it algebraically
unrestricts to the longest common prefix with the next deferred sibling and
then restricts down that sibling.  Thus only one cell and address strings are
live, while the usual DFS locality avoids reconstruction from the global root.
The derivation and deterministic tests are in
`REVERSIBLE_BERNSTEIN_ADDRESS_STACK_2026-08-08.md`; the implementation and
rerunnable verifier are `exact_bernstein_navigation.py` and
`verify_exact_bernstein_navigation.py`.

The reversible method passed 340 direct inverse checks, a complete 181-cell
cell-for-cell DFS comparison, and 100 unrelated-address comparisons.  On the
real frontier it then reproduced every field of the 100-cell checkpoint above
in 895.015 seconds, a factor 3.10 faster at the checkpoint (the capped report
finished cell 100 and emitted its pending partition in 905.656 seconds).  Its
stderr was empty.  The calibration report
`pf_length3_repeated_half_toric_core_odd_Bnegative_q_outer_projective_frontier1_reversible_cal100_20260808.json`
has SHA-256
`8413C3DF60540FAC2AABF046B1B6717FBEC8AB17004CD7DE54E8D3B22B11EB37`.
The exact 61-cell remainder from that report is now the input to the full
reversible continuation.

## 24. The full stability-symbol lift is strictly too strong

For `m` appended factors, linearity gives the natural bivariate symbol

```text
Phi_m(t,w)=sum_j binom(m,j) G_j(t) w^(m-j)
          =S_(p,alpha)[(1-ut)(1-vt)(t+w)^m].
```

Real stability of `Phi_m` would make the map from an arbitrary stable factor
polynomial to its window image a stability preserver, immediately implying
the desired PF theorem.  This strengthening is false already at the proved
`m=2` boundary.  At

```text
(p,alpha,u,v,m)=(17,0,1/10,1/10,2),
t=-7/6+5z,
w=11/6+z,
```

the exact positive-direction restriction is squarefree of degree ten and has
exactly eight real roots.  Hence it has one nonreal conjugate pair and the
bivariate symbol is not stable.  The primitive coefficient digest is
`9aaa145b1b4e9cbc88ae08a1f598cf124556bc7fe982663cf462dc7d33e5db58`.
The replay and record are `disprove_pf_symbol_stability.py` and
`pf_full_stability_symbol_obstruction_exact_20260808.json`.

This does not affect PF compatibility: the algebraic symbol asks for
preservation of every stable input and is strictly stronger than evaluation
against the one-dimensional PF coefficient cone.  It does show that the
arbitrary-length proof must retain either the factor-by-factor PF network or
an equivalent branch-aligned collision invariant; a universal
Borcea--Branden symbol argument cannot supply the lift.

## 25. Poisson-binomial form and adversarial exact audits through length twelve

The PF restriction has a useful probabilistic normalization.  For appended
factors `c_i>0`, put

```text
A(z)=prod_i(z+c_i),
p_i=1/(1+c_i).
```

Then

```text
A(z)/A(1)=prod_i((1-p_i)+p_i*z)
```

is the probability generating polynomial of a Poisson-binomial random
variable `J=sum_i Bernoulli(p_i)`.  Consequently the two rows in Section 4
are exactly

```text
Q_0/A(1)=E[G_J],
Q_1/A(1)=E[G_(J+1)].
```

This converts the arbitrary-factor target into an orientation theorem for a
strongly log-concave Bernoulli convolution, rather than for an arbitrary
positive weight vector.  Mattner, arXiv:2204.06040, proves useful extremal
reductions for Bernoulli convolutions with fixed ordinary moments.  Those
theorems do not directly settle the present pair of kernel-expectation
constraints, so they are route guidance rather than a cited closure.

There is also an exact positive-moment identity in the factorial window.  If

```text
b_h=(p-2h)!/(p+alpha-h)!,
N=p+2*alpha,
x=p-2h,
```

then the adjacent shift multiplier is

```text
b_(h+1)/b_h
 =(p+alpha-h)/((p-2h)(p-2h-1))
 =1/2 integral_0^1 y^(x-2) ((N+1)-N*y) dy.
```

For the `j`th shifted row the same formula uses `h+j`.  The density is
strictly positive on `[0,1]`; this supplies a Hankel-moment representation
for the factor-by-factor shift.  It does not by itself imply a multiplier
sequence: the corresponding degree-two Jensen polynomial already has a
nonreal pair at `(p,alpha)=(17,0)`.  Thus the moment representation must be
combined with the PF convolution or branch topology, not used as an
unrestricted diagonal-preserver claim.

A new exact adversarial audit sampled the actual PF theorem rather than the
false full symbol.  It used rational root isolation for both equal-degree
rows, factors in

```text
{1/100,1/10,1/3,1/2,1,2,3,10,100},
```

random endpoint and interior `u,v`, both parities, and reserve indices through
12.  All cases had strict common-interlacer overlap:

```text
m=3,...,6:   100/100,
m=7,...,9:    45/45,
m=10,...,12:  30/30.
```

The checkpointed verifier is `audit_pf_arbitrary_length_adversarial.py`.
The three reports are
`pf_length4_to_7_common_interlacing_adversarial_exact_20260808.json`,
`pf_length8_to_10_common_interlacing_adversarial_exact_20260808.json`, and
`pf_length11_to_13_common_interlacing_adversarial_exact_20260808.json`, with
SHA-256 hashes respectively
`31106A5B06C2A2A4E4F7D482DC85870A4E71A1D6B2A5DCC06CCDBEC6B81EFC53`,
`A49DBD91C8D776BD1134BD4594EC353E8600B900B50DAC4C7B28651B3A26E5AB`, and
`8443143740609776EEB091C425925BBC0486DAAA14C29BDB3A2632076452C836`.
These 175 cases are finite exact evidence, not an arbitrary-length theorem.

## 26. Exact Jacobi finite-free factorization of the factorial window

Formula (717) has a useful finite-free form.  Put `n=floor(p/2)` and reverse
polynomials at ambient degree `n`.  Define

```text
A(x)=sum_h gamma_h (p-2h)!/((p+alpha-h)!(n-h)!) x^(n-h)
```

and

```text
Q_(p,alpha)(x)
 =(p+2alpha)! sum_(k=0)^n
   (-1)^k n!/(k!(p-2k)!(alpha+k)!) x^(n-k).
```

Then direct coefficient comparison with the degree-`n` multiplicative
finite-free convolution gives the all-order identity

```text
boxed:  rev_n S_(p,alpha)[Gamma]
        = A(x+1) boxtimes_n Q_(p,alpha)(x).                 (26.1)
```

Indeed the coefficient of `x^(n-k)` in `A(x+1)` is

```text
1/(n-k)! sum_(h<=k)
 gamma_h (p-2h)!/((p+alpha-h)!(k-h)!),
```

and the finite-free coefficient multiplier supplied by `Q` is exactly

```text
(p+2alpha)!(n-k)!/((p-2k)!(alpha+k)!).
```

The second factor is real-rooted in all orders.  Write
`p=2n+epsilon`, with `epsilon` equal to zero or one, and
`c=epsilon+1/2`.  Hypergeometric cancellation and Pfaff's transformation
give

```text
Q_(p,alpha)(x)/(p+2alpha)!
 =(-1)^n/(epsilon!(alpha+n)!)
   2F1(-n,-alpha-n;c;-x/4)

 =(-1)^n n!/(epsilon!(alpha+n)!(c)_n)
   (1+x/4)^n
   P_n^(epsilon-1/2,alpha)((4-x)/(4+x)).                   (26.2)
```

Both Jacobi parameters are greater than `-1`.  Its Jacobi zeros lie in
`(-1,1)`, and the inverse Mobius map
`x=4(1-y)/(1+y)` sends them to `(0,infinity)`.  Therefore `Q_(p,alpha)`
has `n` positive simple roots.  In particular, if `A(x+1)` were real-rooted,
the standard positive-semidefinite multiplicative finite-free theorem would
prove the desired window assertion immediately.

That last sufficient condition is false, already at the quadratic base.  At

```text
(p,alpha)=(10,2),
Gamma(t)=(1-t/3)(1-t/2),
```

the nonzero core of `A` is

```text
x^2/15840-x/28512+1/181440,
```

whose discriminant is exactly `-23/142263475200`.  Thus (26.1) is not a
routine application of the standard preservation theorem.  It instead
localizes the abstract window lemma to a specific statement: the positive
Jacobi finite-free factor in (26.2) repairs the structured defect produced
by the two-outlier input and the factorial preconditioner.

The exact replay `verify_window_jacobi_finite_free_factorization.py` made 666
independent rational checks of (26.1), 102 exact checks of (26.2), and 102
positive-root Sturm counts.  Its report is
`window_jacobi_finite_free_factorization_exact_20260808.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`8BC494098BEDA3BA42E5D98D98EAF0E2A0A256A11A088FEE93021E65EF15A57C` and
`355FF41BF1DFC319FC95E9B026929095812646C6422633F4887371F7C3433553`.

## 27. Hermite--Laguerre factorization and a quadratic exceptional core

There is a stronger rearrangement of the coefficient multipliers in (26.1).
Put

```text
n=floor(p/2),  epsilon=p-2n,  beta=epsilon-1/2,  N=p+alpha,
e_h=(-1)^h gamma_h.
```

On the admissible cone, `e_h` is the `h`-th elementary symmetric function of
the factor parameters `lambda_i`, exactly two of which lie in `(0,1]` and all
the others of which are negative.  Define

```text
R_(p,alpha)[Gamma](t)
 =sum_(k=0)^n 1/(p-2k)!
   sum_(h<=k) gamma_h (p-2h)!/((N-h)!(k-h)!) t^k.          (27.1)
```

The original window is obtained from `R` by the coefficient multiplier

```text
[t^k]S_(p,alpha)[Gamma]
 =(p+2alpha)!/alpha! * [t^k]R_(p,alpha)[Gamma]/(alpha+1)_k. (27.2)
```

The sequence `1/(alpha+1)_k` is a classical Laguerre multiplier sequence: its
exponential generating function is `0F1(;alpha+1;z)`, whose zeros are real and
negative.  It is therefore enough to prove that `R` has only negative roots.

The exact Hermite lift of (27.1) is

```text
C_(p,alpha,Gamma)(x)
 =sum_h e_h H_(p-2h)(x/2)/(N-h)!
 =x^p R_(p,alpha)[Gamma](-1/x^2).                         (27.3)
```

Consequently `R` is negative-rooted if and only if `C`, apart from its forced
parity zero, is real-rooted.  The even/odd Hermite--Laguerre identities turn
(27.3) into the consecutive monic-Laguerre combination

```text
C(x)=x^epsilon 4^n/N!
 sum_h e_h (N)_h^fall/4^h
 Lhat_(n-h)^(beta)(x^2/4).                               (27.4)
```

This gives a complete all-parameter proof for the two-positive-factor base.
For `Gamma=(1-ut)(1-vt)`, `0<u,v<=1`, the Laguerre core in (27.4) is

```text
q_n=Lhat_n^beta+g_1 Lhat_(n-1)^beta+g_2 Lhat_(n-2)^beta,
g_1=N(u+v)/4,  g_2=N(N-1)uv/16.                          (27.5)
```

The monic Laguerre recurrence shows that this is the characteristic polynomial
of the usual positive Laguerre Jacobi matrix with only its last diagonal and
last squared coupling changed.  The changed coupling and final Sylvester pivot
are positive precisely when

```text
(n-1)(n+beta-1)-g_2 > 0,                                 (27.6)
(n+beta-1)(n+beta-g_1)+g_2 > 0.                          (27.7)
```

The reserve `p-alpha>=5` gives `N<=4n+2epsilon-5`.  Both expressions are
bilinear in `u,v`, so their minima occur at the four corners of `[0,1]^2`.
At the largest allowed `N`, the exact strict boundary margins are

```text
epsilon=0: (2n-3)/8,  3(2n-3)/8,  3(2n-3)/8;
epsilon=1: (n-1)/4,   5(2n-1)/8,  (3n-1)/4,              (27.8)
```

for the coupling, one-factor pivot corner, and two-factor pivot corner,
respectively.  The minimum possible `n` is three in even parity and two in odd
parity, so all margins are strictly positive.  Thus (27.5) has positive simple
zeros, proving the Hermite lemma for two factors without subdivision.

There is also a rigorous reduction of every higher-length case to a quadratic
exceptional core.  The coefficient polynomial used by the monic-Laguerre
combination theorem is

```text
Q_L(z)=sum_(h=0)^m (-1)^h e_h (N)_h^fall/4^h (z)_(m-h)^rise. (27.9)
```

Set `B=N-m+1` and

```text
P(w)=sum_(j=0)^m e_(m-j) 4^j/(B)_j w^j.
```

If `Poch_1` maps `w^j` to the falling factorial
`w(w-1)...(w-j+1)`, coefficient comparison gives

```text
Q_L(z)=(-1)^m (N)_m^fall/4^m * Poch_1[P](-z).             (27.10)
```

Before the multiplier `1/(B)_j`, the polynomial in (27.10) is a positive
rescaling of `prod_i(w+lambda_i)`.  Since `1/(B)_j` is a multiplier sequence,
`P` is real-rooted with exactly `m-2` positive and two negative roots.  The
classical Pochhammer zero-count theorem says that `Poch_1[P]` has at least as
many positive roots as `P`.  Hence `Q_L` has at least `m-2` negative roots and
can have at most one exceptional quadratic factor.  This is an all-order
theorem, not a finite computation.

Factor any `m-2` such negative roots and write the residual monic quadratic in
the rising-factorial basis as

```text
Q_exc(z)=(z)_2^rise-G_1(z)_1^rise+G_2.
```

Durán's generalized-Laguerre construction appends each removed negative root
while preserving positive simple zeros.  Therefore the entire arbitrary-length
lemma is reduced to the following two scalar inequalities at
`s=n-m+2`:

```text
(s-1)(s+beta-1)-G_2 > 0,                                 (27.11)
(s+beta-1)(s+beta-G_1)+G_2 > 0.                          (27.12)
```

They are exactly the last-coupling and last-pivot conditions for the residual
quadratic Laguerre combination.  Proving (27.11)--(27.12) from (27.9) is now
the only missing arbitrary-length compatibility step.  Antonio Durán's
`Zeros of linear combinations of Laguerre polynomials` (arXiv:2507.22425)
supplies the generalized-Laguerre append theorem, while the positive-zero
count in (27.10) is the classical Pochhammer theorem recorded, for example,
by Golitsyna--Karpenko (2016, DOI 10.1080/10236198.2016.1248956).

The deterministic replay `verify_window_hermite_laguerre_factorization.py`
made 225 exact identity checks, symbolically verified every boundary margin
in (27.8), and then passed 1,400 exact rational adversarial inputs for
`m=2,...,15`.  Each audit used two positive parameters from
`{1/100,1/10,1/3,1/2,1}`, arbitrary negative parameters from
`{-1/100,-1/10,-1/3,-1,-3,-10,-100}`, and exact Sturm root counts.  The
report is `window_hermite_laguerre_factorization_exact_20260808.json`.  The
source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`1C9661B20DEC66F8E07ED6F6188EAB1D7157829B313B311D87A5B0088404CB6A` and
`B5224101CF9A73BDFF26D70C1E5291248CB8E480565A88B8E6CD653E179D3F73`.

## 28. Exact two-outlier discrete reduction

The Pochhammer step in (27.10) admits a sharper factor-by-factor
description.  Write the arbitrary negative parameters as `-c_i`, with
`c_i>0`, put `r=m-2`, and define

```text
F_C(x)=sum_(j=0)^r e_(r-j)(-c_1,...,-c_r)
                  4^j/(C)_j (x)_j^fall.                 (28.1)
```

Before the Pochhammer transform, the coefficient polynomial in (28.1) is
obtained from `prod_i(4x-c_i)` by the classical multiplier sequence
`1/(C)_j`.  It consequently has only positive roots.  Brenti's Pochhammer
mesh theorem then gives

```text
F_C has only positive roots and mesh at least one.       (28.2)
```

For `0<a<=1`, introduce the first-order difference operator

```text
L_(a,C)F(x)=a(C+x)F(x)+(4-a)xF(x-1).                    (28.3)
```

If `B=N-m+1`, direct coefficient comparison gives the all-order identity

```text
B(B+1) T(x)=L_(v,B)L_(u,B+1)F_(B+2)(x),                 (28.4)
```

where `T=Poch_1[P]` is the polynomial in (27.10).  Equivalently, writing
`H=F_(B+2)`,

```text
B(B+1)T(x)
 =u v (B+x)(B+x+1) H(x)
 +2x(B+x)(2(u+v)-u v) H(x-1)
 +x(x-1)(4-u)(4-v) H(x-2).                              (28.5)
```

The first application in (28.4) is completely controlled.  The two
polynomials

```text
(B+1+x)H(x),             xH(x-1)
```

have interlacing roots: their ordered root sets are

```text
-B-1, xi_1, xi_2, ...       and       0, xi_1+1, xi_2+1, ...,
```

and (28.2) supplies `xi_i+1<=xi_(i+1)`.  Their coefficients in (28.3)
are positive.  Hence `L_(u,B+1)H` is real-rooted, with one negative and
`r` positive roots (strictly so away from harmless collision limits).
Thus every arbitrary negative factor and the first bounded positive
outlier are handled in all orders.  Only the second application of `L`
can contain the exceptional quadratic interaction.

This is stronger than the zero-count reduction in Section 27: the remaining
lemma is now a two-step discrete statement over the structured family
`F_C` in (28.1).  The structure in (28.1) must be retained: positive roots
and mesh one alone are not sufficient for the second application.  One can
close the lemma either by proving that the second application puts its
residual coefficients in the four effective-dimension half-spaces preceding
(27.11), or by proving positivity of the corresponding Laguerre combination
directly.

The deterministic replay `verify_window_two_outlier_discrete_reduction.py`
checked (28.4) and (28.5), the positive-root input, and the exact first-step
Sturm counts in 32 rational cases through `r=7`.  Its report is
`window_two_outlier_discrete_reduction_exact_20260808.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`EA93E3142D91F5C348CB4EF2F6E2AB87343E3F9F4C1E9AA98D6D2BBCB58DAA1D`
and
`5662B966A003CF22804472D17004282971A3BC49E0CC4B97E4FBF29A0B7A9C36`.

## 29. All-order closure of the residual last-pivot inequality

One of the two inequalities left in (27.11)--(27.12) is now proved in all
orders.  Put

```text
m=r+2,  s=n-r,  y=s+beta-1,
B=N-r-1,  A=B-y=N-n-beta=n+alpha+1/2.
```

Since `s>=2` and `beta` is `-1/2` or `1/2`, one has `y>0`.  Expand the
structured polynomial `H=F_(B+2)` one falling-factorial summand at a time.
At `x=-y`, the elementary-symmetric sign and the falling-factorial sign
cancel to the common sign `(-1)^r`:

```text
e_(r-j)(-c_1,...,-c_r) (-y)_j^fall
 =(-1)^r e_(r-j)(c_1,...,c_r) (y)_j^rise.                 (29.1)
```

For the summand indexed by `j`, the two shift ratios in (28.5) are exactly

```text
(-y-1)_j^fall/(-y)_j^fall=(y+j)/y,
(-y-2)_j^fall/(-y)_j^fall=(y+j)(y+j+1)/(y(y+1)).          (29.2)
```

Consequently, after removing the positive weight in (29.1), its multiplier
in `B(B+1)T(-y)` is, with `z=y+j`,

```text
K(A,z;u,v)
 =u v A(A+1)-2A(2(u+v)-u v)z+(4-u)(4-v)z(z+1).           (29.3)
```

This polynomial is multiaffine in `u,v`, so its minimum on `[0,1]^2` occurs
at a corner.  The four corner values are

```text
K(0,0)=16z(z+1),
K(1,0)=K(0,1)=4z(3(z+1)-A),
K(1,1)=(A-3z)^2+A+9z.                                    (29.4)
```

The first and fourth are strictly positive.  To handle the middle two, write
the reserve in the weaker-than-admissible form

```text
p-alpha=3r+5+delta,  delta>=0.
```

The actual window reserve `p-alpha>=4m-3=4r+5` is stronger.  With
`p=2n+epsilon`, `beta=epsilon-1/2`, direct substitution gives the exact
margin

```text
3(z+1)-A=2epsilon+3+3j+delta>0.                           (29.5)
```

Thus every summand in (29.1) contributes with the same strict sign, proving

```text
(-1)^r T(-(s+beta-1))>0.                                  (29.6)
```

By (27.10), `Q_L(y)` has the same positive sign, since `m=r+2`.  After any
`r` negative roots of `Q_L` are removed, their factors `y+zeta_i` are
positive.  Therefore the residual quadratic satisfies

```text
Q_exc(s+beta-1)>0,
```

which is exactly

```text
(s+beta-1)(s+beta-G_1)+G_2>0.                             (29.7)
```

Hence (27.12), the last Sylvester-pivot condition, is closed for arbitrary
length.  The only remaining scalar compatibility condition is (27.11), the
upper bound on `G_2`.

The deterministic replay `verify_window_residual_pivot_sign.py` symbolically
checks (29.3)--(29.5), independently checks the two-step operator identity and
the target sign in 88 exact rational cases through `r=10`, and reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_ORDER_RESIDUAL_PIVOT_SIGN` in
`window_residual_pivot_sign_exact_20260808.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`35D6DEDEBF51F95123CFFCFE86CAB47E5C5867DDABB7F72EE3B2D467BD2546B4`
and
`06F956871B02CAB325E062276AF89649B7DC5993C31370BD843CE3BA11A3FB58`.

## 30. Commuting-shift induction for the last coupling

There is a second all-order reduction which isolates (27.11) as a one-step
inductive contraction.  For a polynomial `f`, put

```text
D_a f(x)=a f(x)+(4-a)f(x-1),
K_n(x)=(x+1)(x+2)...(x+n).                                (30.1)
```

The gamma-augmented form of the Pochhammer polynomial is obtained by applying
one operator `D_(lambda_i)` for every factor parameter.  This follows either
from the generating numerator

```text
prod_i(lambda_i+(4-lambda_i)z)
```

or directly from the factorial-basis coefficients.  Since every `D_a` is a
polynomial in the backward shift, all these operators commute.  We may
therefore apply the two positive parameters `u,v` first and the `r` negative
parameters afterwards.

Put

```text
n=B+r+1.
```

Using `K_n(x-1)=x K_(n-1)(x)` twice gives the exact factorization

```text
D_v D_u K_n(x)=K_(n-2)(x) R_0(x),                         (30.2)

R_0(x)=16x^2+4(n(u+v)-4)x+u v n(n-1).                    (30.3)
```

Thus the initial exceptional product is

```text
D_0=u v n(n-1)/16 <= n(n-1)/16.                          (30.4)
```

Suppose `k` negative parameters have next been applied and write the full
augmented polynomial as

```text
K_L(x) R_k(x),       L=n-k-2.                             (30.5)
```

Adding the next parameter `-c`, `c>0`, removes exactly one member of the
forced consecutive block and gives the small-degree recurrence

```text
R_(k+1)(x)
 =(4+c)x R_k(x-1)-c(x+L)R_k(x).                           (30.6)
```

The Pochhammer positive-zero theorem supplies at least `k` positive zeros of
`R_k`.  Remove a set of `k` positive zeros which minimizes the constant of the
remaining monic quadratic, and call that constant `D_k`.  The sole remaining
all-order statement can now be written as the dimension-drop invariant

```text
D_k <= (L+1)(L+2)/16,                                    (30.7)
```

with strict inequality away from the sharp initial corner.  At the final
stage `k=r`, one has `L=B-1`, so (30.7) reads

```text
D_r < B(B+1)/16.                                         (30.8)
```

This is exactly the product bound `u v prod(xi_i)/prod(zeta_i)<=1`, and hence
exactly (27.11) after the reserve-five comparison already used in Section 27.
The reformulation is useful because the right side drops by the transparent
factor `L/(L+2)` at each application of (30.6); the arbitrary factor length no
longer appears in a simultaneous root factorization.

The tempting stronger assertion that the selected positive-root product must
grow by `c(L+2)/4` at every step is false: roots can exchange with the
exceptional pair.  The normalized two-root invariant (30.7), rather than a
rootwise monotonicity claim, is the correct induction object.  Proving that
(30.6) preserves (30.7), presumably together with the companion last-pivot
half-space proved in Section 29, is now the only missing analytic step.

The deterministic replay
`verify_window_commuting_shift_induction_reduction.py` proves (30.2)--(30.6)
symbolically, proves the sharp base margin
`n(n-1)(1-u v)/16`, and makes 112 exact rational root-isolation checks of
(30.7) in 28 cases through six negative factors.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_REDUCTION_AND_FINITE_STAGE_AUDIT` in
`window_commuting_shift_induction_reduction_exact_20260808.json`.  The finite
stage checks are evidence, not a proof of the remaining preservation lemma.
The source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`31A2512D2078112B12872561C920E577B61AFE96C796979DD2D289975BA829FD`
and
`7C959569B1F729516C0882746436F0841015D7972CC878B9F62F8C46AC337195`.

## 31. Polarized Pochhammer form and solved boundary faces

The recurrence in Section 30 has an exact symmetric closed form which makes
its extremal boundary transparent.  For `0<=m<=n` and parameters
`t_1,...,t_m`, define

```text
K_(m,n)(x;t)=sum_(j=0)^m e_j(t) n^fall_j x^fall_(m-j).    (31.1)
```

Here the bounded parameters are `a=u/4`, `b=v/4`, while a negative factor
`-c` contributes the parameter `-c/4`.  Direct comparison of elementary
symmetric functions gives the contiguous identity

```text
K_(m+1,n)(x;t,tau)
 =x K_(m,n)(x-1;t)+tau n K_(m,n-1)(x;t).                 (31.2)
```

Using `L=n-m`, the right side is equivalently

```text
(1-tau)x K_(m,n)(x-1;t)+tau(x+L)K_(m,n)(x;t),            (31.3)
```

which is precisely the normalized commuting-shift recurrence.  Formula
(31.2) exposes both parameter-boundary faces:

```text
tau=0:       K_(m+1,n)=x K_(m,n)(x-1),
tau->-inf:   K_(m+1,n)/tau -> n K_(m,n-1).               (31.4)
```

Thus the zero face inserts a root at zero.  The infinite face deletes one
negative parameter and lowers the ambient index by one.  Sending all `r`
negative parameters to their infinite faces leaves, up to a positive scalar,

```text
K_(2,B+1)(x;a,b)
 =x^2+((B+1)(a+b)-1)x+B(B+1)ab.                          (31.5)
```

Its two-root product satisfies

```text
B(B+1)ab <= B(B+1)/16,                                  (31.6)
```

with equality only at `u=v=1`.  Hence every boundary vertex of the negative
parameter cube is solved: a zero face makes the residual product zero, and
successive infinite faces reduce to (31.5).  The last analytic statement is
now a boundary-maximum principle: the structured two-root product has no
interior maximum above its all-infinite boundary.  It must not be replaced by
coordinatewise monotonicity; exact/numerical probes find local decreases as a
single `c_i` increases, even though the global endpoint envelope continues to
hold.

The deterministic replay
`verify_window_polarized_pochhammer_boundary_reduction.py` proves
(31.1)--(31.6) symbolically through generic degree six and performs an
additional exact rational root-isolation audit through six negative
parameters.  Its report is
`window_polarized_pochhammer_boundary_reduction_exact_20260808.json`.  The
finite audit is evidence only; the boundary-maximum principle remains the
sole missing assertion.  The source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`DF75E0F6028C1DFC753BE40669A631DEF830AB4FEA0E798ADCDD6585EA47C0CF`
and
`F98AFCC6D2E17C7F49B121C691DEBE38303CAD696809690B888AAE5B6FD7DBB6`.

## 32. Exact negative-root/product-transfer normal form

The boundary-maximum statement from Section 31 can be sharpened further by
retaining the unique negative root created by the first positive outlier.  Put

```text
J(x)=u(B+1+x)H(x)+(4-u)xH(x-1),
T(x)=v(B+x)J(x)+(4-v)xJ(x-1),                            (32.1)
```

where `H=F_(B+2)` has `r` positive roots.  The interlacing argument of
Section 28 gives `J` one negative root `q` and `r` positive roots
`eta_1,...,eta_r`.  Let `alpha_1,...,alpha_r` be the `r` positive roots of
`T` whose product is largest, i.e. the roots removed when the residual
