quadratic constant is minimized.

The negative root has the elementary all-order bracket

```text
-u(B+1)/4 < q < 0.                                      (32.2)
```

Indeed, for `x<0`,

```text
H(x-1)/H(x)=prod_i (x-1-xi_i)/(x-xi_i)>1.
```

At `x_0=-u(B+1)/4`, the expression `J(x_0)/H(x_0)` equals

```text
u(B+1+x_0)+(4-u)x_0 H(x_0-1)/H(x_0)<u(B+1)+4x_0=0,
```

whereas `J(0)/H(0)=u(B+1)>0`.  Uniqueness of the negative root gives
(32.2).

The leading and constant coefficients in (32.1) satisfy

```text
lc(J)=4 lc(H),   lc(T)=4 lc(J),
T(0)=v B J(0).
```

Vieta therefore gives the exact residual-product identity

```text
D = v B (-q)/4 * prod_i(eta_i)/prod_i(alpha_i).          (32.3)
```

Consequently the last-coupling target is equivalent, without slack, to

```text
prod_i(alpha_i)/prod_i(eta_i)
    >= 4 v (-q)/(B+1).                                  (32.4)
```

Thus the missing theorem is no longer an unnormalized multivariable maximum
principle: it is the positive-root product-transfer inequality (32.4) for the
structured first-step polynomial `J`.  The actual size of `q` is essential.
For example, the rational case

```text
r=2, B=10, u=1/20, v=1/5, (c_1,c_2)=(1/50,5)
```

is an exact counterexample to the stronger but false bound
`prod(alpha_i)/prod(eta_i)>=v`, while satisfying (32.4).  Hence neither a
rootwise outward-motion claim nor the `v`-only product claim may replace the
normalized transfer theorem.

The deterministic replay
`verify_window_negative_root_product_transfer_reduction.py` proves (32.2)--
(32.4) as reductions and makes 42 exact rational algebraic-root-isolation
checks through seven negative factors on the admissible reserve
`B>=3r+4`.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_REDUCTION_AND_ALGEBRAIC_TRANSFER_AUDIT` in
`window_negative_root_product_transfer_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.
The finite audit is evidence only; (32.4) remains the analytic theorem to
prove.  The source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`934CD82651B334F0B8B5B9E3857B2C54A25C624F54248B036F6FC9077ABEC1ED`
and
`11FBEBF8F4637C7B4C90BBE84AAFB82820B5F2C2EC7551A9A566AA5107E25771`.

## 33. Direct inverse-normalized-Pochhammer comparison

The intermediate roots in (32.4) can be eliminated completely.  If the
positive roots of `H` are `xi_1,...,xi_r`, then the constant and leading
coefficients in the first line of (32.1) give

```text
(-q) prod_i(eta_i)=u(B+1)/4 * prod_i(xi_i).              (33.1)
```

Substituting (33.1) in (32.3) shows that the last-coupling target is
equivalent to

```text
prod_i(alpha_i) >= u v prod_i(xi_i).                    (33.2)
```

This is the cleanest form of the remaining theorem.  It compares the `r`
largest positive roots after the two outliers directly with the positive
roots before either outlier; the unstable first-step negative root and the
root exchanges of the second step disappear.

There is an exact transform formulation.  For

```text
F(x)=sum_j A_j (x)_j^fall,
```

define the normalized Pochhammer source map

```text
L_C[F](z)=sum_j A_j (C)_j^rise z^j.                     (33.3)
```

The definition of `H=F_(B+2)` and the two contiguous operators give

```text
L_(B+2)[H](z)=prod_i(4z-c_i),                            (33.4)

L_B[T](z)=B(B+1)(4z+u)(4z+v)prod_i(4z-c_i).             (33.5)
```

Indeed, if `F=sum_j A_j(x)_j^fall`, then one application of (28.3) sends
the falling-factorial coefficient `A_j` to

```text
a(C+j)A_j+4A_(j-1).
```

Applying this twice and multiplying the resulting coefficient by `(B)_j`
gives the coefficient of `z^j` in the right side of (33.5).  Thus (33.2)
is a special inverse-normalized-Pochhammer root-product comparison: start
with the positive-rooted source in (33.4), append the two constrained
negative roots `-u/4,-v/4`, and lower the normalization parameter by two.

The full structure in (33.4) is essential.  Positive roots and mesh one do
not suffice.  The exact rational example

```text
H(x)=(x-1)(x-2), B=10, u=1/100, v=1/2
```

has mesh one but violates (33.2).  Conversely, 50,000 floating-point
structured probes through eight negative factors found the sorted comparison
`alpha_i>=u v xi_i`; the smallest observed ratio was about `1.01647` and
approached one only near the all-infinite sharp boundary.  For `r>1`, the
product consequence of this rootwise comparison has factor `(uv)^r`, so it is
not stronger than the required single factor `uv` in (33.2).  It is finite
evidence and a possible localization clue, not a theorem or a proof of (33.2).

The upgraded deterministic replay
`verify_window_negative_root_product_transfer_reduction.py` proves
(33.1)--(33.5), verifies (33.5) for generic source coefficients through
degree six, and certifies (33.2) and the sorted comparison in 42 exact
rational algebraic-root-isolation cases through seven negative factors.  It
also certifies the arbitrary-mesh counterexample above.  The source and
report hashes are those recorded at the end of Section 32.

## 34. Generalized-Vandermonde pencil and codimension-two minor

The inverse normalized-Pochhammer transform has an exact all-degree matrix
model.  Let

```text
Q(z)=prod_(i=1)^m(z-lambda_i),
P_C(x)=L_C^(-1)[Q](x),
V_(ij)=(1+lambda_i)^j,          0<=j<m,
D_C=diag(C,C+1,...,C+m-1).                              (34.1)
```

In the shifted-power basis `1,(1+z),...,(1+z)^(m-1)`, multiplication by
`z` on `R[z]/(Q)` has matrix `Z_Q=V^(-1)Lambda V`, while the operator

```text
(1+z)d/dz+C
```

has matrix `D_C`.  Since

```text
z((1+z)d/dz+C)[z^j]=j z^j+(C+j)z^(j+1),                 (34.2)
```

the Newton-basis multiplication recurrence shows that `P_C`, after making
it monic, is the characteristic polynomial of `Z_Q D_C`.  Equivalently,

```text
P_C(x)/lc(P_C)
 =det(xV-Lambda V D_C)/det(V),                          (34.3)
```

and the pencil entries are

```text
A_C(x)_(ij)=(x-lambda_i(C+j))(1+lambda_i)^j.            (34.4)
```

This representation makes the two-outlier comparison a literal
codimension-two minor problem.  Write `a=u/4`, `b=v/4` and order the full
source nodes as

```text
-a,-b,lambda_1,...,lambda_r,
```

where `lambda_i=c_i/4>0`.  Form the `(r+2)`-dimensional pencil (34.4) with
`C=B`.  Delete the two rows belonging to `-a,-b` and delete columns `0,1`.
For the remaining row `lambda_i` and column `j=k+2`, its entry is

```text
(x-lambda_i(B+2+k))(1+lambda_i)^(k+2)
 =(1+lambda_i)^2 A_(B+2)(x)_(ik).                       (34.5)
```

Thus, up to the positive row factor
`prod_i(1+lambda_i)^2`, the complementary `r` by `r` minor is exactly the
pencil whose determinant is `H`.  The desired comparison (33.2) is
therefore a positive-generalized-eigenvalue product inequality between a
matrix pencil and this particular complementary minor.  This is much more
rigid than an arbitrary mesh-one comparison and places the last lemma in the
scope of generalized-Vandermonde total positivity, compound minors, and a
two-by-two Schur complement.

A further empirically stronger localization statement was tested in 100,000
structured floating-point cases through eight negative factors: after the
`r` largest positive roots are removed, both remaining roots lay in

```text
|x| <= sqrt(B(B+1))/4.                                  (34.6)
```

The worst observed ratio of the larger residual modulus to the right side
was about `0.991769`.  Statement (34.6) would immediately imply (33.2), but
it is evidence only and is not used as a theorem.

The deterministic exact replay
`verify_window_normalized_pochhammer_vandermonde_pencil.py` verifies
(34.1)--(34.5) through degree six and checks twelve exact complementary
minor instances through six positive source nodes.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_DEGREE_REDUCTION_AND_RATIONAL_REPLAY` in
`window_normalized_pochhammer_vandermonde_pencil_exact_20260809.json`.
The source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`8B66137FC91323BA282DB746B3C28BD9E3E71663ECED61FE61DBCB00932F67CD`
and
`3EE55B95593E15BA1031CE1CC83086F68817751886E9E78BE5EAA46C83A6AA88`.

## 35. Herglotz exceptional path and exact transition disk

The tempting coordinatewise monotonicity of the normalized positive-root
product is false for a structural reason, rather than because of floating
point noise.  An exact-rational replay at degree nine has nine positive roots
at one value of `u`, only seven real roots at the next value (the other two
form a conjugate pair), and nine positive roots again at the following value.
Exact algebraic root intervals certify a normalized-product drop from more
than `353102` to less than `132.284` and then a jump above `116763`.  The
inequality (33.2) remains very far from equality throughout.  Thus a proof
must follow the exceptional pair through its real/complex exchanges rather
than differentiate the selected positive-root product.

The commuting-shift recurrence has an exact function-theoretic form that
does this.  At stage `k`, factor the selected mesh-one positive roots as

```text
R_k(x)=P_k(x) Q_k(x),
Q_k(x)=x^2-Sx+D,
```

and put `tau=c/(4+c)`.  Away from removable common factors, a zero of
(30.6) satisfies

```text
A(x)=tau Q_k(x)/Q_k(x-1),
A(x)=x/(x+L) P_k(x-1)/P_k(x).                            (35.1)
```

If the roots of `P_k` are
`0<zeta_1<...<zeta_k` with gaps at least one, the poles of `A` are

```text
-L,zeta_1,...,zeta_k
```

and its zeros are

```text
0,zeta_1+1,...,zeta_k+1.
```

They interlace in pole-zero order.  Since `A(infinity)=1`, all residues at
its poles are negative, so

```text
A(z)=1+sum_j r_j/(z-p_j),  r_j<0,
```

and `A` maps the upper half-plane into itself.  Consequently, if
`z=X+iY`, `Y>0`, is a nonreal zero created by the next negative append, then
`Q_k(z)/Q_k(z-1)` must also have positive imaginary part.  Direct expansion
gives the exact identity

```text
Im(Q_k(z) conjugate(Q_k(z-1)))/Y
 =2D-S^2+2SX-S-2X^2+2X-2Y^2
 =-2((X-(S+1)/2)^2+Y^2)+(1+4D-S^2)/2.                  (35.2)
```

Every nonreal next-stage zero is therefore confined to the transition disk

```text
(X-(S+1)/2)^2+Y^2 < (1+4D-S^2)/4.                      (35.3)
```

In generalized-Nevanlinna language, the positive mesh factor is an ordinary
Herglotz factor and the quadratic ratio contributes one negative square.
Thus the two exceptional roots form the unique `N_1` zero path as `tau`
runs from zero to one.  Positive-root births are precisely the points at
which this path meets the real axis; selecting the largest positive roots
then performs the required branch exchange automatically.  The remaining
analytic task is now a two-dimensional path-containment statement: combine
(35.3), the endpoint faces in (31.4), and the reserve `B>=3r+4` to keep this
unique exceptional path within the shrinking admissible disk.  This is
strictly narrower than either coordinatewise monotonicity or an arbitrary
quadratic induction.

The deterministic replay
`verify_window_herglotz_exceptional_path_reduction.py` proves (35.1)--(35.3)
symbolically and audits the negative-residue partial fraction form in exact
rational mesh-one cases through seven positive factors.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_HERGLOTZ_AND_TRANSITION_DISK_REDUCTION` in
`window_herglotz_exceptional_path_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`4DF487D75B375423389835BC23A2BF44D015951900FACFBE35E8398DA74722CB`
and
`414602971B32349A40CEB70EE4AD62BDA380825EE87F151B48DEB6B6E0DAFC60`.
The exact monotonicity-refutation source and report hashes are respectively
`57C6573CA4CD566619E932D9D109EED0A3285AECBB1E21920208B1EE021896BA`
and
`15AF2C6DAE13A7551FB2C0613BD287691B7D040F4C8BEB4A9B877DCDC88F45D5`.

## 36. All-stage negative-axis barrier

One boundary component of the path-containment problem in Section 35 can be
closed in every order.  At stage `k`, write `m=k+2`, `L=n-m`, and use the
polarized form

```text
R_k(x)=K_(m,n)(x;u/4,v/4,-c_1/4,...,-c_k/4).
```

At `x=-y`, group the sum by the number `l` of selected negative parameters.
After removing a positive common weight and the common sign `(-1)^k`, put

```text
z=y+k-l,  A=L-y+1.
```

The remaining multiplier is exactly

```text
K(A,z;u,v)
 =u v A(A+1)-2A(2(u+v)-u v)z+(4-u)(4-v)z(z+1).         (36.1)
```

It is multiaffine in `u,v`.  Its four corner values are

```text
16z(z+1),
4z(3(z+1)-A),
4z(3(z+1)-A),
(A-3z)^2+A+9z.                                          (36.2)
```

If `y>(L-2)/4`, then `z>=y` and

```text
3(z+1)-A >= 4y-L+2 > 0,
A+9z >= L+1+8y > 0.
```

All four corners, and hence every summand, are strictly positive.  Therefore

```text
(-1)^k R_k(-y)>0                 for y>(L-2)/4.          (36.3)
```

After the `k` selected positive factors are removed, their value at `-y`
has the same sign `(-1)^k`.  The exceptional quadratic consequently obeys

```text
Q_k(-y)>0                        for y>(L-2)/4.          (36.4)
```

It has no negative root to the left of `-(L-2)/4`.  Since

```text
sqrt((L+1)(L+2))/4 > (L-2)/4,
```

the exceptional pair cannot escape the stage disk through the negative real
axis.  Together with Section 35, the only unsolved escape mechanism is now a
nonreal crossing of the target circle; positive real crossings are the
allowed branch exchanges handled by selecting the largest positive roots.

The exact replay `verify_window_all_stage_negative_axis_barrier.py` proves
(36.1)--(36.4), including symbolic grouping checks through six negative
factors, and reports `PASS_EXACT_ALL_STAGE_NEGATIVE_AXIS_BARRIER` in
`window_all_stage_negative_axis_barrier_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`4783188DE55E4F823235D9FCC37DEEFA43C1AED6870D3ADD66DB27ADBC684EB5`
and
`C54601004483B3CA2C9B546A1052968B49164B32553E0F3540C5D15B983A96AE`.

## 37. Exact base-stage target-circle theorem

The remaining nonreal-circle mechanism is now closed at the initial
two-outlier stage, uniformly in the reserve.  Put

```text
R(x)=16x^2+4((L+2)(u+v)-4)x+uv(L+2)(L+1),
F(z)=zR(z-1)/((z+L)R(z)),
rho^2=L(L+1)/16.                                        (37.1)
```

For `0<t<1`, let

```text
C_t(z)=zR(z-1)-t(z+L)R(z).                              (37.2)
```

Suppose that a nonreal zero `z` of `C_t` lies on `|z|=rho`.  Its conjugate
is another zero and their product is `rho^2`.  If the coefficients of the
cubic are `a,b,c,d`, Vieta therefore fixes the real third zero and the real
part of the conjugate pair as

```text
w=-d/(a rho^2),
X=(-b/a-w)/2.                                            (37.3)
```

Matching the pairwise-product coefficient gives the Schur-boundary
polynomial

```text
N=c/a-rho^2+w b/a+w^2.                                  (37.4)
```

Thus a circle pair requires `N=0`, while it is nonreal precisely when

```text
W=X^2-rho^2<0.                                           (37.5)
```

After taking the positive-denominator numerators in (37.4)--(37.5), set
`L=7/(1-a)` and multiply both polynomials by `(1-a)^2`.  The resulting
polynomials have degree at most two in each of `a,u,v,t` on the unit
four-cube.  Their normalized degree-two Bernstein tensors represent `8N`
and `2W`.  One split at `u=1/2` gives the exact coefficientwise identities

```text
W-6N >= 0 in Bernstein form,  0<=u<=1/2,
W-8N >= 0 in Bernstein form,  1/2<=u<=1.                 (37.6)
```

All 81 coefficients in each half-cube are nonnegative; their integer
control ranges are respectively `0..41472` and `0..41472`.  On `N=0`,
(37.6) proves `W>=0`, contradicting (37.5).  Therefore

```text
F(z) is not in (0,1) whenever Im(z)!=0 and |z|=rho.       (37.7)
```

This allows real positive crossings (where the selected positive branch is
supposed to exchange) and excludes exactly the nonreal escape needed for the
base stage.  It also explains the numerical sign pattern more accurately:
real values at upper-semicircle intersections can be negative or exceed one,
but never lie strictly between zero and one.

The deterministic exact certificate
`certify_window_base_circle_schur_boundary.py` derives (37.3)--(37.5) from
the cubic, constructs the projective Bernstein tensors, and replays both
identities in (37.6).  It reports `PASS_EXACT_BASE_CIRCLE_SCHUR_BOUNDARY`
after three cells in
`window_base_circle_schur_boundary_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`19484C09078C9AA4A3B2FCAAE7667968FCB45487B155CF39B91D58D9F0075CC8`
and
`BD1453356A3397D2764B9C80319AF439A42373C8A4DE23C47F6D9948B838F781`.

## 38. Exact second-stage degenerate-circle exclusion

The next append gives a quartic.  With `L>=10`, put

```text
R0(z)=16z^2+4((L+3)(u+v)-4)z+uv(L+3)(L+2),
R1(z)=zR0(z-1)-p(z+L+1)R0(z),
Q(z)=zR1(z-1)-t(z+L)R1(z),
rho^2=L(L+1)/16.                                      (38.1)
```

Write `Q=sum(c_j z^j)`, with `c4` the leading coefficient.  If a
target-circle pair is written as

```text
Q/c4=(z^2-2Xz+rho^2)(z^2-Az+B),                        (38.2)
```

coefficient matching gives

```text
C=rho^2 c1-rho^4 c3,
D=-2(rho^4 c4-c0),
E1(X)=C+DX,                                             (38.3)
E2(X)=4c4 rho^2 X^2+2c3 rho^2 X
      +(rho^2 c2-c0-rho^4 c4).                         (38.4)
```

The ordinary resultant argument fixes `X` when `D!=0`.  Its only algebraic
gap is the simultaneous locus `C=D=0`, where the quartic is reciprocal with
respect to the circle.  Put

```text
q=c2+2rho^2 c4.                                         (38.5)
```

An exact five-variable Bernstein certificate proves on `C=D=0` that

```text
c3<=0,
c3^2>=16rho^2 c4^2,
q>=0,
q^2>=4rho^2 c3^2.                                      (38.6)
```

Since `c4=16(1-p)(1-t)>0` in the open parameter domain, (38.6) implies

```text
c3/c4<=-4rho,
q/c4+2(c3/c4)rho>=0.                                   (38.7)
```

The normalized quadratic (38.4) is therefore decreasing on
`[-rho,rho]` and nonnegative at `rho`; it has no zero with `|X|<rho`.
Thus the degenerate locus cannot contain a nonreal target-circle pair.

The compactification `L=10/(1-a)` has one false boundary difficulty at
`a=u=v=1`.  Three exact corner identities remove it for every finite `L`:

```text
C-20D >= (575/2)(1-a)
  on 1/2<=a,u,v<=1, 0<=p,t<=1/2,

C-20D >= 1-a
  on 1/2<=a,u,v<=1, 1/2<=p<=1, 0<=t<=1/8,

C-20D >= 1-a
  on 1/2<=a,u,v<=1, 0<=p<=1/8, 1/2<=t<=1.              (38.8)
```

The first identity has normalized control scales `96` for `C-20D` and
`6` for `1-a`; the exact integer remainder is
`controls(C-20D)-4600 controls(1-a)` with range `0..196305`.
The other two identities have nonnegative direct control ranges
`0..866310`.

The deterministic certificate
`certify_window_one_prior_factor_circle_degeneracy.py` reports
`PASS_EXACT_SECOND_STAGE_DEGENERACY` after 436 processed cells and 234
certified leaves in
`window_one_prior_factor_circle_degeneracy_exact_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`B4EF5BD624748742B78179D8550506036E0DB462317DD801C52BF2DB023A69A7`
and
`DDF491910DA3854C2759423D6E97AB9B247679202C14ADDA03BAAD13B479100A`.

The complementary nondegenerate audit is now complete.  Its former sole tail
was the compactification corner

```text
a -> 1,  u,v -> 0,  p,t -> 1/5.                         (38.9)
```

At the exact boundary, its projected resultant `N` and disk witness `W`
obey

```text
N=125000(1-p)^2(1-t)^2(15pt+p+t-1),
W=1000000(3pt-p-t-1)(5pt-3p-3t+1),
(1-p)(1-t)W-8N
 =4000000(1-p)(1-t)(p-t)^2.                            (38.10)
```

Hence the tail is a tangential equality, not evidence of a negative crossing.
Two exact projective charts extend this identity to a neighborhood.  Put

```text
h=15pt+p+t-1,  D0=8(1-a).
```

The upper chart uses `1/8<=p<=1/4`, substitutes
`t=(1-p+h)/(1+15p)`, and blows up `h=-D0 R/4`.  The lower-tail chart uses
`0<=p<=1/8` and `u,v>=1/2`; append symmetry gives their `p<->t` images.
An exact polynomial identity on the upper chart is

```text
4(3P+11)^2 G + 96(5P-3)(15P+119) Nbar
 =1875(3P+11)^4 (5P-3)^2(3D0+80(2-U-V)),               (38.11)
```

where `G=(F-F0)/D0`,
`F=(8U+8)(8V+8)Wbar-200D0 Nbar`, and

```text
F0=62500(3P+11)^2(5P-3)^2(U+1)(V+1)(UV-1)^2.
```

The companion certificate therefore places every zero of `N` in both charts
inside the closed wedge `-2(1-a)<=h<=0` and proves `W>=0` there.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_NONDEGENERATE_SECOND_STAGE_CORNER` after 112 cells and 57
certified leaves, with no unresolved cell.  Its source and report SHA-256
hashes are respectively
`E6FFA11475DA15E37A487C2724D3839E799AF34A3DF7FAC9A52DEA01B777DD4B`
and
`5F730BEDB0DBC54DC05B4B1A3506DB926E691F1EC1FBF403DE6B951F72BAC7FE`.

The other apparent projective boundary is the all-infinite degree-drop
corner.  An exact Bernstein tensor on
`0<=a<=1`, `7/8<=u,v,p,t<=1` proves

```text
N >= (1-a)((1-u)+(1-v)+(1-p)+(1-t)).                   (38.12)
```

For finite `L`, `a<1`; hence `N=0` in this box forces `u=v=p=t=1`, where
the quartic leading coefficient `16(1-p)(1-t)` vanishes.  Thus this boundary
cannot contain a target-circle crossing in the finite-append domain.

With (38.8), the exact degenerate-neighborhood certificate, (38.11), its
append-symmetric image, and (38.12) installed, the global deterministic
replay reports
`PASS_EXACT_ONE_PRIOR_FACTOR_SECOND_APPEND_CIRCLE_BOUND`.  It processed 8,995
cells and certified 4,498 leaves, with no unresolved cell.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`77D3FBAF3C76A8FF2E3FD2AE4DC28E16265116DFD1667CF84367B7DD234A1FA8`
and
`2CD45D360635DFD7828B6EF2862F89F1CFEB9F46E24DA87C8FEB6F20B0FC459B`.
Consequently the second-append quartic has no nonreal pair on the target
circle for finite `L>=10` and finite append parameters.  The remaining
obligation is now the all-order reachable-locus/last-pivot bridge, not a
second-stage exceptional corner.

## 39. Exact unit-outlier boundary reduction to structured mesh preservation

The direct comparison (33.2) has a particularly rigid boundary at `u=v=1`.
For a source polynomial `Q(z)=sum_j q_j z^j`, define

```text
P_C[Q](x)=sum_j q_j x^fall_j/C^rise_j,
L_(a,C)F=a(C+x)F(x)+(4-a)xF(x-1).
```

Coefficient comparison gives the exact contiguous identity

```text
L_(a,C) P_(C+1)[Q] = 4C P_C[(z+a/4)Q].                (39.1)
```

At `u=v=1`, put

```text
H=P_(B+2)[Q],
J=(B+1+x)H(x)+3xH(x-1),
T=(B+x)J(x)+3xJ(x-1).
```

Then (39.1) gives

```text
J=4(B+1)P_(B+1)[(z+1/4)Q],
T=16B(B+1)P_B[(z+1/4)^2Q].                            (39.2)
```

Suppose the positive roots of `H` are
`xi_1<...<xi_r` with mesh at least one.  The roots
`-B-1,xi_1,...,xi_r` of `(B+1+x)H(x)` strictly interlace the roots
`0,xi_1+1,...,xi_r+1` of `xH(x-1)`.  Hence the positive roots
`eta_1<...<eta_r` of `J` obey

```text
xi_i < eta_i < xi_i+1.                                (39.3)
```

If `J` itself has mesh at least one, then at every positive root `eta_i`,

```text
T(eta_i)=3 eta_i J(eta_i-1).                          (39.4)
```

The signs in (39.4) alternate; the value at `eta_r` is negative, while the
leading sign of `T` at positive infinity is positive.  Thus `T` has a root
between each consecutive pair of positive roots of `J` and a root above its
largest positive root.  Its `r` selected positive roots therefore satisfy

```text
alpha_i>eta_i>xi_i,
prod_i alpha_i > prod_i xi_i.                         (39.5)
```

This proves (33.2) on the entire `u=v=1` boundary conditional on one precise
structured mesh lemma:

```text
If Q has r positive roots and B>=3r+4, then
P_(B+1)[(z+1/4)Q] has mesh at least one.               (39.6)
```

The lemma is exact in rank one.  With `Q=z-lambda` and the monic scaling
`H=x-(B+2)lambda`, direct expansion gives

```text
J=4x^2+(B-2-4(B+2)lambda)x-(B+1)(B+2)lambda,
gap(J)^2-1
 =(B+2)(16B lambda^2+8B lambda+B
        +32lambda^2+32lambda-6)/16.                   (39.7)
```

For `B>=7`, every coefficient after substituting `B=7+beta` is nonnegative
and the constant term is positive.  Thus (39.6), and consequently the unit
boundary product theorem, holds for `r=1` and every positive `lambda`.

The source structure in (39.6) cannot be discarded.  For the arbitrary
mesh-one polynomial

```text
B=10,  H=(x-1/4)(x-5/4),
J=(4x-5)(16x^2+16x-11)/16,
```

the positive-root gap of `J` is exactly `(7-sqrt(15))/4<1`.  Hence a proof
from mesh of `H` alone would be false; it must use that `H` is a normalized
Pochhammer transform of a positive-rooted source.

The deterministic verifier
`verify_window_unit_outlier_mesh_boundary_reduction.py` checks (39.1)--(39.2)
for generic source coefficients through degree six, proves (39.7)
symbolically, and exactly isolates all roots in 12 structured rational cases
through rank six at `B=3r+4` and `B=3r+7`.  Every audited `J` has one negative
root, `r` positive roots, and mesh at least one.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_UNIT_OUTLIER_BOUNDARY_REDUCTION_AND_FINITE_AUDIT` in
`window_unit_outlier_mesh_boundary_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.  The
source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`8BF9F1CE7321A61CB75BABE61D207425930C8A768ADABC68DB8A8FEF009F3664`
and
`53835C564C7ED2B87D574D0AD4ED7BF1B7652D3C3E8FE8DE7BBC1A1067F15EBD`.
The rank-two-through-six audit is finite evidence, not a proof of (39.6).
After (39.6), the remaining step is to extend the boundary argument from
`u=v=1` to arbitrary `0<u,v<=1`, or to prove (33.2) directly.

## 40. All-rank unit-boundary theorem by a proper-position pencil

The conditional mesh lemma (39.6) is not needed.  A stronger direct argument
proves the required `u=v=1` comparison in every rank.  Let `A` be a monic
degree-`r` polynomial whose roots are positive, and use the plain falling
Pochhammer transform

```text
H(x)=Poch[A](x)=sum_j a_j x^fall_j.
```

Write `Delta H=H(x)-H(x-1)` and define

```text
K=((r-1)+2x)Delta H-(x-1)Delta^2 H.                  (40.1)
```

The exact transform identities are

```text
V=zA''+(r+1+2z)A',
K(x)=Poch[V](x-1),
H(x)+yK(x)=Poch[W_y](x-1),
W_y=A+A'+yV.                                         (40.2)
```

We first prove that the pencil in (40.2) is hyperbolic for every real `y`.
The weighted derivative identity

```text
V=z^(-r)e^(-2z) d/dz[z^(r+1)e^(2z)A']               (40.3)
```

shows by Rolle that `V` has `r-1` positive roots.  Its constant and leading
signs force its remaining root to be negative.  Put `P_0=A+A'`.  The roots
`p` of `P_0` solve `A'/A=-1`.  If the roots of `A` are `lambda_i`, then at
every such `p`,

```text
V(p)/A(p)=-F(p),
F(p)=r+1+p+p sum_i 1/(p-lambda_i)^2.                 (40.4)
```

For `p>=0`, `F(p)>0`.  If `p=-q<0`, set
`t_i=1/(lambda_i+q)`.  The root equation gives `sum_i t_i=1`, while
`q<r` and `q sum_i t_i^2<1`; hence `F(p)>r-q>0`.  The signs in (40.4),
together with (40.3), prove that `P_0` and `V` strictly interlace.  Therefore
`W_y=P_0+yV` is hyperbolic for every real `y`.

It remains to show that its Pochhammer image is hyperbolic.  Put
`h=e_r/e_(r-1)`, where the `e_j` are the elementary symmetric functions of
the `lambda_i`.  The only parameters at which the number of negative roots
of `W_y` can change are

```text
y_0=(h-1)/(r+1)       (a root crosses zero),
y_inf=-1/(2r)         (a root crosses infinity).     (40.5)
```

At `y_inf`, the coefficient of `z^(r-1)` is
`a_(r-1)/r=-e_1/r<0`.  It follows that the only regime in which `W_y` has
two negative roots is the interval `y_0<y<y_inf` when `y_0<y_inf<0`.
Outside it, `W_y` has at least `r-1` positive roots.  The classical positive-
zero theorem for the Pochhammer transformation then supplies at least
`r-1` positive zeros of `Poch[W_y]`, so its last degree slot is real.

In the exceptional two-negative regime, the theorem supplies `r-2` positive
zeros.  For `G_y=Poch[W_y]`, exact endpoint evaluation gives

```text
sign G_y(0)=(-1)^(r-1),
G_y(-1)=A(0)+yK(0),
K(0)=(-1)^(r+1) sum_(k=1)^(r-1)(r-k)k!e_(r-k),
sign G_y(-1)=(-1)^r.                                 (40.6)
```

Thus `G_y` has a zero in `(-1,0)`.  Together with its `r-2` positive zeros,
only one degree slot remains, and that slot must also be real.  Critical and
multiple-root cases follow by continuity.  Hence `H+yK` is hyperbolic for
every real `y`; Obreschkoff's theorem makes `H` and `K` interlacing.  The
constant signs orient their roots as

```text
K_1 < H_1 < K_2 < H_2 < ... < K_r < H_r.             (40.7)
```

The Pochhammer positive-root theorem also gives mesh at least one for `H`.
Consequently, at each root `xi_i` of `H`, (40.7) and the sign of
`Delta H(xi_i)=-H(xi_i-1)` imply

```text
K(xi_i)/Delta H(xi_i)>=0,
(xi_i-1)Delta^2H(xi_i)/Delta H(xi_i)
 <= r-1+2xi_i.                                       (40.8)
```

Return now to the normalized source in the forest reduction.  Set
`C=B+2>=3r+6`, so `H=P_C[Q]`; equivalently its ordinary source is
`A=sum_j q_j z^j/(C)^rise_j`, which has positive roots.  For the two
unit-boundary appends, direct expansion gives

```text
T=H(C^2+8Cx-3C+16x^2-24x+2)
  +9x(x-1)Delta^2H
  -3x(2C+8x-10)Delta H.                              (40.9)
```

At every `xi_i>0`, the final sign threshold exceeds (40.8) by

```text
(2C-3r+2xi_i-7)/3>0.                                 (40.10)
```

Therefore `T(xi_i)` has the sign opposite to `Delta H(xi_i)`.  These signs
alternate, `T(xi_r)<0`, and `T(+infinity)>0`; hence `T` has a positive root
between every consecutive pair `xi_i,xi_(i+1)` and another above `xi_r`.
Its `r` selected positive roots `alpha_i` satisfy

```text
alpha_i>xi_i,
prod_i alpha_i>prod_i xi_i.                          (40.11)
```

This proves the direct comparison (33.2) on the whole `u=v=1` boundary in
all ranks.  It supersedes the conditional use of (39.6); the arbitrary-mesh
counterexample in Section 39 remains useful only as a warning that the
earlier sufficient route could not be proved from mesh alone.

The deterministic verifier
`verify_window_unit_boundary_discrete_log_derivative_reduction.py` checks
the transform, pencil, endpoint, and final-sign identities symbolically
through ranks six or seven, and performs 14 exact rational proper-position
audits through rank seven, including source roots ranging from `1/1000` to
`1000`.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_RANK_UNIT_BOUNDARY_PROPER_POSITION_THEOREM` in
`window_unit_boundary_discrete_log_derivative_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.
The finite audits are evidence only; the all-rank result is the analytic
argument (40.1)--(40.11).  The source and report SHA-256 hashes are
respectively
`5BA1014818D5BBFBFF5B235987C99CBCCA89F82B7A0A2F79994E05CEAC34D39E`
and
`948EB529E96FE7D0B40A342ADD7EF296C0EEED24E12484F6F4C06F4514E4B7E1`.
The remaining obligation is to extend this endpoint theorem to arbitrary
`0<u,v<=1`, or to control the exceptional pair during deformation away from
the unit boundary.

## 41. Exact all-rank large-parameter region

The proper-position inequality (40.8) extends beyond the unit boundary.
Keep `C=B+2`, and let the two general appends be

```text
J=u(C-1+x)H+(4-u)xH(x-1),
T=v(C-2+x)J+(4-v)xJ(x-1).
```

If `xi` is a positive root of `H`, put
`d=Delta H(xi)` and `dd=Delta^2 H(xi)`.  Exact expansion gives

```text
T(xi)=xi(4-u)(4-v)d
       *((xi-1)dd/d-Theta),                            (41.1)

Theta=2(xi-1)+(C+xi-2)
      *(u/(4-u)+v/(4-v)).                              (41.2)
```

By (40.8), the first term in parentheses is at most `r-1+2xi`.  The
margin between (41.2) and this bound is exactly

```text
(C+xi-2)(u/(4-u)+v/(4-v))-(r+1).                      (41.3)
```

Therefore, throughout the region

```text
(C-2)(u/(4-u)+v/(4-v)) >= r+1,                        (41.4)
```

the values `T(xi_i)` have the outward alternating orientation used in
Section 40.  The same interval argument proves `alpha_i>xi_i` for all `i`,
and hence proves (33.2), with the stronger conclusion
`prod alpha_i>prod xi_i`.

At the sharp reserve `C>=3r+6`, a uniform sufficient condition is

```text
u/(4-u)+v/(4-v) >= (r+1)/(3r+4).                      (41.5)
```

In particular, the rank-independent condition that the left side is at
least `1/3` closes every rank.  The unresolved parameter region is now the
complementary small corner below (41.4), where rootwise outward placement is
false and the weaker target factor `uv` must be used.

The deterministic replay
`verify_window_large_parameter_proper_position_region.py` checks the full
two-step identity and (41.1)--(41.3) for generic input polynomials through
rank seven.  It reports `PASS_EXACT_LARGE_PARAMETER_ALL_RANK_REGION` in
`window_large_parameter_proper_position_region_exact_20260809.json`.  The
source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`5550608BA1CB61E39CF9B755C33EF80B587D8D3D7F3EEDB5B19D77A3C04C3AAF`
and
`C61FE5E157E3EA6FE61BA7609CF81D9582E90498362BB1A4470323B6AC52B0AA`.

## 42. Exact failure of the unrestricted transfer lemma, but not of the window theorem

The remaining small-parameter extension of (33.2) is false.  An exact
rational counterexample is

```text
r=5, B=19, C=21, u=v=1/20,
L_(B+2)[H](z)=(4z-1/1000)^5.                           (42.1)
```

Exact Sturm isolation gives five positive roots of `H`, one negative and
five positive roots of the first append `J`, and exactly five real roots of
the degree-seven second append `T`; all five are positive, so the remaining
two roots form a nonreal conjugate pair.  If `xi_i` are the roots of `H` and
`alpha_i` the five positive roots of `T`, rational interval arithmetic proves

```text
prod_i(alpha_i)/prod_i(xi_i)
 = 0.002191287074185223457832...
 < 1/400 = uv.                                         (42.2)
```

Equivalently, the residual conjugate-pair product satisfies

```text
D=27.09594771925405647247... > 95/4=B(B+1)/16.         (42.3)
```

Both inequalities are strict comparisons between exact rational isolating
intervals, not floating-point root decisions.  Thus (32.4), (33.2), the
global preservation claim (30.7), and the proposed all-order target-circle
containment in Sections 35--38 cannot serve as universal lemmas.  The exact
base- and second-stage circle certificates remain valid at their stated
orders, but their hoped-for arbitrary-order induction is blocked by (42.3).

Crucially, (42.1) is not a counterexample to the actual window theorem.  It
corresponds to the genuine sharp-reserve input

```text
(p,alpha)=(25,0),
Gamma(t)=(1-t/20)^2(t+1/1000)^5,
p-alpha=25=4 deg(Gamma)-3.                             (42.4)
```

Direct construction from the factorial window formula gives a degree-12
polynomial with 12 strictly negative roots.  Its largest-magnitude and
smallest-magnitude roots are approximately

```text
-9.95389080336610494,
-0.0000103441336170513184.                             (42.5)
```

The same is true of the pre-Laguerre reduced window.  Therefore (27.11), or
equivalently the residual-disk bound, was a sufficient condition for one
particular terminal Jacobi/Duran construction, not a necessary condition
for window hyperbolicity.  The original theorem survives while that
over-restrictive proof route does not.

Sections 40--41 remain valid theorems on the unit and large-parameter
regions, respectively.  The revised small-corner obligation is to prove the
window polynomial directly, allowing the terminal coupling used in Section
27 to become negative while the full Jacobi characteristic polynomial stays
hyperbolic.  The most relevant surviving descriptions are the exact
finite-free factorization of Section 26 and the one-conjugate-pair CZDS
reduction preceding Section 27.

## 43. The PF adjacent-row route survives the transfer counterexample

The exact input of Section 42 is handled by the original PF-constrained
induction from Sections 1--25.  One step before the fifth negative factor,
put

```text
Gamma_4(t)=(1-t/20)^2(t+1/1000)^4,
Q_0=S_(25,0)[Gamma_4],
Q_1=t S_(23,1)[Gamma_4].                               (43.1)
```

Exact Sturm isolation proves that `Q_0` has 12 strictly negative roots,
`Q_1` has 11 strictly negative roots and a root at zero, and their ordered
root intervals have all 11 strict common-interlacer overlaps.  The smallest
certified overlap gap is

```text
0.00001739556627346004381768... .                       (43.2)
```

The factorial recursion is the exact polynomial identity

```text
S_(25,0)[(t+1/1000)Gamma_4]
 = (1/1000)Q_0+Q_1.                                    (43.3)
```

Thus the strict common interlacer in (43.1) proves that the degree-12
polynomial in (42.4) is negative-rooted, even though its auxiliary residual
quadratic violates (42.3).  This cleanly separates the two routes:

```text
unrestricted residual-disk transfer: false;
PF-constrained adjacent-row induction: passes the same adversarial input.
```

The live arbitrary-length obligation therefore returns to the theorem in
Section 4: evaluation of the shifted rows against every PF coefficient
sequence must preserve the required adjacent common interlacer.  The
length-three collision atlas now running is a genuine first structural case
of that theorem, rather than an auxiliary check of the false disk invariant.

The deterministic verifier
`verify_window_transfer_counterexample_pf_recovery.py` certifies (43.1)--
(43.3), all root counts, and every overlap.  It reports

and
`1BC52547A83D9C503CE17E01AAE2208F5B9B4AE12DDDF9D6DE639C99368C1378`.
