## 45. Exact reduction to one weighted derivative chain

The entire shifted-row family has a common source polynomial.  Put

```text
f(x)=S_(p,alpha)[1](x),
kappa_h=(p+alpha)^fall_h / p^fall_(2h),
R_h(x)=kappa_h x^h f^(h)(x).                            (45.1)
```

Direct cancellation in the factorial coefficient formula gives, for every
admissible `j`,

```text
S_(p-2j,alpha+j)[1](x)=kappa_j f^(j)(x).               (45.2)
```

More generally, if `Gamma(t)=sum_h gamma_h t^h`, then linearity and the same
coefficient cancellation give the all-rank identities

```text
S_(p,alpha)[Gamma](x)=sum_h gamma_h R_h(x),             (45.3)
x S_(p-2,alpha+1)[Gamma](x)=sum_h gamma_h R_(h+1)(x).   (45.4)
```

Indeed, the coefficient of `x^k` contributed by `t^h` differs from the
source-one coefficient by

```text
kappa_h k^fall_h,
```

which is exactly the coefficient multiplier in `kappa_h x^h f^(h)`.  The
adjacent formula follows from the telescoping identity

```text
kappa_1(p,alpha) kappa_h(p-2,alpha+1)
 =kappa_(h+1)(p,alpha).                                (45.5)
```

Thus, with `Q_0,Q_1` as in Section 4,

```text
(Q_0,Q_1)=(sum_h gamma_h R_h, sum_h gamma_h R_(h+1)).  (45.6)
```

Appending `(t+c)` is simply the shift update

```text
sum gamma_h R_h -> c sum gamma_h R_h+sum gamma_h R_(h+1),
```

recovering Section 1 without any new factorial manipulation.  The surviving
arbitrary-length theorem is therefore a constrained adjacent-shift theorem
for one Jacobi derivative chain, not a theorem about an unrelated growing
family of window polynomials.  Equivalently, since
`x^h f^(h)=(x d/dx)^fall_h f`, the transform is a diagonal polynomial in the
Euler operator, with the nonconstant hypergeometric weights `kappa_h`.

This representation does not by itself prove common interlacing: the two
positive outliers make the coefficient sequence `gamma_h` signed, and the
unrestricted nonnegative-cone strengthening is already false by Section 3.
Its value is that collision derivatives, Hurwitz minors, or an apolarity
argument can now be formulated using one Sturm derivative chain.  The exact
replay `verify_pf_factorial_window_derivative_chain.py` verifies (45.2)--
(45.5) symbolically and checks the full factorial formula for four generic
rational inputs through degree eight.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_FACTORIAL_WINDOW_DERIVATIVE_CHAIN` in
`pf_factorial_window_derivative_chain_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`9307708E6D78F3FAD0F7FC1823D8780826C9C79358C2A1609218DD8BD38747E4`
and
`287A72A96FABFFA9EB621295A3DC1244661084774ED0E9F46841295DFF5D976A`.

## 46. The false transfer does not kill the actual Durán coefficient route

Section 42 refuted a reordered unrestricted transfer, not the coefficient
polynomial naturally attached to the actual monic-Laguerre combination.  This
distinction is exact and material.  For a degree-`m` input
`Gamma(t)=sum_h gamma_h t^h`, define

```text
Q_D(z)=sum_(h=0)^m gamma_h (N)_h^fall/4^h (z)_(m-h)^rise. (46.1)
```

This is precisely Durán's coefficient polynomial for the monic-Laguerre core
in (27.4), because the Laguerre coefficient there is
`e_h (N)_h^fall/4^h` and `(-1)^h e_h=gamma_h`.  Durán's Theorem 1.3 has two
relevant consequences:

1. If every root of `Q_D` is real and below `beta+1`, the degree-`n` monic
   Laguerre combination has positive simple roots for every `n>=m`.
2. For arbitrary real coefficients it is eventually positive-rooted, but the
   general explicit threshold depends on coefficient size and is not uniform
   enough for the present theorem.

The Pochhammer zero-count theorem used in Section 27 already proves that the
actual `Q_D` has at least `m-2` negative real roots.  Hence only two roots can
ever be exceptional.  The correct sharpened obligation is to control those
two roots, or the corresponding quadratic Durán seed, while retaining the
actual joint transform (46.1).  Section 42 shows only that one cannot first
Pochhammer-transform the negative-factor block and then append the two
positive outliers by the broad transfer operators.

The distinction is visible on the exact counterexample from Section 42.  For

```text
(p,alpha,n,beta,N,m)=(25,0,12,1/2,25,7),
Gamma(t)=(1-t/20)^2(t+1/1000)^5,                         (46.2)
```

the broad transfer polynomial has degree seven but only five real roots.  In
contrast, the actual polynomial (46.1) has seven simple negative roots, with
extremes

```text
-10162.79667846106106176...,
-0.1923198621130257157007... .                          (46.3)
```

Thus every root lies below `beta+1=3/2` and `n=12>=7`; Durán's theorem proves
the positive-rooted Laguerre core directly.  Exact Sturm isolation independently
finds all 12 positive simple roots of that core.  In this adversarial input
there is no exceptional residual quadratic at all.  The nonreal pair in the
Section 42 transfer is an artifact of changing the operator order.

The deterministic replay
`verify_transfer_counterexample_actual_duran_escape.py` certifies both degree-
seven root counts, the Durán hypotheses, and the degree-12 Laguerre core.  It
reports `PASS_EXACT_ACTUAL_DURAN_ESCAPE_FROM_FALSE_TRANSFER_LEMMA` in
`transfer_counterexample_actual_duran_escape_exact_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`660A15CC88E40BD1F650E5654C4940C98489069723EEDFCE11F9659A4BC140F2`
and
`A1D3F9722F8BBECBC5BE2AB85B172FDAEA77A7F044DF7BABDF36DD480E3AE754`.

This reopens the Section 27 strategy in its narrower, valid form: control the
at-most-two exceptional roots of the actual `Q_D` directly.  It does not
reinstate the false arbitrary-source transfer inequalities of Sections 32--38.

## 47. Exact actual-coefficient audit of the exceptional pair

The corrected target in Section 46 can be audited without numerically factoring
the exceptional quadratic.  Isolate any `m-2` negative roots of the actual
`Q_D` as rational intervals.  If their sum and product are `Sigma_b` and
`Pi_b`, Vieta gives rigorous intervals for the two residual roots:

```text
Sigma_exc = Sigma_all-Sigma_b,
Pi_exc    = Pi_all/Pi_b.                                (47.1)
```

Writing the residual coefficient polynomial as

```text
(z)_2^rise-G_1(z)_1^rise+G_2,
```

we have `G_1=1+Sigma_exc` and `G_2=Pi_exc`.  Therefore outward-rounded
rational bounds in (47.1) certify the exact quadratic Laguerre/Jacobi margins

```text
M_1=(s-1)(s+beta-1)-G_2,
M_2=(s+beta-1)(s+beta-G_1)+G_2,                         (47.2)
```

without approximating the exceptional roots themselves.

An exact audit now passes 110 actual PF inputs, both parities and every factor
length `2<=m<=12`.  It includes the sharp reserve, repeated factors of scale
`1000`, alternating scales `1000` and `1/1000`, and deterministic rational
grid inputs.  Every case has at least `m-2` exactly isolated negative roots,
and both lower bounds in (47.2) are strictly positive.  Across the nontrivial
`m>=3` cases, the smallest certified margins are approximately

```text
M_1 > 4.99982515442402022,
M_2 > 6.72315353572864562.                              (47.3)
```

The large separation in (47.3), despite the extreme factor scales, is strong
evidence that the actual two-root inequality has a coarse all-rank proof and
is not balanced on the delicate disk condition refuted in Section 42.  This
remains finite evidence: the missing step is to derive (47.2) uniformly from
the actual coefficient polynomial (46.1) and the two-outlier parameter bounds.

The deterministic replay
`audit_actual_duran_exceptional_pair_margins.py` reports
`PASS_EXACT_ACTUAL_DURAN_EXCEPTIONAL_PAIR_AUDIT` in
`actual_duran_exceptional_pair_margins_exact_20260809.json`.  The combined
primitive digest is
`AE4477885DB49FD5D227271BE601FA4194A01FE8C1ACCC3579CF00A7BBB65BFC`.
The source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`C8A91A06030CC14C3DCD42A1294893BAD27C3E83B5B698F01729289FDF18B996`
and
`3593108284472151064E1D02082FB424132CBC8E50E56C1FD6EB68461759C0A4`.

## 48. All-rank coefficientwise proof of the second exceptional margin

One of the two inequalities in (47.2) has a direct all-rank proof.  Factor
the actual Durán polynomial as

```text
Q_D(z)=B(z)E(z),
E(z)=(z)_2^rise-G_1 z+G_2,                              (48.1)
```

where `B` is monic and its `m-2` roots are the negative roots guaranteed by
the Pochhammer theorem.  Put

```text
L=s+beta-1=n-m+beta+1>0.                               (48.2)
```

Because every root of `B` is negative, `B(L)>0`, while

```text
E(L)=L(L+1-G_1)+G_2=M_2.                              (48.3)
```

It is therefore enough to prove `Q_D(L)>0`.

Write the two positive parameters as `u,v` and the remaining parameters as
`-d_i`, with every `d_i>=0`.  Expanding (46.1) in the elementary symmetric
polynomials of the `d_i`, the coefficient of `e_j(d)` is

```text
w_j-(u+v)w_(j+1)+uv w_(j+2)
=w_j {1-(u+v)r_j+uv r_j r_(j+1)},                     (48.4)

w_j=(N)_j^fall/4^j (L)_(m-j)^rise,
r_j=w_(j+1)/w_j=(N-j)/{4(n+beta-j)}.                  (48.5)
```

All `w_j` are positive.  It remains to check the bilinear bracket on
`0<=u,v<=1`.  Its four corner values are

```text
1,
1-r_j,
1-r_j,
(1-r_j)^2+r_j(r_(j+1)-r_j).                           (48.6)
```

They are strictly positive.  Indeed, write `p=2n+epsilon` and
`beta=epsilon-1/2`.  The reserve gives

```text
alpha<=2n+epsilon-4m+3.
```

For `0<=j<=m-2`, the numerator of `1-r_j`, after multiplying by its positive
denominator, is

```text
2n+3epsilon-2-alpha-3j
 >=m+2epsilon+1+3(m-2-j)>0.                            (48.7)
```

Moreover,

```text
r_(j+1)-r_j
 ={N-(n+beta)}/{4(n+beta-j)(n+beta-j-1)}>0.            (48.8)
```

Thus every coefficient in the nonnegative variables `d_i` is positive, so
`Q_D(L)>0`.  Equations (48.1)--(48.3) prove

```text
M_2=(s+beta-1)(s+beta-G_1)+G_2>0                      (48.9)
```

for every rank and every admissible factor list.  This is an analytic theorem,
not a finite audit.  The arbitrary-length Durán route is now reduced to the
single remaining product inequality

```text
M_1=(s-1)(s+beta-1)-G_2>0.                            (48.10)
```

The deterministic replay
`verify_actual_duran_second_margin_coefficientwise.py` checks all symbolic
ratio, reserve, corner, and multiaffine identities.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_RANK_DURAN_SECOND_MARGIN_THEOREM` in
`actual_duran_second_margin_coefficientwise_exact_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`9F4C6F06B196C6464AE1B3E38376FEBFCCC63291E2A2C9495BEE6EAB9BA14A12`
and
`F5065B51F2C69EDEF8DCCF94D896270310B46C94EF07288BBB7DE1119E42929D`.

## 49. Fixed ambient product reduction for the first margin

The all-infinite endpoint product is stronger than the forest theorem needs.
There is a substantially looser fixed ceiling which is still sufficient.  Put

```text
x=n-m+1=s-1,
delta=p-alpha-(4m-3)>=0,
N=p+alpha.
```

Then, for `epsilon=p-2n` and `beta=epsilon-1/2`,

```text
N=4x+2epsilon-1-delta.                                  (49.1)
```

The exact reserve gap is

```text
16x(x+beta)-N(N-1)
 =4alpha delta+2alpha+3delta^2+8delta m-5delta+4m-4.    (49.2)
```

Every term in the last expression is nonnegative after grouping
`delta(8m-5)`, and `4m-4>0`.  Hence

```text
N(N-1)/16 < (s-1)(s+beta-1).                           (49.3)
```

Consequently the single fixed-ambient estimate

```text
G_2 <= N(N-1)/16                                       (49.4)
```

would prove the remaining first margin strictly.  Unlike the false shrinking
ceiling of Sections 30--42, the right side of (49.4) does not decrease when a
negative factor is appended.

There is an equivalent root-product form.  Write the normalized parameters as

```text
u,v,-d_1,...,-d_r,   r=m-2,
```

and select `r` negative roots `-b_i` of `Q_D`.  Since

```text
Q_D(0)=u v prod_i(d_i) (N)^fall_m/4^m
      =prod_i(b_i) G_2,                                (49.5)
```

(49.4) is exactly

```text
prod_i(b_i)
 >=u v prod_i(d_i) (N-2)^fall_r/4^r.                   (49.6)
```

This is weaker than the benign-product comparisons already found false: both
the factors `u v` and the two-step drop from `N` to `N-2` are essential.

An exact rational audit passes 36 cases, both parities and every degree
`2<=m<=10`, including repeated `d_i=1/1000`, factors at `1000`, and mixed
scales.  A separate floating-point adversarial scan passed 10,000 admissible
cases; its largest observed `G_2/{N(N-1)/16}` was about `0.823`.  The latter is
only search evidence.

The old small-parameter example illustrates the difference between the two
ceilings.  For the correctly normalized input

```text
N=25, m=7, u=v=1/20, d_i=1/1000,
```

exact isolation gives

```text
G_2=27.09594771925405647247...,
B(B+1)/16=23.75,
N(N-1)/16=37.5,
(s-1)(s+beta-1)=39.                                    (49.7)
```

Thus it refutes the shrinking ceiling but satisfies (49.4) with wide room.
The deterministic replay
`verify_actual_duran_fixed_ambient_product_reduction.py` reports
`PASS_EXACT_REDUCTION_AND_FIXED_CEILING_FINITE_AUDIT` in
`actual_duran_fixed_ambient_product_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.  Its
source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`F6C48560DFE933AF704738E5A9AFB21BFA40159A2C8E72FA991FE4F8FA1CE491`
and
`A27E3479240E8B04761D7F139FA9F159EC8ABDC069E77DE51F7697D74E90EAD9`.
Equation (49.4), equivalently (49.6), remains the analytic theorem to prove;
the audit is not that proof.

## 50. Correction of the small-factor transfer parameterization

Sections 42 and 46 compared two different normalized negative parameters.  A
factor `(t+c)` is proportional to

```text
1+t/c,
```

so its normalized negative lambda magnitude is `1/c`, not `c`.  Therefore the
`c=1/1000` transfer polynomial in Section 42 corresponds to

```text
Gamma(t)=(1-t/20)^2(1+t/1000)^5,
```

which is proportional to `(1-t/20)^2(t+1000)^5`.  Section 46 instead computed
the distinct polynomial for `(1-t/20)^2(t+1/1000)^5`, whose normalized
magnitude is `1000`.

The identification is exact.  With `B=N-m+1`, let `T` be the two-step
normalized-Pochhammer transfer built from the negative-block source.  Direct
coefficient comparison gives

```text
Q_D(z)=(-1)^m (N)^fall_m/{4^m B(B+1)} T(-z).            (50.1)
```

For the example `N=25,m=7,B=19`, the scalar in (50.1) is

```text
-398475/1024.                                          (50.2)
```

The identity is replayed symbolically at generic ranks zero through four.  For
the correctly matched small-lambda input, `Q_D` has five negative roots and one
nonreal conjugate pair; its residual product is the value in (49.7).  The
degree-12 Laguerre core nevertheless has 12 positive simple roots, exactly as
the positive margins predict.  The distinct small-factor input from Section 46
has seven negative `Q_D` roots, and its degree-12 core also has 12 positive
simple roots.

Accordingly, the Section 42 polynomial remains a valid counterexample to the
overstrong shrinking `B(B+1)/16` transfer invariant, but the claim in Section
46 that it was not an actual Durán polynomial must be read with this correction:
it is the actual polynomial for the reciprocal factor scale.  It is not a
counterexample to (49.4), to the true first margin, to window hyperbolicity, or
to the Erdős conjecture.

The deterministic replay `verify_transfer_parameterization_correction.py`
reports `PASS_EXACT_TRANSFER_PARAMETERIZATION_CORRECTION` in
`transfer_parameterization_correction_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`4E07D992A26CA2D741D6195E8D592FF7917E91345C8A8A6234296BD86079BA67`
and
`33C19FCAE9A404B44C8D2279D987C7BD9A150AD7ED87D39DB4D977271A6DC19A`.

## 51. Prefix log-majorization formulation of the fixed ceiling

The fixed product statement has a stronger prefix form which appears better
suited to induction.  Let `H` be the degree-`r` positive-rooted normalized
Pochhammer transform of the negative-factor block at ambient parameter
`C=B+2`.  Write its positive roots increasingly as

```text
xi_1<...<xi_r.
```

After adjoining the two outliers `u,v`, let `T` be the degree-`r+2`
transform, and write the `r` largest positive roots selected from `T` as

```text
alpha_1<...<alpha_r.
```

The proposed weak log-majorization inequalities are

```text
prod_(i=1)^k alpha_i/xi_i
 >=u v B(B+1)/{(B+k)(B+k+1)},          1<=k<=r.         (51.1)
```

The right side telescopes as

```text
u v prod_(j=0)^(k-1) (B+j)/(B+j+2).                    (51.2)
```

At `k=r`, use `N=B+r+1`.  Then (51.1) becomes

```text
prod_i alpha_i/xi_i >=u v B(B+1)/{N(N-1)},             (51.3)
```

which, by the exact constant-term identity, is precisely the fixed ceiling
`G_2<=N(N-1)/16`.  Thus an all-prefix theorem would prove more than is
needed, while exposing one two-step factor for every appended benign root.

An exact rational audit passes all prefixes in 21 cases, every rank
`1<=r<=7`, including repeated `1/1000`, alternating `1000` and `1/1000`, and
nonuniform rational factor lists.  A separate 30,000-case floating stress
scan found no violation.  Individual root-by-root analogues do fail; the
surviving pattern is genuinely about prefix products, not coordinatewise
root motion.  These are finite observations, not an all-rank proof.

The deterministic replay `audit_actual_duran_prefix_log_majorization.py`
reports `PASS_EXACT_PREFIX_LOG_MAJORISATION_FINITE_AUDIT` in
`actual_duran_prefix_log_majorization_exact_20260809.json`.  Its source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`13D258C80952485DBE41D66D19A6CB6468C3C5261E849B234F916A9C0F2538F7`
and
`AD35C097BF0095253F244D765B9F1E54B9A57DE6115082C644AD7596A909DB1F`.

## 52. Exact proof of the fixed ceiling in rank one

The first nontrivial case of (49.4) can be proved completely.  Here `r=1`,
so `m=3`.  The forest reserve gives `N>=9`.  Let `0<u,v<=1`, `d>0`, put
`a=u+v`, `b=uv`, and write the monic Durán cubic as

```text
Q(z)=z^3+c_2 z^2+c_1 z+c_0,
c_2=Nd/4-Na/4+3,
c_1=-N^2da/16+N^2b/16+Nda/16+Nd/4-Nb/16-Na/4+2,
c_0=N(N-1)(N-2)db/64.                                 (52.1)
```

Set

```text
R^2=N(N-1)/16,
K=bd(N-2)/4,
F(x)=-Q(-x).                                           (52.2)
```

If `b_*` is the largest positive root of `F`, then `-b_*` is the leftmost
negative root of `Q`.  Since `c_0=K R^2`, proving `b_*>K` gives immediately

```text
G_2=c_0/b_*<R^2=N(N-1)/16.                             (52.3)
```

The root comparison is controlled by one concave quadratic.  Define

```text
P(d)=P_0+dP_1+d^2P_2,
P_0=N^2b-N^2-4Na-Nb+N+32,
P_1=N^2ab-N^2a-2Nab+Na-12Nb+4N+24b,
P_2=N^2b^2-N^2b-4Nb^2+2Nb+4b^2
   =b(N-2){Nb-N-2b}<0.                                (52.4)
```

Direct expansion gives the decisive identity

```text
F(K)=db(N-2)P(d)/64.                                   (52.5)
```

Two exact thresholds are

```text
H=N(1-b)+2b,
d_A={N(a+2)-12}/H,
d_K=2/(3b).                                            (52.6)
```

If `A=c_2-K`, then `A=N/2` at `d=d_A`; and `K=(N-2)/6`
at `d=d_K`.  Exact tensor-Bernstein certificates on
`N=9+n`, `n>=0`, `0<=u,v<=1` prove

```text
P(d)<0                    for 0<=d<=d_A,
P(d_K)<0 and P'(d_K)<0.                                (52.7)
```

For the first assertion, after substituting `d=t d_A`, the three Bernstein
controls in `t` are

```text
P_0,
P_0+d_A P_1/2,
P(d_A).
```

Multiplication by the positive `H^2` gives respectively 16, 25, and 16
tensor controls in `u,v`; every coefficient in `n` is nonpositive and each
control is strictly negative.  For the second assertion, multiplication by
the positive denominators and removal of the factor `uv` gives two sets of
nine strictly negative tensor controls.  Since `P_2<0`, (52.7) also implies

```text
P(d)<0                    for d>=d_K.                   (52.8)
```

It remains only to split by the sign of `P`.

If `P<0`, then `F(K)<0` while `F(+infinity)>0`, so `F` has a root greater
than `K`.

If `P=0`, (52.7)--(52.8) force `d_A<d<d_K`.  Moreover the exact coefficient
identity

```text
c_1=R^2+AK+P/16                                       (52.9)
```

gives

```text
Q(z)=(z+K)(z^2+Az+R^2).
```

Here `A>N/2>2R` and `K<(N-2)/6<R`.  The quadratic therefore supplies a
negative root whose magnitude is greater than `R>K`.

Finally suppose `P>0`.  Again `d_A<d<d_K`, so `0<K<N/4`.  Put

```text
E(d)=64F(N/4)/N.                                       (52.10)
```

This is affine in `d`, with strictly negative slope

```text
E'(d)=-{N(N-4)+aN(N-1)+b(N-1)(N-2)}.                  (52.11)
```

A final nine-control exact Bernstein certificate proves `E(d_A)<0`.
Consequently `F(N/4)<0` for `d>d_A`.  The signs are now

```text
F(0)<0,
F(K)>0,
F(N/4)<0,
F(+infinity)>0.                                       (52.12)
```

The intermediate value theorem gives one positive root in each of
`(0,K)`, `(K,N/4)`, and `(N/4,+infinity)`.  In particular `b_*>K`.
This completes the analytic proof of (49.4) for `r=1`.

The deterministic replay `prove_actual_duran_fixed_ceiling_rank1.py`
checks all polynomial identities and 84 exact Bernstein controls.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_FIXED_CEILING_RANK_ONE_PROOF_CERTIFICATE` in
`actual_duran_fixed_ceiling_rank1_exact_20260809.json`.  The identity digest
inside the report is
`595D1FFEC753F8B03B24F8C90E9DBA4DDCB7B9EAE7CB95FE8B484A64CD53CADB`.
The source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`947BEF396965D01E00135689B3C80C09A654623A8C2D918FCA9484982CDB6632`
and
`E8133F2591711B4573B5567A8F28E2F15968E94321D2CDD9EF913B751B01F306`.

## 53. Fixed-circle crossing as a reachable-weight feasibility problem

The empirical fixed-disk statement is stronger and cleaner than the product
statement: after the `r` selected benign roots are removed, both exceptional
roots appear to remain in

```text
|z|^2 <= R^2=N(N-1)/16.                              (53.1)
```

For fixed structured `H`, let `Tuv` denote the two-append transform and let
`T00,T10,T11` be its three distinct `{0,1}` corners.  Exact expansion gives

```text
Tuv=(1-u)(1-v)T00+{u(1-v)+v(1-u)}T10+uv T11.          (53.2)
```

The first edge is controlled analytically:

```text
T10/T00=3/4+(z+B)H(z-1)/{4(z-1)H(z-2)}.               (53.3)
```

When `H` has positive roots of mesh at least one, the nonconstant ratio in
(53.3) has interlacing real zeros and poles.  It maps the upper half-plane to
the lower half-plane, hence

```text
Im{T00(z) conjugate(T10(z))}>0,   Im z>0.              (53.4)
```

The analogous full-triangle condition for `T00,T11` is false: already at
rank one with `B=10,d=1000`, its circle Wronskian has a root inside the fixed
circle.  This does not refute (53.1), because the weights reachable from
`u,v in [0,1]` form a proper curved subset of the triangle.

Retain that subset by writing

```text
s=u+v,  p=uv,
A=T10-T00,  D=T00-2T10+T11,
Tuv=T00+sA+pD.                                        (53.5)
```

At a nonreal point where `Tuv=0`, Cramer's rule determines the only possible
real `(s,p)`.  With

```text
cross(F,G)=Im{F conjugate(G)},
Delta=cross(A,D),
S=-cross(T00,D),
P=cross(T00,A),                                      (53.6)
```

one has `s=S/Delta`, `p=P/Delta` whenever `Delta!=0`.  Such a pair comes from
some `u,v in [0,1]` exactly when

```text
0<=p<=1,  0<=s<=1+p,  s^2>=4p.                       (53.7)
```

On the upper semicircle, each cross product in (53.6) is `y` times a rational
polynomial in `x`: divide by `y` and replace `y^2` by `R^2-x^2`.  Thus the
remaining all-rank fixed-disk lemma is the exact univariate sign implication

```text
-R<x<R  ==>  at least one condition in (53.7) fails.  (53.8)
```

This formulation survived 5,000 structured floating cases at 179 circle
points per case.  A corrected deterministic exact audit checks the algebraic
reduction and 4,095 rational circle projections across 21 structured cases,
ranks `1<=r<=7`.  It finds zero singular projections and zero feasible
projections.  Of the sampled points, 3,958 violate the discriminant condition,
3,232 have `p>1`, 860 have `s<0`, and 15 have `s>1+p`; violations can overlap.
No sampled point has `p<0`.  These finite checks are evidence, not the
continuum proof required by (53.8).

The deterministic replay `verify_fixed_ambient_corner_halfplane_reduction.py`
reports
`PASS_EXACT_REACHABLE_WEIGHT_REDUCTION_AND_FINITE_GRID_AUDIT` in
`fixed_ambient_corner_halfplane_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`EDD73E083ED501CE2D1EFB07CB1BF925B9EB6C513258B37755956BA59B0A3611`
and
`2C1CFDC1C4441FB0695B505477EF302286AF8F92A83929C48220B70A094DE177`.

Low-rank symbolic splitting is now preferable to a single all-rank expansion.
In rank zero the feasibility discriminant factors completely.  In rank one,
`Delta,S,P` have degrees `1,2,1` in the circle coordinate and the discriminant
has degree four.  The helper `derive_fixed_circle_feasibility_lowrank.py`
(SHA-256
`9AFC93F9F53CF469D74F5D9A1B7820AB5C5A39D4B8E21A74B823DA2B3AA42426`)
replays those formulas.  The next task is to turn the observed implication
"the box constraints force negative discriminant" into a structured
all-rank identity or variation-diminishing argument.

## 54. Topological reduction to the symmetric outlier boundary

The reachable pair of symmetric parameters

```text
(s,p)=(u+v,uv),  0<=u,v<=1,
```

is the compact region

```text
K={p>=0, p<=1, s>=0, s<=1+p, s^2>=4p}.               (54.1)
```

Its boundary has only three types:

```text
p=0:       u=0 or v=0,
s=1+p:     u=1 or v=1,
s^2=4p:    u=v.                                       (54.2)
```

At a fixed upper-semicircle point, Section 53 writes

```text
Tuv=T00+sA+pD,                                        (54.3)
```

an affine map from the `(s,p)` plane to the complex plane.  Combine this
with the negative-axis anchor from Section 36.  If a zero with `(s,p)` in
the interior of `K` appeared while the circle point moved continuously from
the negative real endpoint, its unique affine preimage would have to enter
`K` through the boundary.  At a rank drop the affine zero set is a line (or
the whole plane); any such set meeting the interior of compact `K` also meets
its boundary.  Consequently, excluding circle zeros on the three families
in (54.2) excludes them throughout `K`.

Two of the three boundary families are already controlled.

If `u=0`, the first append is `4zH(z-1)`.  The remaining factor is the
one-outlier normalized Pochhammer transform.  Its source has `r` positive
roots and one negative root, so the positive-zero theorem supplies `r`
positive zeros; the single remaining degree slot is real.  Thus this edge is
hyperbolic and has no nonreal circle zero.

If `u=1`, the large-parameter theorem of Section 41 applies for every
`0<=v<=1`.  Indeed, using `B>=3r+4`, its sign margin has the uniform floor

```text
(B+xi)(1/3+v/(4-v))-(r+1) >=1/3.                      (54.4)
```

It gives `alpha_i>xi_i`.  Hence any residual nonreal pair has product less
than `B(B+1)/16`, strictly below the fixed circle
`N(N-1)/16`, where `N=B+r+1`.  Append symmetry handles `v=0,1` as well.

Therefore the only genuinely new boundary theorem is

```text
u=v=t,  0<=t<=1.                                      (54.5)
```

This is a real reduction in parameter dimension: proving the symmetric
circle theorem for every structured benign source, together with the already
proved edge and anchor statements, proves the full two-outlier fixed-disk
lemma.

## 55. Repeated benign factors give a one-coupling Meixner matrix

The symmetric boundary (54.5) has an exact orthogonal-polynomial model when
the benign parameters are repeated.  Let

```text
M_n(z)=P_B[(4q-d)^n](z),  d>0,
```

and make `m_n` monic.  These are Meixner polynomials.  Direct coefficient
comparison gives the monic recurrence

```text
m_(n+1)=(z-b_n)m_n-a_n m_(n-1),                       (55.1)
b_n=n(1+d/2)+dB/4,
a_n=d(d+4)n(n+B-1)/16.                                (55.2)
```

Put `h=d+t` and `N=B+r+1`.  The elementary source identity

```text
(q+t/4)^2(4q-d)^r
 ={(4q-d)^(r+2)+2h(4q-d)^(r+1)+h^2(4q-d)^r}/16       (55.3)
```

turns the diagonal polynomial, after monic normalization, into

```text
f_r=m_(r+2)+hN/2 m_(r+1)+h^2 N(N-1)/16 m_r.           (55.4)
```

Using (55.1), this is the characteristic polynomial of a tridiagonal Jacobi
matrix whose first `r+1` rows are the positive Meixner matrix and whose only
modified coupling is the last one:

```text
f_r=(z-b_*)m_(r+1)-a_*m_r,                             (55.5)
b_*=r+1-dB/4-tN/2,
a_*=d(d+4)(r+1)(N-1)/16
     -(d+t)^2 N(N-1)/16.                              (55.6)
```

The target radius appears literally in the final term of `a_*`.  The standard
Meixner block has the birth-death factorization

```text
ell_n=n(d+4)/4,
u_n=d(n+B)/4,
b_n=ell_n+u_n,
a_n=ell_n u_(n-1).                                    (55.7)
```

At the modified final row the same factorization remains valid with

```text
u_*=Nt^2/(4d)>0,
ell_*=b_*-u_*,
a_*=ell_* u_r.                                        (55.8)
```

Thus exactly one death rate can be negative.  The matrix is self-adjoint in
a Pontryagin space of index one, explaining structurally why there are at
most two nonreal zeros.  The missing repeated-benign theorem is now a sharp
spectral assertion: the unique possible nonreal pair of this one-negative-
square extension lies in

```text
|z|^2<=N(N-1)/16.                                     (55.9)
```

For a nonreal eigenvector `x`, the index-one metric `G` gives the exact null
relations

```text
x^*Gx=0,  x^*GAx=0.                                   (55.10)
```

Numerical semidefinite reconnaissance indicates that suitable real
multipliers of the two null forms in (55.10) convert
`R^2 I-A^*A` into a positive semidefinite matrix.  This suggests a short
indefinite-energy proof of (55.9), but a closed all-parameter factorization
has not yet been obtained; the numerical LMI is not used as a theorem.

The deterministic verifier
`verify_fixed_circle_topological_meixner_reduction.py` checks the reachable-
boundary algebra, the uniform edge margin, the Meixner recurrence through
degree eight, and the quasi-orthogonal and matrix identities through rank six.
It reports
`PASS_EXACT_TOPOLOGICAL_BOUNDARY_AND_MEIXNER_MATRIX_REDUCTION` in
`fixed_circle_topological_meixner_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`28536DDE83485977E060305E1B47648001CF7EBA37A7E787ABFE17B0CBC30AF0`
and
`875B10E588B83FDAAC3826A6DB2CA95343C488AFEDEBDEE3014AD9D5BF2FED6B`.

## 56. All-rank repeated-Meixner theorem when the final diagonal is nonnegative

One large region of the spectral problem (55.9) admits a complete exact
energy proof.  Put

```text
R^2=N(N-1)/16,
q=-a_*=(N-1){N(d+t)^2-d(d+4)(r+1)}/16,
b=b_*=r+1-dB/4-tN/2.                                  (56.1)
```

If `q<=0`, the final coupling square is nonnegative and the tridiagonal
matrix is diagonally similar to a real symmetric Jacobi matrix.  It has no
nonreal pair.  Suppose `q>0`.  Write the real index-one matrix as

```text
A=[J       sqrt(q)e]
  [-sqrt(q)e^T   b ],                                  (56.2)
```

where `J` is the positive `(r+1)`-dimensional Meixner block.  For a nonreal
eigenvalue `z=X+iY`, its eigenvector `(v,w)` is neutral for
`G=diag(I,-1)`.  Normalize so that `||v||=|w|=1`, and put

```text
mu=v^*Jv,
Var=||Jv||^2-mu^2.                                     (56.3)
```

The real part of the eigenvalue equation and the norm of its first block give

```text
2X=mu+b,
q=||Jv-zv||^2.
```

Eliminating `X,Y` yields the exact energy identity

```text
|z|^2=q+b mu-Var.                                      (56.4)
```

The Meixner birth-death factorization writes `J=CC^T`, where `C` is lower
bidiagonal with diagonal entries `sqrt(u_n)` and subdiagonal entries
`sqrt(ell_n)`.  Hence

```text
mu<=lambda_max(J)<=||C||^2
 <=(sqrt(u_r)+sqrt(ell_r))^2,                          (56.5)
u_r=d(N-1)/4,
ell_r=r(d+4)/4.                                        (56.6)
```

When `b>=0`, (56.4)--(56.6) reduce the disk theorem to

```text
R^2-q-b(u_r+ell_r) >=2b sqrt(u_r ell_r).               (56.7)
```

Both the radical-free margin and its squared discriminant have exact
all-rank Bernstein certificates.  Parameterize `b>=0` by

```text
B=3r+4+c,
b=(r+1)x,
Bd/4=(r+1)(1-x)w,
t=2(r+1-Bd/4-b)/N,                                    (56.8)
```

with `c,x,w>=0` and `x,w<=1`.  In this chart the condition `q>=0` is exactly

```text
0<=x<=x_0(w),
x_0(w)=K(1-w)^2/H,
K=c+3r+4,
H=K(1+w^2)+(2c+10r+12)w.                              (56.9)
```

Set `x=y x_0(w)`, `0<=y<=1`.  After clearing positive denominators, the first
margin in (56.7) has bidegree `(2,6)` in `(y,w)` and all 21 tensor-Bernstein
controls are coefficientwise nonnegative in `(r,c)`.  Its squared
discriminant has bidegree `(4,12)` and all 65 controls are likewise
coefficientwise nonnegative.  Across the 86 controls there are 4,724 exact
parameter coefficients; the smallest is `1`.  This proves (56.7), and hence

```text
|z|^2<=N(N-1)/16                                      (56.10)
```

for every rank, every reserve `B>=3r+4`, every `d>0`, and every `0<=t<=1`
in the repeated-benign symmetric-outlier region `b_*>=0`.  This is an
analytic all-rank theorem, not a finite audit.

There is a second immediate solved region.  If `b_*<0` but `q<=R^2`, then
(56.4), `mu>0`, and `Var>=0` give `|z|^2<=q<=R^2`.  Thus the only repeated-
benign spectral region still open is

```text
b_*<0 and q>R^2.                                      (56.11)
```

The deterministic certificate
`prove_repeated_meixner_nonnegative_final_diagonal_region.py` reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_RANK_REPEATED_MEIXNER_BSTAR_NONNEGATIVE_THEOREM` in
`repeated_meixner_nonnegative_final_diagonal_exact_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`06B9FDA40470628AB1B68596BCD7D5ACDC2C266C86354D8D473C3FBF39DFFE9B`
and
`CD3BE7A60F59F633C541EDEEF5FC54682DF578B43794A3A87B51C6425D654314`.

## 57. The negative final diagonal closes by interlacing and a product identity

The exceptional region (56.11) has a shorter proof that does not require a
new indefinite-energy estimate.  In fact it covers every parameter point
with `b_*<0`, whether or not `q>R^2`.

Let the positive simple zeros of `m_r` be

```text
0<xi_1<...<xi_r.
```

The birth-death factorization (55.7) makes every finite Meixner Jacobi block
positive definite.  Therefore these zeros are positive and strictly
interlace the zeros of `m_(r+1)`.  Evaluate (55.4) at a zero `xi_i` of
`m_r`.  The recurrence (55.1) gives

```text
f_r(xi_i)
 =[xi_i-b_(r+1)+(d+t)N/2]m_(r+1)(xi_i)
 =(xi_i-b_*)m_(r+1)(xi_i),                           (57.1)
```

because the exact diagonal identity is

```text
b_(r+1)-(d+t)N/2=b_*.
```

Suppose `b_*<0`.  Every factor `xi_i-b_*` in (57.1) is positive, so the
signs of `f_r(xi_i)` are exactly the alternating signs of
`m_(r+1)(xi_i)`.  Moreover `m_(r+1)(xi_r)<0`, while the monic polynomial
`f_r` is positive at `+infinity`.  Consequently `f_r` has a positive zero
in each interval

```text
(xi_1,xi_2),...,(xi_(r-1),xi_r),(xi_r,+infinity).
```

Denote these `r` distinct roots by `alpha_1,...,alpha_r` in increasing
interval order.  Then

```text
alpha_i>xi_i,  1<=i<=r.                              (57.2)
```

The constant term of the monic Meixner polynomial is

```text
m_n(0)=(-d/4)^n (B)_n.
```

Using `N=B+r+1`, the two relevant ratios are

```text
m_(r+1)(0)/m_r(0)=-d(N-1)/4,
m_(r+2)(0)/m_r(0)=d^2 N(N-1)/16.
```

Substitution into (55.4) produces the exact cancellation

```text
f_r(0)=R^2[d^2-2d(d+t)+(d+t)^2]m_r(0)
      =R^2 t^2 m_r(0).                               (57.3)
```

If the two roots not listed in (57.2) form a nonreal conjugate pair
`z,conjugate(z)`, Viete's product identity and (57.3) give

```text
|z|^2 product(alpha_i)
 =R^2 t^2 product(xi_i),

|z|^2
 =R^2 t^2 product_i(xi_i/alpha_i)
 <R^2 t^2<=R^2.                                      (57.4)
```

If the last two roots are real, there is no nonreal pair to bound.  When
`r=0`, (57.3) directly says that a possible conjugate pair has product
`R^2t^2<=R^2`.  When `t=0`, (57.3) supplies a zero root; together with the
`r` positive roots above, only one degree slot remains, so a nonreal pair is
impossible.

Combining (57.4) with Section 56 proves the fixed-disk theorem for the entire
repeated-benign symmetric-outlier family, in every rank and for every
`B>=3r+4`, `d>0`, and `0<=t<=1`.  The unresolved step is no longer the
one-negative-square spectral estimate.  It is the lift from repeated benign
parameters to an arbitrary structured list `d_1,...,d_r`; after that lift,
Section 54 supplies the full two-outlier square.

The deterministic symbolic replay
`prove_repeated_meixner_all_parameter_disk.py` reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_RANK_REPEATED_MEIXNER_FULL_PARAMETER_THEOREM` in
`repeated_meixner_all_parameter_disk_exact_20260809.json`.  It checks (57.1)
and (57.3) symbolically in all rank and reconstructs the exact recurrence in
five rational cases.  Strict interlacing is supplied analytically by the
positive Jacobi factorization, not inferred from those finite cases.  The
source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`9632F28B895FACF98D6F94BBA603A654621013965102C8E88EA49A176F5AB3A8`
and
`F3537335D36A52096DBCCFF3DFA44C7A052750750D30B5E5C99D073136344C1A`.

## 58. Grace--Walsh reduces the arbitrary benign lift to a half-angle sector lemma

The repeated theorem can be lifted without a coordinatewise deformation of
the benign parameters if one proves a precise zero-sector statement for the
repeated diagonal.

Fix a point on the open upper target semicircle,

```text
z=R exp(i theta),  0<theta<pi,
R^2=(B+r+1)(B+r)/16,
```

and define the degree-`r` polynomial in the repeated benign parameter

```text
D_r(d)=P_B[(q+t/4)^2(4q-d)^r](z).                    (58.1)
```

The exact sector statement suggested by the computations is

```text
D_r(d)=0  ==>  Im(exp(-i theta/2)d)>0,                (58.2)
```

for `B>=3r+4` and `0<t<=1`.  Equivalently, all zeros of `D_r` lie strictly
above the line through the origin with angle `theta/2`.  The half angle is
not a fitted numerical parameter: writing `s=exp(i theta/2)` gives
`z=Rs^2`, and the separating coordinate is exactly `d/s`.

Here is why (58.2) is enough.  For arbitrary benign parameters put

```text
Phi_z(d_1,...,d_r)
 =P_B[(q+t/4)^2 product_j(4q-d_j)](z).                (58.3)
```

This is symmetric and multiaffine, and its diagonal is exactly (58.1).
The closed half-plane

```text
C_theta={d: Im(exp(-i theta/2)d)<=0}                  (58.4)
```

contains every positive real `d_j`, since its rotated imaginary part is
`-d_j sin(theta/2)<0`.  If (58.3) vanished at positive real
`d_1,...,d_r`, the Grace--Walsh--Szego coincidence theorem applied to the
convex circular domain (58.4) would produce a common `d in C_theta` with

```text
0=Phi_z(d_1,...,d_r)=Phi_z(d,...,d)=D_r(d),
```

contradicting (58.2).  Thus the half-angle sector lemma would prove the
arbitrary-benign symmetric boundary.  Section 54 would then prove the full
two-outlier fixed-circle exclusion.

This is materially stronger than merely knowing the repeated fixed-disk
theorem.  A naive right-half-plane Grace argument is false: at a fixed
circle point the roots of `D_r(d)` can have positive real part.  A naive
upper/lower-half-plane argument is also false on the left arc, where the
roots can occupy both ordinary imaginary half-planes.  The rotating
half-angle line separates all observed root sets and is exactly the domain
that continues to contain the positive real benign parameters.

A deterministic audit checked (58.2) at 20,200 parameter/circle-point sets,
ranks `1<=r<=10`, four reserves per rank, five positive outlier values, and
101 angles.  It classified 111,100 diagonal roots.  The smallest normalized
height

```text
Im(exp(-i theta/2)d)/max(1,|d|)
```

was `0.000212656189001`; there were no nonpositive heights.  Separate wider
exploration checked 73,990 sets through rank 14, again with no violation.
These are finite floating audits, not a proof.  Exploratory threshold scans
suggest that the sector theorem may already hold for `B>=3r+1`, leaving
three units of reserve slack in the actual forest range, but that sharper
threshold is likewise not asserted as a theorem.

The replay `probe_repeated_diagonal_half_angle_grace_lift.py` also verifies
the symmetry, multiaffinity, and diagonal identity in (58.3) symbolically
through rank six.  It reports
`PASS_FINITE_HALF_ANGLE_SECTOR_PROBE_AND_EXACT_POLARIZATION_REDUCTION` in
`repeated_diagonal_half_angle_grace_lift_probe_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`DA09FBB42A80BFC3938E7D9FB2569BD699DD5972688A78DD05E80FA2AEE27149`
and
`E07F50987E216C10709209AFFFCD77488F8C27275B2D2B7C97283EB6F29C9E22`.
The active analytic obligation is now (58.2), most naturally in
Hermite--Biehler, continued-fraction, or dissipative-matrix form.

## 59. Exact proof of the half-angle sector and the arbitrary benign lift

The sector lemma (58.2) follows from a quadratic pencil and one scalar
factorization.  The proof is all-rank.

### 59.1 The base repeated polynomial

For an integer `n>=2`, define

```text
M_n(d;z)=P_B[(4q-d)^n](z).                            (59.1)
```

The raw Meixner recurrence is

```text
M_(k+1)
 ={4(z-k)-d(B+2k) over B+k} M_k
  -{d(d+4)k over B+k}M_(k-1).                        (59.2)
```

Put

```text
w=d/(d+4),
Q_k(w)=(1-w)^k M_k(4w/(1-w);z)/4^k.                 (59.3)
```

Then

```text
(B+k)Q_(k+1)
 =[(z-k)-w(z+B+k)]Q_k-wkQ_(k-1).                    (59.4)
```

Consequently `Q_n(w)`, up to a nonzero scalar, is the determinant of the
`n` by `n` tridiagonal pencil with

```text
diagonal_k = k-z+w(z+B+k),
upper_k    = B+k,
lower_k    = w(k+1).                                  (59.5)
```

Choose `y` with `y^2=w` and symmetrize the off-diagonal entries to

```text
y sqrt((k+1)(B+k)).                                   (59.6)
```

If `M_n(d;z)=0`, the symmetric complex matrix in (59.5)--(59.6) has a
nonzero null vector `v`.  Normalize `sum |v_k|^2=1` and put

```text
D=sum k|v_k|^2,
C=sum sqrt((k+1)(B+k)) Re(conjugate(v_k)v_(k+1)).     (59.7)
```

Then

```text
0<=D<=n-1,
C^2<=D(B+D),                                          (59.8)
```

where the second inequality is Cauchy--Schwarz.  Multiplying the null
equation by `v^*` gives the exact scalar relation

```text
D-z+w(z+B+D)+2yC=0.                                  (59.9)
```

### 59.2 No root can cross the half-angle line

Set

```text
z=R s^2,
s=cos(psi)+i sin(psi),  0<psi<pi/2,
R^2=(B+n-1)(B+n-2)/16.                               (59.10)
```

Suppose, for contradiction, that a root lies on the rotating boundary

```text
d=sx,  x real.                                        (59.11)
```

Eliminate `w,y` from (59.9) using `w=d/(d+4)` and square once.  With
`P=C^2`, this gives the necessary equation

```text
[(B+2D)d+4(D-z)]^2=4P d(d+4).                        (59.12)
```

Divide by `s^2` and compare imaginary parts.  Writing `L=R+D` gives

```text
x[(B+2D)L-2P]=4(R^2-D^2)cos(psi).                    (59.13)
```

In the forest range `B>=3n-2`, one has `R>n-1>=D`; explicitly, after
`B=3n-2+beta`,

```text
R^2-(n-1)^2
 ={beta^2+(8n-7)beta+4(n-1) over 16}>0.              (59.14)
```

Moreover, (59.8) gives

```text
(B+2D)L-2P
 >=B(R-D)+2DR>0.                                      (59.15)
```

Thus (59.13) forces `x>0`.  Comparing real parts in (59.12), substituting
(59.13), and using `sin(psi)>0` shows that a boundary root would require

```text
P(3R+D)>(R+D)^2(B+R+D).                              (59.16)
```

But the upper bound `P<=D(B+D)` contradicts (59.16), because

```text
(R+D)^2(B+R+D)-D(B+D)(3R+D)
 =R[B(R-D)+3DR+R^2]>0.                               (59.17)
```

Therefore no root crosses the half-angle line for `0<psi<pi/2`.

### 59.3 Initial orientation

At `psi=0`, `z=R` and the base polynomial has `n` positive simple `d`-roots.
Indeed,

```text
M_n(d;R)=(-d)^n 2F1(-n,-R;B;-4/d),                  (59.18)
```

up to a positive scalar.  Pfaff's transformation maps its hypergeometric
zeros to those of the Jacobi polynomial

```text
P_n^(B-1,R-n)(1-2u).                                  (59.19)
```

The two Jacobi parameters exceed `-1` because `B>0` and `R>n-1`; hence all
`u`-zeros lie simply in `(0,1)`, which maps back to `d>0`.

It remains to determine which side of the rotating line these roots enter.
At a positive root let `w=d/(d+4) in (0,1)` and take the real null vector in
(59.7).  Differentiating the symmetric pencil at `psi=0` gives

```text
w'=2iR(1-w)/H,
H=B+D+R+C/sqrt(w)
  ={w(B+D+R)+R-D over 2w}>0.                         (59.20)
```

The rotated height of `d` has positive derivative precisely when

```text
w(B+D+R)<3R+D.                                       (59.21)
```

Use (59.9), `C^2<=D(B+D)`, and the same positive margin (59.17) to prove
(59.21): if `w` were at least `(3R+D)/(B+D+R)`, the minimum possible left
side of (59.9) would already exceed `R-D`.  Hence every root initially
enters

```text
Im(exp(-i psi)d)>0.                                   (59.22)
```

No-crossing then proves (59.22) throughout the open upper semicircle.

### 59.4 Two polar derivatives install the outliers

**Correction (2026-08-13).**  The identities in this subsection are exact,
but the preservation inference at the end is invalid.  Laguerre's circular-
region theorem implies preservation when the polar point lies **outside**
the circular region containing the polynomial zeros.  Here the point `-t`
lies inside the desired rotating half-plane.  The exact counterexample in
`ONE_POLAR_STRIP_LAGUERRE_DOMAIN_AUDIT_AND_SECOND_POLAR_COUNTEREXAMPLE_2026-08-13.md`
shows that the second polar derivative can cross the boundary even in the
special Meixner family.  The material below is retained to record the exact
operator identity, not as a proof of sector preservation.

Let `n=r+2`.  Differentiating (59.1) with respect to `d` gives the exact
identity

```text
16 P_B[(q+t/4)^2(4q-d)^r]
 =M_n-2(d+t)M_n'/n+(d+t)^2 M_n''/[n(n-1)].           (59.23)
```

The right side is the twice-normalized polar derivative of `M_n` with polar
point `-t`.  For `0<t<=1`, that point also lies strictly in the half-plane
(59.22), since

```text
Im(exp(-i psi)(-t))=t sin(psi)>0.
```

The formerly asserted conclusion in the preceding version was that
Laguerre preserves a half-plane containing both the polynomial zeros and the
polar point.  That reverses the theorem's domain condition and is false.
Consequently (58.2), the arbitrary-list Grace--Walsh lift, and the full
two-outlier fixed-circle exclusion do not follow from (59.23).  They require
a separate structured two-polar theorem.

The deterministic replay `prove_half_angle_sector_grace_lift.py` verifies
the recurrence through degree seven, the tridiagonal determinant through
degree six, the double polar identity through rank six, and all symbolic
all-rank scalar factorizations above.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_RANK_HALF_ANGLE_SECTOR_AND_ARBITRARY_BENIGN_LIFT` in
`half_angle_sector_grace_lift_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`C784BA85608F87B130BA6AD621AF86A4F465708BF82197FE508BE183CEA76BFA`
and
`8F7301CF2ABCF30E2D64B05C661F693503778AEC61A1D64257D41FE675305CB2`.

## 60. The positive real endpoint closes by reflected Pochhammer variation

The open-circle theorem in Section 59 must still be joined to the real
endpoints.  The positive endpoint admits a general Pochhammer theorem which
does not use the special factor scales.

Let

```text
p(z)=sum_(k=0)^m a_k z^k,
T(x)=Poch_1[p](x)=sum_(k=0)^m a_k (x)_k^fall,
m=r+2,
```

where `p` has exactly `r` positive and two negative roots.  Define a reflected
source `ptilde(z)=sum_l b_l z^l` by

```text
Poch_1[ptilde](x)=(-1)^m T(m-1-x).                    (60.1)
```

Finite-difference inversion gives the exact all-degree coefficient identity

```text
b_l=(-1)^(m+l) sum_(k=l)^m a_k K_(l,k),
K_(l,k)=binom(k,l)(m-1-l)_(k-l)^fall.                 (60.2)
```

For `0<=l<=k<=m-1`,

```text
K_(l,k)={k! over l!} binom(m-1-l,k-l),                (60.3)
```

while `K_(m,m)=1` and the other entries in the last row and column vanish.
The leading block of `K` is therefore a positive row/column scaling of the
Pascal matrix with both row and column orders reversed.  It is totally
nonnegative, as is `K`.

Because `p` is real-rooted with no zero root, Descartes' rule is exact: the
coefficient sequence `(a_0,...,a_m)` has exactly `r` sign changes.  Possible
zero coefficients are handled by perturbing the roots, using the usual
identity between the variations of `p(z)` and `p(-z)`, and passing to the
limit; Descartes supplies the matching lower bound.  Variation diminution for
the totally nonnegative matrix in (60.2) now gives

```text
variations(((-1)^l b_l)_l) <= r.                     (60.4)
```

Thus `ptilde` has at most `r` negative roots.  The classical negative-zero
Pochhammer theorem says that `Poch_1[ptilde]` has no more negative roots than
`ptilde`.  By (60.1), negative roots of `Poch_1[ptilde]` correspond exactly
to roots of `T` in `(m-1,+infinity)`.  Consequently

```text
# {real roots of T greater than m-1} <= r.             (60.5)
```

The polynomial `p` in the Duran reduction has precisely this sign pattern:
the multiplier `1/(B)_j` preserves real-rootedness and leaves `r` positive
and two negative roots.  After the `r` largest positive roots of `T` are
selected, (60.5) therefore places every remaining positive real root at or
below `m-1=r+1`.

In the forest range write `B=3r+4+c`, `c>=0`, and
`R^2=(B+r+1)(B+r)/16`.  The exact strict reserve is

```text
R^2-(r+1)^2
 ={8rc+4r+c^2+9c+4 over 16}>0.                       (60.6)
```

Hence an exceptional real branch cannot leave the fixed disk through the
positive endpoint.  Section 36 supplies the negative endpoint barrier; at the
final stage its threshold is `(B-3)/4`, and

```text
R^2-(B-3)^2/16
 ={7r^2+2rc+30r+7c+19 over 16}>0.                    (60.7)
```

Together with Section 59, both real endpoints and the open circular arc are
now controlled.  The remaining audit is to state the unique `N_1`
exceptional-pair continuation from Section 35 carefully enough that branch
exchanges on the real axis cannot change which two roots define `G_2`.

The deterministic verifier
`prove_positive_endpoint_pochhammer_variation.py` replays (60.1)--(60.3)
symbolically through degree eight, checks all 17,568 minors of the Pascal
blocks through degree eight, and verifies the all-rank margins (60.6)--(60.7).
It reports `PASS_EXACT_ALL_RANK_POSITIVE_ENDPOINT_THEOREM` in
`positive_endpoint_pochhammer_variation_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`02780A3F1C07D9445437308DA43E2EBFE1AB3BFD580DE7F84E6BBD9033784014`
and
`5AAC4114944D5536B5EC68D354C5AD532760729588B9BE80245C9C8A70293815`.

## 61. Exterior-radius reduction (sector conclusion reopened)

**Correction (2026-08-13).**  The radius algebra below correctly shows that
the base `M_n` half-angle theorem of Sections 59.1--59.3 only needs
`rho>n-1`.  It does not repair the invalid polar-preservation step in
Section 59.4.  The former conclusions (61.3)--(61.5) and the claim that the
arbitrary-length coefficient theorem was closed are withdrawn pending a
new structured two-polar theorem.

The radius in Section 59 can be treated as a free variable.  Write it as
`rho`.  Inspection of the proof shows that the only radius hypothesis is

```text
rho>n-1.
```

The quadratic-pencil boundary calculation uses `rho>D`, where
`0<=D<=n-1`; its two exact positive quantities are

```text
(B+2D)(rho+D)-2D(B+D)
 =B(rho-D)+2Drho>0,                                   (61.1)

(rho+D)^2(B+D+rho)-D(B+D)(3rho+D)
 =rho{B(rho-D)+3Drho+rho^2}>0.                       (61.2)
```

The initial Jacobi identification requires the parameter `rho-n>-1`, which
is the same strict condition.  The derivative-orientation proof uses exactly
the margin (61.2).  No identity tying `rho` to `B` or `n` occurs.  Therefore
the base half-angle theorem holds for every `rho>n-1`.  The formerly claimed
arbitrary-outlier extension was

```text
P_B[(q+t/4)^2 product_j(4q-d_j)](z) !=0
when Im(z)>0 and |z|=rho,                              (61.3)
```

for arbitrary positive benign parameters.  This is now the desired target,
not a proved statement.  Grace--Walsh would supply the arbitrary-list lift
once that diagonal target is independently proved.

The reachable-boundary argument of Section 54 is also uniform in `rho` once
`rho` is at least the forest radius.  The zero edge is hyperbolic.  On the
one edge, the large-parameter theorem puts a possible nonreal residual pair
strictly inside `B(B+1)/16`, which is smaller than the forest radius and
hence smaller than `rho`.  At the negative endpoint, Section 36 applies
because `rho>(B-3)/4`.  Thus, conditional on a valid proof of (61.3), the
same topology would extend it to every `0<=u,v<=1`:

```text
the full two-outlier transform has no nonreal zero of modulus
rho for any rho>=R_0,                                  (61.4)

R_0^2=(B+r+1)(B+r)/16.
```

Conditionally this would remove the need to track labels through real branch exchanges.  Any
nonreal residual pair is automatically inside `|z|<R_0` by taking
`rho=|z|` in (61.4).

For a real residual pair, select the `r` largest positive roots.  Section 60
puts every unselected positive root at or below `r+1<R_0`.  Section 36 puts
every unselected negative root above `-(B-3)/4>-R_0`.  Hence both real roots
also lie in `(-R_0,R_0)`.  In all cases the residual quadratic product obeys

```text
G_2<=R_0^2=N(N-1)/16.                                 (61.5)
```

Equation (49.3) would then make the first Duran/Jacobi margin strict, while
Section 48 already proves the second margin strict.  Because the sector
input is reopened, this assembly is not presently an all-order proof.

The assembly verifier `prove_exterior_sector_fixed_ceiling.py` reconstructs

checks the six dependency reports and their hashes.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_RANK_EXTERIOR_SECTOR_AND_DURAN_FIXED_CEILING` in
`exterior_sector_fixed_ceiling_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`3FA719BDE86427A20F4AEDD9F7A3A3A04957A5A2FB14D38924919221CC229AF8`
and
`8EAE81F6080B3093A2C0C70146DE61BBB8DDEEA5B666C5B9CBC41779CC4A974D`.

The fixed ceiling is again one of the remaining obligations.  Independently,
the earlier forest-to-window reduction, every external classical theorem,
