## 62. Clean-room selector audit and a nested-chain reduction

The audit found a further hypothesis independent of the reopened fixed-
ceiling sector step.  In the forest reduction the source gamma polynomial is

```text
Gamma_(N,s)(t)=G_(N,s)(t)-2tG_(N-1,s)(t)+t^2G_(N-2,s)(t),   (62.1)
```

where `G_(M,s)` is the gamma polynomial of the path slice

```text
A_(M,s)(z)=sum_i binom(2M-i-1,i)
                    binom(2M-s+i-1,s-i)z^i.
```

Section 124 of `NYQUIST_RESERVE_INDUCTION_LEMMA_2026-08-02.md` proves every
`G_(M,s)` negative-rooted, but the assertion that
(62.1) has two roots in `[1,infinity)` and all other roots negative was only
an exact finite Sturm observation.  It must therefore remain an explicit
proof obligation.

There is now an all-order hypergeometric/path identity adapted to that
obligation.  Put

```text
r(u)=(1+sqrt(1-u))/2,
F_(M,s)(u)=(1-u)^(s-2M) r(u)^(2M)=sum_h S_(M,s,h)u^h.
```

A specialization of Whipple's quadratic `4F3` transformation gives

```text
[t^h]G_(M,s)
 =4^h binom(2M-s-1,s-2h) S_(M,s,h).                (62.2)
```

In the Catalan coordinate `u=4x/(1+x)^2`, the inner series has the elementary
form and fixed size ratio

```text
F_(M,s)=(1+x)^(2M-2s)(1-x)^(2s-4M),
F_(M-1,s)/F_(M,s)=(1-x)^4/(1+x)^2.                 (62.3)
```

This replaces an opaque three-size hypergeometric coupling by one fixed
rational size-lowering factor.

The root count also has a sharp conditional reduction.  Let the negative
zeros of the three unsigned components, in increasing order, be `a_i,b_i,c_i`.
It is enough to prove the strict nested chain

```text
a_i<b_i<c_i<a_(i+1).                                (62.4)
```

At a zero `c_i` of `G_(N-2,s)`, the two surviving terms in (62.1) have the
same alternating sign.  The leading sign of (62.1) at negative infinity is
the opposite of its sign at `c_1`.  Therefore (62.4) forces one selector
zero in each of

```text
(-infinity,c_1),(c_1,c_2),...,(c_(k-1),c_k),
k=floor(s/2).                                       (62.5)
```

Thus all `k` required negative roots follow without a separate global
real-rootedness theorem; only two degrees remain.  Moreover, the identity

```text
Gamma(t)=Delta_M^2G(t)
 +2(1-t)Delta_M G_(N-1,s)(t)
 +(1-t)^2G_(N-2,s)(t)                               (62.6)
```

shows that coefficientwise monotonicity and convexity in `M` exclude every
root in `[0,1]`.

The replay `analyze_selector_nested_chain_reduction.py` verifies (62.2)
independently, certifies (62.4), both coefficient differences, and the full
selector root count in 396 exact cases: every `2<=s<=100` at
`N=2s+5+e`, `e in {0,1,17,73}`, through unsigned degree 50.  Its report is
`selector_nested_chain_reduction_exact_20260809.json`; source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`E4589F541EC214767C053C7210F0E2E1ACA8FFAD9CAFE25528DACF25B1499B55`
and
`D69B96DC7422AE0B9A7A2E1047AC273BAFD6017F29E2FEA5449B7C280D195512`.
The finite chain and convexity checks are evidence.  Equations (62.2)--(62.3)
and the implication (62.4)--(62.6) are the all-order advances; proving
(62.4), or an equivalent special positive-polarization theorem, is the
current mathematical frontier.

## 63. Coefficient convexity closes the selector unit interval

The coefficient differences invoked after (62.6) can be proved in all
orders.  Write the factorization (62.2) as

```text
g_(M,s,h)=C_(M,s,h)S_(M,s,h),
C_(M,s,h)=4^h binom(2M-s-1,s-2h).                  (63.1)
```

For `M>=s`, the Catalan-coordinate series `F_(M,s)` in (62.3) has
nonnegative coefficients.  Its forward size ratio is

```text
Q(x)=F_(M+1,s)/F_(M,s)=(1+x)^2/(1-x)^4.            (63.2)
```

Both `Q` and `Q-1` have nonnegative coefficients, as does the inverse
Catalan series `x=x(u)`.  Hence, coefficientwise in `u`,

```text
Delta F_M=F_(M-1)(Q-1)>=0,
Delta^2 F_M=F_(M-2)(Q-1)^2>=0.                     (63.3)
```

Thus every `S_(M,s,h)` is nonnegative, nondecreasing, and convex in `M`.
Vandermonde's identity gives the same three properties for the binomial
factor `C_(M,s,h)`, whose upper index advances by two.  The product of two
nonnegative nondecreasing convex sequences is again convex: expanding the
second difference of the product writes it as a sum of the two second
differences and products of nonnegative first differences.  Consequently
every coefficient of `G_(M,s)` is nonnegative, nondecreasing, and convex in
`M`.

Substitution in (62.6) proves

```text
Gamma_(N,s)(t)>0 for 0<=t<1;                       (63.4)
Gamma_(N,s)(1)>0 for s>=2.                         (63.5)
```

The harmless boundary layers `s=0,1` instead have the exact value
`Gamma_(N,s)(1)=0`.  Thus no nontrivial selector root lies in `[0,1]`, and
any two positive roots supplied by the remaining root theorem must be
strictly larger than one.

The independent exact replay `prove_selector_unit_interval_exclusion.py`
checks the Whipple coefficients, both coefficient differences, (62.6), and
21 rational points of `[0,1]` in all 2,121 cases `0<=s<=100` and
`N=2s+5+e`, `0<=e<=20`.  It reports
`PASS_ALL_ORDER_SELECTOR_UNIT_INTERVAL_EXCLUSION_WITH_BOUNDARY_DEGENERACIES`
in `selector_unit_interval_exclusion_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`C527EB8FB85028F454B7B96682A4910B2682C4C7D88E7E7E3F514D52E29CF59D`
and
`7F207BB9DD03B8BE840483DDCF87DB8ADC597F72A50F4C8786BFDAB22F2291C2`.
The replay is transcription evidence; (63.1)--(63.5) are the all-order
argument.

## 64. A shifted-discriminant continuation target

There is a second exact route for the final two selector degrees.  Set

```text
N=2s+5+q,  q>=0,                                    (64.1)
```

so that `q` is precisely the excess above the sharp forest-cone boundary.
For each fixed symbolic layer `2<=s<=10`, direct elimination gives

```text
Disc_t Gamma_(2s+5+q,s)(t)
 = positive rational * D_s(q),                       (64.2)
```

where **every coefficient** of `D_s(q)` is a strictly positive integer.
The nine exact discriminants have degrees from 6 through 98 and contain 399
strictly positive coefficients in total.  Thus no selector collision can
occur anywhere in the full half-line `q>=0` for any of these symbolic
layers.

This observation is not yet an all-order theorem.  It does, however, expose
a clean alternative proof target: establish coefficient positivity of
`D_s(q)` for arbitrary `s`, or derive it from a sign-regular resultant or
interlacing pencil.  Combined with the negative-root count from (62.4), a
positive discriminant rules out the only possible conjugate pair.  The unit
interval theorem in Section 63 and Descartes' rule then force the two
remaining roots to lie strictly above one.

The independent constructor
`probe_selector_shifted_discriminant_positivity.py` builds each path slice,
performs the gamma conversion and discriminant elimination symbolically,
and checks every shifted coefficient.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_FINITE_SELECTOR_SHIFTED_DISCRIMINANT_POSITIVITY` in
`selector_shifted_discriminant_positivity_exact_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`F99C44928677BF9DCA028E80AE0D6309ACB3B5CBFE70B0DA89DC633CB1DD2CF3`
and
`0B91A7B6A069155E4D2F801A78182361626452D21C5AF770FEF952801DBEF098`.

## 65. Möbius conversion to one ordinary PF-infinity target

The two disjoint target intervals can be unified.  Put `k=floor(s/2)`,
`d=k+2`, and use

```text
x=t/(1-t),       t=x/(1+x).                           (65.1)
```

This maps `t<0` bijectively to `-1<x<0` and maps `t>1` bijectively to
`x<-1`.  For every degree-at-most-`k` polynomial define

```text
hat(P)(x)=(1+x)^k P(x/(1+x)),                         (65.2)
```

and define the transformed selector

```text
H_(N,s)(x)=(1+x)^d Gamma_(N,s)(x/(1+x)).              (65.3)
```

The second-difference decomposition (62.6) becomes the positive identity

```text
H_(N,s)
 =(1+x)^2 hat(Delta^2 G_N)
  +2(1+x)hat(Delta G_(N-1))
  +hat(G_(N-2)).                                      (65.4)
```

Section 63 proves that every coefficient inside the three hats is
nonnegative.  The ordinary binomial transform (65.2) preserves coefficient
nonnegativity, and the final term has positive constant coefficient.
Consequently every coefficient of `H_(N,s)` is strictly positive.  The
selector theorem is therefore equivalent to the single standard statement

```text
H_(N,s) is real-rooted.                               (65.5)
```

Indeed, positive coefficients then force all roots of `H` to be negative;
the roots in `(-1,0)` pull back to negative selector roots and the roots
below `-1` pull back to selector roots strictly above one.  Conversely the
target selector pattern plainly gives (65.5).  Thus the mixed-sign
two-outlier problem is now an ordinary PF-infinity problem.

There is a sharper finite guide to (65.5).  In every forest-cone case
checked, each of

```text
hat(Delta^2 G_N),  hat(Delta G_(N-1)),  hat(G_(N-2)) (65.6)
```

is negative-rooted in `[-1,0)`, and their roots obey one strict three-level
nested chain.  The only endpoint equality is the degree-padding root `-1`
of the second difference at `s=2`.  Thus an all-order compatibility theorem
for (65.6) would make the positive sum (65.4) real-rooted and close the
selector obligation directly.

The replay `analyze_selector_pf_mobius_reduction.py` verifies (65.4), strict
coefficient positivity, the three-level component chain, and the final root
split in 396 exact cases: every `2<=s<=100` at
`N=2s+5+e`, `e in {0,1,17,73}`.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_SELECTOR_PF_MOBIUS_REDUCTION_REPLAY` in
`selector_pf_mobius_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`E228B806A63F785A0FB874F11B3DF785E89255054170B0A3A43D5730C8A9CB19`
and
`2CC9D72394C5F3925CBC8AE38DD3442C0853D93B6FED1C0A86B747A6463251AC`.
Equations (65.1)--(65.5) are all-order; the component real-rootedness and
compatibility in (65.6) remain the exact proof frontier.

## 66. Path determinant and one-rank boundary nesting

The unsigned family has an exact matrix model.  Define

```text
p_(M,i)=binom(2M-i-1,i),
P_M(v)=sum_i p_(M,i)v^i,
C_n=tridiag(1,2,1),  n=M-1.                         (66.1)
```

The determinant `det(I+vC_n)` and `P_M(v)` have the same initial values and
continuant recurrence, hence

```text
P_M(v)=det(I+vC_n).                                  (66.2)
```

Let `a+b=1` and `ab=t`.  Homogenizing the palindromic path slice gives

```text
G_(M,s)(t)
 =[U^s]P_M(aU)P_M(bU)
 =[U^s]det(I+UC_n+tU^2C_n^2).                       (66.3)
```

Indeed, the first coefficient extraction is

```text
sum_i p_(M,i)p_(M,s-i)a^i b^(s-i),                  (66.4)
```

which becomes `(1+z)^(-s)A_(M,s)(z)` after `z=a/b`; this is precisely the
gamma substitution `t=z/(1+z)^2`.  The second equality follows by multiplying
the two commuting determinants.

If `lambda_1,...,lambda_n` are the positive eigenvalues of `C_n`, (66.3)
also reads

```text
G_(M,s)(t)
 =[U^s] product_j(1+lambda_j U+t lambda_j^2 U^2),
[t^h]G_(M,s)
 =m_(2^h,1^(s-2h))(lambda_1,...,lambda_n).           (66.5)
```

The last identity is direct: select the quadratic term at `h` indices and
the linear term at `s-2h` disjoint indices.  Thus every coefficient is a
specific monomial symmetric function evaluated on the strictly positive
path spectrum.

Most importantly, the size change is a boundary principal-submatrix change.
If `e` is the final coordinate vector of length `n-1`, then

```text
(C_n^2)[1:n-1,1:n-1]=C_(n-1)^2+ee^T.                (66.6)
```

So the only obstruction to ordinary Cauchy interlacing in the quadratic
matrix pencil of (66.3) is one explicit positive rank-one boundary term.
This sharpens the remaining task: prove that this nested rank-one pencil
makes the three transformed finite differences in (65.6) compatible, or
identify (65.4) directly as a mixed characteristic polynomial.  Either
statement closes (65.5).

The independent exact replay `prove_path_determinant_monomial_identity.py`
checks (66.2)--(66.3), the gamma conversion, and (66.6) for all eight sizes
`2<=M<=9`, covering 80 coefficient layers.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_PATH_DETERMINANT_MONOMIAL_IDENTITY_REPLAY` in
`path_determinant_monomial_identity_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`5E83B1EDA808206572B20D17A1BD701906D072A9635C64C8E8BB16157154EE18`
and
`0AAD0BFB6F6EBC29E1E41FC14E84AEE028F0CB9725599F8450682FD694F25554`.
Equations (66.1)--(66.6) are all-order; the replay is transcription evidence.

## 67. A Turan fixed point forces the two positive selector roots

The final two degrees do not require global real-rootedness of `H`.  There is
a shorter sign argument.  Define the unsigned size Turan polynomial

```text
T_(M,s)(t)
 =G_(M-1,s)(t)^2-G_(M,s)(t)G_(M-2,s)(t).             (67.1)
```

Assume temporarily the strict inequality

```text
T_(M,s)(t)>0 for t>0.                                (67.2)
```

All coefficients of every unsigned `G` are positive.  Hence the continuous
ratio

```text
R(t)=G_(N-1,s)(t)/G_(N-2,s)(t)                       (67.3)
```

satisfies `R(1)>1`.  It is bounded as `t` tends to infinity, since numerator
and denominator have the same degree and positive leading coefficients.
Therefore `R(t)-t` has a zero `t_*>1`.  At that fixed point,

```text
Gamma_(N,s)(t_*)
 =G_N(t_*)-G_(N-1)(t_*)^2/G_(N-2)(t_*)
 =-T_(N,s)(t_*)/G_(N-2)(t_*)<0.                      (67.4)
```

Section 63 gives `Gamma(1)>0`, while the leading coefficient of `Gamma` is
positive.  Thus (67.4) forces one zero in `(1,t_*)` and a second in
`(t_*,infinity)`.  Once the nested chain (62.4) supplies the `floor(s/2)`
negative zeros, these two sign changes account for the full degree and close
the selector theorem.  In particular, the former shifted-discriminant and
full-PF targets are stronger than necessary.

The Turan statement has unusually rigid finite evidence.  In every case
checked, (67.1) is not merely positive on the positive axis: **every
coefficient is strictly positive**.  Moreover, writing
`g_(M,s,h)=[t^h]G_(M,s)`, the adjacent size ratios satisfy

```text
g_(M-1,s,h+1)/g_(M-2,s,h+1)
 <=g_(M,s,h)/g_(M-1,s,h)
 <=g_(M-1,s,h)/g_(M-2,s,h).                          (67.5)
```

This is the characteristic ratio-dominant/total-positive interlacing pattern
behind convolution log-concavity.  It gives a concrete all-order proof
target for (67.2), likely through the Whipple factorization (63.1)--(63.3) or
the rank-one path nesting (66.6).

The independent exact replay
`analyze_selector_turan_fixedpoint_reduction.py` verifies strict positivity
of all 79,793 Turan coefficients and (67.5) in 1,043 cases: every
`2<=s<=150` and forest excess in `{0,1,5,17,73,200,1000}`.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_SELECTOR_TURAN_FIXEDPOINT_REDUCTION_REPLAY` in
`selector_turan_fixedpoint_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`EC70190023496DE18F6C3A57B7E73223C6E73AD41F005C6C85E44C324923277B`
and
`D59E85D31BC2A92220A64B5D73E2523E69C6932B9530D39245A721CF767CF87F`.
The implication (67.2)--(67.4) is all-order; the replay is finite evidence
for (67.2) and (67.5).

## 68. Binomial-moment normalization of the size ratios

The Whipple coefficient has a more rigid normalization than (63.1) first
suggests.  Put

```text
r=M-s,
P_h(r,s)=2^(h+floor(h/2)) h! S_(M,s,h).             (68.1)
```

Lagrange inversion in the Catalan coordinate gives, for `h>=1`,

```text
4^h S_h
 =1/h [x^(h-1)] d/dx((1+x)^(2r)(1-x)^(-4r-2s))
                         (1+x)^(2h).                 (68.2)
```

Consequently `P_h` is a polynomial in `r,s` with integer coefficients.  Its
first layers are

```text
P_0=1,
P_1=3r+s,
P_2=18r^2+12rs+13r+2s^2+5s.                        (68.3)
```

One integration by parts removes the derivative completely:

```text
4^h S_h=[x^h](1+x)^(2r+2h-1)(1-x)^(-4r-2s+1).     (68.4)
```

Thus every normalized layer is one binomial/negative-binomial convolution,
or equivalently one terminating Gauss hypergeometric value at `-1`.

There is also an exact probability representation.  If

```text
A(u)=(1+x)^2/(1-x)^4,
B(u)=1/(1-x)^2,
u=4x/(1+x)^2,
```

then `F_(M,s)=A^rB^s`.  Writing `w=sqrt(1-u)` gives

```text
B=(1+w)^2/(4w^2),        A=B/(1-u).
```

Thus, with `J` binomial with parameters `2(r+s)` and `1/2`,

```text
S_h=1/h! E[(r+J/2)^(rising h)],
P_h=2^(h+floor(h/2)) E[(r+J/2)^(rising h)].         (68.5)
```

This representation identifies `y=3r+s` as twice the mean of the random
argument.  The centered variable is symmetric.  Centering the rising
factorial about `(y+h-1)/2` proves in all orders that

```text
P_h((y-s)/3,s) is divisible by y+h-1 when h is odd. (68.6)
```

The exact adjacent-size coefficient ratio now has only one non-elementary
factor.  From (63.1) and (68.1),

```text
g_(M,s,h)/g_(M,s,0)
 =s^(falling 2h) P_h(r,s)
  /[2^floor(h/2) h! product_(j=0)^(h-1)(r+j)(2r+2j+1)].
                                                               (68.7)
```

It follows that

```text
g_h(r)/g_h(r-1)
 =A_h(r,s) P_h(r,s)/P_h(r-1,s),
A_h(r,s)=((2r+s-1)(2r+s-2))
          /(2(r+h-1)(2r+2h-1)).                    (68.8)
```

The scalar part is decreasing throughout the forest cone.  After taking a
common positive denominator, its backward difference has numerator

```text
(s-2h)(8hr+4hs-10h+8r^2+4rs-20r-5s+11).           (68.9)
```

Substitution `r=s+5+q`, `s=2h+k` turns the second factor into

```text
8q^2+48qh+20qk+60q+72h^2+60hk+180h
 +12k^2+75k+111,                                  (68.10)
```

which is strictly positive.  Therefore the right inequality in (67.5)
reduces to the single discrete log-concavity statement

```text
P_h(r-1,s)^2 >= P_h(r,s)P_h(r-2,s).               (68.11)
```

The left inequality in (67.5) reduces, with no estimate, to

```text
(2r+s-1)(2r+s-2)P_h(r)P_(h+1)(r-2)
 >=(2r+s-3)(2r+s-4)P_h(r-1)P_(h+1)(r-1).          (68.12)
```

This corrects the tempting but false stronger comparison obtained by
dropping the two quadratic size factors in (68.12).

The symbolic evidence is now coefficientwise in the full forest cone.  On
substituting `r=s+5+q`, every coefficient of (68.11) is strictly positive
for `1<=h<=12`, and every coefficient of the difference in (68.12) is
strictly positive for `0<=h<=11`.  In the mean coordinate `y=3r+s`, every
coefficient of `P_h` is nonnegative through `h=12`; the odd factor (68.6)
is separately all-order.  These finite expansions are a guide to the
missing induction, not an all-order positivity proof.

The independent replay `prove_selector_normalized_ratio_reduction.py`
checks (68.1)--(68.12), the all-order recurrence specialized symbolically
through `h=12`, and the two shifted minor families.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_NORMALIZED_P_RATIO_REDUCTION_REPLAY` in
`selector_normalized_ratio_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`28D15904583E45A2978829EDACC5DDDF21BD5789E07244359CC3B895EC178075`
and
`E816AAC9F64990AB6C87866549DE8B8290D99DEE962B92498761197370F00C6A`.
The identities and scalar monotonicity are all-order; (68.11)--(68.12)
remain the exact proof frontier.

## 69. The normalized column inequality is now proved in all orders

The first of the two remaining inequalities in Section 68 follows from a
general convolution principle.  Retain the algebraic series from (68.5):

```text
A(u)=(1+sqrt(1-u))^2/(4(1-u)^2),
B(u)=(1+sqrt(1-u))^2/(4(1-u)),
S_h(r,s)=[u^h]A(u)^rB(u)^s.                         (69.1)
```

Recall the Bender--Canfield exponential lemma: if `x_0=1` and
`x_0,x_1,...` is a nonnegative log-concave sequence without internal zeros,
then the coefficients of

```text
exp(sum_(n>=1) x_n u^n/n)                           (69.2)
```

are log-concave.  The inputs in (69.1) have a particularly simple closed
form.  Put

```text
c_n=binom(2n,n)/4^n.
```

Direct logarithmic differentiation gives

```text
n[u^n]log B=1-c_n,
n[u^n]log A=2-c_n.                                  (69.3)
```

Since

```text
c_(n-1)=c_n 2n/(2n-1),
c_(n+1)=c_n (2n+1)/(2n+2),                          (69.4)
```

the adjacent log-concavity minors in the two input sequences are, for
`n>=2`,

```text
(1-c_n)^2-(1-c_(n-1))(1-c_(n+1))
 =c_n(3-2c_n)/(2(n+1)(2n-1))>0,

(2-c_n)^2-(2-c_(n-1))(2-c_(n+1))
 =c_n(3-c_n)/((n+1)(2n-1))>0.                      (69.5)
```

For `A`, the boundary minor at index one is `5/8`.  For `B^s`, the input
to (69.2) is `s(1-c_n)`, and its boundary minor is

```text
s(2s-5)/8.                                          (69.6)
```

It is nonnegative for every integer `s>=3`.  The exponential lemma now
shows that the coefficient sequences of `A` and `B^s` are log-concave.
All nontrivial forest layers `h>=2` have `s>=2h>=4`; the cases `h=0,1`
are immediate.

It remains to explain why row log-concavity gives log-concavity in the
power of `A`.  The following short lemma supplies exactly that step.

**Convolution-power lemma.**  Let `a,c` be nonnegative log-concave
sequences without internal zeros and put

```text
F_r(k)=(c*a^(convolution r))_k.                     (69.7)
```

Then `F_r(k)` is log-concave in `r` for each fixed `k`.

Indeed, convolution preserves log-concavity, so every row `F_r` is
log-concave.  Consequently, for fixed `j`,

```text
F_(r-1)(k-j)/F_(r-1)(k)
```

is nondecreasing in `k`.  Expanding `F_r=a*F_(r-1)` shows that
`F_r(k)/F_(r-1)(k)` is nondecreasing in `k`.  Finally,

```text
F_r(k)^2-F_(r-1)(k)F_(r+1)(k)
 =sum_j a_j(
    F_r(k)F_(r-1)(k-j)-F_(r-1)(k)F_r(k-j)) >=0.    (69.8)
```

Apply the lemma with `a=[u^k]A` and `c=[u^k]B^s`.  Since the positive
normalizing factor between `P_h` and `S_h` depends only on `h`, (69.8)
is precisely

```text
P_h(r-1,s)^2 >= P_h(r,s)P_h(r-2,s).                (69.9)
```

Thus (68.11) is no longer a finite pattern: it is proved in all admissible
parameters.  Combined with the elementary monotonicity (68.9)--(68.10),
this proves the right-hand coefficient-ratio inequality in (67.5).

The independent replay `prove_selector_column_logconcavity.py` verifies
(69.1)--(69.6), the algebraic logarithmic derivatives, finite instances of
both preservation conclusions through layer 14, and 184 target minors.  It
reports `PASS_EXACT_SELECTOR_COLUMN_LOGCONCAVITY_REPLAY` in
`selector_column_logconcavity_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`C606A13C6BDC77DD1AB0877FDF7B25DEF96E05B63C765CB4F0195ADA98411BEF`
and
`4B4E0884B3F7E14A2EC0E792804370DB638C20C07BE63EAC6AC03091581AB5B5`.
The only normalized ratio inequality still open is the mixed minor
(68.12).

## 70. All-order positivity in the mean coordinate

The coefficient pattern in the mean coordinate from Section 68 is also now
proved in all orders.  Put `y=3r+s`, so `r=(y-s)/3`.  Then

```text
sum_h S_hu^h
 =A(u)^rB(u)^s
 =(A(u)^(1/3))^y(B(u)A(u)^(-1/3))^s.              (70.1)
```

With `c_n=binom(2n,n)/4^n`, (69.3) gives

```text
n[u^n]log(A^(1/3))=(2-c_n)/3>0,
n[u^n]log(BA^(-1/3))=(1-2c_n)/3>=0.               (70.2)
```

The second input vanishes only at `n=1`, since `c_1=1/2` and `c_n<1/2`
for `n>=2`.  Both logarithms therefore have nonnegative coefficients.
Expanding the two exponentials in (70.1) proves that every `S_h`, and hence
every `P_h`, has nonnegative coefficients as a polynomial in `y,s`:

```text
P_h((y-s)/3,s) belongs to Q_{>=0}[y,s] for every h.         (70.3)
```

This is useful extra rigidity for the remaining mixed minor: after the forest
substitution `r=s+5+q`, its mean variable is `y=4s+15+3q`, far inside the
positive cone of (70.3).  The shift by `3` under an adjacent size change is
now the only source of signs.

The independent replay `prove_selector_mean_coordinate_positivity.py`
checks (70.1)--(70.2) and expands (70.3) through layer 16.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_SELECTOR_MEAN_COORDINATE_POSITIVITY_REPLAY` in
`selector_mean_coordinate_positivity_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`0C5EFA5D709C4BBA5838AEFB74B6D9CAC0ECB4DDAC0103D230D66A3EB80940E9`
and
`09DA58DE1D98DF3E6155C97A1D335E855C51993F2FB10A94175D31384968077F`.

## 71. The mixed normalized ratio is now proved in all orders

The second inequality left open in Section 68 also follows from the
log-concavity already established in Section 69.  In the mean coordinate,
write

```text
F_h(y,s)=[u^h] exp(y phi(u)+s psi(u)),
n[u^n]phi=(2-c_n)/3,
n[u^n]psi=(1-2c_n)/3,                              (71.1)
```

where `c_n=binom(2n,n)/4^n`.  Thus `F_h` is `S_h` after the substitution
`y=3r+s`, and differs from `P_h` by a positive scalar depending only on
`h`.  Both `phi` and `psi` have nonnegative coefficients.

The key observation is a general effective-degree lemma.  Put

```text
mu_h=y partial_y log F_h,       delta_h=h-mu_h.
```

Logarithmic differentiation of the generating function in (71.1) gives

```text
delta_h
 =sum_(j>=1) theta_j F_(h-j)/F_h,
theta_j=y(j-1)phi_j+s j psi_j >=0.                 (71.2)
```

At every forest parameter the coefficient sequence `(F_h)_h` is
log-concave by Section 69: it is the convolution of the log-concave
coefficient sequences of `A^r` and `B^s`.  Consequently, for each fixed
`j`,

```text
F_(h-j)/F_h
```

is nondecreasing in `h`.  Equation (71.2) therefore proves

```text
delta_(h+1)>=delta_h,
mu_(h+1)-mu_h<=1.                                  (71.3)
```

Equivalently,

```text
F_(h+1)(y,s)/(y F_h(y,s)) is nonincreasing in y>0. (71.4)
```

The cases below the `s>=3` boundary used in Section 69 are direct:
`delta_0=delta_1=0`, and `delta_2>=0`.  Every remaining admissible layer
has `h>=2` and `s>=2h>=4`, so (71.3)--(71.4) hold throughout the forest
cone.

We need one more elementary inequality.  If `p` has nonnegative
coefficients and degree at most `h`, then for `x>3`,

```text
p(x)^2/(p(x-3)p(x+3))
 <=(x^2/(x^2-9))^h.                                (71.5)
```

To prove it, expand both products and pair the ordered terms with degrees
`k,l`.  The arithmetic--geometric mean inequality gives

```text
((x-3)^k(x+3)^l+(x-3)^l(x+3)^k)/2
 >=(x^2-9)^((k+l)/2).
```

After multiplication by `(x^2/(x^2-9))^h`, the remaining factor relative
to `x^(k+l)` is

```text
(x^2/(x^2-9))^(h-(k+l)/2)>=1,
```

because `k,l<=h`.  Summing proves (71.5).

Now put `Y=3r+s` and `x=Y-3`.  The ratio on the right side of the mixed
minor (68.12) is

```text
C=F_h(Y-3)F_(h+1)(Y-3)/(F_h(Y)F_(h+1)(Y-6)).
```

Using (71.4), then (71.5) and the mean-coordinate positivity from Section
70, gives

```text
C <= x/(x-3) * F_h(x)^2/(F_h(x-3)F_h(x+3))
  <= (Y-3)/(Y-6)
     *((Y-3)^2/(Y(Y-6)))^h.                        (71.6)
```

It remains only to compare (71.6) with the quadratic size factor.  Put
`X=2r+s`.  The elementary bounds

```text
log(1+z)<=z,             log(1+z)>=z/(1+z)
```

show that the logarithm of (71.6) is at most

```text
3/(Y-6)+9s/(2Y(Y-6)),
```

while

```text
log(((X-1)(X-2))/((X-3)(X-4)))
 >=2/(X-1)+2/(X-2).                                  (71.7)
```

The difference between the right side of (71.7) and the preceding upper
bound is strictly positive.  Indeed, substitute `r=s+5+q`.  Its numerator
over a positive denominator is

```text
72q^3+252q^2s+792q^2+274qs^2+1776qs+2700q
 +87s^3+899s^2+2802s+2700,                         (71.8)
```

and every coefficient is positive.  Equations (71.6)--(71.8) prove

```text
(2r+s-1)(2r+s-2)P_h(r)P_(h+1)(r-2)
 >(2r+s-3)(2r+s-4)P_h(r-1)P_(h+1)(r-1).           (71.9)
```

Thus (68.12) is proved in all admissible parameters.  Together with
Section 69, both normalized inequalities in (67.5) are now theorems rather
than finite coefficient patterns.

The independent replay `prove_selector_mixed_ratio.py` checks (71.1)--
(71.2), the effective-degree polynomial through layer 10, 200 exact
log-concavity and deficit-monotonicity instances, all degree pairs in

minors.  It reports `PASS_EXACT_SELECTOR_MIXED_RATIO_REPLAY` in
`selector_mixed_ratio_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and report SHA-256
hashes are respectively
`1A33D32648B86E59E183D0EF5C6E30CBFD6B81CFAB20AD5C6162F146FE097C59`
and
`4A753F3A36F13194D3A5E3B5E6919B42735527D194777390D7F7A7EE2B99EAB8`.

## 72. The adjacent-size path operator has a strictly positive shift kernel

There is a useful operator form of the unsigned nested-chain target (62.4).
Write `s=2D`, allowing `D` to be an integer or half-integer, and center the
palindromic path slice at `i=D`.  If `nu=i-D`, the ratio between its centered
Laurent coefficients at sizes `M` and `M-1` is

```text
theta(nu^2)
 =((A^2-nu^2)((A-1)^2-nu^2))
   /(16((b^2-nu^2)(c^2-nu^2))),                   (72.1)
A=2c+D+1,       b=c+1/2,       c=M-D-1.
```

This follows directly from

```text
p_(M,i)/p_(M-1,i)
 =((2M-i-1)(2M-i-2))
   /((2M-2i-1)(2M-2i-2)).                         (72.2)
```

Thus (72.1) handles even and odd `s` uniformly; the frequencies are integers
in the even case and half-integers in the odd case.  It is the rational
functional calculus of the Chebyshev operator

```text
L=(v^2-1)d^2/dv^2+v d/dv,       L T_n=n^2T_n       (72.3)
```

in the even case, with the conjugate half-integer Jacobi operator in the odd
case.

The apparent obstruction in (72.1) is a negative residue.  Its exact partial
fraction decomposition is

```text
theta(x)=1/16+R_b/(b^2-x)+R_c/(c^2-x),             (72.4)

R_b=-(2D+2c-1)(2D+2c+1)(2D+6c+1)(2D+6c+3)
       /(64(4c+1)),

R_c=(D+c)(D+3c)(D+c+1)(D+3c+1)/(4(4c+1)).         (72.5)
```

Nevertheless the two resolvents combine to a **strictly positive** kernel.
For `0<=nu<c`,

```text
1/(a^2-nu^2)
 =(1/a) integral_0^infinity exp(-au)cosh(nu u)du.  (72.6)
```

Put

```text
w(u)=R_c/c exp(-cu)+R_b/b exp(-bu).                (72.7)
```

Since `c<b`, it is enough to check positivity at `u=0`.  After clearing a
positive denominator,

```text
R_c/c+R_b/b
 =[8D^4+64D^3c+16D^3+208D^2c^2+104D^2c+8D^2
   +320Dc^3+240Dc^2+40Dc
   +168c^4+168c^3+48c^2+3c]
   /[32c(2c+1)(4c+1)] >0.                         (72.8)
```

Every coefficient in the numerator is positive.  The positive term in
(72.7) also decays more slowly, so (72.8) proves `w(u)>0` for every `u>=0`.

If `E_(M,s)(x)` is the centered Laurent form of the path slice, equations
(72.4)--(72.7) become the exact positive-shift formula

```text
E_(M,s)(x)
 =1/16 E_(M-1,s)(x)
  +1/2 integral_0^infinity w(u)
       (E_(M-1,s)(x+u)+E_(M-1,s)(x-u))du.          (72.9)
```

This is a sharper frontier, but not yet the nested-chain proof: positive
averages of hyperbolic shifts are not variation diminishing for arbitrary
polynomials.  What remains is to prove variation diminution on this special
path-slice orbit, or to combine (72.9) with a sharp logarithmic root-mesh
bound.  A random palindromic negative-rooted polynomial supplies immediate
counterexamples to the unrestricted Chebyshev-multiplier statement, so that
extra step cannot be omitted.

The exact replay `prove_path_chebyshev_positive_kernel.py` derives
(72.1)--(72.8) symbolically and checks the centered multiplier transcription
in 25,740 exact integer and half-integer frequencies through `s=100`.  It
reports `PASS_EXACT_PATH_CHEBYSHEV_POSITIVE_KERNEL_REPLAY` in
`path_chebyshev_positive_kernel_exact_20260809.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`C676DDD96B8005AF700A22DC8AD481DF32F752625E4846527240CD45C5FC9294`
and
`A1388DE3F35097EC904EBEDC20BC9B4034988F3789D3A019E8F18A52CFD9AA04`.

## 73. Turan positivity reduces to one local Poincare inequality

The strict Turan target (67.2) has a substantially shorter probabilistic
reduction.  Write

```text
A_h=g_(M,s,h),       B_h=g_(M-1,s,h),
C_h=g_(M-2,s,h),     d=floor(s/2),
x_h=A_h/B_h,         y_h=B_h/C_h,
Delta_h=y_h-y_(h+1).                                (73.1)
```

Sections 69 and 71 prove `Delta_h>=0` and `y_h-x_h>=0`.  More is true if
the following entirely local inequality holds, with absent boundary
differences interpreted as zero:

```text
y_h(y_h-x_h)
 >=1/2{(d-h)Delta_h^2+h Delta_(h-1)^2},
                         0<=h<=d.                   (73.2)
```

Namely, (73.2) implies strict Turan positivity on the whole positive axis.
Here is the proof.  Section 124 of
`NYQUIST_RESERVE_INDUCTION_LEMMA_2026-08-02.md` proves that

```text
C(t)=sum_h C_h t^h=C_0 product_(j=1)^d(1+t/rho_j),
rho_j>0.                                             (73.3)
```

For fixed `t>0`, put `p_j=t/(t+rho_j)` and let

```text
H=X_1+...+X_d,       X_j independent Bernoulli(p_j).
```

Then

```text
Pr(H=h)=mu_h=C_h t^h/C(t).                          (73.4)
```

The normalized Turan gap is exactly

```text
{B(t)^2-A(t)C(t)}/C(t)^2
 =E[y_H]^2-E[y_H x_H]
 =E[y_H(y_H-x_H)]-Var(y_H).                        (73.5)
```

Let `S_j=H-X_j`.  Efron--Stein gives

```text
Var(y_H)
 <=sum_j p_j(1-p_j)E[(y_(S_j+1)-y_(S_j))^2]
 =sum_(h=0)^(d-1)c_h Delta_h^2,                    (73.6)

c_h=sum_j p_j(1-p_j)Pr(S_j=h).
```

Two elementary deletion identities are

```text
(h+1)mu_(h+1)=sum_j p_j Pr(S_j=h),
(d-h)mu_h    =sum_j(1-p_j)Pr(S_j=h).               (73.7)
```

Term by term in the definition of `c_h`,

```text
c_h<=min{(h+1)mu_(h+1),(d-h)mu_h}
    <=1/2{(h+1)mu_(h+1)+(d-h)mu_h}.                (73.8)
```

Substitute (73.8) into (73.6) and shift the first sum.  This gives

```text
Var(y_H)
 <=1/2 sum_(h=0)^d mu_h
       {(d-h)Delta_h^2+h Delta_(h-1)^2}
 <=E[y_H(y_H-x_H)],                                (73.9)
```

where the last inequality is (73.2).  Equations (73.5) and (73.9) prove
the Turan inequality.  In fact strictness needs no separate argument: the
`h=0` instance below is strict and `mu_0>0` for every `t>0`.

The bottom boundary of (73.2) is now proved in all orders.  Substitute
`r=s+5+q` and separately `s=2d` and `s=2d+1` into

```text
y_0(y_0-x_0)-d Delta_0^2/2.                        (73.10)
```

After clearing its positive denominator, the even numerator factors as

```text
d(3d+q+3)(6d+2q+7) E(d,q),                         (73.11)
```

where `E` has 28 terms and every coefficient is positive.  The odd
numerator factors as

```text
(3d+q+5)(6d+2q+9) O(d,q),                          (73.12)
```

where `O` has 42 terms and every coefficient is positive.  Thus (73.10)
is strict throughout the forest cone.

The next layer is also proved in all orders.  At `h=1`, split the parameter
space into the top boundary `d=1` and the interior `d=2+k`, `k>=0`, and
again substitute `r=s+5+q`.  In the even and odd interior cases, clearing
the positive denominator leaves a polynomial in `k,q` with 170 terms; every
coefficient is positive.  At the even and odd top boundaries the respective
numerators have 5 and 7 terms, again all strictly positive.  Hence (73.2)
is now a theorem for `h=0,1` in both parities.

The same exact calculation also closes `h=2`.  In the two interior parities
the cleared numerators each have 299 terms, all strictly positive; at the
even and odd top boundaries they have 9 and 11 positive terms.  Therefore
(73.2) is now a theorem for `h=0,1,2` in both parities.

The next symbolic layer closes `h=3` as well.  In both interior parities the
cleared numerator has 464 terms, every coefficient is strictly positive,
and the minimum coefficient is `19042491875328`.  At the even and odd top
boundaries the numerators have respectively 13 and 15 positive terms, with
minimum coefficients `1259712` and `2099520`.  Hence (73.2) is now an
all-order theorem for `h=0,1,2,3` in both parities.

The remaining statement is therefore only (73.2) for `4<=h<=d`.  It has
strong exact evidence: every one of 34,644 local margins is strictly
positive for `2<=s<=150` and

```text
M=2s+5+e,       e in {0,1,5,17,73,200}.            (73.13)
```

This is materially stronger than the earlier finite Turan check because
it is independent of `t` and, once proved, feeds the all-order argument
(73.3)--(73.9) directly.

The replay `reduce_selector_turan_to_local_poincare.py` verifies the exact
expectation identity (73.5), the deletion identities (73.7), all margins
in (73.13), and the fourteen all-order positive-coefficient certificates for
`h=0,1,2,3`.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_SELECTOR_TURAN_LOCAL_POINCARE_REDUCTION_REPLAY` in
`selector_turan_local_poincare_reduction_exact_20260809.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`6FB6D28A3B4C6ADBBC70C24517B0FB871AAB5B8374251025B18D8C99F8566467`
and
`8A3596BE077EDC4F34988601A8D688DA4A7CD4C4AE125663D43A58C0BECAA9D1`.

## 74. Lagrange coefficient formula and exponent-Turan reduction

There is a substantially simpler all-order formula for the complete unsigned
polynomial.  For `s<2M`, put

```text
R=2M-s-1,
A_t(z)=(1+z+t z^2)/(1-t z^2)^2,
B_t(z)=1/(1-t z^2).
```

Then

```text
G_(M,s)(t)
 =[z^s](1+z+t z^2)^R/(1-t z^2)^(2R+1)
 =[z^s]B_t(z)A_t(z)^R.                              (74.1)
```

Here is an all-order derivation.  Let `q(v),r(v)` be the two roots of
`X^2-X-v=0`, with `q(0)=1` and `r(0)=0`.  The path Binet formula is

```text
P_M(v)={q(v)^(2M)-r(v)^(2M)}/sqrt(1+4v).            (74.2)
```

In the determinant identity (66.3), take `a+b=1`, `ab=t`.  Since
`r(v)^(2M)` starts in degree `2M`, it cannot contribute to `[U^s]` when
`s<2M`.  If

```text
Q=q(aU),  S=q(bU),  w=QS,  z=U/w,
```

then (66.3) and (74.2) give

```text
G_(M,s)(t)=[U^s] w^(2M)/D,
D=sqrt((1+4aU)(1+4bU)).                             (74.3)
```

The quadratic equations for `Q,S` imply, with `sigma=Q+S`,

```text
U=sigma^2-sigma-2w,
tU^2=w(w-sigma+1).
```

Eliminating `sigma` after `U=zw` gives the unexpectedly simple identity

```text
w(1-t z^2)^2=1+z+t z^2,                            (74.4)
```

so `w=A_t(z)`.  Moreover

```text
D=2w(1+t z^2)-1,
{1+z w'(z)/w}/D=(1-t z^2)/(1+z+t z^2).             (74.5)
```

Changing residues from `U` to `z` in (74.3), and using (74.4)--(74.5),
proves (74.1).

Formula (74.1) makes coefficient positivity transparent.  If
`h<=floor(s/2)` and `j=s-2h`, then

```text
[t^h]G_(M,s)
 =sum_(k=0)^h R!/{j! k! (R-j-k)!}
                 binom(2R+h-k,h-k),                (74.6)
```

where out-of-support summands are zero.  More importantly, one size step is
exactly two exponent steps.  Thus the strict Turan target (67.2) is now the
single coefficient-of-powers inequality

```text
([z^s]B A^(R-2))^2
  >([z^s]B A^R)([z^s]B A^(R-4)),                   (74.7)
```

for `t>0` and the forest range `R>=3s+9`.  This is a standard-looking
parameter log-concavity problem for one fixed positive power series, rather
than a three-family gamma identity.  Positivity of the coefficients of `A`
alone does not automatically imply (74.7), so a total-positivity,
Pochhammer-Turan, or direct injection argument is still required.  The
explicit sum (74.6) and the large reserve `R>=3s+9` are the new analytic
leverage.

The replay `prove_selector_lagrange_coefficient_identity.py` verifies the
rational elimination and Jacobian identities, the original gamma transform,
and the positive sum (74.6).  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_SELECTOR_LAGRANGE_COEFFICIENT_IDENTITY_REPLAY` in
`selector_lagrange_coefficient_identity_exact_20260810.json`.  Equations
(74.1)--(74.6), not the finite replay, constitute the all-order proof.  The
source and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`4FED8C725E403F0842D185819CD7B8A83DE20952AF099897EFD57C6B832D1CCF`
and
`F3AF25173C7F3B88B2F856855B826A752DF37D51E3F51C6BECB1D4E5D571FA43`.

## 75. Adjacent unsigned rows have an explicit mixed-slice common interlacer

The path determinant structure proves a new all-order compatibility theorem.
For two polynomials `P(v)=sum p_i v^i`, `R(v)=sum r_i v^i`, define

```text
F_(P,R;s)(z)=sum_i p_i r_(s-i)z^i.                 (75.1)
```

Fix a negative-rooted `R(v)=product_j(1+mu_jv)`, `mu_j>0`, and let

```text
T_(R,s)(z^i)=r_(s-i)z^i.                           (75.2)
```

On a degree ceiling `m` at least as large as `s` and the input degrees, its
algebraic stability-preserver symbol is

```text
T_(R,s)((z+w)^m)
 =w^(m-s)e_s(z,...,z,mu_1w,...,mu_lw).             (75.3)
```

The right side is stable because the elementary symmetric polynomial is
stable and positive scaling and diagonalization preserve stability.  The
finite Pólya--Schur symbol criterion therefore makes `T_(R,s)` a real
stability preserver; in particular, it preserves proper position.

Now let `P_M` be the path polynomial in (66.1) and put

```text
K_(M,s)=F_(P_(M-1),P_M;s).                         (75.4)
```

The path Jacobi matrices are nested principal submatrices, so `P_(M-1)` and
`P_M` are in strict proper position.  Applying (75.3) first with `R=P_M`
and then with `R=P_(M-1)` shows that `K_(M,s)` interlaces `A_(M,s)`, while
its reciprocal

```text
K*_(M,s)(z)=z^sK_(M,s)(1/z)=F_(P_M,P_(M-1);s)(z)  (75.5)
```

interlaces `A_(M-1,s)`.  Since the latter is palindromic, reciprocal
reflection converts the second statement into an interlacing of `K_(M,s)`
with `A_(M-1,s)` itself.  Hence the two adjacent diagonal slices possess an
explicit common interlacer.  It follows that

```text
A_(M,s)+cA_(M-1,s) is negative-rooted for c>=0.    (75.6)
```

The pencil is still palindromic, so reciprocal pairing and the gamma
substitution give the all-order conclusion

```text
G_(M,s)(t)+cG_(M-1,s)(t) is negative-rooted
for every c>=0.                                    (75.7)
```

This is positive compatibility, not full Obreschkoff proper position.  In
equal degree, a positive pencil can remain real-rooted even when its two
root sets are nested inside the same common-interlacer gaps.  Therefore
(75.7) does **not** yet prove the oriented chain (62.4).  For one adjacent
pair it sharpens the frontier to path-specific componentwise monotonicity
inside the common gaps.  The already-proved normalized ratio inequalities
show that

```text
g_(M,s,h)/g_(M-1,s,h)
```

is strictly decreasing in `h`, and hence the sum of the roots becomes
strictly more negative with `M`; this selects the orientation once
componentwise alternation is established, but it does not establish that
alternation by itself.  Even oriented adjacent pairs do not yet imply the
full three-level inequality `c_i<a_(i+1)`: that last comparison requires a
two-step common interlacer between sizes `M` and `M-2`, or a quantitative
path-specific displacement bound.

The full proof and its exact caveat are recorded in
`MIXED_SLICE_COMMON_INTERLACER_2026-08-10.md`.  The independent replay
`prove_mixed_slice_common_interlacer.py` checks four symbol identities, 26
strict mixed-slice alternations, 26 gamma common-interlacer cases, and 78
positive pencils.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_MIXED_SLICE_COMMON_INTERLACER_REPLAY` in
`mixed_slice_common_interlacer_exact_20260810.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`9425B4C7A68DD7122B6ED7CA1551FB4F0A79692BABEBB67DDDD56654F889B3A8`
and
`CA43728238B735F778E9B41DA88584984260A8182C96522F0538D5A8C8650C47`.

## 76. The codimension-two root bridge reduces to one separator inequality

Write the matching polynomial of the `n`-vertex path as

```text
q_n(v)=sum_i binom(n-i,i)v^i
```

and define the mixed homogeneous slice

```text
F_(n,m,s)(z)=sum_i [v^i]q_n(v)[v^(s-i)]q_m(v)z^i.  (76.1)
```

Endpoint deletion on the first path gives

```text
q_n=q_(n-1)+v q_(n-2),
F_(n,m,s)=F_(n-1,m,s)+zF_(n-2,m,s-1).             (76.2)
```

Four iterations of (76.2) produce the exact displacement identity

```text
F_(n,m,s)=F_(n-4,m,s)+zH_(n,m,s-1),               (76.3)
H_(n,m,s-1)=sum_(j=2)^5 F_(n-j,m,s-1).
```

The vertical slice

```text
V_(n,m,s)(z)=zF_(n,m,s-1)(z)                       (76.4)
```

strictly interlaces `F_(n,m,s)`.  This is the adjacent homogeneous-slice
interlacing of the stable elementary-symmetric binary form, with `z`
supplying the endpoint zero.  Let `xi<0` be a nonzero root of (76.4).  At
that root (76.3) becomes

```text
F_(n-4,m,s)(xi)
 =F_(n,m,s)(xi)-xi H_(n,m,s-1)(xi).                (76.5)
```

Consequently the single quantitative inequality

```text
0 < xi H_(n,m,s-1)(xi)/F_(n,m,s)(xi) < 1           (76.6)
```

at every nonzero root of `F_(n,m,s-1)` preserves all separator signs in
(76.5).  The signs at zero and the endpoints agree automatically.  Hence
(76.6) makes `V_(n,m,s)` a strict common interlacer of `F_(n,m,s)` and
`F_(n-4,m,s)`.

Set `n=2M-1`.  With `m=n`, this conclusion says that the direct mixed slice

```text
F_(P_(M-2),P_M;s)                                  (76.7)
```

interlaces the large diagonal `A_(M,s)`.  Repeating (76.6) with `m=n-4`
and applying reciprocal reflection proves that (76.7) also interlaces the
small diagonal `A_(M-2,s)`.  Thus proving (76.6) in these two
specializations supplies the exact codimension-two common interlacer that
Section 75 leaves open.

This is a rigorous reduction, not yet the missing all-order estimate.
Even after positive codimension-two compatibility is established, the
orientation of the diagonal roots must still be fixed before claiming the
strict chain (62.4).  The normalized ratio and root-sum inequalities from
Sections 67 and 75 are the available orientation data.

The note `CODIM2_VERTICAL_SEPARATOR_REDUCTION_2026-08-10.md` records the
proof.  The independent replay `reduce_codim2_mixed_interlacing.py` verifies
(76.2)--(76.5), 44 parameter cases, and 264 exact algebraic sign-retention
checks; the largest observed companion ratio is approximately `0.715271`.
It reports `PASS_EXACT_CODIM2_VERTICAL_SEPARATOR_REDUCTION_REPLAY` in
`codim2_mixed_vertical_separator_exact_20260810.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`FD7D5D4D500664312C45CD5CCB72C154074AC63D011A7892377EF6C4CEA0F8A4`
and
`34A37DD91453C16086F09794D2C73AD4F12A3585766D8E29F08EEF8D31293A5F`.

## 77. The active-box weights are PF-infinity in all orders

Formula (74.6) has a second normalization that exposes the quantitative
curvature missing from Section 73.  Put `j=s-2h` and

```text
C(x)=(1+x)/(1-x)^2,        D(x)=C(x)-1=x(3-x)/(1-x)^2.
```

Then the coefficient in (74.6) can be written

```text
g_(R,s,h)
 =binom(R,j)[x^h]C(x)^(R-j)(1-x)^(-2j-1)
 =sum_(ell=0)^h w_(j,h,ell) binom(R,j+ell),         (77.1)

w_(j,h,ell)
 =binom(j+ell,j)[x^h]D(x)^ell(1-x)^(-2j-1).        (77.2)
```

Every weight in (77.2) is positive.  Their row polynomial has the simple
bivariate generating function

```text
W_(j,h)(z)=sum_(ell=0)^h w_(j,h,ell)z^ell,

sum_(h>=0) W_(j,h)(z)x^h
 =(1-x)/{1-(2+3z)x+(1+z)x^2}^(j+1).               (77.3)
```

The following is an all-order theorem.

```text
For j>=0 and h>=1, W_(j,h) has h simple zeros,
all lying strictly in (-8/9,0).                    (77.4)
```

To prove it, put `lambda=j+1` and, for `-8/9<z<0`, set

```text
y=sqrt(1+z),       u=(2+3z)/(2y).
```

The map `z -> u` is an increasing bijection from `(-8/9,0)` to `(-1,1)`.
The Gegenbauer generating function turns (77.3) into

```text
W_(j,h)(z)
 =y^h{C_h^lambda(u)-y^(-1)C_(h-1)^lambda(u)}.      (77.5)
```

Let `xi_1<...<xi_h` be the zeros of `C_h^lambda`.  Consecutive
Gegenbauer polynomials strictly interlace, so the signs of (77.5) alternate
at successive `xi_i`; this supplies one zero in every interval
`(xi_i,xi_(i+1))`.  At the largest zero (77.5) is negative, whereas at
`u=1`, where `z=0` and `y=1`, it is positive because

```text
C_h^lambda(1)-C_(h-1)^lambda(1)>0.
```

Thus there is one additional zero to the right of `xi_h`.  These are `h`
distinct zeros in `(-8/9,0)`.  Since the coefficient of `z^h` is positive,
the degree is exactly `h`, proving (77.4).

Newton's inequalities now give the strict ultra-log-concavity

```text
w_(j,h,ell)/binom(h,ell) is log-concave in ell.    (77.6)
```

This is the quantitative probabilistic structure needed by the local
Poincare route.  If the summands in (77.1) are normalized to an active-box
law on `K=j+ell`, then multiplication by `binom(R,j+ell)` preserves the
normalized log-concavity.  The terminal normalized odds are explicitly

```text
h*pi_h/pi_(h-1)
 =9(R-j-h+1)/(5h+6j-2).                            (77.7)
```

In the forest reserve this is at least `3` (and at the top `j=0` it is at
least `9`).  Hence the active count `ell` stochastically dominates a
`Binomial(h,p)` law with `p>=3/4` (respectively `p>=9/10` at the top).
The remaining work in (73.2) is no longer to establish concentration from
scratch, but to convert (77.6)--(77.7) into a sufficiently sharp finite-step
lower bound for the size curvature `v_h` and a matching upper bound for the
adjacent-layer slopes.

The proof and the independent Section 73 audit are recorded in
`POINCARE_AUDIT_GEGENBAUER_W_THEOREM_2026-08-10.md`.  The replay
`prove_active_box_gegenbauer_ulc.py` checks 91 cases, 637 active-weight
identities, 91 Gegenbauer identities, 462 strict normalized Newton minors,
546 exact Sturm root counts in `(-8/9,0)`, and 84 terminal-odds identities.
It reports `PASS_EXACT_ACTIVE_BOX_GEGENBAUER_ULC_THEOREM_REPLAY` in
`active_box_gegenbauer_ulc_exact_20260810.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`E819AD697C1BBEFE71A1EE75ECD0231E4FC1FE0C022655CE28C3202F841A7EB2`
and
`C3CA5D6D211ECBC5585B605B11C94963534CFE18837C4F6475702D5D1217BD22`.

## 78. Quantitative curvature leaves one normalized deficit bound

For real `X>=j+h`, normalize the summands in (77.1) to a law on
`K=j+L`.  Ultra-log-concavity and the terminal odds (77.7) give

```text
E L>=hp,        Var L<=hp(1-p),                    (78.1)

p=Omega/(1+Omega),
Omega=9(X-j-h+1)/(5h+6j-2).
```

The size curvature has a useful continuous representation.  Put

```text
a_X(K)=sum_(r=0)^(K-1)1/(X-r),
b_X(K)=sum_(r=0)^(K-1)1/(X-r)^2.
```

Differentiating the finite binomial sum gives

```text
-(log g_(X,s,h))''=E b_X(K)-Var a_X(K).            (78.2)
```

On `K<=D=j+h`, the first function is `1/(X-D)`-Lipschitz.  The
independent-copy variance identity and (78.1) therefore imply

```text
Var a_X(K)<=hp(1-p)/(X-D)^2.                       (78.3)
```

Twice applying Jensen gives the matching lower bound

```text
E b_X(K)
 >=mu_0/{X-(mu_0-1)/2}^2,       mu_0=j+hp.         (78.4)
```

Indeed, for fixed `K`, the average of the `K` inverse squares is at least
the inverse square of the average denominator; the resulting function
`4K/(2X-K+1)^2` is increasing and convex in `K`.

Let `N=3s+7+2e` be the middle exponent in the step-two size quotient,
`e>=0`.  Integrating (78.2)--(78.4) against the triangular second-difference
kernel on `[N-2,N+2]` yields

```text
v_h>=4 Kappa/(1+4 Kappa),                          (78.5)

Kappa=mu_0/{N+2-(mu_0-1)/2}^2
       -hp(1-p)/(N-2-D)^2,
p=9(N-1-j-h)/{5h+6j-2+9(N-1-j-h)}.                (78.6)
```

The remaining adjacent-layer estimate is now isolated as

```text
u_h <=(5/8)(4a-1)/{a(2a+1)},       a=m+h-1.        (78.7)
```

Conditional on (78.7), (78.5) closes every remaining instance of (73.2).
The comparison is an exact positive-coefficient certificate.  Substitute

```text
s=2h+2c+epsilon,       epsilon in {0,1},
N=3s+7+2e,             m=s+4+e.
```

For `h=H+4,c=C+1`, the cleared numerator has 815 positive terms in each
parity.  For `h=H+9,c=0`, it has 135 positive terms in each parity.  All
1,900 coefficients are positive, with common minimum `48157949952`.
The ten top families `c=0,4<=h<=8` are proved directly from the original
local inequality: their cleared numerators and denominators are positive
polynomials in `e` (260 numerator terms in total).

A sufficient effective-degree form of (78.7) is

```text
{delta_h-delta_(h-1)}/(h-1)<=7/(3Y),       Y=3m+s. (78.8)
```

Exact evidence suggests that the left side decreases with `h`; if so,
(78.8) reduces to its explicit `h=2` case.  This normalized
deficit-increment monotonicity is now the sole all-order gap in the local
Poincare route.

The complete reduction is recorded in
`ACTIVE_BOX_CURVATURE_REDUCTION_2026-08-10.md`.  The replay
`reduce_active_box_curvature_to_deficit_bound.py` checks all 1,900 scalar
certificate coefficients and the ten direct top families.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_ACTIVE_BOX_CURVATURE_DEFICIT_REDUCTION_REPLAY` in
`active_box_curvature_deficit_reduction_exact_20260810.json`.  The source
and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`7CA8D5FDCE49692A7E6CAAA89F386C27402145313BCB774FCD79B54D9C52D49A`
and
`9A3C1088FF60F1B16B7B9B31854EC2E8D94C47F71E4070B6C64951CE0CB93FDF`.

## 79. A finite-free reduction of the codimension-two bridge

The codimension-two compatibility sought in Section 76 also has a smaller
one-parameter formulation.  Write

```text
q_(n,i)=binom(n-i,i),
F_(n,m,s)(z)=sum_i q_(n,i)q_(m,s-i)z^i,

B_(n,s)(x)
 =sum_(i=0)^s (-1)^i s!/(s-i)! binom(n-i,i)(x/4)^i. (79.1)
```

For degree-`s` polynomials in normalized binomial basis, let `box_s` denote
finite multiplicative convolution.  Exact coefficient comparison gives

```text
F_(n,m,s)(-x)/q_(m,s)=B_(n,s) box_s R_(m,s),       (79.2)

R_(n,s)(x)
 ={}_2F_2(-s,n-s+1;
          (n-2s+1)/2,(n-2s+2)/2;x).               (79.3)
```

If `a=(n-2s+1)/2`, then the second factor has the classical decomposition

```text
R_(n,s)
 ={}_1F_1(-s;a;x) box_s
   {}_2F_1(-s,s+2a;a+1/2;x).                       (79.4)
```

These are respectively Laguerre and symmetric Jacobi/Gegenbauer blocks.
In the forest reserve `n>=4s+9`, one has `a>=s+5`, strictly inside their
positive-rooted parameter ranges.

Finite multiplicative convolution preserves real-rootedness and
interlacing when the convolving polynomial has roots of one sign.  Hence the
single block lemma

```text
B_(n,s) and B_(n-4,s) strictly alternate
for s>=2 and n>=4s+9                              (79.5)
```

would imply that `F_(n,m,s)` and `F_(n-4,m,s)` are positively compatible
for every admissible second path `m`.  Taking `m=n` and `m=n-4` supplies
both sides of the direct mixed-slice bridge.  This bypasses the stronger
separator inequality (76.6), but it still proves only positive compatibility;
the oriented root order remains a separate necessary step.

There is also an exact random-matrix model for (79.1).  For odd `n=2M-1`,
let `C_(M-1)=tridiag(1,2,1)` and let `X` be an `s` by `(M-1)` standard real
Gaussian matrix.  Cauchy--Binet and the Gaussian determinant moment give

```text
B_(n,s)(x)
 =E det(I_s-(x/4)X C_(M-1)X^T).                   (79.6)
```

Thus (79.5) is an expected-characteristic-polynomial interlacing statement
for the codimension-two principal compression `C_(M-3) subset C_(M-1)`.
The remaining analytic form is the explicit parameter shift

```text
{}_2F_2(-s,s+2a;a,a+1/2;x)
 versus
{}_2F_2(-s,s+2a-4;a-2,a-3/2;x),      a>=s+5.      (79.7)
```

Individual interlacings of the Laguerre and Gegenbauer factors do not by
themselves prove (79.7), because two same-direction interlacings need not
compose across a full gap.  This is precisely the transitivity issue already
identified in Sections 75--76.

The reduction is recorded in `FINITE_FREE_PATH_BLOCK_REDUCTION_2026-08-10.md`.
The replay `prove_finite_free_path_block_reduction.py` verifies 264 exact
coefficient identities, 44 block interlacings by rational root isolation,
and 88 induced mixed-slice interlacings.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_FINITE_FREE_PATH_BLOCK_REDUCTION_REPLAY` in
`finite_free_path_block_exact_20260810.json`.  The source and report SHA-256
hashes are respectively
`086B90B02599F0DFB12CA2D28EED3217D609281DF63F08EC192504B4897893A9`
and
`B17139885BF1524F62460DC24DAE8ED6FF360F1206B01F8290873C0578FD30CB`.

## 80. TP2 cycle insertion closes the local Poincare inequality

The normalized deficit estimate (78.8), and hence the whole local
Poincare obligation (73.2), is now proved in all orders.  Retain (71.1) and
write

```text
x_n=Y(2-c_n)/3+s(1-2c_n)/3,
F_h=[u^h]exp(sum_(n>=1)x_nu^n/n),
c_n=binom(2n,n)/4^n.                               (80.1)
```

The input `(x_n)` is strictly log-concave.  Put

```text
q=x_2/x_1=13/12+s/(6Y),
C_n=x_n/{x_1q^(n-1)},       a=x_1/q,
G(z)=sum_(n>=1)C_nz^n/n.                            (80.2)
```

Then `C_1=C_2=1`, `(C_n)` is log-concave, and after `z=qu`,

```text
F_h=q^hP_h(a),       P_h(a)=[z^h]exp(aG(z)).       (80.3)
```

The key lemma is

```text
R_h-a partial_aR_h<=h-1,
R_h=hP_h/P_(h-1).                                  (80.4)
```

Here is an all-order proof.  Define the partial-Bell array

```text
e_(n,k)=[z^n]G(z)^k/k!.
```

Differentiation gives

```text
n e_(n,k)=sum_(ell>=1)C_ell e_(n-ell,k-1).         (80.5)
```

Thus, if `A_(n,m)=C_(n-m)/n` for `n>m` and is zero otherwise, then
`e_k=Ae_(k-1)`.  The Toeplitz matrix of the log-concave sequence `(C_n)`
is TP2, including the zero boundary, and positive row scaling preserves
TP2.  Starting from `[e_0,e_1]` and applying Cauchy--Binet inductively to

```text
[e_k,e_(k+1)]=A[e_(k-1),e_k]
```

shows that every adjacent pair of columns is TP2.  Equivalently,

```text
e_(m,k)/e_(m,k-1) is nondecreasing in m.           (80.6)
```

Give a permutation of `n` labels weight `a^K` times the product of its
cycle weights `C_ell`, and let `L` be the length of the cycle containing a
uniform label.  Conditional on `K=k`, pointing the label gives

```text
Pr(L=ell|K=k)=C_ell e_(n-ell,k-1)/{n e_(n,k)}.     (80.7)
```

Equation (80.6) says that `L` decreases in likelihood-ratio order when
`k` increases.  Since `r_ell=C_(ell+1)/C_ell` decreases, the insertion
statistic

```text
H=sum_(cycles gamma)|gamma|r_|gamma|
```

has `E(H|K=k)` nondecreasing in `k`; hence `Cov(H,K)>=0`.  Inserting one
new label as a singleton or into an existing cycle gives

```text
R_h=a+E H,
partial_aR_h=1+Cov(H,K)/a.                         (80.8)
```

Since `H<=h-1`, equations (80.8) prove (80.4).

The dependence of the normalized shape `C_n` on `Y` cannot be discarded.
It has the favorable sign.  If `tau=s/Y`, direct differentiation gives

```text
partial_Y C_n>=0                                   (80.9)
```

for every `n`; for `n>=3` this reduces to

```text
{4n-17+(28-2n)c_n}/6>0.
```

The cases `n=3,4` are direct, `5<=n<=14` are immediate term by term, and
for `n>14` the bound `c_n<=1/2` leaves `3n-3>0`.  In the forest range the
input `(1,aC_1,aC_2,...)` is log-concave, so Bender--Canfield makes
`(P_h)` log-concave.  Therefore

```text
(1/R_h)partial_(C_j)R_h
 ={a/j}{P_(h-j)/P_h-P_(h-1-j)/P_(h-1)}>=0.        (80.10)
```

Put `X_h=hF_h/F_(h-1)` and `Q=q-Y partial_Yq=13/12+s/(3Y)`.
Differentiating (80.3) with all shape terms retained, then using
(80.4), (80.9), and (80.10), gives

```text
X_h-Y partial_YX_h
 =Q(R_h-a partial_aR_h)
  -Yq sum_(j>=3)(partial_(C_j)R_h)(partial_YC_j)
 <=Q(h-1).                                         (80.11)
```

Also `X_h=qR_h>=qa=x_1=Y/2`.  Since

```text
delta_h-delta_(h-1)
 ={X_h-Y partial_YX_h}/X_h,
```

(80.11) yields

```text
{delta_h-delta_(h-1)}/{h-1}
 <=13/(6Y)+2s/(3Y^2)<=7/(3Y),                     (80.12)
```

where the last step uses `4s<=Y`.  This proves (78.8).

For completeness, integrating (80.12) over the size step `[Y-3,Y]`
and using `exp(-z)>=1-z` gives, for `T_h=F_h/F_(h-1)`,

```text
T_h(Y)/T_h(Y-3)
 >={Y(Y-3)-7(h-1)}/{(Y-3)^2}.                     (80.13)
```

The same proof applies on the full integration interval because its left
endpoint is `Y-3>=4s+9`, while the argument above only needs `4s<=Y` and
`a>=1`.  After `m=s+4+q_0`, `s=2h+c`, the difference between the right side of
(80.13) and the exact threshold required for (78.7),

```text
{16b^2-12b+5}/{8(b-1)(2b-1)},       b=m+h-1,
```

has a positive denominator and a numerator with 19 strictly positive
coefficients (minimum `16`).  Hence (80.13) proves (78.7).  Section 78's
1,900-coefficient curvature certificate and its ten direct top families
now prove (73.2) for every `0<=h<=floor(s/2)`.  Substitution into
(73.3)--(73.9) proves the strict unsigned Turan inequality

```text
G_(M-1,s)(t)^2-G_(M,s)(t)G_(M-2,s)(t)>0
for every t>0                                      (80.14)
```

throughout the forest cone.

The standalone proof is
`LOCAL_POINCARE_DEFICIT_BOUND_TP2_PROOF_2026-08-10.md`.  Its replay
`prove_local_poincare_deficit_bound.py` checks 16,848 adjacent-column TP2
minors, 990 conditional-`H` monotonicities, 1,386 shape sensitivities, and
198 complete derivative decompositions through layer 12.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_LOCAL_POINCARE_DEFICIT_BOUND_REPLAY` in
`local_poincare_deficit_bound_exact_20260810.json`.  The note, source, and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`9CDF499F07706F45F1BD02B1D2FB708AC244F430A4A88FF1BD03923C0E2A3038`,
`6B0AC76099F03852D4256EC5C1B70774D1D5AB3A4171542E957167FE95B9D07F`,
and
`507C491368768EB5312CA34B6B3CAC9A30DAEDC9D511DDE8D2D75EF8C407DE7E`.
An independent curvature-coordinate derivation and replay are in Section 8
of `local_poincare_subagent_notes_20260810.md` and
