## 81. The four-vertex path block is now in strict proper position

The conditional block lemma (79.5) has an all-order proof in the exact odd
path orbit.  Let

```text
chi_N(y)=det(yI-C_N)=U_N((y-2)/2),
n=2N+1,       m=N-s,       lambda=m+1.             (81.1)
```

Pad the codimension-two compression back to degree `N` as
`y^2chi_(N-2)(y)`.  Their common `m`th derivatives are

```text
chi_N^(m)=m!C_s^lambda(t),

{y^2chi_(N-2)}^(m)
 =m! sum_(j=0)^4 binom(4,j)C_(s-j)^lambda(t),
t=(y-2)/2.                                         (81.2)
```

The second identity follows directly from the Gegenbauer generating
function.  At a zero of `C_s^lambda`, the ratio of the second member to the
derivative of the first has the sign of

```text
Phi=4+6 C_(s-2)^lambda/C_(s-1)^lambda
       +4 C_(s-3)^lambda/C_(s-1)^lambda
       +  C_(s-4)^lambda/C_(s-1)^lambda.           (81.3)
```

Use the three-term recurrence to eliminate the three ratios.  The
denominator is

```text
(2lambda+s-4)(2lambda+s-3)(2lambda+s-2).
```

Under `n>=4s+9`, equivalently `lambda>=s+5`, substitute

```text
s=p+2,       lambda=p+h+7,       t=u-1.
```

The cleared numerator has 38 terms, all with strictly positive integer
coefficients in `p,h,u`; every Gegenbauer zero has `0<u<2`.  Hence the two
derivatives in (81.2) strictly alternate with a fixed direction.

Reciprocal reversal of the normalized derivatives has coefficients

```text
(-1)^i e_i(C)(s)_(falling i)
 /{(N)_(falling i)4^i}.                            (81.4)
```

The diagonal multiplier `(N)_(falling i)` is a strict finite multiplier
sequence because its degree-`s` symbol is the sign reversal of

```text
s!(-x)^sL_s^(N-s)(1/x),                            (81.5)
```

which has simple one-signed roots.  It maps (81.4) exactly to the two path
blocks.  Therefore

```text
B_(n,s) and B_(n-4,s) are in strict proper position
for every odd n>=4s+9.                             (81.6)
```

In particular every signed block pencil `B_(n,s)-cB_(n-4,s)` is
real-rooted.  This is stronger than the positive block compatibility that
Section 79 used conditionally.  One propagation issue must still be kept
explicit: applying the finite-convolution preserver with a *fixed* second
path gives directed mixed-slice interlacing, while the two diagonal slices
change both factors simultaneously.  Thus (81.6) is not yet, by itself, a
proof of the diagonal gamma signed pencil or of (62.4).

The proof is recorded in
`ODD_PATH_BLOCK_GEGENBAUER_PROOF_2026-08-10.md`.  The replay
`prove_odd_path_block_gegenbauer.py` checks the generic 38-term certificate,
88 derivative identities, 88 block transforms, 44 Laguerre symbols, and 44
strict directed interlacings.  It reports
`PASS_EXACT_ODD_PATH_BLOCK_GEGENBAUER_REPLAY` in
`odd_path_block_gegenbauer_exact_20260810.json`.  The note, source, and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`B8BBBFF08080E0311C92D9920DF4E2DAC0DB8B0B0919E5C8886502F1EEEEE8A0`,
`825FC685A666DD808FE760B32B5203143D6DB09A16BC8B122C41017AAC2F8529`,
and
`CED04FE246CC5EC0A38CAC8DC8EF73349F92F5879136730876E650134D65047B`.

## 82. A diagonal moving-root crossing closes the selector theorem

The signed diagonal pencil and the stronger oriented chain (62.4) are not
needed.  Put

```text
d=floor(s/2),
G_0=G_(N,s),       G_1=G_(N-1,s),       G_2=G_(N-2,s),
Gamma(t)=G_0(t)-2tG_1(t)+t^2G_2(t).                 (82.1)
```

Section 75 gives strict positive compatibility of the two adjacent pairs.
Sections 79 and 81 give the missing endpoint compatibility, but the direction
must be tracked through the two mixed slices.  With the notation

```text
X=B_n box R_n,       Y=B_(n-4) box R_(n-4),
K=B_(n-4) box R_n,   K*=B_n box R_(n-4),             (82.2)
```

fixed-factor preservation and reciprocal reflection give the strict bracket

```text
K* prec X prec K,       K* prec Y prec K.            (82.3)
```

Thus the first `s-1` roots of `K` form a common strict interlacer of the two
diagonal path slices.  Positive diagonal combinations are therefore simple
real-rooted; palindromicity and the gamma substitution make
`G_0+cG_2` simple negative-rooted for every `c>0`.  This is only positive
compatibility: the abstract signed diagonal pencil can fail, as the exact
counterexample in `BLOCK_TO_DIAGONAL_PROPAGATION_AUDIT_2026-08-10.md` shows.

All three pairs in `{G_0,G_1,G_2}` are now strictly positively compatible.
For each root-gap index, their three open gap intervals intersect pairwise.
Intervals on the real line have Helly number two, so they have a common strict
interlacer.  Consequently, for every `u>0`,

```text
Q_u(t)=G_0(t)+2uG_1(t)+u^2G_2(t)                    (82.4)
```

has `d` simple negative roots.  Order them continuously as
`lambda_1(u)<...<lambda_d(u)<0`.  Since

```text
u^(-2)Q_u -> G_2                                    (82.5)
```

coefficientwise without a degree drop, each root branch remains bounded and
converges to the corresponding root of `G_2`.  Hence
`lambda_i(0)<0` while `lambda_i(u)+u>0` for large `u`.  The intermediate value
theorem supplies a distinct `u_i>0` with `lambda_i(u_i)=-u_i`; distinctness
follows from simplicity of `Q_(u_i)`.  The exact diagonal identity

```text
Gamma(-u)=Q_u(-u)                                  (82.6)
```

therefore supplies `d` distinct negative roots of `Gamma`.

Strict preservation is not essential to this conclusion.  Starting only from
the proved pairwise positive compatibility, the three rows have a possibly
non-strict common interlacer.  Perturb their negative root sets inside the
common closed gap intervals to obtain strict common interlacers, apply the
crossing argument, and pass to the coefficientwise limit.  The selector roots
cannot escape to infinity because the leading coefficient has a positive
limit, and the `d` negative roots cannot converge to zero because
`Gamma(0)=G_0(0)>0`.  Hence the original selector has `d` negative roots
counted with multiplicity even without invoking a strict-preserver theorem.

Section 80 proves the strict Turan inequality needed by Section 67.  Its fixed
point gives one selector root in `(1,t_*)` and another in
`(t_*,infinity)`.  Since `deg Gamma=d+2`, these `d+2` real roots exhaust the
degree.  Therefore for every `s>=2` in the forest cone, `Gamma` has exactly
two roots in `(1,infinity)` and every other root is negative, counted with
multiplicity.  Strict preservation makes those roots simple, but simplicity
is not needed by the downstream window theorem.
The boundary layers are direct:

```text
Gamma_(N,0)=(1-t)^2,
Gamma_(N,1)=2(N-3)(t-1)(t-(N-1)/(N-3)).            (82.7)
```

This closes the selector obligation from Section 62 without proving (62.4).
The full argument is recorded in
`SELECTOR_DIAGONAL_CROSSING_THEOREM_2026-08-10.md`.  The independent replay
`prove_selector_diagonal_crossing.py` checks 26 exact common-interlacer and
selector cases through `s=14`, together with 78 positive pencils, and reports
`PASS_EXACT_SELECTOR_DIAGONAL_CROSSING_REPLAY` in
`selector_diagonal_crossing_exact_20260810.json`.

## 83. Every shifted lower row is a mixed derivative of one artificial row

The lower-layer shift in (565) has an exact differential interpretation.
Put

```text
r=N-d,             k=s-r>0,
P=d+s=N+k,         p_-=P-2k=N-k.
```

Let

```text
C_s(z)=sum_j binom(P,j)R_(N,d,s)(j(P-j))z^j
```

be the palindromic pre-window selector from (709), and homogenize it to
`C_s(x,y)`.  Define the artificial `alpha=0` row

```text
Qsharp_(N,d,s)(z)
 =sum_j binom(P,j)^2R_(N,d,s)(j(P-j))z^j.          (83.1)
```

If `D_s(x,y)` is the homogenization of

```text
sum_(h=0)^p_- binom(p_-,h)
 R_(N,d,s)(kN+h(p_--h))z^h,
```

then coefficient cancellation gives

```text
D_s(x,y)={p_-! over P!}(partial_x partial_y)^k C_s(x,y).  (83.2)
```

Indeed, at `j=k+h`,

```text
j(P-j)=kN+h(p_--h)
```

and

```text
binom(P,k+h)(k+h)_k(P-k-h)_k
 ={P! over p_-!}binom(p_-,h).                     (83.3)
```

The same cancellation after the outer binomial multiplier proves the
stronger identity needed for (565): its complete lower residual row is

```text
{p_-! over P!}(partial_x partial_y)^k
 Qsharp_(N,d,s)(x,y).                              (83.4)
```

Consequently one theorem would close every shifted lower layer at once:

> **Artificial-row theorem.**  For `r>=0`, `delta>=0`,
> `d=r+5+delta`, `N=d+r`, and `0<=s<=N+r`, the polynomial
> `Qsharp_(N,d,s)` is real stable.

When `s>=N`, the path slice has an endpoint support gap
`a=s-N+1`.  Equation (83.1) then has the exact homogeneous factor `(xy)^a`;
these forced zero roots are harmless.  After they are removed, exact and
high-precision audits find only simple negative roots.  In all 2,720 lower
layers with `5<=d<=20`, the artificial-row theorem holds, and (83.4) then
reproduces the already observed negative-rooted lower row.  This is finite
evidence, not the missing all-order proof.

In gamma coordinates a single mixed derivative acts by

```text
D_P f=t(4t-1)f''+{P-1-(4P-6)t}f'+P(P-1)f.          (83.5)
```

Thus the shifted gamma polynomial is the iterated
`D_P,D_(P-2),...,D_(P-2k+2)` image of the selector in Section 82.
Generic preservation is false: exact rational inputs with two roots above
one and the rest negative can acquire a conjugate pair under `D_P`.  Likewise
the natural three-term decomposition

```text
S_(P,0)[Gamma]=K_0-2tK_1+t^2K_2,
K_q=S_(P-2q,q)[G_(N-q,s)]                            (83.6)
```

does not have a common interlacer in general.  Hence (83.1), rather than a
generic derivative or second diagonal-crossing lemma, is the precise
path-specific frontier.

## 84. The raw selector is stable in every grade, but collapsed transport fails

Let `V=O union {a_1,a_2,b_1,b_2}`.  Section 99 proved the degree-four raw
two-pair selector stable.  For every `d>=4`, define

```text
Sigma_(M,d)
 =d!e_d(V)
  -(d-2)!a_1a_2e_(d-2)(V\{a_1,a_2})
  -(d-2)!b_1b_2e_(d-2)(V\{b_1,b_2})
  +(d-4)!a_1a_2b_1b_2e_(d-4)(O).                  (84.1)
```

There is an exact squarefree-product identity

```text
Sigma_(M,d)
 =(d-4)!{e_(d-4)(V) Sigma_(M,4)}_sf.               (84.2)
```

For a fixed `d`-set, summing the degree-four weights over its four-subsets
gives respectively

```text
d!,       d!-(d-2)!,       d!-2(d-2)!+(d-4)!       (84.3)
```

according as it completes zero, one, or two marked pairs.  This proves
(84.2).  Elementary symmetric polynomials and `Sigma_(M,4)` are stable, and
Sinclair's squarefree-product theorem therefore proves

```text
Sigma_(M,d) is real stable in every grade.          (84.4)
```

This is the complete raw fixed-grade selector, not a finite-order pattern.
The replay `prove_all_order_raw_two_pair_selector.py` checks 127,858 exact
subset coefficients and reports `PASS` in
`all_order_raw_two_pair_selector_exact_20260810.json`.

The direct collapsed-determinant transport is rigorously impossible.  The
unique selector forced after polarizing the exact constant-coefficient
contraction has diagonal Rayleigh difference

```text
x^(2d-8){A x^4-Bx^2t}{Bx^2-Ct},
A=(M)_d, B=(M)_(d-2), C=(M)_(d-4).                  (84.5)
```

At `x=1`, choose

```text
(M-d+2)(M-d+1)<t<(M-d+4)(M-d+3).
```

The two factors in (84.5) have opposite signs.  Thus the collapsed selector
is not stable for any `M>=d>=4`.  The exact no-go and 703 replay witnesses are
in `COLLAPSED_DETERMINANT_SELECTOR_NOGO_2026-08-10.md` and
`prove_collapsed_determinant_selector_nogo.py`.

The remaining shared-homogenizer theorem is therefore sharply located: one
must transport (84.2) through the **slot-preserving** path/Laguerre
ordered-partition normalization.  It cannot factor through the collapsed
spectral determinant.  A fixed-grade version of this transport may also
prove the artificial-row theorem (83.1).

## 85. The original affine bridge closes at `r=0` in even parity

The post-selector clean-room audit reduces the independent affine comparison
to the direct group and bottom coefficient families `(A_grp)` and `(A_bot)`.
Their first complete parameter slice now has an all-order proof.  At
`epsilon=0,r=0`, put `C=c-1>=0` and `M=m-3>=0`.  Exact bounded coefficient
extraction gives

```text
F_grp/binom(2m-4,m-2)
 =16(2M+3)(2M+5)P_grp(C,M,x)
   /prod_(j=2)^8(M+j),                               (85.1)

F_bot/binom(2m-5,m-2)
 =32(2M+3)(2M+5)P_bot(M,x)
   /prod_(j=2)^6(M+j).                               (85.2)
```

The polynomial `P_grp` has 76 monomials and every coefficient is a strictly
positive integer.  The bottom numerator is the explicit positive polynomial

```text
P_bot=
48M^5+1086M^4+9872M^3+44901M^2+101944M+92262
+2x(2M+7)(6M^3+85M^2+404M+642).                    (85.3)
```

All denominators and the central binomial factors in (85.1)--(85.2) are
positive, so both affine targets are strictly positive for every
`c>=1,m>=3,x>=0`.

The bounded extraction is exact.  In the group case only total kernel degrees
12--14 can reach the diagonal target; in the bottom case only degrees 14--15
can.  The discarded quadratic-in-`x` affine-projection pieces begin in degrees
16 and 17 respectively and therefore contribute zero.  The bottom surviving
slices are also coefficientwise positive, giving an independent direct proof
of (85.2).

The theorem and replay are in
`AFFINE_BRIDGE_R0_EVEN_THEOREM_2026-08-10.md` and
`prove_affine_bridge_r0_even.py`; the latter reports
`PASS_AFFINE_BRIDGE_R0_EVEN_ALL_PARAMETER`.  Odd parity and `r>=1` remain
open, but this is the first infinite parameter family of the independent
affine bridge to be closed rather than sampled.

## 86. The odd affine boundary closes, so all of `r=0` is proved

The same bounded-extraction method closes `epsilon=1`.  With
`C=c-1>=0`, `M=m-3>=0`, the group target has the exact form

```text
F_grp/binom(2m-3,m-2)
 =32(2M+5)(2M+7)P_odd(C,M,x)
   /prod_(j=3)^8(M+j),                               (86.1)
```

where `P_odd` has 35 monomials, arranged in seven `C^i x^j` blocks, and
every coefficient is a strictly positive integer.  The bottom target is

```text
F_bot/binom(2m-4,m-2)
 =64(2M+3)(2M+5)(2M+7)
   (3M^3+44M^2+216M+354)
   /prod_(j=2)^6(M+j).                               (86.2)
```

Every factor in (86.1)--(86.2) is positive on the full parameter range.
Only total kernel degrees at most 13 for the group and 14 for the bottom can
reach the target.  The discarded affine-projection pieces again begin in
degrees 16 and 17.  Moreover the sole reachable bottom degree-14 slice is
coefficientwise positive.

Thus `(A_grp)` and `(A_bot)` are proved for every `c>=1,m>=3,x>=0` at
`r=0` in **both** parities.  The theorem and exact replay are
`AFFINE_BRIDGE_R0_ODD_THEOREM_2026-08-10.md` and
`prove_affine_bridge_r0_odd.py`, which reports
`PASS_AFFINE_BRIDGE_R0_ODD_ALL_PARAMETER`.  The affine frontier is now solely
the extension from this complete boundary to `r>=1`.

## 87. Every remaining affine coefficient is one Newton difference

The `r>=1` affine frontier has an exact fixed-target normalization.  Put

```text
A=(1+z)(1+w),  S=z^2+w^2+zw(z+w),  W=A-1=z+w+zw.
```

After reciprocal reversal of the fixed bidegree kernels, both the group and
bottom coefficient packages have the form

```text
Phi_r(K)=[z^Nw^N]A^aS^bW^rK^vee,                  (87.1)
```

where the target `N` is independent of `r`.  Define

```text
H_K(n)=[z^Nw^N]A^(a+n)S^bK^vee,
G(n)=H_B(n)+n{H_P(n)-H_P(n-1)}.                    (87.2)
```

Since `W=A-1`, elementary finite-difference calculus gives the exact
all-order identity

```text
F_r=Phi_r(B)+rPhi_r(P)=Delta^rG(0).                (87.3)
```

Consequently the full affine bridge is equivalent to the single Newton
positivity statement `Delta^rG(0)>=0`.  Newton interpolation makes its
induction step

```text
G(r)>=sum_(s<r)binom(r,s)F_s.                       (87.4)
```

There is a local positive-convolution formulation.  If

```text
calF_r(i,j)=[z^iw^j]A^aS^bW^r(B^vee+rP^vee),
calR_r(i,j)=[z^iw^j]A^aS^bW^rP^vee>=0,
```

then multiplication by `W` gives

```text
calF_(r+1)(i,j)=calF_r(i-1,j)+calF_r(i,j-1)
                +calF_r(i-1,j-1)+calR_(r+1)(i,j).  (87.5)
```

By symmetry, the minimal boundary inequality needed at the central target is

```text
2calF_r(N-1,N)+calF_r(N-1,N-1)+calR_(r+1)(N,N)>=0. (87.6)
```

This explains why the proved central `r=0` coefficient cannot propagate by
itself: two shifted spatial predecessors are indispensable.  It also rules
out the most natural scalar Turan induction.  Already in the smallest even
group and bottom cases its step determinants are respectively
`-44,632,672,944` and `-15,984,785,008`, even though both adjacent bridge
values are positive.

Thus (87.6), or equivalently (87.4), is the precise remaining affine lemma;
generic central-ratio monotonicity is false.  The reduction and exact replay
are `AFFINE_BRIDGE_SINGLE_NEWTON_SEQUENCE_REDUCTION_2026-08-10.md` and
`verify_affine_bridge_single_newton_sequence.py`, whose report is
`affine_bridge_single_newton_sequence_reduction_20260810.json`.

## 88. The coupled binomial kernel is valid, but independent grade extraction is impossible

The extra coefficient multiplier in the artificial row is one strict finite
multiplier sequence.  For `0<=j<=P`, put `lambda_j=binom(P,j)`.  Its finite
symbol is

```text
J_P(z)=sum_j binom(P,j)^2z^j
      =(1-z)^P Legendre_P((1+z)/(1-z)),              (88.1)
```

so every symbol root is simple and negative.  Hence on the symmetric
multiaffine shared-slot subspace

```text
sum_j c_j e_j(u_1,...,u_P)
 -> sum_j binom(P,j)c_j e_j(u_1,...,u_P)             (88.2)
```

preserves stability, by diagonal finite Polya--Schur followed by
polarization.  This is the correct single contraction; splitting it into
the two reciprocal-factorial pieces is invalid on the intermediate rows.
The operator (88.2) also fails on arbitrary nonsymmetric multiaffine inputs,
so exchangeability is essential.

There is a decisive restriction on any proposed ordered-partition parent.
Suppose a jointly stable, `S_P`-symmetric parent `F(v,u_1,...,u_P)` used an
ordinary independent variable `v` to extract path grade `s`, while (88.2)
acted only on the shared slots.  Then the two operations commute:

```text
[v^s] B_P F = B_P [v^s]F.                            (88.3)
```

The operator `B_P` is invertible on the symmetric subspace.  If the left
side diagonalized to `Qsharp`, the right side would force `[v^s]F` to be the
polarization of the pre-binomial row `C_s`.  But `C_s` is nonstable: each
positive selector gamma root `rho>=1` contributes

```text
rho z^2+(2rho-1)z+rho,
```

whose discriminant is `1-4rho<0`.  Since coefficient extraction from a
stable parent preserves stability, this is a contradiction.  The first
exact witness is `(N,d,s)=(5,5,1)`, where

```text
Gamma=4(t-1)(t-2),
C_s=4(z+1)^2(z^2+z+1)(2z^2+3z+2),                  (88.4)
```

while `Qsharp` has six negative roots.

Therefore a successful parent cannot use an independent fixed-grade
projection.  It must couple the path grade to the shared-slot allocation so
that the contraction and extraction genuinely do not commute.  The proofs
and exact replays are `QSHARP_COUPLED_BINOMIAL_KERNEL_2026-08-10.md`,
`prove_qsharp_coupled_binomial_kernel.py`,
`QSHARP_INDEPENDENT_GRADE_PARENT_NOGO_2026-08-10.md`, and
`prove_qsharp_independent_grade_parent_nogo.py`.

## 89. The lower selector reduces to a coherent adjacent pencil

The selector itself admits an extension beyond the old forest reserve.  With
`G_q=G_(N-q,s)` and `a=max(0,s-N+1)`, formula (74.6) shows that every
coefficient of `G_(M,s)` is nonnegative, nondecreasing, and convex in the
size exponent `R=2M-s-1` throughout `0<=s<=2N-5`.  Therefore

```text
Gamma=(G_0-2G_1+G_2)+2(1-t)(G_1-G_2)+(1-t)^2G_2
```

is strictly positive on `0<=t<1`, and at `t=1` for `s>=2`.  Thus any
positive selector roots in the lower range remain strictly above one.  This
unit-interval exclusion is now all-order.

Endpoint compatibility of `G_0,G_2` is false in this range, but it is
stronger than the moving-root proof needs.  Define

```text
F_u=G_0+uG_1,       H_u=G_1+uG_2,
Q_u=F_u+uH_u=G_0+2uG_1+u^2G_2.                    (89.1)
```

Both adjacent pencils are negative-rooted by Section 75.  Their remaining
proper-position condition is the one quadratic Wronskian

```text
W_u=W_01+uW_02+u^2W_12,
W_ij=W(G_i,G_j).                                    (89.2)
```

A sharp sufficient scalar inequality is

```text
W_02(t)^2-4W_01(t)W_12(t)<0                        (89.3)
```

for every real nonzero `t`, after removing the forced even power.  Conditional
on (89.3), the Section 82 moving crossings supply every negative selector
root after stripping `t^a`.  Degree exhaustion then needs only the all-range
Turan inequality

```text
G_1(t)^2-G_0(t)G_2(t)>0,       t>0.                (89.4)
```

In fact (89.4) is not a second independent obligation.  Put

```text
T=G_1^2-G_0G_2,       S=(G_1')^2-G_0'G_2'.          (89.5)
```

Direct expansion gives the exact identity

```text
W_02^2-4W_01W_12=(T')^2-4TS.                        (89.6)
```

If the left side is negative on `t>0`, then `T` cannot vanish there: at a
zero of `T` the right side becomes `(T')^2>=0`.  The first nonzero
coefficient of `T` is positive after removing the forced power, so continuity
gives (89.4) automatically.  Thus (89.3) is the single remaining
lower-selector theorem.

There is strong additional structure.  Every exact case through `N=20` has

```text
W_02^2-4W_01W_12=-t^e D_(N,s)(t),                   (89.7)
```

with every coefficient of `D_(N,s)` strictly positive.  A tempting attempted
proof through the derivative Turan coefficients is false: the inequality

```text
[t^(n-2)]S >= floor(n^2/4)[t^n]T                   (false) (89.8)
```

already fails at `(N,s,n)=(9,5,2)`, where the two sides are respectively
`2,074,240` and `2,075,872`.  A broader replay finds 445 failed coefficients
in 188 parameter cells through `N=20`.  Thus the exact identity (89.6) and
the coefficientwise sign pattern (89.7) survive, but the proposed termwise
derivative domination does not.  Moreover, coefficientwise positivity of
`-D` proves the desired sign only on the positive axis; excluding negative
real zeros still needs an all-order Gram/SOS, Sturm, or proper-position
argument.  The finite Sturm replay supplies evidence, not that theorem.

The corrected replay reveals a stronger viable target.  After stripping the
forced power, the core `D_(N,s)` is strictly Hurwitz stable in every one of
the 288 exact rational Routh tests through `N=20`.  Equivalently, if

```text
D_(N,s)(t)=E(t^2)+tO(t^2),                            (89.9)
```

the Hermite--Biehler target is directed strict alternation of `E(-x)` and
`xO(-x)`.  This would give the full real-axis sign required by (89.3).  The
active-weight identity (77.1) factors the coefficient kernel as a binomial
TP kernel times a second connection matrix; exact minors of that connection
matrix suggest that a compound TP/Hurwitz-matrix factorization is the right
proof.  Hurwitz stability and those minors remain finite evidence until that
factorization is supplied.

The first six nontrivial grades are now proved all-order.  For every

```text
2<=s<=7,       s<=2N-6,
```

the Lagrange/continuant formula becomes symbolic in `N`; after the shift
`R=X+s+3`, every coefficient of the core `-(W_02^2-4W_01W_12)` and every
leading Hurwitz minor is a polynomial with strictly positive coefficients
in `X`.  Exact support-boundary checks cover the harmless degeneracies.
Therefore the core is strictly Hurwitz stable, (89.3) holds on the whole
real axis, and the coherent adjacent-pencil argument closes these grades in
all orders.  The 153-cell Sturm check through `N=30` is replay only, not the
proof.  The theorem, replay, and report are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_COHERENT_PENCIL_LOW_GRADE_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md`,
`prove_lower_selector_coherent_pencil_low_grade.py`, and
`lower_selector_coherent_pencil_low_grade_exact_20260813.json`, with current
SHA-256 hashes
`2B956DC90503F8EAA2B91761199CC882ABA4C3AA55310387A1E95D9660301A5A`,
`2F034E299196CF5CBE0C1FA7C831FD73FB7B8BD8DAEDE0FD35026605E2B0692F`,
and
`E8BEC78BC9BCCA04560814EF6140CA54D00FD20C6942F1A13A947BF8D95F162E`.
The uniform theorem for unbounded `s` remains open.

Exact replay certifies (89.3), coefficientwise (89.4), and the resulting
selector pattern in 144 cases.  At the terminal family `s=2N-5`, `G_2=0` and the core is
an explicit quadratic with one negative and one positive root; this boundary
is handled separately.  The reduction and replay are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_COHERENT_PENCIL_REDUCTION_2026-08-10.md` and
`verify_lower_selector_coherent_pencil.py`.  The independent Gram/discriminant
audit is `LOWER_SELECTOR_WRONSKIAN_GRAM_REDUCTION_2026-08-10.md` with replay
`verify_lower_selector_wronskian_gram_reduction.py`; it covers 288 cases
through `N=20`, records 3,280 negative discriminant coefficients, and also
records all 445 failures of the false bound (89.8).

## 90. The minimal artificial ceiling is the raw degree-four allocation

The artificial row has a distinguished base ceiling `p=s+4`.  If
`a_(M,i)=binom(2M-i-1,i)` and `Qsharp_(p,0)` denotes the alpha-zero binary
row, then its coefficients satisfy

```text
[z^j]Qsharp_(p,0)
 =binom(p,j) sum_(q=0)^2 r_q sum_i
    a_(N-q,i)a_(N-q,s-i)
    binom(4-2q,j-q-i)/(4-2q)!,                       (90.1)

(r_0,r_1,r_2)=(24,-4,1).
```

After the displayed factorials are absorbed, the three selector weights are
exactly `(1,-2,1)`.  Before normalization, `(24,-4,1)` are the raw
degree-four selector weights: unrestricted weight `24`, the two completed
marked-pair corrections `-2-2`, and the both-pairs correction `+1`.  Thus
the minimal ceiling is the inverse-factorial path allocation of the stable
raw degree-four selector from Section 84.

Identity (90.1) does not by itself transport stability.  In the upper grades,
`Qsharp_(s+4,0)` is a mixed derivative of the unresolved fixed-grade
conditioning of that raw selector.  In the lower grades the derivative
direction reverses: the actual lower row is a mixed derivative of `Qsharp`,
so derivative closure cannot prove the base.  A proof must preserve the
correlated path-grade/slot allocation in (90.1); Section 88 rules out an
independent grade variable.

There is also an exact ceiling-raising identity.  If `F_p` is the homogeneous
binary form, then

```text
F_(p+1)=(p+1)(I_x+I_y)F_p,                           (90.2)
```

where `I_x,I_y` are zero-constant antiderivatives.  Generic stability
preservation is false even at `p=2 deg W`: starting from
`W=(t+1)(t+2)` at `p=4`, (90.2) produces a degree-five binary form with a
nonreal conjugate pair.  The observed strict interlacing of the path rows
therefore requires a path-specific theorem, equivalently total positivity of
the full Athanasiadis--Wagner laced matrix, not merely adjacent ordinary
coefficient minors.

The exact audit verifies 112 raw-selector identities, 104 minimal-ceiling
root cells, 672 raising identities, 3,324 strict adjacent window minors, and
10,960 nonnegative contiguous minors through order five.  The artifacts are
`QSHARP_MINIMAL_CEILING_AND_RAISING_REDUCTION_2026-08-10.md` and
`analyze_qsharp_minimal_ceiling_raising.py`; the finite checks are evidence
for the two remaining statements, not an all-order proof.

## 91. The affine boundary triple requires compensated planar layers

Section 87 leaves the two shifted predecessors

```text
U_r=calF_r(N-1,N),       Z_r=calF_r(N-1,N-1).        (91.1)
```

All eight `r=0` cases (group/bottom, both parities, both shifts) are now
proved for every allowed parameter.  After the standard substitution
`m=M+3`, each normalized numerator is coefficientwise positive; the eight
certificates contain respectively 174, 250, 100, 156, 27, 38, 17, and 27
positive terms.

For later orders split `V^r=(t+z+w)^r` by the number `j` of constant steps.
With `L=m+5` and

```text
H_r(z,w)=A(z,w)^a T(z,w)^b {B(z,w)+rP(z,w)},         (91.2)
```

define, for `0<=j<=r` and `eta in {0,1}`,

```text
L_(r,j,eta)
 =sum_(k=0)^(r-j) binom(r-j,k)
   [z^(L+j+1+k) w^(L+r+eta-k)] H_r.                 (91.3)
```

Then the two predecessors reassemble exactly as

```text
U_r=sum_(j=0)^r binom(r,j)L_(r,j,0),
Z_r=sum_(j=0)^r binom(r,j)L_(r,j,1).                (91.4)
```

The initially proposed all-order planar-layer statement

```text
L_(r,j,eta)>=0                                      (91.5)
```

would imply `U_r,Z_r>=0`, hence the minimal central inequality (87.6) and the
affine bridge.  It is false.  In the bottom package with

```text
m=120, x=240, r=80,
```

the terminal diagonal layer is negative in both parities.  In the paired
notation

```text
D_(r,h)=[z^(L+r+1)w^(L+r+1)](z+w)^h H_r,
```

one has `D_(80,0)<0`; the exact debt/payment ratios are approximately
`1.002850486695209` in even parity and `1.0029121593943036` in odd parity.
Thus (91.5), positivity of every `D_(r,h)`, and any proof that pays each
homogenizer layer separately are all ruled out.

This does **not** refute either reassembled shifted predecessor or the affine
bridge.  The pairing identity is

```text
2U_r+Z_r=sum_(h=0)^(r+1) binom(r+1,h)D_(r,h).       (91.6)
```

The exact remaining target is therefore the compensated sum in (91.6), or
more minimally that sum plus `calR_(r+1)(N,N)` as in (87.6).  A negative
terminal layer may be absorbed by the preceding positive layers.  Earlier
finite checks through `r=50` did not reach the first obstruction and are no
longer evidence for termwise positivity; they remain evidence only for the
reassembled predecessors over their stated range.

The all-parameter boundary proof is
`AFFINE_BRIDGE_R0_SHIFTED_PREDECESSOR_THEOREM_2026-08-10.md` with replay
`prove_affine_bridge_r0_shifted_predecessors.py`.  The layer reduction and
finite probes are `AFFINE_BRIDGE_SHIFTED_PREDECESSOR_LAYER_REDUCTION_2026-08-10.md`,
`probe_affine_bridge_boundary_layer_cone.py`, and the associated exact JSON
reports.  The exact counterexample and three-way replay are
`AFFINE_BRIDGE_PLANAR_LAYER_COUNTEREXAMPLE_2026-08-10.md` and
`verify_affine_bridge_planar_layer_counterexample.py`.

## 92. Euler transfer leaves a growing compensated prefix

Write the affine kernels in the common form

```text
B=VQ+sR,       P=sR,       X=A^a T^b,
E=z partial_z+w partial_w,  D=m+k+5.
```

Coefficient adjunction gives the exact all-order identity

```text
[z^D w^D] X V^k(B+kP)
 =[z^D w^D] V^(k+1){XQ+(2D-E)(XR)}.                 (92.1)
```

If the right side is split by homogeneous powers of `z+w`, its layers have
the simple form

```text
e_h=q_h+h rho_h,
[z^D w^D] X V^k(B+kP)=sum_h binom(k+1,h)e_h.       (92.2)
```

Neither a coefficientwise cone for the transferred kernel nor a fixed-size
initial compensation block is valid.  The coefficientwise cone fails in all
16 hard audit records.  On the bottom ray `(m,x)=(120,240)`, the negative
layers at `k=81` form an initial interval through `h=18`, and the least
nonnegative retained prefix ends at `h=22`; by `k=100` the negative interval
extends through `h=27` and the least nonnegative prefix through `h=31`.

Nevertheless the fully weighted expression at the first termwise obstruction
`(m,x,r)=(120,240,80)` is a positive 275-digit integer in each parity, and it
remains positive after the next reserve term is added.  Thus the surviving
affine target is a single-crossing/growing-weighted-prefix theorem for (92.2),
not termwise layer positivity.  The exact derivation and replay are
`AFFINE_BRIDGE_EULER_TRANSFER_AND_COMPENSATED_BLOCK_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md`
and `verify_affine_bridge_euler_transfer_identity.py`; the large
ray values are in `affine_bridge_reassembled_large_ray_exact_20260811.json`.

## 93. The lower Duran second margin is strict in all orders

Let the original selector have ambient ceiling `P=d+s`, degree `M`, and
`n=floor(P/2)`.  At the Duran comparison point
`L=n-M+beta+1`, the second margin has the exact central-coefficient form

```text
D_P[Gamma](L)
 = n!/{4^n (beta+1)_(n-M)} [t^n]S_(P,0)[Gamma].     (93.1)
```

Forced zero removal is harmless and shifts the ambient parameter exactly:

```text
D_P[t^a Gammahat](L)=(P)_a/4^a D_(P-a)[Gammahat](L). (93.2)
```

For the path selector, expand the binary central coefficient allocation by
allocation.  If `X_0,X_1,X_2` are the three contributions corresponding to
`G_N,-2tG_(N-1),t^2G_(N-2)`, then

```text
X_1/X_0 <= binom(d-2,l-1)/binom(d,l)
         =l(d-l)/{d(d-1)} <= d/{4(d-1)} <=5/16.
```

Consequently every allocation contributes

```text
X_0-2X_1+X_2 >= 3X_0/8+X_2,
```

and central support is nonempty, so (93.1) is strictly positive.  This proves
the corrected lower-layer second exceptional-root margin in all orders.  The
proof and exact replay are `LOWER_SELECTOR_DURAN_SECOND_MARGIN_THEOREM_2026-08-12.md`
and `prove_lower_selector_duran_second_margin.py`; the replay checks 1,248
lower cells and 594,685 exact allocations.  The first Duran margin remains
open.

## 94. Positive endpoint randomization reduces the lower selector to two leaves

Put

```text
A_r=P_N+rP_(N-1),       B_r=P_(N-1)+rP_(N-2),
K_r=J_(A_r,s)+u J_(B_r,s).
```

Here `B_r` is the opposite-endpoint principal minor of the positive endpoint
path pencil `A_r`.  Mixed-slice proper position gives a common interlacer for
the two diagonal slices, hence each `K_r` is negative-rooted.  For the boundary
two-point law `R in {u,-1}` with probabilities `1/(u+1),u/(u+1)`, the coherent
quadratic selector is exactly

```text
Q_(N,s,u)={K_u+uK_(-1)}/{u+1}.                      (94.1)
```

Thus the remaining lower coherent-pencil theorem is reduced to compatibility
of two individually proved path polynomials `K_u,K_(-1)`.  Exact pencils pass
through `N<=35` over a seven-decade grid in `u` and the mixture weight, but the
all-order compatibility proof is still missing.  The reduction and replay are
`CORRELATED_ENDPOINT_POSITIVE_RANDOMIZATION_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md` and
`verify_correlated_endpoint_positive_randomization.py`.

## 95. The first lower Duran margin is path-specific

The remaining first margin is proved on the complete first lower-row family.
There `r=0`, `s=1`, and

```text
Gamma=2(d-1)-4(d-2)t+2(d-3)t^2,
G_2=d(d+1)(d-3)/{16(d-1)},
base=(d-1)(d-2)/4.
```

Writing `d=5+c`, the cleared margin is

```text
(3c^3+31c^2+108c+132)/{16(c+4)}>0.                 (95.1)
```

The isolated terminal degree-two cell is also direct.  However, the same
corrected Duran normalization together with the abstract root pattern (two
positive selector roots and all remaining roots negative) is insufficient.
At `(p,alpha,m,n,beta)=(9,0,4,4,1/2)`, the exact polynomial

```text
z^4+(4953/2)z^3+447527z^2-1121286z+1181250
```

has only the two real roots isolated in `(-2281,-2280)` and `(-199,-198)`;
the residual conjugate-pair product violates the first target.  Hence a proof
of the remaining margin must use the path allocation, not just the normalized
degree and sign pattern.  The exact proof/obstruction and Sturm replay are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_DURAN_FIRST_MARGIN_BOUNDARY_AND_OBSTRUCTION_2026-08-12.md`
and `verify_lower_selector_duran_first_margin_obstruction.py`.

## 96. The first lower Duran margin closes through row three

For lower selector depths `s=2,3`, the corrected Duran polynomial is cubic.
Let its leading coefficient be positive, its constant coefficient positive,
and choose one negative root `-b`.  Put

```text
A=(s_D-1)(s_D+beta-1),   C=Q_D(0)/LC(Q_D),   T=C/A.
```

The following root-free criterion is sufficient:

```text
Q_D(-T)>0.                                             (96.1)
```

Indeed `Q_D(-infinity)<0`, so (96.1) forces `b>T`; Vieta then gives the
residual product `G_2=C/b<A`, which is precisely the first margin.

Substitute `d=2k+5` and `d=2k+6` for the two parities.  For each `s=2,3`
and every allowed `0<=r<s`, the numerator and denominator of (96.1) have
strictly positive coefficients in `k`.  This gives ten all-order symbolic
families, covering every parity and lower-offset case at depths two and
three.  Together with Section 95 and the second-margin theorem, both Duran
margins are therefore closed for every lower row of depth `1,2,3`.

The proof and exact replay are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_DURAN_M1_CUBIC_ROWS_THEOREM_2026-08-12.md` and
`derive_lower_selector_m1_cubic_rows.py`.  The replay verifies all ten
symbolic families plus 60 independent integer transcription cells.  The
unbounded-depth first-margin theorem remains open.

## 97. Strengthened affine scalar target

In the Euler notation of Section 92 define the binomial transforms

```text
R_k=sum_h binom(k+1,h)rho_h,
E_k=sum_h binom(k+1,h)e_h=F_k.
```

Exact auditing reveals the stronger candidate

```text
F_k>=2R_k.                                           (97.1)
```

Across 208 exact package/parity/parameter records through `k=20`, (97.1)
has no failure; the smallest ratio is

```text
F_k/R_k=348354423/161671196 =2.154709...,
```

at the even bottom cell `(m,x,k)=(3,0,3)`.  The large bottom ray
`(m,x)=(120,240)` also passes through `k=100`, with minimum ratios above
`2.52` in both parities.  In the same audit, the weighted reserve polynomial
has only negative roots, the weighted `e` polynomial interlaces it and has
exactly one positive root below one, and the raw `e_h` sequence has a single
sign crossing with its Newton inequalities intact.

Writing

```text
R(y)=sum_h binom(k+1,h)rho_h y^h,
Q(y)=sum_h binom(k+1,h)q_h y^h,
```

equation (92.2) becomes `F_k=Q(1)+R'(1)`, so (97.1) is the scalar
log-derivative bound

```text
Q(1)/R(1)+R'(1)/R(1)>=2.                            (97.2)
```

This is presently finite evidence, not a proof, but (97.2) is substantially
smaller than the original signed coefficient cone and is the active affine
target.  The exact lattice artifact is
`probe_affine_bridge_weighted_ratio_lattice.py` with report
`affine_bridge_weighted_ratio_lattice_exact_20260812.json`.

A still more concrete sufficient lemma emerges directly at the layer level.
Let

```text
t=max{h:e_h<0},       j(h)=2t+2-h.
```

If the negative layers form the initial interval `0<=h<=t`, if `2t+2<=n`,
and

```text
binom(n,h)e_h+binom(n,j(h))e_(j(h))>=0              (97.3)
```

for every `h<=t`, then the reflection `h -> j(h)` is injective and pays the
entire negative block with disjoint positive layers; all unpaired layers are
nonnegative, so `F_k>=0`.  This offset-two pairing passes 1,867 exact hard
pairs through `k=50` and another 1,660 pairs on the 208-record lattice, with
no failure.  The nearer offset-one reflection fails 63 of the hard pairs,
which isolates the precise two-step reserve in (97.3).  This rigorous
reduction is `AFFINE_BRIDGE_REFLECTION_PAIR_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md`;
the all-order sign-crossing and pair inequality (97.3) remain to prove.

The reflection payments themselves have a local ratio reduction.  With
`g_h=e_h/rho_h`, `bar_rho_h=binom(n,h)rho_h`, `h=t-ell`, and
`j=t+2+ell`, the outward payment ratio factors as

```text
(bar_rho_(j+1)bar_rho_h)/(bar_rho_j bar_rho_(h-1))
 *(g_(j+1)/g_j)*((-g_h)/(-g_(h-1))).                (97.4)
```

All 1,225 hard-record steps and 213 lattice steps satisfy the split lower
bounds `2` and `1/2`, respectively.  The second factor splits once more:
`g_(j+1)/g_j>=1` is the positive-side determinant orientation, while the
only new negative-side estimate is

```text
(-g_h)/(-g_(h-1)) >= 1/2,
0 <= Delta_(h-1) <= -e_(h-1)rho_h/2,   1<=h<t.      (97.5)
```

The interior scope in (97.5) is essential: extending it to `h=t` fails 297
of 300 audited hard cases.  On the required range it follows from a cleaner
claim, discrete convexity `Delta^2 g_h>=0` throughout the negative block.
Indeed `g_(h+1)<0` and convexity give
`2g_h<=g_(h-1)+g_(h+1)<g_(h-1)`.  The exact curvature numerator has a
three-copy symmetrization into pairwise kernels

```text
(Q_iR_j-Q_jR_i)R_k(s_i-s_j)(s_i s_j-s_k^2),        (97.6)
```

so this is a concrete TP3/path-kernel target rather than an unexplained
numerical inequality.

The reserve factor `S_l=R_(j+1)R_h` is nondecreasing in the reflected
distance `l` exactly when the paired ratio curvatures satisfy
`K_(h-1)>=K_(j+1)`, where `K_r=R_r/R_(r+1)`; global monotonicity of `K` is
false, so the reflected positions and sign crossing are essential.  Thus,
after the two central payments, every farther reflected payment is no worse
than its predecessor.  The affine theorem is reduced to the sign crossing,
the two central pairs, the paired reserve-curvature inequality, and the local
determinant sandwich (97.5).

The two central payments reduce to reserve comparisons alone once this same
convexity is available:

```text
bar_rho_(t+2)>=bar_rho_t,
bar_rho_(t+3)>=bar_rho_(t-1).                       (97.7)
```

The complete local package—1,225 interior sandwiches, 272 central reserve
factors `S_1>=2`, 953 reflected curvature comparisons, and both central
comparisons—passes the exact hard replay.  Generic PF-infinity sequences
violate both the reflected curvature and `S_1>=2`, so (97.6)--(97.7) must use
the normalized path/Hahn representation, not real-rootedness alone.

The reserve curvature has an exact binomial/path split.  If
`r_h=rho_h/rho_(h-1)` and `R_h=bar_rho_h/bar_rho_(h-1)`, then

```text
R_h={(n-h+1)/h}r_h,
K_h=R_h/R_(h+1)=B_h kappa_h,
B_h={(h+1)(n-h+1)}/{h(n-h)},
kappa_h=rho_h^2/{rho_(h-1)rho_(h+1)}.              (97.8)
```

At the reflected indices, the pure binomial quotient has the required
orientation whenever `n>2t+2`; the remaining raw-curvature loss is small but
not sign-definite.  The latest 208-record exact lattice has the stronger
endpoint slack `n>=2t+4` in all 1,011 negative-block sequences.  Equivalently,
one only needs positivity of the single central layer
`e_(floor(n/2)-1)`.  This isolates a quantitative path-specific bound on
`kappa`, rather than generic log concavity.

## 98. A fixed mixed-slice candidate for every conditional endpoint

Let `P=P_N`, `C=P_(N-1)`, `D=P_(N-2)`, and let `J_s(A,B)` denote the gamma
polynomial of the symmetrized mixed slice.  For fixed `u>0`, set

```text
U=J_s(P,P)+uJ_s(C,C),
X=J_s(P,C)+uJ_s(C,D),
Y=J_s(C,C)+uJ_s(D,D),
K_r=U+2rX+r^2Y.                                    (98.1)
```

Section 94 gives the target as `(K_u+uK_(-1))/(u+1)`.  The sharper candidate
is now one fixed polynomial: prove that

```text
X+cK_r is negative-rooted for every r>=-1,c>=0.     (98.2)
```

Then `X` is a common interlacer for both conditional endpoints and the lower
coherent selector follows immediately.

There is a second, more structured candidate at the singular endpoint.  The
path continuant obeys

```text
P_M=(1+2v)P_(M-1)-v^2P_(M-2),
P_M-P_(M-1)=v S_(M-1),
S_M=2P_M-vP_(M-1).                                  (98.2a)
```

Moreover `S_M/2` is exactly the determinant of the same tridiagonal path
with one endpoint diagonal changed from `2` to `3/2`; consequently
`(S_M,S_(M-1))` is again a nested positive Jacobi chain.  Put

```text
A=P_N+uP_(N-1),  B=P_(N-1)+uP_(N-2),
A_-=P_N-P_(N-1), B_-=P_(N-1)-P_(N-2),
C=J_s(A,A_-)+uJ_s(B,B_-).                           (98.2b)
```

Exact testing through `N<=15`, all layers, and five decades of `u` finds
that this aligned cross slice is positively compatible with both `K_u` and
`K_(-1)` in all 715 cases.  Also

```text
K_(-1)=t{J_(s-2)(S_(N-1),S_(N-1))
             +uJ_(s-2)(S_(N-2),S_(N-2))}.          (98.2c)
```

Thus the endpoint theorem may reduce to an aligned direct-sum mixed-slice
lemma for two nested Jacobi chains, substantially more structured than the
fixed candidate (98.2).  This remains finite evidence pending that lemma.

For arbitrary endpoints `a,b>=-1`, define

```text
B(a,b)=J_s(A_a,A_b)+uJ_s(B_a,B_b),
A_r=P_N+rP_(N-1),  B_r=P_(N-1)+rP_(N-2).           (98.2d)
```

The natural cross candidate is `B(u,-1)`, and both positive pencils with the
endpoint self-slices reduce algebraically to members of (98.2d).  Hence it
would suffice to prove `B(a,b)` negative-rooted throughout the endpoint
square.  The raw bilinear coefficient matrix in the basis
`(P_N,P_(N-1),P_(N-2))` is

```text
[1   b       0]
[a   ab+u   ub]
[0   ua     uab],                                   (98.2e)
```

of rank two.  Its symmetrization has determinant
`-u(a-b)^2(ab+u)/4`; thus it is generally indefinite.  This rules out a
simple PSD Gram/ordinary Hermitian expected-characteristic-polynomial proof,
but leaves the aligned apolar/common-gap lemma intact.

Before gamma symmetrization define

```text
R=F_s(P,C)+uF_s(C,D),       R*=z^sR(1/z),
L_r=F_s(P+rC,P+rC)+uF_s(C+rD,C+rD).                 (98.3)
```

The two nested Jacobi pairs `C proper-position P` and
`D proper-position C` make each summand in (98.3) a mixed-slice
interlacing construction.  However, the tempting directed direct-sum
strengthening is false.  At `(N,s,r,u)=(5,2,-1,1)`,

```text
R=31z^2+72z+13,       L_(-1)=8z,
W(R,L_(-1))=8(31z^2-13),
```

so the raw Wronskian changes sign; the two component cross-Wronskians change
sign as well.  The surviving obligation is therefore positive compatibility
of the gamma polynomials in (98.2), equivalently the common-gap/Bezoutian
condition, not globally oriented raw proper position.

The exact replay checks 7,245 identities and 5,796 certified common-gap
instances through `N=25`, all lower grades, three decades in `u`, and four
endpoint parameters, with forced zero roots retained.  No failure occurs.
The reduction and replay are
`CONDITIONAL_ENDPOINT_COMMON_INTERLACER_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md` and
`verify_conditional_endpoint_common_interlacer_reduction.py`.  The finite
audit is evidence; the all-order gamma common-gap theorem remains open.

The common-gap condition has boundary collisions, so a collision-free
orientation homotopy is unavailable.  In the first degree-one family the
resultant already vanishes at two endpoint parameters inside `r>=-1`.
Nevertheless, after `q=r+1`, many low-degree discriminants of

```text
Z(t)=X(t)+cK_r(t)
```

has strictly positive coefficients in `(c,q,u)`: this holds symbolically for
the core-degree two and three cells checked, and for several representative
degree-four and degree-five cells (hundreds of positive terms each).  It is
not uniform even in low core degree: the exact cell `(N,s)=(12,13)` has a
negative discriminant coefficient after two forced zeros are stripped.  Positive
discriminant plus a real-rooted base point and a fixed positive leading
coefficient gives a rigorous connected-domain homotopy proof in each such
symbolic family.  The emerging all-order target is therefore coefficientwise
positivity of the discriminant, or equivalently the required principal
Hermite subdiscriminants, rather than a raw Wronskian sign.  The route note is
`CONDITIONAL_ENDPOINT_DISCRIMINANT_POSITIVITY_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md`.
Thus coefficientwise discriminant positivity is only a partial-cell
certificate, not the uniform factorization; a weaker positivity-on-the-cone
or Hermite-minor theorem is still needed.

The first coefficientwise obstruction is nevertheless pointwise positive on
the full cone.  Its 339-term discriminant has exactly two negative monomials,
at exponents `(6,11,5)` and `(5,10,5)` in `(c,q,u)`.  One shared positive
monomial at `(6,12,4)`, split in half, and positive partners at `(6,10,6)`
and `(4,8,6)` pay them by weighted AM--GM; both squared coefficient margins
are strictly positive.  Thus the fixed cell `(12,13)` also satisfies the
discriminant theorem.  This suggests an all-order sparse AM--GM matching of
the exceptional negative monomials, weaker than coefficientwise positivity.
The corrected verifier now exhausts all 112 core-degree-two-through-five
cells with `N<=15`: 100 cells have coefficientwise-positive discriminant
(18,738 positive coefficients), and all 12 exceptional cells are repaired
by the same two-pair AM--GM template.  Degree four uses the support points
`(6,12,4)`, `(6,10,6)`, and `(4,8,6)`; degree five uses their shifted
counterparts `(8,16,6)`, `(8,14,8)`, and `(6,12,8)`.  In each exceptional
cell the two negative coefficients occur in ratio `2:1`, and the exact
squared AM--GM margins are positive.  This is a rigorous finite-family
theorem, not yet an all-order proof.

Beyond the committed replay, the two lower-tail degree-four cells for every
`12<=N<=40` admit exact dyadic midpoint allocations: every negative support
point is the midpoint of two positive support points, all AM--GM inequalities
hold exactly, and no positive coefficient is overallocated.  The number of
negative monomials grows, so the uniform target is a support-matching/SONC
certificate rather than a fixed two-term repair.

The all-`N` symbolic proof for the persistent first pair in the two
degree-four tail families has now survived a transcription correction.  An
audit initially found three quartic-discriminant terms with the leading and
constant exponents reversed; after correcting them, the full sparse builder
agrees coefficient-by-coefficient with direct SymPy discriminants at
`N=12,13,40`, and the five critical coefficients and both AM--GM ratios are
unchanged.  Hence the two persistent midpoint debts are rigorously absorbed
for every `N>=12` as stated in
`DEGREE4_TAIL_SYMBOLIC_AMGM_2026-08-12.md`.  This still does not settle the
whole discriminant: eventual sign classification finds 19 negative
coefficient families in one tail and 40 in the other, requiring a uniform
parametric SONC allocation.

## 99. A Schur--Sturm index formulation of the first lower margin

For a corrected lower Duran polynomial `Q_D` put

```text
A=(s_D-1)(s_D+beta-1),       R=sqrt(A).
```

The first margin is implied if at most `m-2` roots lie outside the disk
`|z|<R`, with all exterior roots negative.  This can be checked without
choosing or isolating the benign roots: the number of exterior roots is the
negative inertia of the Schur--Cohn Hermitian form, while the number on the
negative ray is the Sturm index of `Q_D` on `(-infinity,-R)`.

In all 770 exact lower cells of the corrected audit, these two integers agree
and are at most `m-2` (apart from the already-direct terminal cell).  Thus the
disk criterion proves every audited first margin by a root-free calculation.
The precise remaining all-order statement is equality of those two indices
and the bound `<=m-2` for the path selector.  The reduction and exact replay
are `LOWER_SELECTOR_M1_SCHUR_STURM_AND_COMPATIBILITY_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md`
and `audit_lower_selector_m1_schur_sturm_indices.py`.  Compatibility of the
endpoint-conditioned fibers alone cannot prove this diagonal disk statement;
the note includes an exact abstract counterexample, so the all-order index
proof must retain the path determinant identities.

## 100. The first lower Duran margin closes at depth four

At lower depth `s=4`, the corrected Duran polynomial `q` is quartic.  Put

```text
F(x)=q(-x),
A=(s_D-1)(s_D+beta-1),
C=q(0)/LC(q),
T=C/A.
```

If the two positive roots of `F` are `b_1,b_2`, then the first margin is
equivalent to `b_1b_2>T`.  This boundary is detected without solving for the
roots by

```text
Res_x(F(x),x^4F(T/x))=0.                             (100.1)
```

Write `d=2k+5` and `d=2k+6` for the two parities.  Across the eight families
`0<=r<4`, the nontrivial resultant factors have degrees 24--26.  Exact Sturm
isolation locates all their nonnegative roots strictly between consecutive
integers.  The discriminant factors have no root for real `k>=0`, so `F`
retains exactly two positive simple roots.  Their product is continuous and
can cross the threshold only at (100.1); one exact rational M1 certificate in
each resultant chamber fixes the strict safe sign throughout that chamber.
Therefore every integer `k>=0` satisfies the first margin.

This proves both Duran margins in all orders for every lower row of depth
`1,2,3,4`.  The proof and replay are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_DURAN_M1_QUARTIC_ROW_THEOREM_2026-08-12.md` and
`prove_lower_selector_m1_quartic_row.py`, which reports
`PASS_EXACT_ALL_ORDER_LOWER_DURAN_M1_ROW_4` for all eight symbolic families.
Depth five and the uniform-depth theorem remain open.

## 101. The Schur disk bound reduces to a fixed Toeplitz two-plane

Scale the corrected Duran polynomial to the target radius and write

```text
p(w)=q(Rw)=c_0w^m+c_1w^(m-1)+...+c_m.
```

Its Schur--Cohn form has the universal rank-two displacement and path-Gram
factorization

```text
H-JHJ^T=uu^T-vv^T,
H=C_uC_u^T-C_vC_v^T.                                (101.1)
```

Both controllability matrices are full rank, so displacement rank alone does
not prove the needed inertia bound.  Congruence by `C_v` gives the useful
normal form

```text
H=C_v(TT^T-I)C_v^T,       T=C_v^(-1)C_u,             (101.2)
```

where `T` is lower triangular Toeplitz.  Its first column `h_j` is the
truncation modulo `z^m` of

```text
(c_0+c_1z+...+c_mz^m)/(c_m+c_(m-1)z+...+c_0z^m).   (101.3)
```

The restriction of `TT^T-I` to the final two coordinates is

```text
[[E-1,C],[C,F-1]],
E=sum_(j=0)^(m-2)h_j^2,
F=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)h_j^2,
C=sum_(j=0)^(m-2)h_jh_(j+1).                       (101.4)
```

Therefore

```text
E>1,       (E-1)(F-1)-C^2>0                         (101.5)
```

exhibits a fixed positive two-plane, forcing at most `m-2` exterior roots.
Both inequalities pass exactly in all 769 nonterminal lower cells; the sole
terminal cell has already been handled directly.  This replaces hundreds of
varying Schur-minor sign patterns by two uniform quotient-moment inequalities.
Together with equality of the Schur exterior index and negative-ray Sturm
index, (101.5) would prove the first margin in every depth.

The all-order matrix identities and exact audits are recorded in
`LOWER_SELECTOR_SCHUR_TOEPLITZ_MOMENT_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md`,
`verify_lower_selector_schur_ranktwo_displacement.py`, and
`audit_lower_selector_schur_toeplitz_moment_plane.py`.  The remaining proof
is path-specific positivity of (101.5) plus the Sturm-index equality.

## 102. One tail minor pays the full Schur moment debt

The two long-vector inequalities in (101.5) collapse further.  Put

```text
a=(h_0,...,h_(m-2),0),       b=(h_1,...,h_(m-1),h_0).
```

Then `||a||^2=E`, `||b||^2=F`, and `<a,b>=C`.  Lagrange's identity gives

```text
EF-C^2=sum_(i<j)(a_i b_j-a_j b_i)^2.                (102.1)
```

In particular the final adjacent summand is

```text
W=(h_(m-3)h_(m-1)-h_(m-2)^2)^2.                   (102.2)
```

Thus the two last-three-coefficient estimates

```text
h_(m-3)^2+h_(m-2)^2>1,
W>E+F-1                                                (102.3)
```

imply `E>1` and

```text
(E-1)(F-1)-C^2=(EF-C^2)-(E+F-1)>0.                 (102.4)
```

An independent exact replay checks the Gram identity in all 770 lower cells
of its stated `d<=14` range.  The tail certificate (102.3) passes every one
of the 713 cells there with `m>=4`; it additionally passes 40 cubic cells,
for 753 certified cells total.  The 17 remaining low-degree cells are
covered by the direct depth-one-through-four theorems, including the terminal
exception.

This attractive collapse is **not** all-order.  The first later exact
counterexample currently known is

```text
(d,r,s,m)=(19,3,8,6),       A=68,
W/(E+F)=0.3290420569...,
W-(E+F-1)<0.                                      (102.5)
```

The full determinant `(E-1)(F-1)-C^2` at (102.5) remains strongly positive;
other Gram minors in (102.1) supply the missing mass.  Thus (102.1) remains a
useful exact decomposition, but a uniform proof must retain a larger or
adaptive minor block rather than only (102.2).

The first expanded audit supplies a clean repair rather than a growing block.
Through `d<=30` (10,377 exact cells with `m>=4`), the fixed three-index tail
sum

```text
S_3=W+h_0^2(h_(m-3)^2+h_(m-2)^2)                 (102.6)
```

satisfies the stronger `S_3>4(E+F-1)` in every cell, including (102.5); the
smallest exact ratio in that range is `4.704529...` at (102.5).  A subsequent
pure-integer/FLINT replay exhausts the entire lower diamond through `d=50`:
all 53,777 cells with `m>=4` satisfy the same strengthened inequality.  Both
extra terms in
(102.6) are themselves Gram-minor squares involving the wraparound coordinate
`h_0`, so (102.6) is a genuine sub-sum of (102.1).  This is currently expanded
exact evidence, not yet a packaged all-order theorem; it shows that the first
single-minor failure does not force a growing tail block.

The expanded replay factors the radical scale out exactly: if
`h_j=R^(m-j)H_j`, `R^2=A`, then

```text
H_j={q_(m-j)-sum_(ell=1)^j q_ell A^ell H_(j-ell)}/q_0. (102.7)
```

This evaluates every energy and minor over the rationals and eliminates the
large-cancellation errors that affected preliminary floating probes near
`d=50`.  The script is `probe_lower_selector_tail3_exact_expanded.py`.

Within the complete `d<=50` diamond, (102.2) itself fails only at the single
cell (102.5); every other `W` already pays the full debt.  This unusually
isolated failure suggests a potentially shorter proof split: establish the
one-minor inequality outside a finite exceptional parameter chamber and use
the wraparound minors in (102.6) only there.  This remains a finite audit, not
an all-order exclusion of later exceptions.

The original one-minor audit is
`audit_lower_selector_schur_tail_minor_certificate.py`, with report
`lower_selector_schur_tail_minor_exact_20260812.json`; it reports
`PASS_EXACT_753_CELL_LAST_THREE_QUOTIENT_COEFFICIENT_CERTIFICATE`.  The
authoritative expanded audit is
`audit_lower_selector_schur_tail3_full_diamond.py`, driven by

`lower_selector_schur_tail3_full_diamond_exact_20260812.json`; it reports

Its source, engine, and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`9A3132B79BD9288F6BF5859E2680F094AD56549CF86D767370BAB1D50A4BEBDA`,
`AE9EC0CDFA3490046ACC3526C2ED724A23993BCD9AF5C2F0D2B24838B526D858`,
and `A79E6A1E01AD94F36345DC407F605EFC9117AA9AA40F40BC3852C3503BFCBC60`.
Both scopes are exactly finite.  The full moment inequality (101.5) and the

## 103. The standard generalized-Hurwitz shortcut is strictly too strong

There is a natural way to reformulate the remaining index equality.  The
Cayley polynomial

```text
C(w)=(w-1)^m q(R(w+1)/(w-1))
```

maps roots of `q` outside `|z|=R` to the right half-plane and maps the
negative exterior ray to the positive real axis.  Thus the required equality
says that every right-half-plane root of `C` is positive real.  One might try
to prove the stronger statement that `C` is a generalized Hurwitz polynomial
in Tyaglov's sense, using the alternating Hurwitz-minor criterion.

That shortcut is false already at the exact cell `(d,r,s,m)=(19,3,8,6)`.
Here `R^2=68`; the Schur exterior index and the number of positive real roots
of `C` are both exactly two, so the desired index equality holds.  However,
the Hurwitz minors in orders `5,3,1` have signs `(-,+,+)`, whereas the
generalized-Hurwitz criterion requires them all positive.  The obstruction is
conceptual: generalized Hurwitz polynomials impose an additional interlacing
pattern between their positive and negative real roots, which our selector
does not satisfy and does not need.

The exact replay is
`audit_lower_selector_cayley_generalized_hurwitz_obstruction.py`, with report
`lower_selector_cayley_generalized_hurwitz_obstruction_exact_20260812.json`.
It reports `PASS_EXACT_GENERALIZED_HURWITZ_SHORTCUT_OBSTRUCTED`; the source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`F0BAE7EF6DA8C07A491B9B11C3BB8D4B4D133E1F09A2B16761C477B56696DBE4`
and `5FA16396FA8E0AB91EBAEDFD19468FAFC340B3F7143BA659DE92D9E25D67A348`.
Therefore the proof must establish the weaker path-specific half-plane/real-
axis equality directly; the standard generalized-Hurwitz theorem cannot be
used as a black box.

There is nevertheless a simpler exact finite reformulation.  In every one of
the 770 lower cells from the Section 99 audit, the ordinary number of sign
changes in the coefficient sequence of `C(w)` equals the Schur exterior index.
By the already-certified Schur--Sturm equality and the Cayley map, the same
integer is also the number of positive real roots of `C`.  Thus Descartes'
upper bound is exact in all audited cells and, more importantly, the difficult
matrix-versus-Sturm comparison can plausibly be replaced by a path-specific
coefficient-variation theorem for a Krawtchouk/Cayley transform.

The new replay `audit_lower_selector_cayley_sign_variation.py` performs the
770 comparisons exactly over the quadratic field without numerical roots.  It
reports `PASS_EXACT_770_CELL_CAYLEY_SIGN_VARIATION_AUDIT` in
`lower_selector_cayley_sign_variation_exact_20260812.json`.  The source and
report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`4CB1395E7E3DFF28231662E1115B266584C318347FE05D6F0F2A08CAFD2D46BF`
and `E45DA68D8E209D04146603FE5E26D8CD2EFBA441CA341382DA2F229DA3F1BBC1`.
This is still finite evidence: exact Descartes variation for the Cayley
coefficient sequence must be proved in all orders.

A separate FLINT replay now tests the weaker coefficient-only consequence on
the complete lower diamond through `d<=30`.  Excluding the already direct
terminal degree-drop cell `(5,0,5)`, all 10,529 cells satisfy

```text
variation(coefficients(C))<=m-2.                    (103.1)
```

The observed deficits `m-variation(C)` range from two through seven, with 128
cells attaining the sharp value two.  This larger replay deliberately does
not compute a Schur or Sturm index: it strengthens the evidence for (103.1),
but it does not prove that every right-half-plane Cayley root is positive real.
That count equality remains an independent all-order obligation.  The script
`audit_lower_selector_cayley_variation_bound_expanded.py` and report
`lower_selector_cayley_variation_bound_expanded_exact_20260812.json` report
`PASS_EXACT_D5_TO_D30_CAYLEY_VARIATION_BOUND_AUDIT`; their SHA-256 hashes are
`933B39778AB0E7B3E7EA146BA8F4A6F27BBE5910C067492065C7DFBA5AAE1612`
and `8F28D6D56742B3F6833B7D64C7C13FFC6D677633E51368F29382F1953DE9D36F`.

## 104. Unequal polar identity and a reopened lower subregion

**Correction (2026-08-13).**  Identity (104.1) and all parameter/parity
calculations in this section are exact.  The claimed sector conclusion is
not: both polar points lie inside the desired rotating half-plane, whereas
Laguerre preservation requires them outside the circular region containing
the polynomial zeros.  The subregion (104.3) is therefore a valid reduction
and finite audit, but is not closed by the argument originally written here.

The base half-angle sector theorem from Sections 59.1--59.3 motivates the
following exact unequal-polar identity.  For

```text
M_m(d;z)=P_B[(4q-d)^m](z),
Pi_a^(j)f=f-(d-a)f'/j,
```

successive normalized polar differentiation gives the exact identity

```text
Pi_(-v)^(m-1) Pi_(-u)^m M_m
 =M_m-(2d+u+v)M_m'/m
     +(d+u)(d+v)M_m''/[m(m-1)]
 =16P_B[(q+u/4)(q+v/4)(4q-d)^(m-2)].              (104.1)
```

At `z=rho exp(2i psi)`, both polar points `-u,-v` lie in the same rotating
half-plane as the base `d`-zeros.  This membership does **not** invoke
Laguerre preservation; it is the domain error corrected above.  Consequently
the asserted arbitrary unequal-outlier circle exclusion for `rho>m-1`
remains a separate structured theorem to be proved.

For a corrected lower selector put `a=max(0,s-N+1)`.  Apart from the direct
terminal cell `(5,0,5)`, its parameters simplify all-order to

```text
m=floor(s/2)+2-a,
x=floor((d+s)/2)-floor(s/2)-1,
A=x(x+beta),
B=d+ceil(s/2)-1.                                    (104.2)
```

The condition `sqrt(A)>m-1` is exactly

```text
s<=d-5; or
d even and s=d-3; or
d odd  and s=d-4.                                   (104.3)
```

on the unforced rows, and it fails on every forced row except the direct
terminal degree drop.  Moreover (104.3) implies the real negative-axis
barrier `sqrt(A)>(B-3)/4`.  At the four parity cutoffs the cleared square
margins are

```text
7D^2+2D-25,
7D^2+6D-17,
7D^2+6D-17,
7D^2+16D-16,
```

all strictly positive in their admissible ranges.  The reflected positive
endpoint theorem bounds an unselected negative residual root by `m-1`, and
the negative-axis barrier bounds a positive residual root by `(B-3)/4`.
The previous argument claimed that (104.1) put every residual root inside
`|z|<sqrt(A)`.  Because its sector step is invalid, the first Durán margin
in the complete region (104.3) is not proved by this route.

The reduction and replay are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_UNEQUAL_POLAR_SECTOR_SUBREGION_2026-08-12.md` and
`prove_unequal_outlier_polar_sector_subregion.py`, with report
`unequal_outlier_polar_sector_subregion_exact_20260812.json`.  The corrected
status is `PASS_EXACT_UNEQUAL_POLAR_IDENTITY_AND_PARAMETER_REDUCTION_ONLY`:
this certifies the identities and parameter transcription, not the missing
analytic sector implication.  The corrected note, source, and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`8C39DF60814BB2F59CA9E521217D743B3671369C96E7DBEC31F1C4203FC22502`,
`4053EA7CD9FBB3C53E4BF400F764E6515858C6CDD2E3995DD804A56F3BDBA542`,
and `730C82A1746F833B05EE7AE215C8F72C66DC3B6D56D344E3DE470EC7F8C054FA`.
The `d<=50` transcription sweep places 17,297 of 54,050 degree-at-least-two
lower cells in this candidate subregion; it no longer counts them as closed.
Its 36,753-cell high-depth complement and the reopened candidate subregion
both require a valid proof.

There is a useful interaction with Section 102: the unique failure of the
one-minor certificate `W>E+F-1` in the complete `d<=50` diamond is
`(d,r,s)=(19,3,8)`, and this cell satisfies `s<=d-5`.  It was previously
declared closed by (104.3), but that conclusion is now reopened.  The simpler
one-minor certificate has no exact failure in the high-depth complement
through `d<=50`, but its unique known failure is again part of the open set.
This does not make the complement finite or prove the inequality all-order,
but it removes the only known need for the wraparound terms from the region
where a Toeplitz proof is still required.

## 105. The endpoint square reduces to three aligned Jacobi rays

The remaining two-conditional-leaf compatibility has a sharper translation.
Set `x=a+1,y=b+1`, so `x,y>=0`, and use

```text
V=P_N-P_(N-1)=vS_(N-1),
W=P_(N-1)-P_(N-2)=vS_(N-2),
S_M=2P_M-vP_(M-1).
```

For the symmetric bilinear gamma slice define

```text
E=J_s(C,C)+uJ_s(D,D),
F=J_s(C,V)+uJ_s(D,W),
G=J_s(V,V)+uJ_s(W,W),
C=P_(N-1), D=P_(N-2).
```

Then bilinearity gives the all-order endpoint-square identity

```text
B(a,b)=J_s(A_a,A_b)+uJ_s(B_a,B_b)
      =G+(x+y)F+xyE.                               (105.1)
```

Consequently it is enough to prove that `F` is positively compatible with
both `E` and `G` (and equivalently include the `E,G` pair).  Pairwise
compatibility for equal-degree positive-leading polynomials gives a common
interlacer by the interval Helly theorem, after which every combination in
(105.1) is negative-rooted.  Thus the entire endpoint square is reduced to
the two aligned pencils `E+cF` and `F+cG`, `c>=0`.  The factors `S_M/2` are
again positive endpoint-Jacobi determinants, so this is a path-specific
aligned direct-sum mixed-slice lemma, not a generic coefficient-matrix claim.

The updated exact replay checks 1,491 identities, 4,347 certified pairwise
common gaps, 2,898 `F/G` four-leaf gaps, and 1,449 good `E/F` leaf crosses
through `N<=25`, all layers, and three decades of `u`.  It also records
an exact warning: adding the two reciprocal raw deletion separators is not a
valid symmetrized separator; at `(N,s,a,b,u)=(5,2,-1,-9/10,1/1000)` one such
pencil has discriminant

```text
-809897637549/5000000.
```

The package is `ALIGNED_ENDPOINT_JACOBI_THREE_RAY_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md`,
`verify_aligned_endpoint_three_ray_reduction.py`, and
`aligned_endpoint_three_ray_exact_20260812.json`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`F1560F99A9E63647C3508149317D1BF060175F207C6157BC1AFE2869B14440A9` and
`16D1EDC1B545B3D780008C2C96CAC9236088FDD28D2841F3E15650C48ECAE2F0`.
The common-gap sweep remains finite evidence; the two pencils above are the
all-order endpoint bottleneck.

The still stronger signed-reciprocal version of the `F/G` route is false.
Write `V=vS,W=vT`, put `A=C+cV,B=D+cW`, and, after stripping forced powers,
let

```text
R=F_(s-1)(A,S)+uF_(s-1)(B,T).
```

At `(N,s,c,u,q)=(6,6,12,1/1000,9/10)`, the discriminant of
`R-qz^(deg(R)+1)R(1/z)` is exactly

```text
-125853587816677788406356208825767/12207031250000000.
```

Thus the monomial Casoratian `AT-BS=v^(2N-5)` does not imply full reciprocal
proper position.  The required positive palindromization remains
negative-rooted in this cell, so the counterexample removes only the
all-signed Hurwitz/self-interlacing shortcut.  The exact package is
`ENDPOINT_SIGNED_RECIPROCAL_COUNTEREXAMPLE_2026-08-12.md`,
`verify_endpoint_signed_reciprocal_counterexample.py`, and
`endpoint_signed_reciprocal_counterexample_exact_20260812.json`.

The surviving `F/G` route has an exact parameter-derivative form.  Put
`A_c=C+cV,B_c=D+cW` and

```text
K_c=J_s(A_c,A_c)+uJ_s(B_c,B_c)=E+2cF+c^2G.
```

Then

```text
(1/2) partial_c K_c=F+cG.                           (105.2)
```

Writing `V=vS,W=vT,R=P_(N-3),n=s-1`, the continuant identities
`C=(S+vD)/2,D=(T+vR)/2` sharpen this to

```text
F+cG=(1/4)L_n+(t/2)M_(n-1)+ctL_(n-1),              (105.3)
L_j=J_j(S,S)+uJ_j(T,T),
M_j=J_j(D,S)+uJ_j(R,T).
```

Thus `F/G` is reduced to compatibility of three consecutive
vertical/deletion rows.  The replay checks 11,592 instances of (105.2)--
(105.3) and 13,041 exact positive pencils between the three pieces through
`N<=25`, all layers.  The all-order identities and exact remaining theorem
are recorded in `ENDPOINT_FG_PARAMETER_DERIVATIVE_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md`;
the replay and report are `verify_endpoint_fg_parameter_derivative_reduction.py`
and `endpoint_fg_parameter_derivative_exact_20260812.json`.

### 105.1 Positive spectral directions close each individual derivative block

There is an all-order theorem behind the individual-block part of (105.2).
For positive spectral parameters put

```text
P_lambda(z)=product_i(1+lambda_i z),
F(z,w)=P_lambda(z)P_lambda(w),
L_delta(z)=sum_i delta_i z/(1+lambda_i z),
```

where every `delta_i>=0`.  Direct differentiation gives

```text
D_delta F=F{L_delta(z)+L_delta(w)}.                 (105.4)
```

Every map `z/(1+lambda_i z)` sends the open upper half-plane into itself.
Consequently `D_delta F`, `F+cD_delta F`, and
`D_delta F+cD_eta F=D_(delta+c eta)F` are stable for nonnegative
`c,delta,eta`.  They have nonnegative coefficients, so their fixed
total-degree homogeneous components are stable.  Passing through the
palindromic gamma transform proves that a diagonal gamma slice, every
positive spectral directional derivative of it, and every positive pencil
between two such derivatives are negative-rooted.

Each endpoint Jacobi matrix is `D_cJD_c`, with one endpoint coordinate of
`D_c` equal to `sqrt(c)`.  Its spectrum equals that of
`J^(1/2)D_c^2J^(1/2)`, an affine positive-semidefinite rank-one perturbation,
so its eigenvalue velocities are nonnegative.  Thus the
parameter derivative of **each individual block** in `K_c` is now proved
negative-rooted and compatible with that block in every order.  The sole
remaining issue in (105.2) is cross-compatibility after the two nested-block
derivatives are added with the common weight `u`; it is not individual root
motion.

The theorem and proof are
`SPECTRAL_DIRECTIONAL_DERIVATIVE_STABILITY_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md`.  The
supporting replay `verify_spectral_directional_derivative_stability.py`
checks four generic identities, 42 gamma derivatives, 21 exact root sets,
and 189 positive pencils, reporting
`PASS_EXACT_SPECTRAL_DIRECTIONAL_DERIVATIVE_STABILITY_REPLAY` in
`spectral_directional_derivative_stability_exact_20260813.json`.  The note,
source, and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`6B5140A6AA0A81BFE86836E1FE257429FA6D9E01C719549C6163662CBAA1BDDF`,
`E39FFB96A273D84A14E03679157EB8A9F7C5BD2818DFD1E436827EA0D4A7ABBF`,
and
`EC5A9284F9CC4C1C86760E303DEE547ABF8BB2E139E1720774C3DF1C15FC0A7D`.

### 105.2 The endpoint rays close uniformly through layer fifteen

The forest reserve is `N=2s+5+q`, `q>=0`.  In these coordinates, direct
symbolic elimination proves both endpoint pencils

```text
E+cF,       F+cG
```

negative-rooted for every real `c,u,q>=0` and every integer `2<=s<=15`.
For `s=4,...,8` the two discriminants have respectively

```text
(54,72,425,525,1666),   (45,63,375,470,1519)
```

strictly positive coefficients in `(c,q,u)`, 5,214 coefficients in total.
Section 75 supplies the rooted endpoints `E` and `G`; positive discriminant,
positive leading coefficient, and homotopy from those endpoints prohibit a
collision or degree loss.  Positive coefficients put every nonzero root on
the negative axis.  The smaller layers have core degree at most one, and
boundary parameters follow by closure.  This is an exact all-excess theorem
in seven layers, not a bounded parameter scan.

```text
K_j=A_jK_(j-1)-N_(j-1)^2K_(j-2)/4
```

is exact for every number of odd blocks; coefficientwise positivity of its
members is what the replay proves at `s=12,...,15`.  The independent full
replay completes in about 322 seconds and passes every exact sign check.
The theorem, replay, and report are
`ENDPOINT_RAYS_FOREST_LAYERS12_15_TRIDIAGONAL_SONC_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md`,
`prove_endpoint_rays_forest_layers12_15_tridiagonal_sonc.py`, and
`endpoint_rays_forest_layers12_15_tridiagonal_sonc_exact_20260813.json`;

`BFB160BF4323A364017E48D3CCD8ADD641FC61AC19BB00714F208BD550932A49`,
`43569DAB0A7CB7F0BCD84C29660750F46B12ECF257141104B3C7ACEB8571655F`,

For the remaining layers, a certified finite audit covers `2<=s<=30`, five

```text
-(F+cG)(lambda)/partial_t K_c(lambda),
K_c=E+2cF+c^2G,
```

have one common sign within each sampled cell; no mixed-sign cell occurs on

the two certified velocity signs are `(-1,+1)`.  At `(17,6,1,119/100)`

even in the forest cone.  This does not affect the exact low-layer theorem:
`F+cG` can be negative-rooted without interlacing `K_c`.

The finite grid audit is

For `s>=16`, the endpoint task remains direct negative-rootedness of both

At `s=16`, the harder-looking `F+cG` Gram obstruction also passes exactly:

the negative odd blocks are `c^11,c^13,...,c^23`.  The eight exact leading

The last determinant is a negative 13,762-digit integer.  Since the
