## 106. Conditional radius split and the low-degree one-minor base

Section 104 identifies `A>(m-1)^2` as the candidate exterior-radius region,
but no longer proves its lower first margin.  The unique failure of the one-
tail-minor inequality in the authoritative `d<=50` audit,
`(d,r,s,m)=(19,3,8,6)`, has `A=68>25`; it therefore remains an open cell and
cannot be discarded via Section 104.

The complementary inequality itself gives a useful all-order reduction.  If
`d=2D`, then the smallest target value allowed by the parity formulas is

```text
A>=(D-1)(D-3/2).
```

If `d=2D+1`, its smallest value is

```text
A>=(D-1)(D-1/2).
```

Thus even `d>=2m+2` or odd `d>=2m+1` forces `A>(m-1)^2`.  Equivalently,

```text
A<=(m-1)^2  ==>  d<=2m,
```

with `d<=2m-1` in odd parity.  Hence each fixed degree in the complement is
genuinely finite.  Exact rational evaluation proves the initial base of 21
cells with `m=4`, 45 with `m=5`, and 77 with `m=6`, for 143 cells in total.
In all of them,

```text
h_(m-3)^2+h_(m-2)^2>1,
(h_(m-3)h_(m-1)-h_(m-2)^2)^2>E+F-1.
```

The smallest ratio `W/(E+F-1)` is `5.42618286258173...` at
`(d,r,s,a,m)=(6,1,8,2,4)`.  Therefore the one-minor route is reduced to the
single all-order statement

```text
m>=7,       A<=(m-1)^2.
```

The proof and replay are `LOWER_SELECTOR_POST_SECTOR_W_REDUCTION_2026-08-12.md`
and `prove_lower_selector_w_complement_low_degree.py`; the report is
`lower_selector_w_complement_low_degree_exact_20260812.json` and reports
`PASS_EXACT_POST_SECTOR_W_CERTIFICATE_FOR_M4_TO_M6`.  The source and report
SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`F7C4BE37EC396CC5068D041D08B1C17319C04D31B73737921108B34E17316F69` and
`9AF70740211796F6185312B36F02DE9CBB51C0CB337532EC29F836B5F4C6F50E`.
The zero failures among the 36,606 cells satisfying the remaining statement
in the `d<=50` diamond are exact finite evidence, not its proof.

For later analytic work, put `sigma=s mod 2` and `e=2m-d`.  The complement
has the exact coordinates

```text
d=2m-e,
s=2m+2a-4+sigma,
N_D=4m+a-e-4+sigma.
```

It is exactly `e>=0` except for the sector-safe parity `(e,sigma)=(0,1)`.
On the unforced chart `a=0`, putting `g=N-s` gives

```text
r=e+g-4+sigma,
max(1,4-e-sigma)<=g<=2m-2e-1-sigma.
```

On the forced chart,

```text
r=e+a-3+sigma,
1<=a<=2m-2e-2-sigma,       e+sigma>=2.
```

The radical-free quotient recurrence also has the explicit rational form
recorded as (8)--(12) in the companion note: `q_k` is a finite
gamma/unsigned-Stirling sum, reciprocal coefficients `v_j` invert
`sum q_jA^jz^j/q_0`, and

```text
H_j=q_0^(-1) sum_(k=0)^j q_(m-k)v_(j-k).
```

Consequently the desired `W` inequality is a rational inequality on these two
integer cones, with no square roots.  The replay
`verify_lower_selector_post_sector_natural_coordinates.py` checks the charts
in all 36,683 remaining `d<=50` cells (19,409 unforced, 17,274 forced) and
reconstructs the rational formulas exactly.  Its source and report hashes are
`5BE13317CBB4F419DA1BB89FF463A8971360921E3694FDC8A553DBBD1CE46DE2` and
`28C9D1A955ADA83EF38F7AFACFB6EDFFE05A24D05DE98C572AAFCBA158D697EE`.

### 106.1 The first strip below the sector reduces to one path coefficient

There is a rigorous order-one quasi-orthogonal extension of Section 104 at
the real anchor.  Let `R^2=A` and suppose

```text
m-2<R<m-1.                                           (106.11)
```

For `B>0`, the Pfaff conversion identifies the base polynomial
`M_m(d;R)` with

```text
P_m^(B-1,R-m)(x),       d=4(1+x)/(1-x).
```

Here `R-m` lies in `(-2,-1)`.  The Jacobi contiguous relation with parameter
`R-m+1 in (-1,0)` expresses this polynomial as a positive combination of
two consecutive members of one positive Jacobi system.  Interlacing and the
sign at `x=-1` therefore show that `M_m(d;R)` has exactly `m-1` positive
simple roots and one root in `(-4,0)`.

For `u,v>0`, put

```text
F(d)=Pi_(-v)^(m-1) Pi_(-u)^m M_m(d;R).
```

Two real polar differentiations leave at most one nonpositive root.  Write

```text
theta=m-1-R in (0,1),       K=B+m-2.
```

Direct Pochhammer evaluation gives

```text
F(0)=positive_factor * Psi,
Psi=uv+4(u+v)(1-theta)/K
       -16theta(1-theta)/(K(K+1)).                  (106.12)
```

If `u,v>=1/K`, then, with `c=K/(K+1)`,

```text
K^2 Psi>=9-8theta-16c theta(1-theta)
        >=5-4c-1/c=(3K-1)/(K(K+1))>0.              (106.13)
```

The product of the roots of `F` is consequently positive, so the possible
last nonpositive root is positive as well.  Thus all roots of `F` are
positive at the real anchor under (106.11) and `u,v>=1/K`.

For the corrected lower selector, `K=N_D-1` and the two polar parameters are
the reciprocals of the two positive selector roots.  Hence the path-specific
ceiling

```text
rho_1,rho_2<=N_D-1                                  (106.14)
```

closes this real-anchor orientation.  The already-proved selector Turan
inequality reduces (106.14) further to the single integer evaluation

```text
G_(N-1,s)(K)<K G_(N-2,s)(K),       K=N_D-1.         (106.15)
```

Indeed, (106.15) puts the Section 67 fixed point below `K`, while

```text
Gamma(K)={(KG_2(K)-G_1(K))^2
           +(G_0(K)G_2(K)-G_1(K)^2)}/G_2(K)>0,
```

so both positive selector roots lie below `K`.

Formula (74.1) converts (106.15) into one explicit coefficient target.  Put

```text
A_K(z)=(1+z+Kz^2)/(1-Kz^2)^2,
B_K(z)=1/(1-Kz^2),
R_0=2(N-2)-s-1.
```

Then (106.15) is exactly

```text
[z^s]B_K(z)A_K(z)^R_0{K-A_K(z)^2}>0.               (106.16)
```

There is a sharper coefficient-response form of the same inequality.  Set

```text
c_(R,s,h)=[t^h]G_(N-2,s),       R=2(N-2)-s-1,
j=s-2h.
```

Vandermonde summation in (74.6) gives the exact all-order identities

```text
c_(R,s,h)
 =binom(R,j) sum_(k=0)^h binom(R-j,k)binom(2R+h-k,h-k)
 =binom(R,j)[x^h](1+x)^(R+s-h)(1+2x)^(R-s+2h).     (106.17)
```

The `G_(N-1,s)` coefficient is obtained by replacing `R` by `R+2`.
Consequently, if

```text
q_h=c_(R+2,s,h)/c_(R,s,h),
d_h=Kc_(R,s,h)-c_(R+2,s,h),
```

then (106.15) is `sum_h d_h K^h>0`.  Thus an all-order proof separates into
two concrete scalar statements: `q_h` is decreasing in `h`, so `(d_h)` has
at most one sign change from negative to positive, and the positive tail
pays the negative head at the single evaluation `K`.

The near strip has only the four all-order parity types

```text
(e,sigma)=(0,0),(1,0),(1,1),(2,1).
```

On the forced chart `R_0=2m-6`; on the unforced chart
`R_0=s+2g-5>=2m-6`.  Thus (106.16) is a two-chart positive-coefficient or
injection problem, not an unstructured new cone.

This is a conditional reduction, not yet a proof of the Durán margin.  Two
steps remain: prove (106.15)--(106.16) in all orders, and extend the resulting
real-anchor orientation through the rotating half-angle sector, where the
base pencil can have one exceptional boundary branch with `D>R`.

The exact integer replay verifies (106.15) in all 3,131 near-strip cells with
`m>=7` and `d<=50`; its largest ratio `G_1(K)/(KG_2(K))` is
`0.5585889035405944...` at `(d,r,s,a,m,K)=(13,8,29,9,7,32)`.  An independent
exact Sturm audit verifies (106.14) in all 311 such cells through `d<=20`.
These bounded checks are evidence only.  The theorem, replay, and report are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_NEAR_SECTOR_QUASI_JACOBI_REDUCTION_2026-08-13.md`,
`verify_lower_selector_near_sector_quasi_jacobi_reduction.py`, and
`lower_selector_near_sector_quasi_jacobi_exact_20260813.json`; their SHA-256
hashes are respectively
`644F3B2FCC8E43A2684A5628093DF7881408A756A45E468CD0D2EE2F43A5E90C`,
`2551978D0DDDBC616C4DC6BCE0305708870FFF4C60CCAE112DB59F87FBF9F594`,
and `CAAC72E46D20D748A30AF35840E6DFC70CCA09CB93D514C2F9967EB10FAACB5B`.

The independent coefficient-response replay verifies (106.17), strict
decrease of `q_h`, the one-sign-change pattern, and the final weighted
payment in all 3,131 cells through `d<=50`.  Its sharp forced cell is
`(e,m,a,s,R,K)=(1,7,9,29,8,32)`, where the unweighted negative-head to
leading-coefficient ratio is
`180043620/309540569=0.5816478...`, while the `K`-weighted leading payment
exceeds the entire negative head by a factor above 2,073.  This remains
finite evidence, but (106.17) is an all-order identity and is now the
smallest exact selector-ceiling target.  The note, replay, and report are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_NEAR_SECTOR_COEFFICIENT_RESPONSE_REDUCTION_2026-08-13.md`,
`verify_lower_selector_near_sector_coefficient_response.py`, and
`lower_selector_near_sector_coefficient_response_exact_20260813.json`;
their SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`DA513155646099B5F0EE7580CAEDE1CC2B9392C8B87CCFA4C63F5E39DDEB3A3C`,
`D09027CF3871C30C168F889227A4F1E7A572A6B2C8BD9BE9A81A573255DA64C6`,
and
`008953CDA80B83124EB557257DDF48EABE62331DD8CDB2339106ED4B9CC49C1D`.

Most of the unforced chart can in fact be proved coefficientwise.  Put
`y=2g-4`, `K=2s+kappa`, where `kappa=3-sigma-e`, and assume `g>=3`.
Writing `j=s-2h` and

```text
C(x)=(1+x)/(1-x)^2=1+D(x),
```

the response coefficient has the active-box expansion

```text
c_(R,s,h)=sum_(ell=0)^h w_(j,h,ell)binom(R,j+ell),
w_(j,h,ell)>0,
```

where the weights are independent of `R`.  Hence
`c_(R+2,s,h)/c_(R,s,h)` is a positive weighted average of
`binom(R+2,n)/binom(R,n)` with `n<=s`.  This ratio increases with `n`, so

```text
c_(R+2,s,h)/c_(R,s,h)
 <=(R+2)(R+1)/{(R+2-s)(R+1-s)}.                  (106.18)
```

After `R=s+y-1`, the sufficient and exact chart inequality `K>b_s` is

```text
(2s+kappa-1)y^2+(kappa-1)y-s(s+1)>0.             (106.19)
```

Therefore every response coefficient `d_h` is positive whenever (106.19)
holds.  This proves the selector ceiling and real-anchor orientation in an
all-order far-unforced subregion, without needing the conjectural ratio or
tail-payment lemmas.  The simpler uniform condition `(2g-4)^2>=s` implies
(106.19).  In the old `d<=50` transcription it covers 1,947 of 2,098
unforced near-sector cells (92.8 percent); the count is finite evidence, but
the positive-mixture proof is all-order.  Forced cells, the small-`g`
boundary, and rotating-sector continuation remain.

The same active-box representation sharpens (106.18) to

```text
c_(R+2,s,h)/c_(R,s,h)
 <={(s+y)(s+y+1)}/{(y+h)(y+h+1)}.                (106.20)
```

This proves the ratio monotonicity and one-sign-change pattern for every
unforced `g>=3`, including the residual small-`g` strip.  A direct
positive-term injection also gives

```text
c_(h+1)/c_h >=
 {(s-2h)(s-2h-1)(2R+1)}/
 {(R-s+2h+2)(R-s+2h+1)(h+1)}.                    (106.21)
```

Together these reduce the remaining weighted payment on the entire
unforced `g>=3` chart to the single scalar inequality (19) in the companion
note.  That scalar inequality is now proved in all orders.  In the notation
of the companion theorem, put

```text
A=(s+y)(s+y+1), E=A-Ky(y+1), p=y+H,
Delta=K(p+1)(p+2)-A.
```

Exact chart brackets give `Delta>=E/(2s)`, so `Delta K/E>=1`.  The remaining
height inequality follows from the chart endpoint
`s>=2p^2+2H-1` and reduces to

```text
4p^2(2p^2-1)y(y+1)
 >(p+2)(2p-1)(p-1)^2,
```

which is immediate from `y>=2` and the displayed polynomial bounds.  Thus
the selector ceiling and real-anchor orientation are complete for the full
unforced `g>=3` chart.  Unforced `g=1,2`, the forced chart, and rotating
half-angle continuation remain.  The theorem, replay, and report are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_NEAR_SECTOR_UNFORCED_SCALAR_PAYMENT_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md`,
`prove_lower_selector_near_sector_unforced_scalar_payment.py`, and
`lower_selector_near_sector_unforced_scalar_payment_exact_20260813.json`,
with SHA-256 hashes
`5007145F7DC3A0E6A23DF4EC3904570D29C98AF569D7F4D255B7E66655D6B7A6`,

and
`492475174759A6EB916E1F81A0F9971AE1738DD775CDB1B78DBD111A7F9E7681`.

The forced chart has `R<s`, so the binomial-support proof above does not
apply.

The boundary `g=2` (hence `y=0`) is also closed in all orders for its two

```text
c_(R+2,s,h)/c_(R,s,h)<=s(s+1)/{h(h+1)},  h>=1,
```

and the same growth injection applies.  Exact parity intervals for the last

The final unforced boundary `g=1` is also closed in all orders.  Removing

and
`95E225FF91058452D3F41D48FCF6D68D00A04ED7B017037E65C386CF690BB2E6`.

On the forced chart, write `h=a+2+ell`, `j=R-1-2ell`, and
`V=1+2ell`.  The active-box expansion splits exactly as

```text
c_R=sum_(v<=V)w_v binom(R,j+v),
```

Thus each response step has precisely two uncompensated support-edge births,

```text
c_(T-1)^+ +Kc_T^+<K^2c_T.                          (106.23)
```

for products `prod_(i=1)^n(1+lambda_i x)`, `lambda_i>=1`.  The interior

```text
 >={22R^2-242R-1139}/7>0
```

for `m>=11`; the 88 admissible cells at `m=7,8,9,10` form an exhaustive

### 106.2 The rotation obstruction compresses to one safe-core boundary coordinate

Put `r=m-2`, `S=u+v`, `P=uv`, and

```text
F_r=16P_B[(q+u/4)(q+v/4)(4q-d)^r].
```

Two applications of the exact Meixner recurrence give

```text
(B+r)(B+r+1)F_r=A_rM_r-dr(d+4)L_rM_(r-1),          (106.24)
L_r=Bd+(B+r+1)S+4(z-r-1),
```

where `A_r` is the explicit quadratic displayed in the standalone note.
Equivalently, since `M_r'=-rM_(r-1)`, the right side is

```text
A_rM_r+d(d+4)L_rM_r'.                               (106.25)
```

After the Section 59 substitution `w=d/(d+4)`, this becomes

```text
A_rQ_r-drL_rQ_(r-1).                                (106.26)
```

Thus, when `A_r!=0`, a zero is the characteristic equation of the
`r`-dimensional Section 59 tridiagonal core plus the single last-coordinate
perturbation

```text
delta e_(r-1)e_(r-1)^T,
delta=(B+r-1)drL_r/A_r.                              (106.27)
```

This removes the old exceptional bulk branch.  In the near strip `R>r`, so
every normalized core vector has

```text
0<=D<=r-1<R,
C^2<=D(B+D),
(r-1)|v_(r-1)|^2<=D.                                (106.28)
```

At a hypothetical boundary zero the exact scalar equation is

```text
D-z+w(z+B+D)+2yC+delta|v_(r-1)|^2=0,   y^2=w.        (106.29)
```

Without the last term, the proved Section 59 margin excludes the crossing.
The rotating continuation is therefore reduced to controlling one explicit
boundary mass, rather than a possible Rayleigh parameter `D>R`.

This last term cannot be discarded using only the real-anchor ceiling.
There is an exact generic counterexample at

```text
m=7, r=5, B=27, R^2=55/2, u=v=1/32=1/(B+r).         (106.30)
```

All five anchor roots are positive, but the smallest one lies in
`(1/6000,1/5000)` and has strictly negative initial rotating height.  Exact
quadratic-field Sturm counts certify the root interval and the two signs in
the implicit-velocity formula.  This is not a selector cell; it proves that
the final estimate must retain more of the actual path pair `(u,v)`.

### 106.3 One polar derivative isolates a sharper strip theorem

There is a smaller reduction than (106.24)--(106.29).  Put `k=m-1` and

```text
H_u=4P_B[(q+u/4)(4q-d)^k].
```

One Meixner recurrence gives the exact identities

```text
H_u=M_(k+1)+(d+u)M_k,
(B+k)H_u=A_uM_k+d(d+4)M_k',                         (106.30)
A_u=4(z-k)+u(B+k)-kd.
```

After the Section 59 substitution the zero equation is
`A_uQ_k-dkQ_(k-1)=0`, hence a last-coordinate perturbation of the safe
degree-`k` core with `delta=(B+k-1)dk/A_u`.  At a rotating-boundary zero,
putting

```text
X=4(D-z)+d(B+2D),
Y=(B+k-1)dk(d+4)p
```

gives the cleared necessary equation

```text
(A_uX+Y)^2=4A_u^2 C^2d(d+4),                        (106.31)
0<=D<=k-1<R, C^2<=D(B+D), (k-1)p<=D.
```

At the real anchor, with `theta=k-R`, the constant-term sign is exactly
`u/4-theta/(B+k)`.  At the opposite endpoint the leading coefficient has
the sign of `u/4-R/B`.  This identifies the natural universal statement:

```text
B>=3k-1, k-1<R<k,
4(k-R)/(B+k)<u<4R/B                              (106.32)
```

should imply that every zero of `H_u(d;Rs^2)` lies in
`Im(d/s)>0`.  **Correction (2026-08-13):** the second polar derivative does
not then follow from Laguerre, because its point `-v` lies inside rather than
outside that half-plane.  The exact counterexample below has both `u,v`
inside (106.32), the first-polar polynomial on the desired side, and the
second-polar polynomial outside it.  Thus (106.32) is still a plausible
one-polar lemma, but a distinct structured two-polar theorem is also needed.

The failure of the automatic step is exact and occurs inside the same
near-strip chamber.  Take

has unique root

## 107. A three-step block-energy route has large uniform slack

The natural-coordinate recurrence in Section 106 suggests a coarser target
than direct control of the full reciprocal sequence.  Put

```text
s_j=A^(m-j)H_j^2,
T=s_(m-3)+s_(m-2)+s_(m-1),
W=A^4(H_(m-3)H_(m-1)-H_(m-2)^2)^2.
```

If the two all-order inequalities

```text
30s_j <= s_(j+1)+s_(j+2)+s_(j+3),       0<=j<=m-4,   (107.1)
W>3T,                                                   (107.2)
```

hold in the remaining post-sector cone, then the first Durán margin follows
with room to spare.  Indeed, if `E_0=sum_(j=0)^(m-4)s_j`, summing (107.1)
and counting each right-hand term at most three times gives

```text
E_0 <= (1/10)(E_0+T),       hence E_0<=T/9.             (107.3)
```

The Schur moment debt is strictly below twice the total energy, and therefore

```text
debt < 2(E_0+T) <=20T/9 <3T<W.                         (107.4)
```

Unlike adjacent-ratio monotonicity, (107.1) tolerates the internal dips and
sign changes actually present in `H_j`; it asks only for contraction over a
three-coordinate block.

An exact FLINT replay over all 36,606 post-sector cells with `m>=7` and
`d<=50` checks 827,704 instances of (107.1) and every instance of (107.2).
The largest observed left/right ratio in (107.1) is

```text
0.03312253150721723... <1/30
```

at `(d,r,s,a,m,j)=(26,5,25,0,14,10)`.  The smallest observed `W/T` is
`2251.465015915994...` at `(d,r,s,a,m)=(9,4,11,0,7)`, enormously above the
needed constant three.  This is exact finite evidence, not a proof of
(107.1)--(107.2).

The replay is `audit_lower_selector_post_sector_block_energy.py`, with report
`lower_selector_post_sector_block_energy_exact_20260812.json`.  Their SHA-256
hashes are respectively
`F01DC29A42025056E03270186414CD8B5227EB7BF198FD1901472417A36A2D11` and
`402A919F9C8305D2FC0CBF6772486B579F42C39B9F53A1FF26ACE39BC875E7F9`.
The extremal cells and ratios are unchanged from the earlier `d<=30` audit;
the larger scope is still exact finite evidence, not an all-order theorem.

There is a useful exact transfer-function interpretation of the same
recurrence.  Put `R^2=A`, normalize

```text
P(z)=q(Rz)/q_0,
P*(z)=z^mP(1/z),
```

and expand at the origin.  Equations (106.9)--(106.10) give

```text
P*(z)/P(z)=sum_(j>=0) h_j z^j,
h_j=R^(m-j)H_j.                                      (107.5)
```

Thus (107.1) is not an arbitrary coefficient inequality: it is a
three-step energy-growth theorem for the initial impulse response of the
reciprocal transfer function `P*/P` attached to the path selector.  This
opens a Schur--Levinson or pole-residue route to the all-order bound.  The
identity (107.5) is algebraic; no root-location assumption is being made.

### 107.1 A bounded sixth-degree Cohn core is an alternative exact target

The stronger block-energy estimates are not logically necessary for the
Schur exterior-index bound.  Apply the radical-free Cohn descent to
`P_m(z)=q(Rz)`, `R^2=A`.  If `b_0,...,b_n` are the ascending coefficients
before powers of `R` are installed, then

```text
kappa_n^2=A^n(b_n/b_0)^2,
b'_j=b_j-A^(n-j)(b_n/b_0)b_(n-j),       0<=j<n.        (107.6)
```

Cohn's rule sends the disk count `I_(n-1)` either to itself or to
`n-I_(n-1)`, according as `kappa_n^2<1` or `>1`.  Consequently the interval

```text
2<=I_n<=n-2                                               (107.7)
```

is invariant upward.  It suffices all-order to exclude unit pivots and show
that the terminal degree-six core has `I_6 in {2,3,4}`.  Equivalently, only
fourteen of the sixty-four six-bit reflection words must be excluded.  This
is an all-order Cohn reduction, not yet the required path theorem.

An exact audit of 7,106 post-sector cells through `d<=30` finds no unit pivot
or forbidden word; the `I_6` histogram is `832,4322,1952` for counts
`2,3,4`.  Degree five is genuinely too short: the path cell
`(d,r,s,a,m)=(13,8,22,2,11)` has `I_5=4`.  The note, replay, and report are
`LOWER_SELECTOR_POST_SECTOR_SIXTH_COHN_CORE_REDUCTION_2026-08-13.md`,
`verify_lower_selector_post_sector_sixth_cohn_core_reduction.py`, and
`lower_selector_post_sector_sixth_cohn_core_exact_20260813.json`, with
SHA-256 hashes
`DAFB3832EE2878D3C509BAE2843C50CDFF57AF1011B33373714DB7D4E06BD548`,
`DE8C4AFEE955230916E82702027489660376309CE7A86C09473E1DFEE5BEF209`,
and
`F48A840E529B04928374692E81C01EB456FE35E03E16349746C14C9479CF4C03`.

## 108. The required affine reflection atoms are all curvature-safe in the exact census

The Laguerre--Jensen affine route has a material scope correction.  The
offset-two reflection argument uses only the four-layer windows

```text
h-1,h,h+1,h+2,       h=t-ell-1,       1<=ell<t-1,
```

where `t` is the last negative Euler layer.  Across the 20 hard source
records there are 953 such windows, and their left-half defect
`s=n-2h-2` lies in `[12,47]`.  Reconstructing all active two-colour atoms in
these windows gives 4,062,983 exact adjacent-curvature comparisons and no
failure.  Summing every complete binomial colour fibre gives another 97,608
exact comparisons and no failure; the least fibre quotient among the four
families is approximately `1.00619414`.

This removes an apparent obstruction from the earlier unrestricted atom
probe.  Its 82 failures at `(c,m,x,n,h)=(30,3,0,32,15)` are genuine, but
that order has no negative Euler layer, hence no reflection window at all.
The correction does not close the route: positive mixtures of safe fibres
still require the exact covariance budget, and colour splitting can reduce
the merged slack.  A genuine required atom has the exact split/merged ratio

```text
58117856319/58140880000 < 1.
```

The standalone exact census is
`AFFINE_BRIDGE_REQUIRED_WINDOW_CURVATURE_CENSUS_2026-08-13.md`, replayed by
`verify_affine_bridge_required_window_curvature.py` into
`affine_bridge_required_window_curvature_exact_20260813.json`.  The source,
note, and report SHA-256 hashes are respectively
`185C34FE48C3564FB14A2F937AF4BFEB0666FE46BD419DDE91EC4F642CA970AF`,
`1517073C557989E60BE33633D81BB5ACB35EBA7350A9A07A6F18D45C98320EE5`,
and
`292726D5997F6608A929D2D24616C899D1F3B87657C97E5E5E4BA902298BDC3C`.
This is exact finite evidence and a corrected analytic target, not an
all-order theorem.

### 108.1 An exact all-window fibre covariance lemma

The remaining positive-summation issue has a sharp all-order reduction.
For a positive component mixture on four consecutive positive layers,
normalize the component law at layer `h-1` and put

```text
x=a_h/a_(h-1),       y=a_(h+1)/a_h,       z=a_(h+2)/a_(h+1),
Q_theta=xz/y^2.
```

The full adjacent-curvature quotient is

```text
Q_mix=(E x)^3 E(xyz)/(E(xy))^3.                    (108.1)
```

If every component has `Q_theta>=lambda`, then

```text
xyz=Q_theta y^3 >= lambda y^3.
```

Jensen's inequality gives `E(y^3)>=(E y)^3`; substituting into (108.1)
proves the all-order bound

```text
Q_mix >= lambda {E x E y/E(xy)}^3.                 (108.2)
```

Thus the weakest component reserve pays exactly the cube of the
multiplicative covariance inflation.  In particular, it is sufficient that

```text
lambda >= {E(xy)/(E x E y)}^3.                     (108.3)
```

Use as components the complete binomial colour fibres from Section 108,
one for each specialized source monomial `(p,q)`.  Although 209 windows
contain births of individual `v` branches, another branch of the same fibre
is already active: the exact replay finds **zero complete-fibre births**.
Thus the four positive fibre layers needed above exist in all 953 required
windows, with the branch inflow retained inside `Q_theta` rather than
discarded.  An independent exact rational replay checks (108.3) in all 953
windows.  Every one has strictly positive `Cov(x,y)`, so this is a genuine
payment rather than the easy opposite-covariance case.  There is no failure.
The least ratio of the two sides of (108.3) is approximately
`1.0039956063`, at the bottom/odd point

```text
(m,x,n,h)=(30,60,50,11).
```

At that point the weakest fibre quotient is approximately `1.0061941426`,
the covariance inflation is approximately `1.0007293968`, and the full
mixture quotient is approximately `1.0062325511`.

The derivation of (108.2) is all-order.  What remains unproved all-order are
the two matched path-source hypotheses in (108.3): a lower bound for every
complete fibre quotient and an upper bound for its multiplicative covariance
inflation.  The earlier branch-level inflow identity remains correct, but it
is no longer a separate case after complete-fibre aggregation.

### 108.2 Complete colour fibres have an all-order merged payment

The complete `v`-fibre admits a second exact collapse.  Fix a source
monomial `z^p w^q`, write `A=(1+z)(1+w)` and
`T=z(1+z)+w(1+w)`, and let `a,b,D` be the outer exponents and diagonal
target.  Summing all `v` branches with their binomial weights gives

```text
F_j=[z^(D-p)w^(D-q)](z+w)^j A^a T^b.              (108.4)
```

Put `d=p+q`, `E=2a+b`, and `alpha=2D-d-b`.  Merging the two colours is
equivalent to setting `z=w=u`, so the corresponding total-degree row is

```text
S_j=2^(j+b) binom(E,alpha-j).                      (108.5)
```

For the binomially weighted rows `binom(n,j)F_j` and
`binom(n,j)S_j`, let `Q_fib` and `Q_mer` denote the adjacent-curvature
quotients and set `P_j=F_j/S_j`.  Direct cancellation gives the exact
third-difference factorization

```text
Q_fib=Q_mer C_h,
C_h=P_h^3 P_(h+2)/(P_(h-1)P_(h+1)^3)
   =exp(Delta^3 log P_(h-1)).                      (108.6)
```

Writing `t=E-alpha`, the merged factor is explicit:

```text
Q_mer={(h+1)^2(n-h+1)(n-h-1)
       ·((alpha-h)^2-1)(t+h+1)^2}
      /{h(h+2)(n-h)^2
       ·(alpha-h)^2(t+h)(t+h+2)}.             (108.7)
```

There is now an all-order quantitative payment theorem in the actual
merged-atom chamber.  If

```text
n=2h+2+s,  alpha=2n-2+q-s,
h>=1, s,q>=0, 0<=t<=2h+28s,
```

then

```text
Q_mer (1-1/(hE^2)) >= 1.                          (108.8)
```

For completeness, the exact proof first differentiates the only
`t`-dependent factor in (108.8).  Half of its logarithmic derivative is

```text
1/{E(hE^2-1)}-1/{r(r+1)(r+2)},    r=t+h.
```

After `h=H+1`, `r=R+1`, and `E=r+1+G`, the denominator comparison has 16
nonnegative integer monomials, so the expression is nonpositive and the
minimum occurs at `t=2h+28s`.  At that endpoint, clearing the positive
denominator after `h=H+1` produces 178 monomials in `H,s,q`, all with
strictly positive integer coefficients.  This proves (108.8) in all orders.

Consequently the sole remaining local colour-split inequality is

```text
C_h >= 1-1/(hE^2).                                (108.9)
```

The independent exact replay checks all 97,608 required fibres and finds no
failure of (108.9).  The largest observed normalized loss `hE^2(1-C_h)` is
the exact rational

```text
609169870400311398680248835590868262369716236389054
------------------------------------------------------
680890970721832913217688814318022549144355612330207
=0.8946658078818583... .
```

It occurs at the bottom/even cell
`(m,x,n,h,p,q,E)=(3,48,37,3,5,5,106)`.  The stronger constant `1/2` is
false in 574 audited cells, so (108.9) has the correct tested scale.  The
merged-payment theorem (108.8) is all-order; (108.9) remains an explicit
hypergeometric third-difference conjecture, supported here only by the exact
required-window census.

The coarse parameter chamber displayed before (108.8) is not sufficient by
itself to prove (108.9).  There is an exact bottom/odd path fibre with

```text

(a,b,D,E,alpha,t,s,q_*)=(64,7,62,135,108,27,45,45),
```

for which `hE^2(1-C_h)=1.7570123049...>1`.  Thus (108.9) fails even
with `s>=12`, `q_*>=1`, and `t<=2h+11s`.  This is not a counterexample in
a required reflection window: the exact Euler-negative layers of the same
order are only `0,1,2,3`, so their last index is `t_-=3` and the reflection
construction permits only `h<=t_--2=1`.  The successful required-window
census therefore uses an essential extra invariant tying `h` to the actual
Euler sign source.  Any all-order proof of (108.9) must retain that invariant
rather than proving a free chamber inequality.  The counterexample note and

### 108.3 Degree ULC gives a telescoped covariance envelope

There is also a parameter-explicit route to the second hypothesis of
(108.3).  Aggregate the complete fibres by source degree `d=p+q`, let
`F_(d,j)` be the resulting degree row, and put

```text
x_d={binom(n,h)F_(d,h)}/{binom(n,h-1)F_(d,h-1)},
y_d={binom(n,h+1)F_(d,h+1)}/{binom(n,h)F_(d,h)}.    (108.10)
```

For `alpha_d=2D-d-b` and `t_d=E-alpha_d`, the corresponding merged ratios
are the rational functions

```text
xbar_d=2(n-h+1)(alpha_d-h+1)/{h(t_d+h)},
ybar_d=2(n-h)(alpha_d-h)/{(h+1)(t_d+h+1)}.          (108.11)
```

Their exact relative drops under `d -> d+1` are

```text
b^x_d=(E+1)/{(alpha_d-h)(t_d+h)},
b^y_d=(E+1)/{(alpha_d-h-1)(t_d+h+1)}.              (108.12)
```

The path-specific adjacent TP2 target is

```text
x_d>=x_(d+1), y_d>=y_(d+1),
x_d/x_(d+1)-1<=b^x_d,
y_d/y_(d+1)-1<=b^y_d.                              (108.13)
```

Unlike a generic smoothness estimate, (108.13) telescopes exactly.  If the
degree support is `[d_0,d_1]` of length `L`, set

```text
A=alpha_(d_1)-h, B=t_(d_1)+h, B_0=B-L.
```

Then (108.12)--(108.13) imply

```text
Lip(x)/min(x) <= R_x=(E+1)B/{(A+1)B_0(B_0+1)},
Lip(y)/min(y) <= R_y=(E+1)(B+1)/{A(B_0+1)(B_0+2)}. (108.14)
```

Indeed `1+b^x_d` and `1+b^y_d` each factor into two consecutive ratios;
all intermediate terms cancel.  The group support is `[8,16]`, so `L=8`,
and the bottom support is `[9,20]`, so `L=11`.

Now normalize the degree masses at layer `h-1`.  If
`pi_(d_0+k)/binom(L,k)` is log-concave, the ULC variance theorem gives

```text
Var(D)<=mu(1-mu/L)<=L/4.                           (108.15)
```

A short proof uses the decreasing ratios
`theta_k=(k+1)pi_(k+1)/{(L-k)pi_k}` and opposite-monotonicity covariance
under the law tilted by `L-k`; it yields
`E[D(D-1)]<=(L-1)mu^2/L`, equivalent to (108.15).  The independent-copy
identity then gives

```text
Cov(x(D),y(D))<=Lip(x)Lip(y)Var(D),
E(xy)/(E x E y)<=1+(L/4)R_xR_y.                   (108.16)
```

Together with the scalar split target (108.9), every fibre quotient is at
least

```text
lambda_0=Q_mer(d_1)(1-1/(hE^2)),                  (108.17)
```

because the right side decreases with source degree.  The full covariance
payment has therefore become the single explicit rational comparison

```text
lambda_0 >= {1+(L/4)R_xR_y}^3.                    (108.18)
```

An exact replay checks 9,341 normalized-ULC inequalities and 10,294
adjacent degree pairs across all 953 required windows.  There are no ULC,
monotonicity, merged-slope, or conditional-budget failures.  The minimum
ratio in (108.18) is the exact rational

```text
2668108644301520997643045021124544000
-------------------------------------
2662206447053091578702031257710948409
=1.002217032136994...,
```

again at the bottom/odd cell `(m,x,n,h)=(30,60,50,11)`.  There
`lambda_0=1.006192530060623...` and the inflation increment in (108.16) is
at most `0.001320490076527...`.

The ULC variance lemma, independent-copy covariance lemma, telescoping, and
the reduction to (108.18) are all-order.  The path-specific ULC and TP2
hypotheses (108.13), scalar split bound (108.9), and parameter-uniform proof
of (108.18) remain open; the replay is exact finite evidence for them.

### 108.4 The exact Euler-coupled target bypasses the split/covariance split

There is a strictly weaker direct target.  Let

```text
a_j=binom(n,j)rho_j.
```

Then the full unsplit reserve-mixture curvature is exactly

```text
Q_h=a_h^3 a_(h+2)/(a_(h-1)a_(h+1)^3).             (108.19)
```

Thus `Q_h>=1` closes the required adjacent curvature without separately
proving the fibre split conjecture (108.9) and the covariance envelope.
Among all 17,970 audited positive four-layer hard windows, the condition
that actually characterizes the reflection range is `e_(h+2)<0`: exactly
953 windows satisfy it, none fails (108.19), and the least exact quotient is
`1.0062325511287966...`.  Outside that range, 9,077 of 17,017 windows fail,
with quotient as low as `0.7500225...`.  This makes the Euler sign a sharp
empirical separator.

An even stronger source-coupled scalar target also passes all 953 windows:

```text
Q_h >= 1-g_(h+2)/(hn),       g_j=e_j/rho_j.        (108.20)
```

After clearing positive factors, (108.20) is one explicit polynomial
inequality coupling the exact `Q` and `R` source packages.  Its least
observed normalized surplus is `1.0863701676...`.

Neither the Euler sign nor generic PF-infinity structure proves (108.19).
There is an exact degree-18 PF-infinity row, obtained from roots
`{1,1,3,3,5,10,20,20,1/1000 repeated ten times}`, with `h=5`, last negative
index `t=7`, `n=2t+4`, and a nondecreasing convex sign source
`g_j=-1` through `7`, `g_j=1` afterward, but `Q_5=0.9351494...<1`.
Therefore an all-order proof must retain the precise path relation between
the two source packages; abstract Euler sign, endpoint slack, PF-infinity,
determinant orientation, and negative-side convexity are insufficient.

Even strict univariate differential proper position is insufficient.  Let
`R(y)=prod_(i=1)^18(1+r_i y)` for the distinct positive rational values
`{1,101/100,3,301/100,5,10,20,2001/100,1/1000,...,10/1000}` and set
`E=yR'-8R`.  Then `R` is PF-infinity, `E` strictly interlaces `R` with one
positive root, `e_j=(j-8)rho_j`, `n=18=2t+4`, and `e_7<0`; nevertheless at
`h=5` the exact quotient is `Q_5=0.9434950932...<1`, and the stronger
coupled target fails as well.  Thus a proof must use the literal finite
path-source relation, not only Euler coupling plus interlacing.  An
additional exact 208-record path lattice contains 64 genuine windows and no
failure, but that remains finite evidence.  The theorem/no-go note, replay,

## 109. Clean-room dependency audit: the endpoint is not yet the forest theorem

An independent clean-room audit confirms that the fixed-layer advances and
the all-order reductions above do not yet yield forest unimodality.  The
shortest sufficient statement remains the prefix pendant cascade `PGC` from
the earlier reduction: once that inequality is proved for every forest and
pendant edge, iteration gives prefix GSB and meets the known bipartite
decreasing tail.

Within the present analytic architecture, the remaining cut set has three
genuinely mathematical packages:

1. a uniform lower/group-reserve theorem, including shifted lower rows and
   common-homogenizer compatibility;
2. both original group and bottom affine coefficient families in both
   parities;
3. the protected/Lambda/nested/mixed bridge through P1--P4 and the remaining
   collision/rank-four recurrences to PGC.

Rowwise stability does not imply the common homogenizer: the homogeneous
pieces of `1+x+y+2xy` are stable, but its diagonal `1+2z+2z^2` is not.
Likewise, benign PF convolution cannot replace the protected forest
coupling.  The exact rows

```text
A=(1+x/3)^3, Q=(1+2x)(1+3x), P=A+xQ
```

have positive rank-two and rank-three cascade margins, but multiplying both
`P,Q` by `1+x` changes the rank-two margin to `-50/27`.  This is a no-go for
the abstract PF-lifting shortcut, not a counterexample to PGC for forests.

There is a stronger exact no-go.  The integer polynomials

```text
P=(1+400x+2x^2)(1+2x)(1+8x),
Q=(1+380x+4x^2)(1+7x),
A=P-xQ
```

all have only negative real roots, and `Q` strictly interlaces `P`.  Yet at
the required prefix rank `k=2`, `H_2(P)-H_1(Q)=-89032/5`.  Thus even the
deletion identity `P=A+xQ`, PF-infinity of all three rows, and strict proper
position do not imply PGC abstractly.  Elementary order-two forest
coefficient bounds show these integer rows are not forest independence
polynomials, so this is not a forest counterexample; it isolates the missing
input as genuinely forest-specific.  The note, replay, and report are
`DIRECT_PGC_REAL_ROOTED_NOGO_AND_PROTECTED_AUDIT_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_direct_pgc_real_rooted_nogo.py`, and
`direct_pgc_real_rooted_nogo_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`991997CD16A4B4D9413EA1B71B958CF9B1B5CCA5DBCCD0D0A694226C95517320`,
`6BDD15340D0F162DB1EA6BA1233DD3962A46246504321F7D446593E8D8322CAF`,
and
`E989AC6D17CC1595259C05394EE6B444F520EDC6E538074DCA3C5562B9A69D35`.

The audit note, replay, and report are
`GLOBAL_PROOF_CHAIN_WAVE3_AUDIT_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_global_proof_chain_wave3_audit.py`, and
`global_proof_chain_wave3_audit_exact_20260813.json`; their SHA-256 hashes
are respectively
`6DD026F736204F862B3D2EB98D045F5C7A05D2789DED1DB8EF2AABCEE9883F94`,
`FBE2F5FC9D009AD3A3556DFA472089A2C1D1787F4C011FF99B02E1AB53EC6BBE`,
and
`F537ED77C24892CE057C60DFF0AAE393B05CA4EB5516D391F88BDDFBF6D5CF22`.

### 109.1 Higher-order adversarial tree search remains negative

A new non-duplicative direct search uses the exact rooted-tree recurrence

```text
E_v=product T_child,
S_v=x product E_child,
T_v=E_v+S_v
```

under subtree-prune/reattach and leaf mutations, deduplicating complete
labeled edge encodings and scoring only the possible pre-tail rebound ranks.
At orders `60,72,84,96,108,120` it examined 6,071,001 distinct encodings and
found no nonunimodal tree.  The closest order-120 pre-tail ratio is exactly

```text
37480091378342716583828916 /
39671334157813943451518821 < 1.
```

An independent arbitrary-integer implementation reconstructs all six
champions.  This is broad adversarial finite evidence, not an exhaustive
isomorphism census or a proof.  The report, campaign data, independent
verification, and source are `ADVERSARIAL_TREE_DP_SEARCH_2026-08-13.md`,
`adversarial_tree_dp_search_6orders_g600_20260813.json`,
`adversarial_tree_dp_search_6orders_g600_verified_20260813.json`, and
`adversarial_tree_dp_search.cpp`, with SHA-256 hashes

### 109.2 PGC has an exact component-separated pendant normal form

For a pendant leaf `l` with neighbor `p`, let

```text
F=G-{l,p},       S=N_G(p)\{l},
B=I(F),          C=I(F-S).
```

Then exactly

```text
I(G)=(1+x)B+xC.                                      (109.1)
```

Because `G` is a forest, `S` meets each component of `F` at most once;
conversely, every product of rooted component pairs with this property
constructs a pendant forest.  Thus PGC can be attacked directly on

```text
B=product_i B_i,       C=product_i C_i,
```

where each `C_i` is either `B_i` or the independence polynomial obtained by
deleting one marked vertex in that component.  This is the exact forest
coupling missing from the abstract PF counterexamples above.

Even the stronger nested algebra `A=B+xC`, `P=A+xB` plus PF-infinity and
strict interlacing does not suffice abstractly.  There are exact PF-infinity
rows `A,B,C,P` with `C` interlacing `B` interlacing `P` but

```text
H_2(P)-H_1(B)=-1544450/59<0.
```

These rows are not forest-realizable (their first two coefficients violate
necessary deletion bounds), so this is a failure shield, not a forest
counterexample.  The note, replay, and report are
`COMPONENT_SEPARATED_PGC_REDUCTION_AND_NOGO_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_component_separated_pgc_nogo.py`, and
`component_separated_pgc_nogo_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`4406FAC7A7D60FDC5840BC87C9737C4BF4E32AC956936A705E84F35ACA6852C0`,
`408CE4652087DAE472BD537983C5377B6DC6B6061C0FBE58AEB5268DC5E62ADB`,
and
`180E44A77756E945E9BF3FE87EF1DCA826BB84508942672BC15147A6486C704D`.
The existing exact order-16 census covers 332,799 pendant instances and
1,511,925 prefix ranks without failure; it remains finite evidence.

The component coupling has a sharper marked-root polarization.  For every
touched component write

```text
B_i=C_i+xD_i,       D_i=I(F_i-N[s_i]),
Phi(x;y)=U(x) product_i(C_i+y_i xD_i).
```

Then `B=Phi(x;1)` and `C=Phi(x;0)`.  If `q_r=[x^r]C/[x^r]B`, PGC becomes one
explicit six-scalar inequality coupling three adjacent coefficient ratios of
`B` with `q_(k-2),q_(k-1),q_k`.  This is an exact forest-specific reduction.

Canonical conditioning is not negatively associated in the needed sense.
Two marked `K_(1,2)` components at rank two have root-count polynomial
`6+4y+y^2`, discriminant `-8`, positive covariance `2/121`, and total
variance `52/121`, exceeding the sum `48/121` of marginal variances.  Four
marked edges plus one marked `K_(1,8)` at rank five give
`792+1321y+724y^2+150y^3+12y^4+y^5`, which is not even log-concave at the
top (`12^2<150`).  Both forests nevertheless satisfy every required PGC
rank exactly.  Thus naive variance tensorization, negative association, and
root-count log-concavity are false shortcuts, not PGC counterexamples.  The
note, replay, and report are
`COMPONENT_ROOT_OCCUPATION_POLARIZATION_AND_NOGO_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_component_root_occupation_polarization.py`, and
`component_root_occupation_polarization_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256
hashes
`120D89FC4C320F674A8493AD720E5F0A9D158B680BAC76624775AA48300F56E1`,
`7EB1AD21D006D745155D4C1BDC33C146972F20A24A31D4936B282B4CEAC3496B`,
and
`0247018E5652E5A32C9DA5FA76CDCC0DCA332A940C6520F3690BFA0481909FC5`.

### 109.3 Independent literature and normalized-Schur audit

An independent search dated 2026-08-13 still finds Problem 993 publicly
open.  The Erdős Problems entry and the current public repository claim no
solution; the newest primary papers found treat special tree families,
existence constructions, or weaker Schur/log-concavity properties rather
than all forests.

Algebraically,

```text
H_k(r)=k^2(r_k^2-r_(k-1)r_(k+1))/r_(k-1)
       +k(r_k-r_(k+1)).                               (109.2)
```

Thus PGC separates into a normalized cross-rank two-row Schur margin and a
first-difference transport margin.  Existing same-rank 2-Schur positivity
does not control the component-coupled cross-rank term, and its deletion
recurrence retains a sign-indefinite error.  In an exact census of all 32,507
unlabeled trees through order 16, 244,692 pendant instances, and 1,103,823
required prefix ranks, the normalized Schur margin has no failure and exact
minimum `34/5`; this is finite evidence.  The first-difference term can be
negative (`-1463` for `K_(1,13)` at rank four), while the Schur payment
`5863` leaves PGC margin `4400`.

The audit, replay, and report are
`FINAL_ROUTE_LITERATURE_AND_SCHUR_LIFT_AUDIT_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_final_route_literature_schur_lift.py`, and
`final_route_literature_schur_lift_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`23574B116CE9D52D037432EEA872BB66FFFE9B221601624F3A645B7D7FBF8E05`,
`C35122BF99A2A00AF754BB5B5F20C153EB8D9EC75B69FFCFD60E815C91D3C25E`,
and
`86EB6EBB230C51917ED46A7C09390D73264F66FE2D541BFF20CFA39D06AB3359`.
No audited source or route closes the conjecture.

The normalized cross-rank Schur bracket is nevertheless now proved at its
first nontrivial rank.  For every pendant forest `G`, with
`P=I(G)` and `B=I(G-{l,p})`, one has the all-order theorem

```text
4 (p_2^2-p_1 p_3)/p_1 >= b_1^2-b_2.
```

Writing `n=|V(G)|`, `m=|E(G)|`, `d=deg(p)`, and
`W=sum_v binom(deg(v),2)`, exact counting reduces the cleared margin to

```text
2n^4-3n^3+7n^2-6n+24m^2-30mn+6dn-24nW.
```

The forest bounds `W<=binom(m,2)`, `m<=n-1`, and `d>=1` give the explicit
lower bound `(n-4)(n-2)(2n^2-3n+3)`, with orders two and three checked
directly.  This proves only the Schur-payment part at rank two; the
first-difference transport in (109.2), and all ranks at least three, remain
open.  A polynomial-complete forest audit through order 16 covers 332,799
pendant pair instances and 1,511,925 required ranks without an all-rank Schur
failure, but that portion is finite evidence.  The note, replay, and report
are `RANK2_COMPONENT_SCHUR_PAYMENT_AND_ALL_FOREST_AUDIT_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_rank2_component_schur_payment.py`, and
`rank2_component_schur_payment_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`CCD372C314E4BEC87DBF30A0DC777C119665B3A3422CFAE4E3C84573C3AD2CCE`,
`8491624A3BA7AC7EA20006E3FBB827E66D41B6ACB05CF4620E8FED3DD014AF93`,
and
`E46E08EE391C9826B949C028CDE79190F7D09A17775C265A280265677BABFDDD`.

The component Schur theorem is also closed all-order at rank three.  Whenever
rank three is in the PGC prefix (`alpha(G)>=6`), exact forest configuration
counting and degree-excess moment bounds prove

```text
9 (p_3^2-p_2 p_4)/p_2 >= 4 (b_2^2-b_1 b_3)/b_1.
```

The first-difference transport can be negative (`K_(1,8)` gives Schur
payment `266` and transport `-14`), so the surplus is essential.  The already
proved rank-three three-quarters cascade and rank-two forest positivity give
the uniform full PGC reserve

```text
H_3(P)-H_2(B) >= H_2(B)/3 >= 0.
```

Thus ranks two and three of the Schur-payment conjecture are now all-order
theorems, and rank-three PGC is closed; ranks at least four remain open.  The
note, replay, and report are
`RANK3_COMPONENT_SCHUR_PAYMENT_AND_TRANSPORT_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_rank3_component_schur_payment.py`, and
`rank3_component_schur_payment_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`9BAE03E5605668CA8290B10BA2E345E0BD7553F15583D5E49813989E0A27D729`,
`9016E72070698CDBF58BAA9474E0B3523E9D233A083351B6F74CB00C58361412`,
and
`A57F9550C731D70073DD52DB4B80BE20BB32AA29ED57757EA0D0B6BF629B366E`.

Rank four is now closed as well.  Whenever `alpha(G)>=7`, exact identities
with `P=(1+x)B+xC` give

```text
16 Delta_4(P)/p_3-9 Delta_3(B)/b_2
 =2Q_4(P)/p_3+2c_3+4c_4+L(B)/b_2,

H_4(P)-H_3(B)
 =2Q_4(P)/p_3+6c_3+3U(B)/b_2,
```

where `Q_4` is the proved rank-four three-halves reserve,
`U=b_2b_3+6b_2b_4-3b_3^2`, and
`L=2b_2b_3+15b_2b_4+4b_2b_5-9b_3^2`.  Literal forest configuration counts,
degree-moment bounds, exact Bernstein certificates, and the finite
`alpha=7` boundary prove `U,L>=0` in their required ranges.  Hence both the
rank-four component Schur payment and full PGC transport are nonnegative.
Negative transport is real (`K_(1,10)` has Schur `1218`, transport `-42`),
but is paid.  The note, replay, and report are
`RANK4_COMPONENT_SCHUR_PAYMENT_AND_TRANSPORT_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_rank4_component_schur_payment.py`, and
`rank4_component_schur_payment_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`134727625692B79423680E01E5331705E19BACAFF36F491CA3128F2CB974BAFA`,

and
`044C5B3955A49C4D987BE296E9FD60CA1E59A8B65672B048CF3BA7A7C12CF4CB`.
The final clean-room audit corrected the large-order edge parameter from
`1<=e<=n-2` to the full forest range `1<=e<=n-1`; the corrected replay still

Thus ranks two, three, and four are theorem-level; the first open Schur rank

### 109.4 Boundary-SM3 reduces further to a one-root augmenting-tree payment

The first conditional half of the exceptional scaled-three leaf boundary has
a sharper component-labelled form.  In the notation

```text
F=T-p,       H=T-N[p],       alpha(T)=alpha(F)=beta,
r=floor(2 beta/3),       D_r(H)=3h_r-h_(r-1),
```

the neighbors `S=N_T(p)` lie in distinct components of `F`.  With the exact
marked-root polarization

```text
Phi(x,y)=product_i(C_i(x)+y x D_i(x)),
H(x)=Phi(x,0),
M_1(x)=[y]Phi(x,y),
```

`m_(r,1)=[x^r]M_1` counts the independent `r`-sets of `F` containing exactly
one neighbor of `p`.  Therefore

```text
D_r(H)<0  and  h_r+m_(r,1)>=h_(r-1)                  (109.3)
```

imply the surviving conditional payment `f_r>=h_(r-1)`.  The second
inequality in (109.3) is the new **one-root boundary payment** target.

It also has a precise matching formulation.  Join an independent
`(r-1)`-set of `H` to an independent `r`-set of `F` containing at most one
marked root when their symmetric difference induces a connected tree.
Such an edge is a minimal independent-set augmenting-tree move.  A matching
saturating the smaller rank proves (109.3).

The exact bounded audit through every unlabeled tree of order 16 checks
54,772 conditional prefix instances and finds no failure of (109.3).  The
two largest individual neighbor-branch contributions already suffice in all
54,772 instances; one branch alone fails 432 times.  This two-branch
sharpening is finite evidence only.  The
matching replay through order 12 covers 807 negative-closed-row pendant
setups and finds no one-root matching failure.  This is evidence, not an
all-order Hall theorem.  The condition and exchange scope are both sharp
against simple shortcuts: unrestricted prefix half occupation fails 3,400
times, ordinary inclusion matching first fails at order 9, a fixed-root map
first collides at order 7, and a naive degree bound first fails at order 9.

The note and replays are
`BOUNDARY_SM3_ONE_ROOT_AUGMENTING_TREE_REDUCTION_2026-08-13.md`,
`probe_noncore_vertex_half_occupation.py`, and
`probe_boundary_sm3_connected_injection.py`.  This advances only the first
conditional half.  Boundary-SM3 still additionally requires

```text
D_(r+1)(F)+D_r(F)>=f_r.
```

The ORP structure can be sharpened to two critical root channels.  For a
rooted branch put `C_i=F_i-s_i`, `D_i=F_i-N[s_i]`, and call it critical when
`alpha(D_i)=alpha(C_i)`.  If `t` is the number of critical branches, then the
all-order identity

```text
beta-alpha(H)=t
```

is immediate from `alpha(F_i)=max(alpha(C_i),1+alpha(D_i))`.  If
`mu_i=[x^(r-1)]I(D_i) product_(j!=i)I(C_j)`, the codomain consisting of
`I_r(H)` and two labelled critical-root channels has exact size
`h_r+mu_i+mu_j`.  Through every tree of order 13, the two largest critical
channels pass both this scalar payment and the full connected Hall test in
all 1,485 negative exceptional setups; one critical channel fails seven
times.  The missing all-order assertions are the sign-sensitive lower bound
on `t`, the two-critical scalar payment, and, for the stronger injection, the
proper-subfamily Hall inequalities.  Whole-rank cardinality is not equivalent
to the matching theorem.  The exact reduction, replay, and report are
`ORP_TWO_CRITICAL_HALL_REDUCTION_2026-08-13.md`,
`probe_orp_two_critical_hall.py`, and `orp_two_critical_hall_n13.json`, with
SHA-256 hashes
`03D067F03C257183BAA74738D9EDB582BFDBFD66FB7E31FD958DA6FB8426C43F`,
`559F399D377CB3C5D25958D5E3E2CFEEFC2E31891EE30C09CB01BFC5DE1A4CC4`,
and
`478CEC8037B9E9D7E11CA140BC93B50227D88AA2C161E2847AE23D94B8CF74E8`.

That last conditional inequality is now **exactly false** in the required
tree setting.  Let `T_m` have one center, `m` adjacent supports, and two
leaves at each support, so

```text
I(T_m)=(1+3x+x^2)^m+x(1+x)^(2m).
```

Take `F=T_17 union 3K_1`, join a new vertex `p` to the center and the three
isolates, and then attach a leaf to `p`.  With `H=17K_(1,2)`, `beta=38`, and
`r=25`, exact graph dynamic programming and the closed product formulas agree
on

```text
D_25(H)=-107372408<0,
3f_26+f_25-f_24=-339459400<0.
```

Thus the proposed second half, and therefore the two-payment split, cannot be
used all-order.  The stronger single target (14) of the fugacity-three note is
also false here by `-446831808`.  Crucially, the actual coupled Boundary-SM3
margin remains

```text
D_26(F)+D_25(F)+D_25(H)=57086629816>0,
```

and the resulting 57-vertex tree has a unimodal independence polynomial.
This is a route counterexample, not a counterexample to Problem 993.  The live
target is therefore the **unsplit** coupled Boundary-SM3 inequality.  The
exact note, replay, and report are
`BOUNDARY_SM3_SECOND_SPLIT_COUNTEREXAMPLE_2026-08-13.md`,
`verify_boundary_sm3_second_split_counterexample.py`, and
`boundary_sm3_second_split_counterexample_exact.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`3A49BEA693FCFECE726ED6A2992069BD80BE905455E089FF2BD293D1AA2F0413`,
`4AE41317D329E49DEFBDDE8A522FFE8A8E21565518C9CBE0E67A238A0B7B389D`,
and
`00C28B72FE30FF05818263AF51E76604A63662A6D7AB7C35A918E93F2205230A`.

For the whole two-parameter family behind that counterexample, the unsplit
compensation has an exact one-row form.  Put `A=1+3x+x^2`, let the branch
count be `m`, let `q` isolates also meet `p`, and write
`n=2m+q=3a+epsilon`, `epsilon in {0,1}`.  If

```text
J=A^m ((1+x)^(q+1)+x^q)=sum_j j_j x^j,
```

then direct palindromic coefficient extraction gives

```text
Boundary-SM3 margin
 =3j_a-j_(a+1)
  +3 binom(n,a)+2 binom(n,a+1)-binom(n,a+2).
```

The binomial summand is strictly positive all-order.  Thus this adversarial
family reduces to the single compensator inequality `j_(a+1)<=3j_a`, which
passes 120,200 exact parameter pairs (`m<=300`, `q<=600`) but remains
unproved.  The exact identity is valuable because it exhibits the cross-term
that both false split targets discarded.  The note, replay, and report are
`BOUNDARY_SM3_TM_FAMILY_COMPENSATING_IDENTITY_2026-08-13.md`,
`analyze_boundary_sm3_tm_family.py`, and
`boundary_sm3_tm_family_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`93EB8FE43E4C764484E3F653D06CA06B59A16056B4CDC0C028522A98FD7BC18C`,
`72027D3836E6DB3FCE44B4D1689D666BE364F0DA0ADD8A502651F71075BA2CDB`,
and
`F82B8890A5AA45683A83DA75853E790C11D13B9F829F7183D7B7A1F7A3799C31`.

That remaining compensator inequality is now an all-order theorem for this
family.  After the tilt `b_k=j_k/3^k`, the row of
`J=A^m((1+x)^(q+1)+x^q)` is unimodal: the modified binomial factor is
log-concave, `A^m` is PF, and convolution preserves log-concavity.  At
`x=1/3` the normalized row is a two-component probability mixture with
explicit mean and variance.  The lattice mean-mode bound

```text
|mean-mode| <= sqrt(3(variance+1/12))
```

then excludes modes above `a`; uniform rational tail bounds leave 9,865
finite endpoint pairs, all checked exactly.  Hence

```text
[x^(a+1)]J <= 3[x^a]J
```

for all `m>=1,q>=0`, proving the coupled Boundary-SM3 inequality for the
entire `T_m` plus isolates adversarial family.  This does not cover arbitrary
rooted branch products.  The theorem, replay, and certificate are
`BOUNDARY_SM3_TM_FAMILY_ALL_ORDER_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md`,
`verify_boundary_sm3_tm_family_all_order.py`, and
`boundary_sm3_tm_family_all_order_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`5A4117D5AEAE66C3D4C381D1B16EF1B2B7CB240783ABBCD9FF25E4CF56E594F3`,
`B175A179A3EE6F2ADA9E85B79A736D4E0FBBEEC29B6360512710D3A6EF282D1D`,
and
`192C7721D41940A90B403F53A5CB836D9906F96B0E26C9C6836FC18A4F45D824`.

The compensation is not confined to that family.  For arbitrary rooted
branches, put `B_i=C_i+xD_i`, let `alpha_i=deg B_i`, and define the reciprocal
defect states

```text
E_i=x^(alpha_i) C_i(1/x),
Z_i=x^(alpha_i-1) D_i(1/x).
```

If `beta=sum alpha_i`, `r=floor(2beta/3)`, `a=beta-r`, then the exact tensor

```text
J=(1+x) product_i(E_i+Z_i)+product_i E_i
```

satisfies the all-order identity

```text
D_(r+1)(F)+D_r(F)+D_r(H)=3[x^a]J-[x^(a+1)]J.
```

Thus the surviving unsplit Boundary-SM3 theorem for all rooted branch
products is **equivalent** to the single adjacent tensor inequality
`j_(a+1)<=3j_a`.  This is now the correct general boundary target.  It
retains all root channels before estimation; channelwise positivity is
rigorously false (`K_(1,4)` has total margin `20`, while every one-root
channel has margin `-1`).  Exact replay covers 11,336 exhaustive eligible
setups through order 13, 3,384 random rooted setups, and 12,200 random branch
products with no aggregate failure, but the general sign remains open.  The
note, replay, and report are
`BOUNDARY_SM3_RECIPROCAL_TENSOR_COMPENSATION_2026-08-13.md`,
`verify_boundary_sm3_tensor_compensation.py`, and
`boundary_sm3_tensor_compensation_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256 hashes
`38301F63360B4EA4E652DFE60CFCEE252B0716333031DF0B39FD80632C5D18FA`,

`5FE2C8945321B4FE193E83B4D9781CC49687FB0325689BF88F4084391218CB4D`.

The `T_m` tilted mean-mode proof extends conditionally to the general tensor:
if `J=U+V`, exact aggregate log-concavity is

```text
Delta_k(J)=Delta_k(U)+Delta_k(V)
           +2u_kv_k-u_(k-1)v_(k+1)-v_(k-1)u_(k+1)>=0,
```

and explicit `x=1/3` mixture moments plus the lattice mean-mode reserve imply
the desired crossing.  These hypotheses are not universal.  The two known

### 109.5 Critical-last recurrence and the exact remaining boundary overshoot

The general tensor has a sharper append order when one last branch is
critical.  Write its root-deletion rows as `C,D`, with equal degree `c`, and
let `F_0,G_0` be the forest and final-leaf tree formed by all preceding
branches.  If `beta` is the common final independence number and
`r=floor(2beta/3)`, then direct reciprocity gives the all-order identity

```text
Boundary-SM3 margin
 =D_(r+1)(C union G_0)+D_r(D union F_0 union K_1).
```

The second term lies inside the ordinary SM3 prefix.  Thus the only new sign
obligation is the special one-rank overshoot

```text
D_(floor(2beta/3)+1)(C union G_0)>=0
```

for forests produced by a critical-last rooted branch.  This restriction is
essential: arbitrary append order has a literal order-seven negative payment,
and unrestricted one-rank overshoot already fails for `P_5` by `D_3=-3`.
Exact replay checks 42,438 critical-last choices through tree order 14 and
33,350 eligible random rooted products with no negative term, but this is
bounded evidence, not a sign theorem.  The note, replay, and report are
`BOUNDARY_SM3_CRITICAL_LAST_RECURRENCE_REDUCTION_2026-08-13.md`,
`verify_boundary_sm3_critical_last_recurrence.py`, and
`boundary_sm3_critical_last_recurrence_exact_20260813.json`, with SHA-256
hashes
`627B7AAE653ADE804A12B8F095C0798EAD03658578DF4AB88AEDF43E6B23D1A7`,
`08309E5FCE396EC7F71603B5F063CB801AD0AE1CB9BEC86374D5E72C8DB2DB7F`,
and
`1C9DB37A026C56859ADB665B634DBB6B214E4C519166265FD8922BFBE4EBAA18`.

### 109.6 Global completion audit: exact open cut

The clean-room dependency audit leaves the shortest sufficient chain as

```text
PGC for every required rank k>=6
 -> pendant iteration
 -> prefix GSB
 -> the known bipartite decreasing tail
 -> unimodality.
```

Ranks two through five are now proved (rank five is closed in Section
109.7 below).  Boundary-SM3 is a separate
coefficient induction: even a proof of its tensor sign would not propagate
the factorial-curvature/normalized-Schur summand needed by higher-rank PGC.
An exact arbitrary-row failure shield satisfies the displayed low-rank PGC
and factor-three inputs but has a negative rank-five gap; it is not a forest
counterexample, but it rules out the proposed algebraic induction without a
new forest-specific invariant.  That failure shield explains why the
rank-five proof needs the additional forest defect ceiling used below; it no
longer marks the live cut.  The first open PGC rank is now six, and Problem
993 remains open.  The final audit, replay, and report are
`WAVE12_GLOBAL_COMPLETION_AUDIT_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_wave12_global_completion_audit.py`, and
`wave12_global_completion_audit_exact_20260813.json`.

### 109.7 Rank-five PGC is now closed all-order

The first open rank from the completion audit admits a short exact
decomposition.  For a pendant edge `lp`, put `P=I(G)`,
`B=I(G-{l,p})`, and write `P=(1+x)B+xC`.  Then

```text
H_5(P)-H_4(B)
 =5Q_5(P)/(2p_4)+15c_4/2+V_5(B)/(2b_3),

V_5(B)=7b_3b_4+55b_3b_5-32b_4^2.
```

The rank-five forest theorem gives `Q_5(P)>=0`; the middle term is
manifestly nonnegative.  The remaining `V_5` inequality follows from two
complementary certified forest inputs.  If `6b_4>=13b_3`, subtracting eleven
times the forest two-extension inequality leaves

```text
2b_4(6b_4-13b_3)>=0.
```

If `6b_4<13b_3`, subtracting `55/3575` times the sharp rank-(3,4,5)
defect ceiling leaves

```text
b_4(5005b_3-704b_4)/715>0,
```

because `5005*6-704*13=20878`.  The defect theorem covers every forest
of order at least 16.  The complete remaining boundary `order<=15` and
`alpha(B)>=8` consists of 25,996 distinct forest rows; all pass, with
minimum `V_5=43120` at `(1+x)^8`.  Hence

```text
H_5(I(G))>=H_4(I(G-{l,p}))
```

for every required rank-five pendant instance (`alpha(G)>=9`).  An
independent polynomial-complete audit checks 294,045 pendant pairs through
order 16, with exact minimum margin `2552316/3953`.  Therefore PGC is now
proved at ranks two through five, and rank six is the first open rank.

The theorem, replay, and report are
`RANK5_COMPONENT_PGC_PAYMENT_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md`,
`replay_rank5_component_pgc_payment.py`, and

`DA6E12D3EE511E5CD028FC565D401643463EE2CCDAD96ABFCBA1E11D4B83F79B`,

and
`286BD3AAE113FCFB6C589BD878E6C5910EB94277BC5D25F2AC51AF2BCFB6D40E`.

### 109.8 Rank-six exact coupled reduction

At the next rank the same algebra gives

```text
H_6(P)-H_5(B)
 =3Q_6(P)/p_5+9c_5+V_6(B)/b_4,

V_6(B)=4b_4b_5+39b_4b_6-25b_5^2.
```

Unlike `V_5`, the standalone `V_6` term is genuinely false: twelve exact
forest rows through order 18 have `V_6<0`, beginning with a 16-vertex tree
whose row is `(1,16,105,365,724,822,507,150,18,1)` and whose value is
`-195936`.  Thus rank six must retain the coupled payment from `Q_6(P)` or
`c_5`; the rank-five split cannot be iterated mechanically.  A complete
forest-polynomial pendant audit through total order 16 checks 198,037
required rank-six instances without failure, and an independent forest scan
finds `Q_6>=0` in all 347,429 rows through order 18 with `alpha>=10`.
Both are bounded evidence.  The all-order live target is the cleared
component-coupled inequality

```text
3b_4Q_6(P)+9c_5p_5b_4+V_6(B)p_5>=0.
```

The reduction and failure shield are recorded in
`RANK6_COMPONENT_PGC_COUPLED_REDUCTION_2026-08-13.md`.

### 109.9 Rank-six PGC is now closed all-order

The open cut in Section 109.8 has now been resolved.  Three exact inputs

First, for every forest `F` with `alpha(F)>=10`,

```text
Q_6(I(F))=12i_6^2-i_5i_6-14i_5i_7>=0.
```

The proof lifts the existing tree theorem through exact high/high,
low/high, and low/low factorial-convolution cones.  The two hard faces are
closed by complete 74-block and 89-block AM-GM allocations.  All 874 small

Second, every forest `B` with `alpha(B)>=10` satisfies

```text
V_6(B)=4b_4b_5+39b_4b_6-25b_5^2>=0.
```

For order at least 21 this follows from the sharp forest rank-(4,5) path
ratio and an extension-moment bound for a uniform independent four-set.
The disconnected path ratio is transferred rigorously by leaf-to-leaf
bridges and the sharp forest rank-(2,3) ratio.  The complete remaining
orders through 20 pass, with exact global minimum `343980`.  This theorem
is in `FOREST_V6_ALPHA10_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md` and is replayed by
`prove_forest_v6_alpha10.py`.

Finally, in a required rank-six pendant instance `alpha(G)>=10` and
`alpha(B)=alpha(G)-1`.  Only `alpha(B)=9` can therefore escape the preceding
split.  Bipartiteness forces `|B|<=18`.  Exact enumeration finds precisely
twelve negative `V_6` rows; their first two coefficients show that all are
connected, and all twelve are uniquely realized trees.  For a connected
`B`, forest structure forces the remaining deletion row to be either
`C=B` or `C=I(B-v)` for one vertex `v`.  Exhausting all 193 rooted
occurrences (157 distinct `(B,C)` rows) makes the coupled margin strictly
positive.  Its exact minimum is

```text
H_6(P)-H_5(B)=2306335815/123623>0.
```

This finite calculation is exhaustive, not merely bounded evidence: the
independence-number bound limits the order, the polynomial census lists all

Consequently PGC is proved for every required rank `2<=k<=6`; rank seven is

### 109.10 Rank-seven residual is positive from order 298 onward

The rank-seven decomposition introduces

```text
V_7(F)=9i_5i_6+105i_5i_7-72i_6^2.
```

There is now a simple universal large-order theorem for this term.  If `F`
is any forest of order `n`, the forbidden-edge union bound and `e(F)<=n-1`
give

```text
i_k(F)>=L_k:=C(n,k)-(n-1)C(n-2,k-2),   k=5,6,7,
i_6(F)<=C(n,6).
```

For `n>=298`, all three `L_k` are positive, and exact simplification yields

```text
V_7(F)
 >= 9L_5L_6+105L_5L_7-72C(n,6)^2
  = (n-5)(n-4)^2(n-3)^2(n-2)^2(n-1)^2
    (n^3-317n^2+5910n-23400)/28800.
```

At `n=298+r`, the cubic becomes

```text
r^3+577r^2+83390r+50504,
```

so it is strictly positive for every `r>=0`.  This proves `V_7(F)>0`
for every forest of order at least 298, without any independence-number
hypothesis.  The theorem and exact symbolic replay are
`FOREST_V7_LARGE_ORDER_UNION_BOUND_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md` and
`verify_forest_v7_large_order_union_bound.py`.

This is an all-order tail theorem, not a finite scan.  It confines the
standalone rank-seven residual problem to orders at most 297, but a sharper
ratio or coupled-deletion theorem is still needed because exhaustive forest
enumeration over that full boundary is not feasible.

### 109.11 The rank-seven residual tail is sharpened to order 25

The coarse cutoff in Section 109.10 is superseded by a two-stage discrete
extension-moment theorem.  For a uniform independent `k`-set `S`, put

```text
X=|V(F-N[S])|,   Y=i_2(F-N[S]),
mu_k=(k+1)i_(k+1)/i_k.
```

If `Phi` is the convex piecewise-linear interpolation of

```text
h(0)=h(1)=h(2)=0,  h(q)=C(q-1,2) for q>=3,
```

then exact double counting and Jensen give

```text
mu_(k+1) >= 2 Phi(mu_k)/mu_k.
```

Starting from the proved sharp forest rank-(4,5) path ratio and applying this
transfer twice proves

```text
V_7(F)=9i_5i_6+105i_5i_7-72i_6^2 >= 0
```

for every forest of order at least 26.  Order 25 is closed separately: a
leaf-to-leaf bridge correction handles disconnected forests; a quantitative
rank-(4,5) curvature reserve handles trees with `B_2>=2`; and the remaining
path plus 48 three-arm spiders are evaluated exactly.  The exact minima are
`6591506220` for the path and `7249560525` for the spiders.

The theorem and replay are `FOREST_V7_ORDER25_THEOREM_2026-08-13.md` and
`prove_forest_v7_order25.py`.  Together with the census through order 20,
only orders 21--24 remained at the time this theorem was written; their
complete connected and disconnected audit is the next finite closure step.

### 109.13 Half of the rank-seven terminal-broom Newton cone is proved

There is also a new all-order connected-tree reduction for `Q_7`.  If a
rooted tree `A` has root-deleted forest `H=A-q`, and `G_t` is obtained by
adjoining a support at `q` with `t>=1` new leaves, exact expansion gives

```text
7 c_6 h_5 Q_7(G_t)
 = R_t + 7 h_5 p_6(t) Q_7(A) + 8 c_6 p_6(t) Q_6(H),
```

where `R_t` has degree thirteen in `t` and admits the Newton expansion

```text
R_t=sum_(j=0)^13 binom(t-1,j) Delta^j R_1.
```

The previously proved `Q_6` theorem makes the last term nonnegative, so a
terminal-broom induction reduces to the fourteen coefficients
`Delta^j R_1`.  Exact extension inequalities, the rank-four through
rank-six reserves, and a 1,050-coefficient tensor-Bernstein certificate now
prove

```text
Delta^j R_1 >= 0 for every 7<=j<=13
```

for every rooted tree core of order at least 15.  Thus seven of the fourteen
Newton coefficients are closed all-order.  The lower coefficients
`Delta^0` through `Delta^6` remain open; the partial certificate must not be
mistaken for `R_t>=0`.

for a vertex `v` of `B`: two attachments to the same connected component

### 109.16 The forest lift for rank-seven `Q_7` is conditionally closed

The passage from connected trees to arbitrary forests has now been proved

```text
low/high, and low/low rank-seven convolution cones are nonnegative,
then Q_7>=0 for every forest with alpha>=12.
```

The high/high cone was already proved, and Sections 109.27 and 109.31 now

### 109.17 A rank-seven rooted cross theorem from order 39

The first new structural input for the seven open low Newton coefficients is
an all-order rooted-cross theorem.  For any tree `T` of order `n>=39` rooted
at `p`, put

```text
d=i_5(T), e=i_6(T), f=i_7(T),
h=i_5(T-p), k=i_6(T-p).
```

Then

```text
C_7(T,p)=d(e^2-df)-2e(eh-dk)>0.
```

The proof is short after the earlier rank-six and path-ratio theorems.  If
`Q_6=12e^2-de-14df` and
`S_7=d(2e+d)-28(eh-dk)`, exact expansion gives

```text
14C_7=eS_7+dQ_6.
```

For `F=T-N[p]`, write `a=i_4(F)`, `b=i_5(F)`, `x=e/d`, and
`L=(n-6)/5`.  Root deletion and extension counting imply

```text
eh-dk=db-ea,
(eh-dk)/d^2 <= (L-x)/(1+L).
```

The sharp rank-`(4,5)` path ratio and the proved extension transfer give

```text
x >= [(n-7)(n-8)/(n-3)-3+2(n-3)/((n-7)(n-8))]/6.
```

Substitution reduces `S_7/d^2` to

```text
[n^5-45n^4+75n^3+7923n^2-69788n+168234]
 /[3(n-8)(n-7)(n-3)(n-1)].
```

After `n=m+39`, the numerator has coefficients

```text
1, 150, 8265, 199218, 1780216, 65664,
```

all strictly positive; the endpoint value is exactly `1/62`.  The theorem
also has a degree-sensitive form because a root of degree `delta` improves
the extension ceiling to `(n-delta-5)/5`.  Exact shifted-numerator
certificates prove the staircase

```text
minimum root degree:  9  8  7  6  5  4  3  2  1
minimum order:       19 25 29 32 34 35 37 38 39.
```

Thus the middle band is confined to explicitly bounded small-root-degree
cells rather than every root.

The theorem
and exact replay are
`RANK7_ROOTED_CROSS_LARGE_ORDER_THEOREM_2026-08-16.md` and
`prove_rank7_rooted_cross_large_order.py`; the generated report is

are respectively
`85BFF49E993C36C8BA4CE7A24887A4E2BBAB93223AA5E7EF137916FC94B42489`,

### 109.18 The low-rank pendant decompositions are one uniform identity

The separate rank-five, rank-six, and rank-seven calculations are instances
of one exact all-rank formula.  For `P=(1+x)B+xC`, define

```text
Q_k(P)=2k p_k^2-p_(k-1)p_k-2(k+1)p_(k-1)p_(k+1),

V_k(B)=(k+2)b_(k-2)b_(k-1)
       +k(2k+1)b_(k-2)b_k
       -2(k-1)^2 b_(k-1)^2.
```

Then direct algebra gives

```text
H_k(P)-H_(k-1)(B)
 =kQ_k(P)/(2p_(k-1))
  +3k c_(k-1)/2
  +V_k(B)/(2b_(k-2)).
```

In extension means `mu_j=(j+1)b_(j+1)/b_j`, the last sign is equivalently

```text
V_k(B)/(b_(k-2)b_(k-1))
 =(k+2)+(2k+1)mu_(k-1)-2(k-1)mu_(k-2).
```

This is a reduction, not an all-rank sign theorem: both `Q_k` and `V_k` can
require literal forest coupling at their exceptional boundaries.  Its value
is that ranks above seven no longer require a new algebraic decomposition;
the uniform obligations are exactly the reserve `Q_k`, the affine
extension-mean residual `V_k`, and their positive pendant coupling.

The general residual already closes in a uniform high-extension chamber.
The proved forest two-extension lemma gives

```text
mu_(k-1)>=u-3+2/u,  u=mu_(k-2)>=2.
```

Hence

```text
V_k/(b_(k-2)b_(k-1))
 >=3u-(5k+1)+(4k+2)/u.
```

After multiplication by `u>0`, its sign is the sign of

```text
3u^2-(5k+1)u+4k+2.
```

The affine endpoint `u=(5k-1)/3` lies to the right of this quadratic's
vertex and gives the exact positive value `2(k+4)/3`.  Therefore the sharper
uniform theorem is

```text
mu_(k-2)>=(5k-1)/3  ==>  V_k(B)>0.
```

This improves the previous rational threshold by exactly `2/3`; the exact
radical threshold is the larger quadratic root.  Only the resulting smaller
bounded low-extension chamber can require literal `Q_k`/deletion coupling.

The standalone derivation and symbolic replay are

```text
(8b+ta+a3+a4+a5+a6) R_b1.
```

The quotient has 136,451 terms and 316 negative monomials.  An exact

and no overuse.  Hence this whole enlarged face is nonnegative.  Direct
one-variable reconstructions for `a,a0,a2,b2,...,b6` introduce no new
negative family, so `b1` is the unique forced one-variable enlargement.

### 109.26 Every all-rank `V_k` obligation has an explicit finite order band

The uniform residual of Section 109.18 admits a stronger all-order order
reduction.  For a uniform independent `s`-set `S` in an `n`-vertex forest,
there is an explicit injection from downward selected-parent incidences to
upward selected-child incidences.  Root each component at a vertex contained
in the largest number of independent `s`-sets.  The injection, together with
the root contribution, proves

```text
E[sum_(v in S) deg(v)] <= 2s-2s/n.
```

Since the neighbor set of `S` is no larger than its selected-degree sum,
the extension mean satisfies the new universal bound

```text
mu_s>=n-3s+2s/n.                                      (109.26a)
```

There is also an exact residual-moment identity.  For a uniform independent
`(k-2)`-set, let `X` be the residual vertex count, `C` the residual component
count, and `u=E X`.  Then

```text
V_k/(b_(k-2)b_(k-1))
 =3u-(5k+1)+(2k+1)(Var(X)+2E(C))/u.                  (109.26b)
```

Combining (109.26a) with the larger root

```text
rho_k=((5k+1)+sqrt(25k^2-38k-23))/6
```

of the transfer quadratic proves `V_k>=0` whenever

```text
n-3k+6+2(k-2)/n >= rho_k.                            (109.26c)
```

Thus, under the natural proposed range `alpha(F)>=2k-2`, every possible
failure lies in the explicit finite band

```text
2k-2 <= alpha(F) <= n <= N_k-1,
N_k=min{n>=2k-2: n-3k+6+2(k-2)/n>=rho_k}.
```

For example, the future rank-eight residual is now analytic from order 31,
and only orders 14--30 remain.  This is a genuine all-rank large-order
theorem, not a disposal of the rank-dependent finite bands and not a proof
of the separate `Q_k` reserve.

The theorem note, exact replay, report, and hash manifest are

### 109.29 The entire pendant cascade has one residual-forest moment form

The `Q_k+V_k` decomposition admits a still shorter all-rank formulation.
Choose a uniform independent `j`-set `S` in a forest and let `X` and `C` be
the vertex and nonempty-component counts of the residual forest `G-N[S]`.
With

```text
mu_j=E X=(j+1)i_(j+1)/i_j,
D_j=Var(X)+2E C,
```

an ordered compatible-extension-pair double count gives exactly

```text
mu_j-mu_(j+1)=3-D_j/mu_j.                         (109.29a)
```

Consequently, at `j=k-1`, the all-rank reserve and cascade functional are

```text
Q_k/(p_(k-1)p_k)=5-2D_(k-1)/mu_(k-1),             (109.29b)
H_k(P)=p_(k-1)(4mu_(k-1)-D_(k-1)).                 (109.29c)
```

In particular, `Q_k>=0` is precisely the residual Poincare bound

```text
Var(X)+2E C <= (5/2)E X.                           (109.29d)
```

For a pendant edge, PGC is therefore equivalent to one comparison between
the literal residual laws of `G` at rank `k-1` and `G-{l,p}` at rank `k-2`:

```text
p_(k-1)(4mu_P-D_P) >= b_(k-2)(4mu_B-D_B).          (109.29e)
```

This is an exact reduction, not the missing sign proof.  It shows that the
separate `Q_k` and `V_k` obligations are opposite sides of the same
variance-plus-components statistic and identifies a coupled switching or
martingale comparison (109.29e) as a possible uniform all-rank endpoint.

The literal pendant coupling gives an even sharper two-class identity.
Partition the independent `(k-1)`-sets of `G` according to whether they
contain the pendant leaf.  The containing class has size
`a=b_(k-2)` and its residual law is exactly the rank-`k-2` residual law in
`G-{l,p}`.  If the non-containing class has size `z` and residual statistics
`(mu_Z,D_Z)`, while the containing class has mean `mu_A`, variance
decomposition gives

```text
H_k(P)-H_(k-1)(B)
 =z(4mu_Z-D_Z)-az/(a+z)(mu_A-mu_Z)^2.             (109.29f)
```

Thus PGC is precisely the assertion that the non-leaf class pays one
explicit between-class variance penalty.  This retains more structure than
separate `Q_k,V_k` signs, but it is a residual-components normalization of
the earlier exact leaf-mixture PGC identity, not a strictly weaker target.
The Galvin local-payment counterexample still forbids discarding the
same-rank reserve inside the non-leaf term.  The replay checks (109.29f) in
811 exact pendant/rank setups in addition to its symbolic proof.

### 109.30 The second terminal-broom Newton coefficient is closed from order 39

For every rooted tree core `A` of order `n>=39`, the terminal-broom residual

successive ratios `x=c_3/c_4`, `y=c_4/c_5`, `z=c_5/c_6`, the defects
`D_4,D_5,q_6`, and the root-retention ratios `s=h_5/c_5,d=h_6/c_6`.
The sharp tree ratio bounds, rank-four through rank-six reserve bounds,
two-extension ceilings, path minimality, half retention, and rooted-cross
`C_7` theorem give a closed rational domain.

Separate root-variable concavity reduces `d` to its two endpoints.  On the
rooted-cross endpoint `d=s-q_6/2`, the exact second derivative

```text
-4y(49s+12z)(x+1)
```

reduces `q_6` as well; the independent `c_7` concavity handles the other
endpoint.  Splitting `|A-N[q]|<=17` from the complementary range leaves
eight exact rational Bernstein boxes.  All eight pass with nonnegative
cleared numerator and denominator tensors; the four large-root numerator
tensors contain `1,090,584` or `1,332,936` exact entries.

### 109.31 The complete rank-seven low/low convolution cone is closed

The second and final low convolution cone is now an exact theorem.  The old
hard face was not the complete negative-support locus: exact probes found
additional negatives involving `a0` and `b3` through `b6`.  The minimal
enlarged face is

```text
a=a2=b2=0.
```

It contains `15,205,643` nonzero coefficients and 790 raw negatives.  The
old 230 quotient AM-GM blocks pay 594 of them; 196 new, source-disjoint
midpoint blocks pay the other 196.  Hence the full enlarged face is
nonnegative.

A memory-bounded exact scan dehomogenizes the degree-fourteen margin and
slices on `(a,a0,a2,b2)`.  Eight disjoint range reports cover all 3,060
slices.  The exact merger proves no gaps or overlaps and checks every
exceptional slice against the standalone face reconstruction.  The full
cone has `118,516,125` nonzero coefficients.  Exactly `103,310,482` lie
outside the enlarged face; all are nonnegative, with minimum one.  The
largest recorded private-memory peak was `4.985 GiB`.

Together with the high/high certificate recorded in Section 109.16 and the
low/high theorem in Section 109.27, this closes all three convolution cones
required by the conditional
all-forest `Q_7` lift.  The connected-tree `Q_7` theorem and its finite
middle-order rooted-cross band remain separate obligations.

### 109.33 The fourth terminal-broom Newton coefficient is closed from order 39

For every rooted tree core of order at least 39,

```text
Delta^4 R_1>=0.
```

Root/D6 and conditional D5 concavity reduce the proof to sixteen exact
full-D4 endpoint branches.  Fifteen pass directly.  The sole rectangular
failure is the formal corner `D5,D6` lower and `(s,d)=(1,1/2)`.  It is not a
tree counterexample: for `J=A-N[q]`,

```text
c5-h5=i4(J),   c6-h6=i5(J),
```

so `s=1` forces `d=1`.  More quantitatively, extension counting gives

```text
d>=1-K(1-s),   K=(n-6)(c5/c6)/5>1.
```

Splitting at `s0=1-1/(2K)` leaves one new capacity-edge certificate.
After exact denominator clearing its Bernstein tensor has degrees
`(44,20,9,8,2)`, 255,150 entries, and minimum zero.  This repairs the only
fake corner while preserving its log as an enclosure no-go.

### 109.35 The all-forest rank-eight residual `V_8` is closed

For every forest `F` with `alpha(F)>=14`, writing
`I(F;x)=sum_j b_jx^j`, one now has

```text
V_8(F)=10b_6b_7+136b_6b_8-98b_7^2>=0.
```

The general residual-moment identity specializes to

```text
V_8/(b_6b_7)=3u-41+17(Var(X)+2E C)/u,
u=7b_7/b_6,
```

for the residual of a uniform independent six-set.  The sharp forest
rank-(4,5) path ratio followed by two exact discrete extension transfers
gives, at order 30,

```text
mu4>=506/27,  mu5>=4012/253,
mu6>=1533/118, mu7>=2222/219,
10+17mu7-14mu6>=7787/12921>0.
```

Monotonicity of the transfer map then proves `V_8>0` for every forest of
order at least 30, without an independence-number restriction.

Orders 14 through 29 were exhausted exactly.  In particular, the five
largest bands have the following eligible-forest counts and exact minima:

```text
order 26:    631,168,028     min 16,005,146,410
```

The order-27 through order-29 totals agree with independent Euler-transform

obligation only; `Q_8` and the complete rank-eight pendant step remain.
